✅ done & tested · 🟡 in progress / stubbed · ⬜ not started

## Phase 2 - Accounting Core
| Increment | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **2A** Chart of accounts | ✅ | `LedgerAccount` (composite AccountCode+CostCentre+Department via `AccountKey`; account types + normal-balance rules; `ControlAccountType`; manual-journal lock), CostCentre/Department dimensions; EF config + migration |
| **2B** Posting engine | ✅ | Central non-bypassable `IPostingEngine`: enforces **Dr=Cr in base + per transaction currency**, open-period check (per-module), account validity/active, control-account manual-journal block, currency restriction, FX rate lookup, gapless journal numbering, and `gl.period_balance` update - all in one transaction. `JournalEntry`/`JournalLine`/`GlPeriodBalance`. **7 accounting tests green** (balanced posts + TB nets to zero, unbalanced rejected, control-account block, sub-ledger posting, closed-period rejected, multi-currency, sequential numbering) |
| **2B++** Recurring journals | ✅ | `RecurringJournal`/`RecurringJournalLine` templates (code, description, source module, narrative, active flag, balanced lines) + `IRecurringJournalService`: `CreateAsync` (validates balance + unique code), `PostAsync` (generates a real journal through the posting engine each run), **`PostAllActiveAsync`** (month-end run - posts every active template on a date, one result line each), `SetActiveAsync`. For rent, standing charges, provisions. `AddRecurringJournals` migration. Tests: reject an unbalanced template; Dr rent / Cr bank 1000 template posted for two months → rent Dr 2000, bank Cr 2000, deactivate blocks further posts; a month-end run posts RENT + INSUR (skipping an inactive template) → expense Dr 1250; TB balances. |
| **2B+** Journal reversal | ✅ | `IPostingEngine.ReverseAsync(journalId, date, reason)` posts the exact inverse of a posted journal (every debit ↔ credit, original base amounts preserved) on the given date, links it via `JournalEntry.MarkAsReversalOf`/`IsReversal`, and leaves the **original Posted so both net to zero** in the trial balance (accrual/prepayment/error-correction style; the TB only counts Posted journals, so it must not exclude the original). Refuses a blank reason, a reversal-of-a-reversal, or a journal already reversed (detected by a linked reversal). No migration. Test: post a 500 salary accrual (Dr 6100 / Cr 2410) → reverse next month → both accounts net to zero, a linked reversal journal exists, blank reason + double reversal refused; TB balances. |
| **2C** Period control + year-end | ✅ | `IPeriodService` (open/soft-close/permanently-close per module, one-way lifecycle enforced), `IYearEndService` (validates, posts P&L closing journal to retained earnings, opens next year, permanently closes prior year). Year-end test green: profit → retained earnings, next year opened, prior year locked, statements still balance. Manual journals post via the engine directly. |
| **2D** Tax engine | ✅ | `TaxCode` with effective-dated rate history, types (Standard/Zero/Exempt/OutOfScope/ReverseCharge/Withholding/Deemed), input/output accounts, recoverable %, return-box, jurisdiction; `ITaxCalculator` (on-net + tax-inclusive extraction, rate resolved at tax point); `IFiscalDeviceProvider` hook + `NullFiscalDeviceProvider`. Migration applied. Tax test green (effective-dated 14%→15%, exempt, inclusive) |
| **2E** FX revaluation (IAS 21) | ✅ | `IFxRevaluationService`: retranslates foreign monetary accounts at the closing rate, posts the unrealised gain/loss to FX gain/loss control accounts, auto-reverses next period; non-monetary items excluded. FX test green (100 EUR @1.10→1.20 = +10 unrealised gain, balance restored after reversal). Realised FX on settlement lands with AR/AP in Phase 4 |

**Phase 2 status: COMPLETE.** Accounting core (COA, posting engine, period control, year-end, tax, FX revaluation) + the first four financial reports, all enforced and tested (14 accounting tests). The trial balance always balances.
| **2F** Trial Balance, Nominal Activity, Balance Sheet, P&L | ✅ | `IGeneralLedgerReports` query services over posted journals: Trial Balance (balances), Income Statement (net profit), Balance Sheet (balances, with current-year earnings in equity), Nominal Activity (opening/movements/running/closing). 4 report tests green (11 accounting total) |

## Phase 3 - Reporting Engine + Designer
| Increment | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **3A** Report definition model | ✅ | `ReportDefinition` (page setup, parameters, data sources, groups, bands, elements with conditional formatting) + `.mrpt` JSON serializer |

| **3C** Rendering engine + renderers | ✅ | Banded `ReportEngine` (multi-level grouping, scoped aggregates, expressions, conditional formats, suppression) → HTML, PDF (QuestPDF), CSV, plain-text/ESC-POS renderers. 16 unit tests green |
| **3D** Persist definitions + bind Phase 2 reports + bursting | ✅ | `rpt.report_definition` entity + migration; `SystemReports.TrialBalance()` definition; `ReportCatalogueSeeder` (idempotent); `IFinancialReportRenderer` renders the live Trial Balance to HTML/PDF/CSV/text through the engine (definition resolved user→company→system); `ReportBurster` splits a run by key. Verified with a rendered PDF. 6 more tests (72 unit + 16 accounting) |
| **1++** Permission-grid editor | ✅ | New `PermissionEditorWindow` + `PermissionGridViewModel` (Desktop, **Maintenance → Roles & Permissions…**): pick a role and tick, per catalogue feature, the common operations (View/Create/Edit/Delete/Print/Export/Post/Approve/Void-Reverse) in a `DataGrid` - each cell enabled only where the permission offers that operation, with a module/feature filter. Saves through the `Role` aggregate (`Grant`, which drops empty grants), writing the whole catalogue so stale grants are cleaned up; **non-common flags on a grant (authorise levels, price/credit overrides, back-dating) are preserved** so grid edits never silently drop them. Builds Debug+Release; UI glue over the already-tested `Role.Grant`/permission-resolution domain. |
| **1++** JWT API path | ✅ | The `One4All.Server` API now issues and validates JWTs. New `IJwtTokenService`/`JwtTokenService` (HMAC-SHA256, `JwtOptions` for issuer/audience/key/expiry) issues identity-only tokens (sub, unique_name, name, admin flag) from a successful `IAuthenticationService` login - permissions stay resolved server-side per request, so a token never grants more than the user holds. `Program.cs` wires embedded infrastructure (migrate + seed on startup), JWT-bearer auth, and minimal endpoints: `POST /api/auth/login` (anonymous → token), `GET /api/me` and `GET /api/reports` (`[Authorize]`). Claim remapping disabled so claim types stay verbatim. New `System.IdentityModel.Tokens.Jwt` (Infrastructure) + `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Authentication.JwtBearer` (Server) packages; the template WeatherForecast controller removed. Tests: `JwtTokenServiceTests` (issue → validate round-trip, short-key rejection, wrong-key failure). |
| **3.3** Approvals inbox | ✅ | The approver's worklist the workflow engine lacked. `IWorkflowService.PendingForUserAsync` lists the in-progress workflows whose current (value-band-applicable) stage the user may act on - the stage names them in `EligibleUserIds`, or names nobody - and that the user has not already approved, with the document, its value, the stage and how many of the stage's quorum approvals are already in. Pure read over the existing instances/definitions; eligibility mirrors `ActAsync`'s rule exactly. Test: `ApprovalsInboxTests` starts a workflow whose stage names one approver and asserts it appears in that approver's inbox (0 of 1 approvals) but not in another user's; after the approver acts, the inbox is empty. |
| **3.3** Approvals surface (API + desktop) | ✅ | The inbox exposed to users. Server: `GET /api/workflow/inbox?companyId&userId` returns the user's `PendingApproval` list; `POST /api/workflow/{instanceId}/act?userId&action&comment` forwards Approve/Reject/Return to `IWorkflowService.ActAsync` (200 with the new status, 404 unknown instance, 400 rule/forbidden failures via `ValueResponse`). Desktop: new `ApprovalsWindow` + `ApprovalsViewModel` (**Transactions → My Approvals…**) lists the signed-in user's inbox (document, value, stage, approvals-of-quorum, originator), with an optional comment and Approve / Reject / Return buttons enabled only when a row is selected; after each action the status line reports the resulting workflow status and the list reloads. Thin glue over the tested inbox/action services; build-verified Debug+Release. |
| **4.5** Credit control (review / hold / release) | ✅ | The credit-controller's run the ledger lacked. New `ICreditControlService`: `ReviewAsync(companyId, asAt, overdueToleranceDays)` examines every active customer not already on stop and places on hold those whose AR balance (debits less credits, base currency - the same measure the invoice-time check uses) exceeds a set credit limit, or who have debit items overdue by more than the tolerance; returns a `CreditReviewResult` with each `CreditHold` (reason OverLimit / Overdue / both, balance, limit, overdue amount, oldest-overdue days). The review never releases anyone; `HoldAsync`/`ReleaseAsync` are the hand controls, both reason-mandatory, refusing already-held / not-held customers. Sales-order entry now refuses a customer on stop (`Sop.CustomerOnHold`, mirroring AR's `Ar.CustomerOnHold`) - previously only invoicing did. Test: `CreditControlTests` (over-limit after a limit cut and a 90-day-overdue customer are held, a prompt payer is not; re-run skips held customers; held customer refused at order and invoice, released customer trades again, hand hold blocks a clean one). |
| **4.2/4.3** Stock replenishment | ✅ | Reorder suggestions that know what is already on its way. New `IReplenishmentService.SuggestAsync(companyId, warehouseId?)` lists active stock-tracked items with a reorder level whose **available** quantity - on hand plus the un-received balance of open (not cancelled/closed) purchase-order lines - is at or below the level, with the suggested quantity (the item's reorder quantity, else top-up to the level) and the item's last purchase price and supplier (most recent non-cancelled PO line). The existing INV reorder report only looked at on-hand, so goods already ordered were suggested again. `RaiseOrderAsync(companyId, supplierId, orderNumber, date, lines)` raises one PO through `IProcurementService` (ordinary PO lifecycle, supplier's currency), pricing each line at the given price or the last purchase price; refuses empty / non-positive / unknown-item requests. Test: `ReplenishmentTests` - an item covered by an open PO is not suggested; a low item carries on-hand including a GRN receipt, the *newer* PO's price, and its reorder quantity; the raised PO carries last price (explicit price wins) and takes the item out of the suggestions. |
| **4.13** Price lists & customer pricing | ✅ | A source of truth for selling prices (SOP lines took an explicit price with nothing behind it). New `PriceList` aggregate (`inv.price_list` / `inv.price_list_line`, migration `AddPriceLists`): per item, quantity-break prices in the list's currency; `SetPrice` replaces an existing break; `Resolve(item, qty)` picks the highest break not exceeding the quantity; one list per company flagged `IsDefault`. `Customer.PriceListId` (nullable) links a customer to a list. `IPricingService`: `CreatePriceListAsync` (unique code; making a list default clears the previous default), `SetPriceAsync` (min qty ≥ 1, price ≥ 0, item must exist), `AssignCustomerAsync` (refuses a list in a different currency from the customer), `PriceAsync(companyId, customerId?, itemId, qty)` → `ResolvedPrice` (price, list, break, whether the customer's own list supplied it) - customer list first, default second, `Price.NoPrice` when neither prices the item. Test: `PriceListTests` (breaks at 1/10/100, break replacement, customer list overriding the default with gap fall-through, currency mismatch, default switching, unlinking). |
| **4.13** Price-list pricing of sales orders | ✅ | The price lists now feed order entry. `SalesOrderLineRequest.UnitPrice` is nullable: a keyed price is taken as-is, an unkeyed one is resolved by `IPricingService.PriceAsync` for the order's customer and the line quantity (customer list → default list, honouring quantity breaks), failing with `Price.NoPrice` when nothing prices the item and `Sop.PriceCurrency` when the list's currency differs from the order's. Existing callers (quotation and opportunity conversion, tests) keep passing explicit prices unchanged. Test: `SalesOrderPricingTests` - two unpriced lines straddling a 20-unit break land at 12.00 / 10.00 while a keyed 9.99 is kept; an unpriced item is refused unless a price is keyed; a EUR order against a USD list is refused. |
| **4.2/4.13** Stock availability & back-order book | ✅ | What can actually be promised. New `IStockAvailabilityService`: `AvailabilityAsync(companyId, itemId, warehouseId?)` → `StockAvailability` (on hand; **allocated** = undespatched balance of lines on non-cancelled sales orders; on order = un-received balance of open POs; `Free` = on hand − allocated, may go negative when over-committed; `AvailableToPromise` = free + on order). `BackOrdersAsync(companyId)` → every undespatched line on non-cancelled orders, oldest order first, each with the item's on-hand and whether it **can be fulfilled after the orders ahead of it** (on-hand is allocated oldest-first, so a newer order shows its true shortfall). Pure read model over existing balances/orders. Test: `StockAvailabilityTests` - 50 received, 10 despatched, 20 + 40 still outstanding, 25 inbound → on hand 40, allocated 60, free −20, ATP 5; the older order is fulfillable, the newer shows a 20 shortfall. |
| **API/RPT** Credit, stock & pricing surface | ✅ | Today's services exposed. Server: `POST /api/credit/review?companyId&asAt&overdueToleranceDays`, `POST /api/credit/{customerId}/hold|release?companyId&reason` (new `ActionResponse` helper: 200 / 404 / 409 rule / 400), `GET /api/stock/replenishment?companyId&warehouseId?`, `GET /api/stock/{itemId}/availability?companyId&warehouseId?`, `GET /api/stock/back-orders?companyId`, `GET /api/pricing/price?companyId&customerId?&itemId&quantity`. Catalogue report **INV-BACKORDER** "Back-order Book" (Inventory; `BackOrders` command on `VerticalReportDataProvider`, which now takes `IStockAvailabilityService`): order, date, customer code, item, outstanding, on hand, Fulfil? Yes/No, shortfall. Test: `BackOrderReportTests` renders it through the runner (5 on hand vs 12 ordered → 7 shortfall, "No"). |
| **4.3/4.13** Order cancellation | ✅ | Both order aggregates had a `Cancelled` status nothing could reach. `SalesOrder.Cancel()` / `PurchaseOrder.Cancel()` (Core) refuse an order already cancelled or with any despatched / received quantity (goods have moved - return them first), and `RecomputeStatus` leaves a cancelled order cancelled. `ISalesOrderService.CancelOrderAsync` and `IProcurementService.CancelPurchaseOrderAsync` (reason mandatory, company-scoped lookup). Cancelling releases the stock commitment / inbound figure in availability and replenishment, which already excluded cancelled orders. Test: `OrderCancellationTests` - clean SO/PO cancel (allocated 18→8, on order 17→7), despatched / received ones refuse, reason required, double-cancel refused. |
| **UI** Credit Control screen | ✅ | Desktop **Enquiries → Credit Control…**: `CreditControlWindow` + `CreditControlViewModel` list every customer with exposure (from `ITradingReports.CreditExposureAsync`: limit or "Unlimited", balance, available, status On hold / Over limit / OK), with **Run review** (overdue tolerance in days, today's date; the status line names each customer placed on stop and why), and **Hold** / **Release** for the selected customer with a mandatory reason - each enabled only when it applies (Hold for a clean account, Release for a held one). Thin glue over `ICreditControlService`; build-verified Debug+Release. |
| **4.5/4.6** Statement of account document | ✅ | The printable statement the open-item `TraderStatement` report never had. Pure `StatementFormatter.ToPlainText(statement, companyName, asAt, width=64)` (Application): heading, trader, statement date and currency, one line per open item (date, type abbreviation INV/CRN/RCT/PAY, number, amount, outstanding), balance due, and an **ageing footer** from `StatementAgeing.Of(statement, asAt)` (Current / 1–30 / 31–60 / 61–90 / Over 90 by due date, credits netting in their bucket). `IStatementService.RenderCustomerStatementTextAsync` / `RenderSupplierStatementTextAsync` reuse `ITradingReports.CustomerStatementAsync` / `SupplierStatementAsync` (not-found → `Ar.CustomerNotFound` / `Ap.SupplierNotFound`). Tests: `StatementFormatterTests` (unit: buckets, width, truncation) and `StatementDocumentTests` (three invoices age to 100 current / 250 1–30 / 400 over-90, balance 750, every line ≤ 64 chars). |
| **4.3/4.13** Automatic order numbering | ✅ | Orders no longer need a hand-keyed number. `SalesOrderService` and `ProcurementService` take `INumberSequenceService`; a blank / whitespace `OrderNumber` allocates the next number from the company's `SO` / `PO` sequence (auto-created `SO-` / `PO-` prefix, 5-digit padding, gapless) and an explicit number is kept verbatim. Quotation and opportunity conversion are unaffected (they pass their own numbers). Test: `OrderNumberingTests` - two blank SOs get `SO-…00001` / `…00002`, a keyed `MY-SO-7` is kept; same for POs; the two sequences are independent. |
| **4.13** Pick list document | ✅ | What the warehouse picks from. Pure `PickListDocument` / `PickListLine` (`Short` = max(0, to-pick − on-hand), `IsComplete`) and `PickListFormatter.ToPlainText(doc, width=64)` (Application/SalesOrders): heading, order / customer / warehouse, one line per outstanding order line with quantity to pick, on hand in the picking warehouse, a tick box or `SHORT n`, and a COMPLETE PICK / PART PICK footer with picked-by / checked-by signature lines. `IPickListService.BuildAsync(companyId, salesOrderId, warehouseId)` lists only undespatched quantity against **that warehouse's** balances (refuses an unknown order / warehouse and a cancelled order); `RenderTextAsync` formats it. Tests: `PickListFormatterTests` (unit: shortage, completeness, width clamp) and `PickListTests` (8 widgets left after a part despatch vs 16 on hand; 8 gadgets vs 3 in Main → SHORT 5 while Other's 50 is ignored; cancelled order refused). |
| **API** Documents & cancellation endpoints | ✅ | Server: `GET /api/trading/customers/{id}/statement?companyId&asAt&width?` and `GET /api/trading/suppliers/{id}/statement` return the statement as `text/plain` (new `TextResponse` helper: 200 text, 404 / 409 / 400 by error type); `GET /api/sop/{orderId}/pick-list?companyId&warehouseId&width?` returns the pick list text; `POST /api/sop/{orderId}/cancel?companyId&reason` and `POST /api/pop/{orderId}/cancel` forward to the cancellation services via `ActionResponse` (409 for despatched / received / already-cancelled rules). Build-verified; the underlying services are tested. |
| **2** Intercompany journals & elimination | ✅ | Multi-company gained its missing link. `IIntercompanyService.PostAsync(IntercompanyJournalRequest)` posts the mirrored pair through the central engine - from-company **Dr due-from / Cr source account**, to-company **Dr target account / Cr due-to** - in **one database transaction** (the engine only calls SaveChanges, so a transaction on the scoped context covers both; a failure on either side rolls back both). Refuses same company both sides, non-positive amount, missing codes, unknown company, mixed base currencies. `IConsolidationService` gained an overload taking the intercompany account codes: when they net to zero across the group their rows are dropped and `IntercompanyEliminated` reports the gross; a residual is reported as `IntercompanyUnmatched` with the rows kept (never silently dropped). The three-argument `ConsolidatedTrialBalance` still constructs (new fields default to 0). Test: `IntercompanyTests` - parent funds subsidiary 1,000; each TB balances with its half; a second-side failure leaves no journal in either company; consolidation with elimination removes 1700/2700 and the bank nets away; eliminating only one side reports 1,000 unmatched. |
| **4.12** Percentage-of-completion revenue recognition | ✅ | Job costing recognised revenue only as certified; IFRS 15 over-time recognition now exists. Pure `PercentageOfCompletion.Compute(contractValue, budgetCost, actualCost, certifiedToDate, priorAdjustment)` → `PocCalculation` (cost-to-cost % capped at 100, 0 without a budget or costs; earned revenue; cumulative adjustment = earned − certified, positive = contract asset, negative = contract liability; `Movement` vs the prior run). `JobContract.RevenueAdjustmentToDate` (migration `AddContractRevenueAdjustment`) remembers the position. `IJobCostingService.RecogniseRevenueAsync(companyId, contractId, date, contractAssetAccount, contractLiabilityAccount, documentNumber)` posts one journal that **unwinds the prior position and books the new one** (asset: Dr asset / Cr revenue; liability: Dr revenue / Cr liability), so re-runs after new costs or certificates post only the difference and an unchanged position posts nothing (`DocumentType "POC"`, reference = contract number). Tests: `PercentageOfCompletionTests` (unit) and `RevenueRecognitionTests` - 25% complete vs 200k certified → 50k asset; re-run no-op; certify to 350k → asset unwound, 100k liability, revenue stays at the 250k earned; TB balances throughout. |
| **4.12** Budget revision & onerous-contract provision | ✅ | The forecast could not be revised and an expected loss was never provided. `JobContract.ReviseBudget(newBudgetCost)` (refuses negative or below cost incurred), `ExpectedLoss` (= max(0, budget − contract value)), cumulative `LossProvisionToDate` (migration `AddContractLossProvision`). Pure `ExpectedLoss.Compute(contractValue, budgetCost, priorProvision)` → `ExpectedLossCalculation` (`Movement`, `IsOnerous`). `IJobCostingService.ReviseBudgetAsync` (reason mandatory) and `ProvideForExpectedLossAsync(companyId, contractId, date, lossExpenseAccount, provisionAccount, documentNumber)` - IAS 37: the **whole** expected loss is provided at once (Dr expected-loss expense / Cr provision), later runs post only the movement, an improved forecast releases it (the reverse), no movement posts nothing (`DocumentType "ONEROUS"`). Tests: `ExpectedLossTests` (unit) and `OnerousContractTests` - profitable → nothing; revise to 1.12m → 120k provided; re-run no-op; revise to 1.05m → 70k released; revise to 0.95m → remaining 50k released; TB balances. |
| **4.7** Bank statement CSV import | ✅ | Statement import took pre-parsed lines; banks export CSV. Pure `BankStatementCsvParser.Parse(csv, BankCsvLayout)` (Application/Banking): a layout names delimiter, header, zero-based date / description / reference columns, a date format, and either a signed amount column or a debit + credit pair; parses RFC-4180 quoting (embedded delimiters, doubled quotes), thousands separators, bracketed negatives, blank lines; bad lines are **reported with line numbers**, never thrown (`BankCsvParseResult.Errors`, `IsClean`). `BankCsvLayout.Simple` = Date, Description, Amount, Reference. `IBankReconciliationService.ImportStatementCsvAsync(companyId, bankAccountId, csv, layout)` refuses an empty file, a file with no data lines, or **any** parse error (`Cb.StatementParse` listing the first five bad lines) so a partial statement is never imported, else reuses `ImportStatementLinesAsync`. Tests: `BankStatementCsvParserTests` (unit: simple layout, debit/credit + UK dates + semicolons, error numbering, invalid layout) and `BankStatementCsvImportTests` (clean file → 2 lines; file with a bad line → refused, nothing imported). |
| **API** Banking, intercompany & job period-end endpoints | ✅ | Server: `POST /api/banking/{bankAccountId}/statement-csv?companyId&dateColumn&descriptionColumn&amountColumn?|debitColumn&creditColumn&referenceColumn?&dateFormat?&delimiter?&hasHeader?` takes the raw CSV as the request body (`text/csv`) and builds the `BankCsvLayout` from the query (200 count / 400 parse errors listing bad lines); `POST /api/interco/post` (JSON `IntercompanyJournalRequest` → both journal numbers); `POST /api/jobs/{contractId}/recognise-revenue`, `/expected-loss` (query: date, accounts, document number → run result) and `/revise-budget?newBudgetCost&reason` (`ActionResponse`). Build-verified; services tested. |
| **UI** Stock Replenishment screen | ✅ | Desktop **Transactions → Stock Replenishment…**: `ReplenishmentWindow` + `ReplenishmentViewModel` list `IReplenishmentService.SuggestAsync` rows in a `DataGrid` (item, on hand, on order, reorder level, last price) with an **Order** tick and an **editable order quantity** pre-filled from the suggestion; a supplier combo (active, not on hold; selecting a row defaults it to that item's last supplier) and an order date; **Raise order** calls `RaiseOrderAsync` with a blank number (auto-allocated `PO-…`) for every ticked row with a positive quantity, reports the order number, and reloads so the items drop off the list. Thin glue; build-verified Debug+Release. |
| **4.5** Customer invoice / credit note document | ✅ | The one customer document the ledger never printed. Pure `InvoiceDocument` (+ `InvoiceDocumentLine`, `Net`, `Gross`) and `InvoiceFormatter.ToPlainText(doc, width=64)` (Application/Trading): TAX INVOICE / INVOICE / CREDIT NOTE heading with the issuer's VAT number, customer and their VAT number, number / date / currency / due date, narrative, numbered lines, Net / VAT / TOTAL, balance due (or unapplied credit) and payment terms. `IInvoiceDocumentService.BuildAsync(companyId, openItemId)` reads the AR open item (invoice or credit note only - `Ar.NotADocument` otherwise) and its posted journal: the AR-control leg is skipped, VAT-control / tax-coded legs sum to VAT, every other leg is a line named by its narrative or account name, in transaction currency; totals and outstanding come from the open item; `RenderTextAsync` formats it. Tests: `InvoiceFormatterTests` (unit) and `InvoiceDocumentTests` - invoice 1,000 + 150 VAT → one Sales line, VAT 150, gross and outstanding 1,150, due +30 days; credit note 200 + 30; a receipt is refused. |
| **UI/API** Customer Documents screen & invoice endpoint | ✅ | Desktop **Enquiries → Customer Documents…**: `CustomerDocumentsWindow` + `CustomerDocumentsViewModel` - pick a customer (combo), see their invoices and credit notes (type, number, date, due, amount, outstanding), **Preview document** renders the invoice / credit note via `IInvoiceDocumentService`, **Preview statement** renders the statement via `IStatementService` as at a chosen date, both into a monospace preview; **Save…** writes the preview to a `.txt` (suggested file name from the document). Server: `GET /api/trading/items/{openItemId}/document?companyId&width?` returns the invoice / credit note as `text/plain`. Thin glue over tested services; build-verified. |
| **4.3** Purchase order document | ✅ | The order a supplier actually receives. Pure `PurchaseOrderDocument` (+ `PurchaseOrderDocumentLine.LineTotal`, `Total`) and `PurchaseOrderFormatter.ToPlainText(doc, width=72)` (Application/Procurement): buyer with VAT number, supplier, order number / date / currency / status, numbered lines with item code, description, quantity, price and line total, ORDER TOTAL, payment terms and an authorisation line. `IPurchaseOrderDocumentService.BuildAsync(companyId, purchaseOrderId)` reads the order, supplier (terms), items and company (refuses a cancelled order); `RenderTextAsync` formats it. Server: `GET /api/pop/{purchaseOrderId}/document?companyId&width?` as `text/plain`. Tests: `PurchaseOrderFormatterTests` (unit) and `PurchaseOrderDocumentTests` (two-line order → codes, 45-day terms, total 165; cancelled refused). |
| **3.3** Period-close checklist | ✅ | What a controller checks before closing a period, in one call. `IPeriodCloseService.ChecklistAsync(companyId, periodEnd)` → `PeriodCloseChecklist` of `PeriodCloseCheck`s (code, name, count, value, blocking?, detail; `Passes`), with `IsReadyToClose` (no blocking check outstanding) and `Outstanding`. Checks, all as at the period end: **UnpostedJournals** (draft / deferred journals dated in or before the period - blocking), **UnsyncedOffline** (pending / failed offline POS operations - blocking), **UnreconciledBank** (statement lines dated ≤ period end not yet matched - blocking), **OpenStocktakes** (draft sessions dated ≤ period end - warning), **Grni** (PO lines received but not invoiced on live orders, with the accrued value - warning), **ApprovalsInProgress** (workflow instances in progress - warning). Pure read model over existing tables. Test: `PeriodCloseChecklistTests` - clean company passes all six; two in-period statement lines (an April line ignored), a 10 × 2.50 GRN without invoice (value 25.00) and an open approval → 3 outstanding, not ready; approving the workflow clears its check. |
| **UI/API** Period Close screen & endpoint | ✅ | Desktop **Utilities → Period Close Checklist…** (the first Utilities item): `PeriodCloseWindow` + `PeriodCloseViewModel` - period-end date (defaults to the last day of last month), **Run checks**, a green / red verdict banner ("Ready to close… / NOT ready… n blocking check(s)"), and the checks in a grid (outstanding count, value, Blocks close / Warning, Pass / Outstanding coloured, what is counted). Server: `GET /api/accounting/period-close?companyId&periodEnd` returns the checklist. Thin glue over the tested service; build-verified. |
| **3.2** Deferred journal release | ✅ | Deferred postings could be created (`JournalRequest.Deferred`) but never reached the ledger. `IPostingEngine.ReleaseDeferredAsync(companyId, journalId)` re-resolves the open period for the journal's module and date (a closed period refuses), marks it posted **keeping its already-allocated gapless number**, updates the period balances and returns the receipt; refuses a journal that is not deferred (`Posting.NotDeferred`). `ReleaseAllDeferredAsync(companyId, upToDate)` releases every deferred journal dated on or before the date, oldest first, continuing past failures and reporting them (`DeferredReleaseResult.Released / Failed`). This is what clears the checklist's blocking **UnpostedJournals** check. Test: `DeferredPostingReleaseTests` - three deferred journals are numbered but leave cash at 0 and the checklist at 3; releasing one moves cash to 100, a second release is refused; batch release to 31 March takes only the March one (cash 150, April stays deferred) and the March checklist clears. |
| **UI/API** Release deferred journals from the close screen | ✅ | The Period Close screen now fixes its own blocking check: a **Release deferred journals** button (enabled only when the Unposted journals check is outstanding) calls `IPostingEngine.ReleaseAllDeferredAsync` up to the chosen period end, reports released / failed journals in the status line, and re-runs the checks. Server: `POST /api/accounting/release-deferred?companyId&upToDate` returns the `DeferredReleaseResult`. Thin glue over the tested engine; build-verified. |
| **4.7** Smarter bank auto-matching | ✅ | The auto-matcher matched on exact amount alone - first candidate, no date check - so a January receipt could be matched to a June line of the same amount. New pure `BankAutoMatcher.Match(statementLines, glLines, dateToleranceDays=7)` (Application/Banking): a candidate must have the same signed amount **and fall within the date window** (negative = unlimited); among candidates one whose journal document number or reference equals the bank reference wins, then the closest date, then the earliest posting; each cash-book line is used once; statement lines are taken in date order. `GlLine` now carries the journal's document number and reference, and `AutoMatchAsync` gained an optional `dateToleranceDays` (default 7) so existing callers are unchanged. Tests: `BankAutoMatcherTests` (unit: window, closest date + single use, reference beats closer date and sign must agree) and `BankAutoMatchWindowTests` (two 300 cheques tied to their own postings by reference; a June 900 line ignores a January posting until the window is unlimited). |
| **4.7/UI** Bank reconciliation screen & work list | ✅ | The reconciliation finally has a screen. `IBankReconciliationService.WorkListAsync(companyId, bankAccountId, asAt)` → `BankWorkList` of id-bearing `StatementWorkItem`s (date, description, reference, amount, matched GL line id) and `GlWorkItem`s (unpresented postings: journal, document number, amount), derived exactly as the report is. Desktop **Transactions → Bank Reconciliation…**: bank-account combo and as-at date; cash-book / statement balance tiles and a **difference tile that turns green at zero**; **Auto-match** with a date-tolerance box; two lists (statement lines with Open / Matched status, unpresented postings) and **Match selected** / **Unmatch** on the chosen pair / line, reloading after each action. Server: `GET /api/banking/{id}/work-list?companyId&asAt`, `POST /api/banking/{id}/auto-match?companyId&dateToleranceDays?`. Test: `BankWorkListTests` (auto-matched deposit carries its GL id; a cheque with a folded-in fee stays open with its posting unpresented; manual match by the exposed ids clears it; unmatch restores it). |
| **3.4/UI** Account enquiry with journal drill-down | ✅ | Nominal activity existed only as a catalogue report; there was no way to open the journal behind a posting. `IGeneralLedgerReports.JournalDetailAsync(companyId, journalNumber)` → `JournalDetail` (number, date, status - any status, so deferred / reversed journals can be inspected -, module, document type / number, reference, narrative, **reversal links both ways**, and every line with account name, cost centre / department, base debit / credit, transaction currency and signed amount, tax code, narrative; debits first; `TotalDebit` / `TotalCredit`), null when the number is unknown. Desktop **Enquiries → Account Enquiry…**: account combo, from / to dates, Opening / Debits / Credits / Closing tiles, every posting with right-aligned amounts and running balance, and selecting a posting shows its whole journal (heading with status and "reverses / reversed by", lines, totals). Server: `GET /api/gl/accounts/{code}/activity?companyId&from&to`, `GET /api/gl/journals/{number}?companyId` (404 when unknown). Test: `JournalDetailTests` - a credit-first journal shows its debit first with names, narratives and signed transaction amounts; original ↔ reversal links resolve; a deferred journal reads as Deferred; unknown → null; every nominal-activity line drills to an existing journal and the deferred one is not on the ledger. *(First increment under Opus 5.5.)* |
| **4.6** Supplier statement reconciliation | ✅ | The AP clerk's month-end job: agree the supplier's statement to the purchase ledger. Pure `SupplierStatementReconciler.Reconcile(statementLines, ledgerItems, dateToleranceDays=7)` (Application/Trading), both sides signed the supplier's way (invoices +, payments / credits −): pass 1 pairs by document number compared on letters and digits only (`INV-0042` = `inv 0042` = `INV0042`); pass 2 pairs what is left by **equal amount within the date window, closest date first** - how a payment described in the supplier's own words ("Receipt – thank you") is agreed. Each `SupplierReconLine` is **Agreed**, **AmountDiffers**, **MissingFromLedger** (unposted invoice / unapplied payment) or **NotOnStatement** (in transit / wrong supplier), with statement and ledger amounts, our open item, whether it paired by amount, and its difference; mismatches list first. `SupplierReconciliation` carries both balances, `Difference` (always equal to the sum of line differences) and `IsAgreed`. `ISupplierStatementService.ReconcileAsync(companyId, supplierId, statement, tolerance)` loads the supplier's outstanding AP items (credit items +, debit items −, transaction currency). Server: `POST /api/trading/suppliers/{id}/reconcile-statement?companyId&dateToleranceDays?` (JSON statement lines). Tests: `SupplierStatementReconcilerTests` (unit: all four outcomes, the balance identity, date window, fully agreed) and `SupplierStatementReconciliationTests` (real postings: a paid-and-allocated invoice drops out of both sides; ledger 550 vs statement 665 → 115 = 25 price difference + 90 unposted invoice; the payment on account agrees by amount). |
| **4.5/4.6** Auto-allocation | ✅ | Receipts, payments and credit notes had to be allocated one pair at a time. Pure `AllocationPlanner.Plan(debits, credits)` (Application/Trading): pass 1 pairs a debit and credit whose outstanding amounts are **identical** (a receipt for exactly one invoice), earliest first; pass 2 applies the rest **oldest-due first**, credits in due-date order against debits in due-date / date / id order, splitting as needed; never exceeds either side; symmetric, so AR (invoices are debits) and AP (invoices are credits) both work. `IAutoAllocationService.AllocateTraderAsync(companyId, ledger, traderId, date)` and `AllocateLedgerAsync(companyId, ledger, date)` (only traders with both sides outstanding) plan per currency and **execute each pair through the ordinary `IAllocationService`**, so realised FX and the audit trail are identical to a manual allocation; items dated after the allocation date are left alone; a refused pair is reported and the run carries on (`AutoAllocationResult`: traders, allocations, exact matches, amount, failures). Server: `POST /api/trading/auto-allocate?companyId&ledger&traderId?&date`. Tests: `AllocationPlannerTests` (unit: exact beats oldest, no over-allocation, empty sides, AP symmetry) and `AutoAllocationTests` (receipts 250 + 300 and a 50 credit note against invoices 100 / 250 / 400 → 4 allocations, 1 exact, 600 allocated, 150 left on the newest; re-run no-op; ledger run skips an invoice-only customer; AP 500 payment settles the 500 invoice, older 300 stays open). |
| **UI** Supplier Statement Reconciliation screen | ✅ | Desktop **Transactions → Supplier Statement Reconciliation…**: supplier combo; paste the supplier's statement as one document per line (number, date, amount; date format, header-row and tab-separated options for spreadsheet pastes); **Reconcile** shows their total, our ledger and a difference tile that turns green when everything agrees, and every line coloured by outcome (Agreed / Agreed by amount / Amount differs / Not on our ledger / Not on their statement) with both amounts and the difference; **Auto-allocate supplier** applies their payments and credits first. Pasted text is read by new pure `SupplierStatementText.Parse(text, dateFormat, hasHeader, delimiter)` (Application/Trading), which reuses the tested bank CSV parser (quoting, thousands separators, bracketed negatives) and reports bad lines rather than throwing. Test: `SupplierStatementTextTests` (unit: comma with quotes and bracketed credit, tab paste with header and UK dates, bad lines reported). |
| **4.13** Price list maintenance | ✅ | Price lists could only be edited one break at a time. Pure `PriceAdjustment.Apply(price, percent, roundTo, PriceRounding)` (Application/Inventory): applies a percentage change then snaps to a **price-point increment** (0.05, 0.50, 1.00 …; 0 = cents) rounding Nearest (halves away from zero) / Up / Down; refuses −100% or below. `IPricingService.AdjustPricesAsync(companyId, priceListId, percent, roundTo, rounding, itemIds?)` re-prices every break on a list (or only chosen items) and returns how many changed; `CopyPriceListAsync(companyId, sourceId, code, name, percent, roundTo, rounding)` creates a new non-default list in the source's currency with every break copied and adjusted - e.g. a trade list at −10%. Server: `POST /api/pricing/lists/{id}/adjust?companyId&percent&roundTo?&rounding?&itemIds?`, `POST /api/pricing/lists/{id}/copy?companyId&code&name&percent&roundTo?&rounding?`. Tests: `PriceAdjustmentTests` (unit theory: 8 cases across increments and directions, 0% still snaps, −100% refused) and `PriceListMaintenanceTests` (+5% up-to-0.05 on widgets only → 10.50 / 9.45, gadget untouched; copy at −10% nearest 0.10 → 9.50 / 8.50 / 18.00 priced for the assigned customer; standard stays default; duplicate code and −100% refused; 0% change moves nothing). |
| **4.14 FIX** Depreciation run guards | ✅ | **Defect fix.** The monthly depreciation run charged every active asset regardless of date: an asset acquired in August was depreciated by a June run, and running the same month twice charged it twice. `FixedAsset.LastDepreciatedPeriod` (`yyyyMM`, migration `AddAssetLastDepreciatedPeriod`) now records the last month charged; `SkipReasonFor(runDate)` returns `NotActive`, `NotYetAcquired` (acquired after the run month - **full-month convention**: the acquisition month itself is charged) or `AlreadyDepreciated` (that month or a later one already charged); `ApplyDepreciation(charge, runDate)` stamps the period. `RunDepreciationAsync` skips accordingly, so a re-run of a month posts nothing, and `DepreciationRunResult` reports `SkippedNotYetAcquired` / `SkippedAlreadyDepreciated` (defaulted, so existing callers are unchanged). Tests: `DepreciationScheduleRuleTests` (unit: acquisition month, charged month, disposed / fully depreciated, period key) and `DepreciationRunGuardTests` (Feb run charges only the January van and skips the March laptop; a second Feb run posts nothing; March charges both 1,100; accumulated 2,100 equals the 1650 credit on a balanced TB). |
| **4.14 FIX** Depreciation forecast timing | ✅ | **Defect fix**, the forecast's twin of the run fix: `DepreciationForecastAsync` charged every active asset from the first forecast month, so an asset bought later in the window and a month the run had already charged were both forecast - overstating near-term depreciation. Each simulated month now applies the run's rule: no charge before the asset's acquisition month, none for a period ≤ `LastDepreciatedPeriod` (closing NBV still includes registered assets). Test: `DepreciationForecastTimingTests` - van charged Jan–Feb, forklift bought in April: Feb 0, Mar 1,000, Apr / May 1,500, NBV 28,000 → 24,000; the March run then posts exactly the forecast 1,000. The existing forecast test is unchanged. |
| **4.15 FIX** Payroll period guard | ✅ | **Defect fix.** `IPayrollService.RunAsync` had no guard: running a period twice produced a second set of payslips and a second net-pay journal - every employee paid twice. The run now trims the period label, refuses a blank one (`Payroll.PeriodRequired`) and refuses a period that already has payslips (`Payroll.PeriodAlreadyRun`, a conflict) before anything is built or posted; the existing (company, employee, period) payslip index is now **unique** (migration `MakePayslipPeriodUnique`) as the database backstop. Test: `PayrollPeriodGuardTests` - blank refused; 2026-01 pays 1,500; a re-run (and one with stray whitespace) is refused; 2026-02 runs; two payslips in total and net-pay control 3,000 on a balanced TB. |
| **4.16 FIX** Municipal billing run guard | ✅ | **Defect fix.** `RunFixedChargeBillingAsync` numbered bills `{prefix}-{property}-{yyyyMMdd}` and never checked for an existing bill, while the sales ledger accepts duplicate document numbers - so re-running a cycle (or running it on another day of the month) billed every ratepayer again. Billing is now **monthly**: bills are numbered `{prefix}-{property}-{yyyyMM}`, the numbers already on the receivables ledger for the company are loaded in one query, and those properties are skipped and counted (`MunicipalBillingRunResult.AlreadyBilled`, defaulted so callers are unchanged); a blank prefix is refused (`Mun.PrefixRequired`). Test: `MunicipalBillingRunTests` extended - a second March run on another day bills nobody (`AlreadyBilled` 2) and refuse revenue stays at 30 on a balanced TB. |
| **4.6** Duplicate supplier-invoice control | ✅ | The purchase ledger accepted the same supplier invoice any number of times - the classic route to paying a supplier twice. Both entry routes now refuse it: `IPurchaseLedgerService.PostInvoiceAsync` and the three-way match `IProcurementService.MatchInvoiceAsync` check the supplier's existing AP invoices and return `Ap.DuplicateInvoice` (a conflict naming the number it clashes with) before anything posts; a blank invoice number is refused (`Ap.InvoiceNumberRequired`). Numbers compare through new pure `DocumentNumbers.Key / Same` (Application/Trading - letters and digits only, case-insensitive, so `INV-0042` = `inv 0042` = `INV/0042`; empty never matches), which the supplier statement reconciler now shares instead of its private copy. Other suppliers may reuse a number; recurring purchase invoices are numbered per month so they are unaffected. Tests: `DocumentNumbersTests` (unit theory) and `DuplicateSupplierInvoiceTests` (re-keyed duplicate refused via the ledger and via the three-way match; another supplier's same number accepted; only 0042 / 0043 / 0044 exist afterwards). |
| **4.5 FIX** Finance charges: each day charged once, no interest on interest | ✅ | **Defect fix.** `RaiseChargesAsync` charged interest from each item's due date to the run date on every run, so monthly runs re-charged the same days (month 1 charged 30 days, month 2 charged 60 on the same balance - 90 billed for 60 elapsed), a re-run of the same date charged again, and earlier finance charges - themselves overdue invoices - earned interest too. `OpenItem.InterestChargedTo` (migration `AddOpenItemInterestChargedTo`) records how far each item has been charged; a run charges only the days from the later of the due date and that date, and advances it on the items it charged (a customer below the minimum charge keeps the days for a later run). The finance-charge item is reclassified `OpenItemType.Interest` (`MarkAsInterestCharge`, AR debit invoices only) and interest items are excluded from the interest-bearing balance; they still age, appear on statements (INT) and now print through the invoice document and the Customer Documents screen ("Finance charge"). `MaxDaysOverdue` still reports the true age. Test: `FinanceChargeRerunTests` - 31 Jan charges 9.86; a same-day re-run charges nothing; 2 Mar charges 9.86 (30 days, not 60, nothing on January's charge) with max days 60; two Interest items; interest income 19.72 on a balanced TB; the charge prints. The existing single-run test is unchanged. |
| **4.7** Post cash-book entries from statement lines | ✅ | A statement line the books did not yet have had to be keyed elsewhere and then matched. New `IBankStatementPostingService` posts the entry **and** matches it in one database transaction (never one without the other): `PostCustomerReceiptAsync` (money-in only; receipt through the sales ledger, unallocated credit on the customer's account), `PostSupplierPaymentAsync` (money-out only; payment through the purchase ledger) and `PostToAccountAsync` (either direction, cash-book journal against a nominal account - bank charges, interest, VAT paid, transfers; **AR / AP / stock / GRNI controls are refused** so sub-ledgers stay in step). Each posts at the statement date in the bank's currency, numbers the document as given, else the bank's reference, else `BNK-{line}`, finds the journal's bank leg and matches the line to it; a reconciled line, wrong direction or unknown line / bank is refused before anything posts. Server: `POST /api/banking/{bank}/lines/{line}/post-receipt?companyId&customerId&documentNumber?`, `/post-payment?…&supplierId…`, `/post-to-account?…&accountCode&narrative?`. Test: `BankStatementPostingTests` - 640 receipt (numbered from the bank reference), 215 debit order to a supplier and 12.50 fee to expense all post and match; reconciliation difference 0 with nothing unmatched or unpresented; wrong-direction, control-account and re-post attempts refused; TB balanced. |
| **UI** Post statement lines from the Bank Reconciliation screen | ✅ | The reconciliation screen gains a **"Not in the books yet? Post the selected line as…"** panel under the statement lines: a kind (Customer receipt / Supplier payment / Post to account - defaulted from the selected line's direction), a counterparty picker that refills with customers, suppliers or the chart of accounts to suit the kind, an optional document number / narrative (blank uses the bank reference), and **Post & match**, enabled only for an open line with a counterparty chosen; it calls `IBankStatementPostingService`, reports what was posted, and reloads balances and lists. `StatementRowVm` now carries the raw signed amount. Thin glue over the tested service; build-verified. |
| **4.7** Bank rules | ✅ | Recurring statement lines (fees, interest, standing orders) no longer need hand-posting. New `BankRule` (`cb.bank_rule`, migration `AddBankRules`): description phrase (case-insensitive *contains*), direction (Either / MoneyIn / MoneyOut), optional absolute-amount range, optional bank account (null = every bank), target account, optional narrative, priority, active flag. Pure `BankRuleMatcher.Match(rules, bankAccountId, description, amount)` (Application/Banking): lowest priority wins, then a bank-specific rule over a general one, then the longest (most specific) phrase, then lowest id; another bank's rule never applies. `IBankRuleService`: `CreateRuleAsync` (refuses blank phrase / account, unknown account or bank, inverted range, and AR / AP / stock / GRNI controls), `SetActiveAsync`, `ListAsync`, and `ApplyAsync(companyId, bankAccountId, upToDate)` - every open line on or before the date that a rule recognises is posted to the rule's account **and matched** through `IBankStatementPostingService` (each in its own transaction); refusals are reported and the run carries on. Server: `GET/POST /api/banking/rules`, `POST /api/banking/rules/{id}/active`, `POST /api/banking/{bank}/apply-rules?companyId&upToDate`. Tests: `BankRuleMatcherTests` (unit: phrase case, direction, range, priority / bank-specific / longest-phrase / other-bank) and `BankRuleTests` (validation; March run posts the fee to 6800 and interest to 4800 and matches both, leaves the customer EFT and April's fee open; re-run does nothing; deactivated rule ignores April; TB balanced). |
| **UI** Bank Rules screen & Apply rules | ✅ | Desktop **Utilities → Bank Rules…**: `BankRulesWindow` + `BankRulesViewModel` list the rules in the order they are tried (priority, name, phrase, direction, amount range, account, bank or "All banks", Active / Off) with **Switch on / off**, and a form to add one (phrase, optional name, direction, optional amount range, applies-to bank or all, post-to account picker, narrative, priority) validated by `IBankRuleService`. The Bank Reconciliation screen gains **Apply rules** beside Auto-match: runs the rules up to the as-at date, reports how many lines were posted and matched (and the first refusal), and reloads. Thin glue over tested services; build-verified. |
| **3.4 FIX** Control reconciliation as at a past date | ✅ | **Defect fix.** `ControlReconciliationAsync` (report GL-CTRL) compared the GL control balance *at the date* with sub-ledgers *as they stand today*: AR / AP used each item's current outstanding (so allocations made after the date reduced a past balance) and stock used today's stock balances regardless of the date - a March reconciliation run in May showed false differences. Sub-ledgers are now rebuilt as at the date: AR / AP = every item dated by then at its original base amount, less the base drawn by allocations made by then that had not been reversed by then (`Allocation.ReversedOn`); stock = the sum of stock-movement values dated by then. Test: `ControlReconciliationAsAtTests` - after an April receipt, supplier payment (both allocated) and stock issue, March still reconciles AR 1,000 / AP 500 / stock 200 (previously 600 / 0 / 140 on the sub-ledger side), and April reconciles 600 / 0 / 140. The existing current-date test is unchanged. |
| **4.5/4.6 FIX** Ageing and credit exposure as at a past date | ✅ | **Defect fix**, the trading-report twin of the control-reconciliation fix: aged debtors / creditors and credit exposure took an as-at date but read each item's *current* outstanding, so a receipt allocated after the date made a past ageing understate what was owed then (and an un-allocation after the date did the opposite). New internal `OpenItemsAsAt.LoadAsync` (Infrastructure/Trading) rebuilds a ledger's items as they stood at a date - original base less allocations made by then and not reversed by then - which is exact because outstanding amounts only ever change through allocations (verified: `ReduceOutstanding` / `RestoreOutstanding` are called only by allocate / un-allocate). Ageing, credit exposure and the control reconciliation now all use it, so the three can never disagree. Test: `AgeingAsAtTests` - a January invoice paid and allocated on 20 April still ages 1,000 in 31–60 days and shows 1,000 exposure at 31 March, is settled at 30 April; after an un-allocation on 10 May, April stays settled, March still shows 1,000, and May nets to nil. All 27 existing ageing / exposure test files still pass. |
| **6B** Audit trail query & hash-chain verification | ✅ | The change log was SHA-256 hash-chained but nothing ever verified the chain outside tests, and there was no way to read it bar one receipting report. New `IAuditService`: `QueryAsync(AuditQuery)` filters a company's trail by date range, entity type / id, user (case-insensitive), action, newest first, capped at 5,000 (`AuditEntryView`: when, who - "system" when none -, action, entity, changed fields, old / new JSON, reason, terminal); `VerifyChainAsync(companyId?)` walks the company's chain in id order in batches of 2,000, recomputing each entry from its predecessor's stored hash (the first from empty) via `ChangeLogEntry.VerifyAgainst`, and reports the **first broken entry** with a one-line description, or "intact: n entries verified". Server: `GET /api/audit?companyId&from?&to?&entityType?&entityId?&userName?&action?&take?`, `GET /api/audit/verify?companyId?`. Test: `AuditTrailTests` - a customer's insert and credit-limit update come back newest first with the changed field and old / new values; action filter and `Take` work; the chain verifies intact; after the update row is rewritten by direct SQL (2,500 → 25,000) verification fails **at exactly that entry**. |
| **6B** Append-only change log (database-enforced) | ✅ | Prevention to go with detection: migration `MakeChangeLogAppendOnly` adds `audit.change_log_append_only()` and two triggers on `audit.change_log` - `BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE … FOR EACH ROW` and `BEFORE TRUNCATE … FOR EACH STATEMENT` - that raise *"audit.change_log is append-only: <op> is not allowed"* (SQLSTATE insufficient_privilege) whoever issues the statement; the application only ever inserts, so nothing legitimate is affected (Down drops both and the function). Anyone able to switch the trigger off can still alter a row, which the hash chain then exposes. Test: `AuditTrailTests` extended - an ordinary UPDATE and DELETE on the log now throw; with the trigger disabled (as the table owner could), the rewrite succeeds and `VerifyChainAsync` still pinpoints the altered entry; the trigger is re-enabled in `finally`. |
| **UI** Audit Trail screen | ✅ | Desktop **Utilities → Audit Trail…**: `AuditTrailWindow` + `AuditTrailViewModel` - filters for date range (default last week), record type, record id, user and action; entries newest first (local time, user, action, record type #id, changed fields), capped at 1,000 with a prompt to narrow; selecting an entry shows a **before / after** table built from its old / new JSON (unparseable values shown raw); a **Verify chain** banner runs `IAuditService.VerifyChainAsync` and turns green ("intact: n entries verified") or red with the broken entry. Thin glue over the tested service; build-verified. |
| **3.2** Posted journals immutable (database-enforced) | ✅ | The brief's "no other way to write to the ledger" is now enforced below the application: migration `MakePostedJournalsImmutable` adds `gl.journal_posted_guard()` / `gl.journal_line_posted_guard()` and `BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE` row triggers on `gl.journal` and `gl.journal_line`. For a journal that is Posted (2) or Reversed (3): its lines cannot be deleted, and no line's journal, account, cost centre, department, currency, rate, transaction / base / reporting amounts or tax code can change; the journal cannot be deleted, and its company, number, batch, module, document type / number, reference, narrative, posting date, currencies, fiscal year / period and entry timestamp cannot change. Allowed: Posted → Reversed, setting the reversal link once (`is_reversal` false → true, link null → value), audit stamps - exactly what `ReverseAsync` and `ReleaseDeferredAsync` do. Draft and deferred journals are untouched until posted. Errors read *"Journal … is posted and cannot be altered / deleted; reverse it instead"*. Test: `PostedJournalImmutabilityTests` - SQL edits of a posted line's amount and account, line and journal deletes, and narrative / date / status rewrites are all refused; a deferred journal's narrative can still be edited, then it is released and is protected; the posted one is reversed normally; TB balanced (cash 40). The whole existing suite - reversals, deferred releases, year end, every posting path - passes with the triggers in place. |
| **FIX** Journal numbering across open years | ✅ | `NumberSequence.Allocate` kept one counter and restarted it at 1 whenever the document year changed, so a late adjustment into the prior (still open) year followed by a new-year posting re-issued `JNL-2026-000001` and failed on the unique `(company, journal_number)` index. Yearly-reset sequences now keep a counter per year (`NumberSequenceYear`, owned collection, `sys.number_sequence_year`, migration `AddNumberSequenceYearCounters`); `CurrentYear` / `NextValue` mirror the year last used, so every allocation still changes the parent row and its `xmin` check serialises concurrent allocators. The migration seeds each sequence's live counter and rebuilds earlier journal years from the highest `JNL-yyyy-n` already issued. Tests: unit `NumberSequenceTests` (continuous vs per-year, alternating years); accounting `JournalNumberingAcrossYearsTests` - Dec 2026 / Jan 2027 / Dec / Jan / Dec postings number 2026-1, 2027-1, 2026-2, 2027-2, 2026-3; TB balanced. |
| **FIX** Recurring journals post once per month | ✅ | Continuing the periodic-run audit: `PostAllActiveAsync` re-posted every template when a month-end run was repeated, and a run that failed part-way could only be retried by re-posting the templates that had succeeded. `RecurringJournal.LastPostedPeriod` (yyyyMM, migration `AddRecurringJournalLastPostedPeriod`, backfilled from posted `Recurring` journals numbered with the template code) makes posting forward-only, as for depreciation: `PostAsync` refuses the same or an earlier month (`Gl.RecurringAlreadyPosted`); the run skips such templates and reports them with `RecurringRunLine.AlreadyPosted`. The mark is set before posting so the engine's single save persists it with the journal, and is discarded (entry reload) if the post fails. Test: `RecurringJournalRerunTests` - March run then a repeat (both skipped), single re-post for March / February refused; April run fails on a template whose account is missing after posting the others, the failed template is not marked, and once the account exists a re-run posts only it; TB balanced (6300 = 2,550). |
| **FIX** School term billing bills each student once | ✅ | Periodic-run audit: `BillTermAsync` raised a fresh invoice for every enrolled student each time it ran, so a repeated run - or a retry after one student failed - billed guardians twice. The run now requires a prefix identifying the term (`School.PrefixRequired`), skips students whose number (prefix + admission number) is already on the receivables ledger and reports them in `TermBillResult.AlreadyBilled`; `BillStudentAsync` refuses a number already raised (`School.AlreadyBilled`). Same approach as the municipal billing run. Test: `SchoolTests` extended - a repeated run bills nobody (2 already billed, 0 net), blank prefix refused, single re-bill refused; debtors still 1,750. Audited and safe in the same pass: FX revaluation (posts only the difference to the closing rate), VAT settlement (its own journal zeroes the period's VAT movement), dunning (stateless assessment). |
| **3.2** Check data (ledger integrity) | ✅ | Sage-style *Check Data*: `ILedgerIntegrityService.CheckAsync(companyId)` recomputes every derived figure from the records it comes from and lists disagreements as `IntegrityIssue`s (check, severity, subject, expected vs stored, explanation), errors first. Checks: **unbalanced journal** (posted / reversed journals whose base debits ≠ credits - error); **journal number gap** (pure `JournalNumberGaps.Find/Describe` in Application/Accounting: per-year runs missing from `JNL-yyyy-n`, including a missing start - warning); **period balance** (cached `gl.period_balance` vs the posted lines grouped by period + account + cost centre + department, base and reporting - error); **stock quantity** (on hand vs Σ movements, exact - error) and **stock value** (vs Σ movement values beyond a cent per movement of average-cost rounding - warning); **open item outstanding** (stored outstanding vs original less unreversed allocations, the only path that changes it - error). `RebuildPeriodBalancesAsync` is the one automatic repair (balances are purely derived; `GlPeriodBalance.Restate`): corrects, creates or removes rows. Stock and open-item differences are reported for investigation, not repaired. Endpoints `GET /api/accounting/check-data`, `POST /api/accounting/check-data/rebuild-period-balances`; desktop **Utilities → Check Data…** (verdict banner, coverage line, findings grid, *Rebuild period balances* when applicable; build-verified). Tests: unit `JournalNumberGapsTests` (contiguous, runs per year incl. leading gap, ignored formats); accounting `LedgerIntegrityTests` - after AR / AP invoices with allocations, stock at rounding costs, a released deferred journal and a reversal the company is clean and a rebuild has nothing to do; then a period balance, a stock quantity, an open item's outstanding, a deleted deferred journal (number gap) and a posted line edited with its guard trigger disabled are each reported (5 errors, 1 warning); a rebuild corrects the 2 period balances and only the other 3 errors remain. |
| **3.3** Revalue foreign-currency debtors and creditors | ✅ | The GL revaluation only covered accounts restricted to a foreign currency, so foreign-currency receivables and payables sat at historic rates at period end (IAS 21 requires monetary items at the closing rate). `ITradingRevaluationService.RevalueAsync(companyId, ledger, asAtDate, autoReverse = true)` rebuilds each foreign open item's outstanding at the date in its own currency and at its booked base (`OpenItemsAsAt` now also returns the transaction-currency outstanding), retranslates at the closing rate and books the total difference on the AR / AP control against FX gain / loss (`TradingRevaluation`, reversed next day as `TradingRevaluationReversal`, both in one DB transaction). The open items keep their historic base, so allocation still realises the exchange difference as before and check data is unaffected. The run posts only the target less what is already booked on the control at the date, so a repeat posts nothing. The control reconciliation gains a `Revaluation` reconciling item (`Difference` = GL − sub-ledger − revaluation). Endpoint `POST /api/trading/revalue`. Test: `TradingRevaluationTests` - EUR 1,000 AR invoice at 1.10 with EUR 400 received leaves EUR 600 booked 660, revalued 720 at 1.20 (gain 60); EUR 500 AP booked 550 → 600 (loss 50); a March run is refused (`Reval.NoClosingRate`); a repeat AR run posts 0; the reconciliation at 30 June shows 720 / 660 / 60 / 0 and 600 / 550 / 50 / 0, and on 1 July 660 / 660 / 0; TB balanced with 7100 Cr 60 and 7200 Dr 50; check data clean. |
| **3.3** Revaluation on the period-close checklist | ✅ | `ITradingRevaluationService.PreviewAsync` works out the revaluation without posting (shared computation with `RevalueAsync`). The period-close checklist gains a **Foreign balances not revalued** warning (`ForeignRevaluation`): for AR and AP it previews the revaluation at the period end and counts the foreign items when anything is unbooked (value = unbooked total), or reports a missing closing rate; companies without FX accounts are skipped. Desktop **Period Close Checklist** gains *Revalue foreign balances* (enabled when that check is outstanding): runs AR and AP at the period end, reports what was posted and re-runs the checks (build-verified). Test: `TradingRevaluationTests` extended - before the run the check shows 2 items / 110 unbooked and a preview reports 60 without posting; after both runs the check passes. |
| **FIX** GL revaluation across several foreign currencies | ✅ | `FxRevaluationService` refused any run touching more than one foreign currency (`Fx.MixedCurrencies`, "run per currency") but offered no way to run per currency, so a company with, say, EUR and CHF bank accounts could not revalue at all. A currency-restricted account only takes lines in its own currency, so the run now posts one revaluation journal (and next-day reversal) per currency, all in one DB transaction. `FxRevaluationResult` carries `JournalNumbers` / `ReversalJournalNumbers` (the old `JournalNumber` / `ReversalJournalNumber` remain as the first of each). Test: `FxRevaluationMultiCurrencyTests` - EUR 100 booked 110 → 120 (gain 10) and CHF 200 booked 210 → 200 (loss 10) in one run: two journals and two reversals, TB balanced with 7100 Cr 10 / 7200 Dr 10, both banks back at 110 / 210 the next day. |
| **2** Consolidation across base currencies (IAS 21 translation) | ✅ | Consolidation refused companies with different base currencies, so a group with a foreign subsidiary could not be consolidated. `IConsolidationService.TranslatedTrialBalanceAsync(companyIds, presentationCurrency, asAtDate, intercompanyAccountCodes?)` translates each company by the current-rate method: asset and liability balances at the closing rate at the date; equity, income and expense movements at the daily rate of each posting date. Each company's imbalance after translation is its translation difference; their sum is the cumulative translation reserve, shown as a `CTA` row so the result balances. Rates are found quoted either way round (an inverse quotation is inverted); a missing rate is `Consolidation.NoRate`. Intercompany elimination runs after translation. `TranslatedTrialBalance` lists per-company `CompanyTranslation` (base currency, closing rate, difference). New endpoint `GET /api/consolidation/trial-balance` (plain, or translated when `presentationCurrency` is given). The scenario seeder takes an optional base currency. Test: `TranslatedConsolidationTests` - USD parent + EUR subsidiary (capital EUR 1,000 and sales EUR 500 at 1.10, bank EUR 1,500 at the 1.20 close): in USD bank 3,800, capital 1,100, sales 2,550, reserve Cr 150; in EUR the parent goes through inverse rates (bank 3,166.67, sales 2,318.18, reserve Dr 151.51); both balance; plain consolidation still refuses; March (no closing rate) refused. |
| **FIX** Payment run nets supplier credits | ✅ | The payment run paid every due invoice in full even when the supplier had an unused credit note or payment on account - an overpayment. It also paid in the supplier's default currency rather than the invoices' (an allocation mismatch part-way), reported a "base" total that was a sum of mixed transaction amounts, restarted payment numbers at 0001 on every run of the day, and committed supplier by supplier, so a failure between a payment and its allocation left a payment that a re-run would pay again. Now each supplier's unused AP debit items (credit notes, debit notes, payments on account dated by the run date) are allocated against the due invoices oldest first, and only the remainder is paid, in the invoices' currency; a supplier fully covered by credits gets a line with nothing paid and is left out of the EFT file. `PaymentRunLine.CreditsApplied`; `PaymentRunResult.Total` is the base value paid; numbering continues the day's `PMT-yyyyMMdd-nnnn`; the whole run is one DB transaction. Test: `PaymentRunCreditsTests` - supplier A owes 100 + 200 with a 50 credit note → pays 250 (`PMT-20260401-0001`), both settled; supplier B's 100 on account covers its 80 invoice → nothing paid, 20 left (creditors −20), EFT has only A; a second run that day numbers `PMT-20260401-0002`; TB balanced. The existing payment-run test still passes. |
| **4.6** Payment run proposal, API and screen | ✅ | The payment run posted directly and had no endpoint or screen. `IPaymentRunService.ProposeAsync` returns what a run would do without posting (`PaymentRunProposal`: per supplier and currency the invoices due, credits available / to apply, amount to pay, and suppliers on hold listed with nothing to pay; `PaymentCount`, `ToPayByCurrency`), sharing its selection with `RunAsync`. Endpoints `POST /api/trading/payment-run/propose` and `POST /api/trading/payment-run` (EFT file base64 in JSON). Desktop **Transactions → Supplier Payment Run…**: pay-from bank, payment and due dates, *Propose* → grid (supplier, invoices, due, credits applied of available, to pay, status Pay / Settled by credits / On hold), *Run payments* (one transaction), *Save EFT file…* (build-verified). Test: `PaymentRunCreditsTests` extended - the proposal for A (300 due, 50 credits, 250 to pay), B (80 due, 80 of 100 credits, 0) and held C (70 due, on hold, 0) matches the run that follows; the run skips C. |
| **FIX** Dunning nets unallocated customer credits | ✅ | Dunning graded and chased customers on their overdue invoices alone, so a customer whose payment had arrived but not yet been allocated (or who held a credit note) still got a reminder - up to a final notice - for money they no longer owed. Pure `Dunning.ApplyCredits` (Application/Trading; `OverdueItem`, `NetOverdue`) sets the customer's unallocated receipts and credit notes against the overdue invoices oldest due first, as allocation will; the level comes from the oldest invoice still unpaid and the notice amount is what remains. A fully covered customer gets no notice and a letter is refused (`Dunning.CoveredByCredits`); the reminder letter shows a *Less payments / credits not yet allocated* line and the net total (`DunningReminder.UnallocatedCredits`). Tests: unit `DunningCreditTests` (no credits, oldest covered lowers the age, part-covered keeps it, fully covered → level 0); accounting `DunningCreditAccountingTests` - an unallocated 1,000 receipt removes a 90-day final notice; a 300 credit note covering the oldest invoice turns the customer's final notice into a first reminder for 500, with the deduction on the letter. The existing dunning tests still pass. |
| **FIX** Credit review nets unallocated customer credits | ✅ | The credit review put a customer on stop for overdue invoices even when their payment had arrived and sat unallocated, blocking their sales orders for money already received. The overdue test now applies the customer's unallocated receipts and credit notes oldest first (`Dunning.ApplyCredits`, shared with dunning); the hold's overdue amount and age are what remains. Over-limit testing already used the net balance. Test: `CreditReviewCreditsTests` - a 250 invoice covered by an unallocated 250 receipt is not held; a 400 invoice with a 150 receipt is held as Overdue for 250. The existing credit-control test still passes. |
| **FIX** Finance charges stop at the payment date | ✅ | Finance charges accrued on an overdue invoice's full balance up to the run date even when the customer had already paid (or been credited) and the receipt simply had not been allocated yet - overcharging interest. Pure `FinanceCharge.InterestAfterCredits` (`ChargeableItem`, `UnallocatedCredit`, `CreditAdjustedInterest`) sets the customer's unallocated receipts and credit notes against the overdue invoices oldest first: the covered part accrues only from its charge-from date to the credit's date (nothing when the credit came first), the rest to the run date. The run reports the overdue amount net of credits. The once-per-period rule (`InterestChargedTo`) is unchanged, so a later run charges nothing more on covered parts. Tests: unit `FinanceChargeCreditTests` (no credits; full payment stops at its date - 60 not 90 days; credit before the period; part payment splits; a credit spilling from the oldest invoice to the next); accounting `FinanceChargeCreditAccountingTests` - paid in full 2 March → 19.73 not 29.59; 400 paid → 25.64 on 600 overdue; May run charges the payer nothing and the part-payer 5.92. The existing finance-charge tests still pass. |
| **FIX** Year end refuses unposted journals and is atomic | ✅ | Year end ignored draft and deferred journals dated in the year: they were left out of the closing profit, and once the year's periods were permanently closed they could never be released - stranded postings. It also saved the closing journal separately from closing the year, so a failure in between left a posted closing journal on an open year. `IYearEndService.RunAsync` now refuses with `YearEnd.UnpostedJournals` (count and the first journal numbers) while any draft or deferred journal is dated in the year, and runs the closing journal, the next year's creation and the period closure in one DB transaction. Test: `YearEndUnpostedTests` - a deferred journal blocks the close (year stays open, message names it); after releasing it the year closes and the closing profit is 130 including it. The existing year-end tests still pass. Test infrastructure: the scenario's random company code widened from 6 to 10 hex digits after a run hit a duplicate-code collision. |
| **FIX** Permanent period close refuses unposted journals | ✅ | Same stranding as at year end, one period at a time: closing a module's period *permanently* while a draft or deferred journal of that module was dated in it left the journal impossible to release. `IPeriodService.SetStatusAsync` now refuses `PermanentlyClosed` with `Period.UnpostedJournals` while any exist (a plain close, which can be reopened, is still allowed, as is another module's close). Test: `PeriodPermanentCloseTests` - a deferred GL journal in March blocks GL's permanent close but not a plain close or AR's permanent close; after reopening and releasing it, GL closes permanently. |
| **FIX** Allocation is all-or-nothing | ✅ | `AllocateAsync` / `UnallocateAsync` changed both open items' outstanding amounts in memory *before* posting the realised-FX journal; when that posting was refused (e.g. the allocation date fell in a closed AR / AP period) the method returned an error with the items still altered, so any later save in the same scope persisted a reduced outstanding with no allocation row - a sub-ledger that no longer reconciles. Now the FX journal is posted first and the items are changed only after it succeeds, with the journal and the allocation committed in one transaction (joining the caller's transaction when there is one, as in the payment run). Test: `AllocationAtomicityTests` - EUR 500 invoiced at 1.10 and received at 1.20, allocated into a closed May: refused, and even after a save in the same scope both items are still 500 outstanding, no allocation exists and check data is clean; reopened, the allocation realises the 50 gain and check data stays clean. |
| **FIX** Stock postings are all-or-nothing | ✅ | The same ordering flaw in inventory: `IssueAsync` and the stocktake `AdjustAsync` reduced (or raised) the stock balance in memory *before* posting the journal, so a refused posting - a closed INV period, say - left the balance altered for any later save in the scope to persist (on-hand and value no longer matching the movements or the GL). The cost is now worked out without touching the balance (`StockBalance.CostOfIssue`), the journal is posted, and only then are the balance and the movement changed; receipt, issue, issue reversal and adjustment each commit the journal and the stock movement in one transaction (joining a caller's transaction when there is one). Test: `StockPostingAtomicityTests` - with April's INV period closed an issue and a stocktake shortage are refused, a later save in the same scope persists nothing (10 on hand, value 40) and check data is clean; reopened, the issue posts at 12. |
| **FIX** A refused depreciation run no longer marks the month charged | ✅ | `RunDepreciationAsync` applied each asset's charge (accumulated depreciation and `LastDepreciatedPeriod`) before posting the journal. If the posting was refused - a closed GL period - the assets stayed marked in memory; a later save in the scope persisted them, and the next run then skipped the month as already depreciated although nothing had been charged. Charges are now collected and applied only after the journal posts. Test: `DepreciationAtomicityTests` - with February's GL closed the run is refused and, even after a save in the scope, the van is unmarked with nothing accumulated; reopened, February is charged 1,000. |
| **2** Group Consolidation screen | ✅ | Consolidation had no screen. Desktop **Enquiries → Group Consolidation…**: tick the companies (each shown with its base currency), choose the as-at date, the presentation currency and optional intercompany accounts to eliminate; *Consolidate* runs `IConsolidationService.TranslatedTrialBalanceAsync` and lists the trial balance (the translation reserve row emphasised), the totals with a balanced check, and each translated company's closing rate and translation difference, or the elimination outcome. Thin glue over the tested service; build-verified. |
| **FIX** Posting is safe after a caller's rollback | ✅ | Several services post more than one journal inside a DB transaction and roll back on failure (intercompany, bank statement post-and-match, revaluations, payment run). A rollback reverts the database but not EF's change tracker: the journal number sequence (with its per-year counters) and the GL period balances stayed tracked with the rolled-back values and concurrency token, and the next posting in the same scope - bank rules keep looping after a failed line - either failed on a stale token or built on the phantom values (skipped numbers, double-counted period balances). `PostingEngine` now forgets an unchanged tracked copy of those counter rows (`ForgetIfUnchanged`, including the sequence's owned year rows) and reads them afresh before each posting. Test: `RollbackSafePostingTests` - post JNL-…-000001; in one scope post …-000002 inside a transaction, roll back, post again: it succeeds as …-000002, check data is clean and cash is 130. |
| **FIX** Sales-order despatch and invoicing are single units of work | ✅ | Three defects in SOP. (1) `InvoiceAsync` marked lines invoiced before posting the AR invoice, so an invoice refused for a normal reason - customer on hold, credit limit - left lines showing invoiced with no invoice once anything else in the scope saved. (2) It credited the whole net to the *last* line's revenue account, misclassifying revenue on orders mixing items with different revenue accounts.
