(3) `DespatchAsync` issued stock line by line, so a failure on a later line left earlier lines' stock issued (a retry issued it twice), and it accepted over-despatch and cancelled orders. New `AtomicWork.AtomicAsync` (Infrastructure/Persistence) runs a unit of work in a transaction - joining a caller's if one is open - and on failure rolls back and clears the change tracker. Despatch and invoice now validate every line first (`Sop.OverDespatch`, `Sop.InvalidQuantity`, `Sop.OrderCancelled`, `Sop.NotDespatched`), post, then update the lines, atomically. `SalesInvoiceRequest.RevenueLines` (`RevenueLine`) splits an AR invoice's net across revenue accounts (must add up; `Ar.RevenueLinesMismatch`; the last share absorbs base rounding); order invoices group net by each line's revenue account. Test: `SalesOrderAtomicityTests` - over-despatch refused; a despatch whose second line lacks stock issues nothing (stocked part still 10, nothing despatched); an invoice for a held customer is refused and, even after a save in the scope, no line is invoiced; released, the invoice credits 4000 with 20 and 4300 with 50; check data clean. |
| **FIX** Procurement steps are single units of work | ✅ | `ReceiveGoodsAsync` received stock line by line and marked PO lines received before later lines were checked; `MatchInvoiceAsync` marked lines invoiced before later lines' price / quantity checks and the GRNI→AP journal; `ReturnToSupplierAsync` reduced the stock balance before its journal. A failure part-way left stock received without a GRN, PO lines invoiced with no AP invoice (so the real invoice could no longer be matched), or stock reduced with no journal or credit. All three now run through `AtomicWork.AtomicAsync`: one transaction, rolled back with the change tracker cleared on failure. Test: `ProcurementAtomicityTests` - a receipt whose second line is unknown receives nothing (no stock, no GRN); a match whose second line is outside tolerance marks no line invoiced; a return refused by a closed POP period leaves 10 bolts in stock; each even after a save in the same scope; check data clean. |
| **FIX** Restaurant bills and tips are single units of work | ✅ | A restaurant bill posts revenue, then issues each recipe ingredient line by line (marking lines billed as it goes), then records the bill and tenders; gift-card bills redeem the card after the bill is committed, bill reversals reverse stock after the reversal journal. A failure part-way (an ingredient issue refused, a card that cannot be redeemed) left revenue posted with the order still open and part-billed - a re-bill posted revenue again. `BillOrderAsync`, `BillWithTendersAsync`, `BillPartialAsync`, `BillSeatAsync`, `BillToVoucherAsync`, `BillToHouseAccountAsync`, `ReverseBillAsync`, `RecordWastageAsync` and the tip postings now run through `AtomicWork.AtomicAsync` (wrappers generated over private `*CoreAsync` bodies). Test: `RestaurantBillAtomicityTests` - with May's INV period closed a burger bill is refused after its revenue step; even after a save in the scope the order is open with nothing billed, no May journal exists, the 5 patties are untouched and check data is clean; reopened, the bill goes through and stock falls to 3. |
| **FIX** A failed unit of work forgets only what it touched | ✅ | `AtomicWork.AtomicAsync` cleared the whole change tracker on failure. Callers that loop over their own tracked entities and tolerate a failed item - offline sync marking an operation failed, bank rules moving to the next line - would have had those entities detached, so their later updates were silently never saved. It now snapshots the tracker before the work and, on failure, puts it back: entities the work started tracking are removed from already-tracked entities' collections and detached, and already-tracked entities get their values, original values (concurrency token included) and state restored in place - the same instances, so collections holding them stay valid (merely detaching a changed PO line left a stale copy in its order's collection, which a later match then read). Test: `AtomicWorkTrackerTests` - with a bystander customer tracked, an order invoice for a held customer fails; the bystander is still tracked, its rename saves, and the order line is not invoiced. |
| **FIX** Remaining posting services run as single units of work | ✅ | Closing out the audit's list of methods that changed records before a posting that could fail: fixed assets (register - an asset was added to the register before its capitalisation journal, so a refused posting left an asset that would depreciate with no cost in the GL - dispose, revalue), gift-card breakage (single and expired run), housing (acquire land, subdivide, sell stand, transfer), job costing (capture cost, certify progress, release retention, recognise revenue, expected-loss provision), the payroll run, receipting (post / reverse receipt, refunds, suspense allocation, session close, dishonour, pending tender), receipt import, cash banking (bank, confirm, transfer), POS (complete sale, void, cash-up) and the recurring sales / purchase invoice runs (a later template failing no longer strands earlier invoices without their advanced run date). All now go through `AtomicWork.AtomicAsync`, which gained an overload for plain `Result` work. Test: `ServiceAtomicityTests` - with May's GL and VOU closed, registering a printer and recognising an expired card's breakage are refused and, after a save in the scope, there is no asset and the card still holds 100; check data clean; reopened, both succeed. |
| **FIX** No service opens a transaction of its own any more | ✅ | FX revaluation, intercompany posting, year end, the payment run, trading revaluation and bank-statement post-and-match each opened a database transaction unconditionally - called from inside another unit of work they would have thrown (a connection cannot open a second transaction), and on failure they left the change tracker holding the rolled-back state. Their own begin / commit / rollback calls are gone; their public entry points (and bank posting's post-and-match step) now run through `AtomicWork.AtomicAsync`, joining a caller's transaction or owning one with the tracker restored on failure. Only the raw SQL script runner (its own connection) still begins a transaction. Verified by the existing tests of all six services (intercompany pair, multi-currency and single FX revaluation, year end incl. unposted-journal refusal, payment run with credits, trading revaluation, bank statement posting and bank rules), all passing unchanged. |
| **FIX** Trading journals record their document type | ✅ | Every sales / purchase invoice, credit note, receipt, payment and receipt-reversal journal recorded an account code ("4000", "1100", "6800") as its document type - the posting helper's last argument had been fed the revenue / bank / expense account - so the account enquiry, audit trail and journal listings showed account codes where the document kind belongs. `TradingService.DocumentTypeFor(ledger, itemType)` now names them: SalesInvoice, SalesCreditNote, CustomerReceipt, ReceiptReversal, PurchaseInvoice, PurchaseCreditNote, SupplierPayment. Nothing read the old values; journals already posted keep theirs (posted journals are immutable). Test: `TradingDocumentTypeTests` - each of the six documents' journals carries its type. |
| **FIX** Accruals post with their reversal or not at all | ✅ | An accrual or prepayment posts its journal and then its automatic reversal; when the reversal date fell in a closed period the reversal was refused but the accrual stayed posted - an accrual that never reverses. `PostAccrualAsync` / `PostPrepaymentAsync` now run as one unit of work (`AtomicWork`), as does `PeriodService.OpenYearAsync` (a module failing part-way no longer leaves earlier periods opened in memory). Test: `AccrualAtomicityTests` - with April's GL closed, a 31 March accrual reversing on 1 April is refused and no accrual journal exists even after a save in the scope; reopened, the accrual and its 1 April reversal both post. This closes the audit's list. |
| **3.2** Concurrent postings retry instead of failing | ✅ | Every posting updates the journal number sequence (and period balances) under an optimistic-concurrency token, so when two users posted at the same moment the one that lost the race failed with an unhandled concurrency exception - or, depending on statement order, a duplicate journal number - instead of simply taking the next number. `AtomicWork` now retries a unit of work it owns, up to three attempts in all, when the save lost a race: a concurrency conflict, or a unique violation on the race-prone constraints (journal number, period balance, number sequence, year counter). The transaction is rolled back and the tracker restored before each retry, and the posting engine re-reads the sequence and balances anyway. `PostingEngine.PostAsync`, `ReverseAsync` and `ReleaseDeferredAsync` now run as such units of work themselves. Genuine rule failures and other errors are not retried. Test: `ConcurrentPostingTests` - while one posting holds its transaction (and the sequence row) open, a second posting starts, blocks, loses the race when the first commits and is retried: the two journals are …-000002 and …-000003, check data is clean and cash totals 60. |
| **3.2** Concurrent allocations and stock movements retry too | ✅ | Allocation / unallocation and the stock receipt, issue, issue-reversal and adjustment used their own transaction helper with no retry, so two users allocating against the same invoice, or issuing the same item, at the same moment left the loser with an unhandled concurrency error. They now run through `AtomicWork` like everything else (retry on a lost race, tracker restored on failure; the local helpers are gone), and a first-time stock balance created by two postings at once is treated as a race. Test: `ConcurrentStockIssueTests` - while one issue of 3 holds its transaction open, a second issue of 4 races it, is retried against the new balance and stands: values 15 and 20, 3 left on hand, check data clean. |
| **FIX** Trading documents commit journal and open item together | ✅ | Every sales / purchase document posted its journal (its own committed unit) and then saved the open item separately, and write-off, write-back, settle- / pay-with-discount and contra post a document and then allocate it - a failure between the steps left a journal with no open item, or a write-off that never settled the invoice. All twelve public posting methods of `TradingService` (invoices, credit notes, receipt, payment, receipt reversal, contra, write-off / write-back, discount settlement / payment) now run through `AtomicWork` (joined by the payment run, bank posting and the other callers that already own a unit of work). Verified by the existing trading, allocation, write-off, discount, contra and payment tests, all passing unchanged. |
| **FIX** Last posting services become single units of work | ✅ | The remaining services that pair a posting with a record update now run each call through `AtomicWork`: petty-cash disbursement and float reimbursement, a single recurring-journal post, expense-claim approval and reimbursement, municipal service billing, employee loan grant and repayment, leave accrual / taking / provision, agent collections and remittances, and the finance-charge run (a charge posted but its items left unmarked would have been charged again). Batch runs designed to resume after a failure keep committing item by item - the recurring-journal, school term and municipal fixed-charge runs skip what is already done - so each item is atomic while the run stays resumable. Verified by those services' existing tests, all passing unchanged. |
| **3.3** Exchange rates: inverse quotes and triangulation | ✅ | Every rate lookup (posting engine, sales / purchase ledger, POS tenders, GL and trading revaluation, consolidation) wanted a quote stored exactly as foreign → base, so a rate table that publishes USD → ZAR, or quotes GBP only against EUR, made ZAR or GBP postings fail with no rate. New internal `ExchangeRateResolver.FindAsync(from, to, type, date)` finds the direct quote, else the inverse of the opposite quote, else **triangulates** through a currency quoted against both (stable alphabetical choice), each leg at its latest quote of the type on or before the date, honouring direct / indirect quotation (brief §3.3). All seven lookups use it. Test: `ExchangeRateRouteTests` - with only USD→ZAR 18 published, ZAR 1,800 posts as USD 100; with only GBP→EUR 1.15 (and EUR→USD 1.10), a GBP 100 journal posts as 126.50 and a GBP 200 sales invoice as 253; JPY with no route is refused (`Posting.NoExchangeRate`); TB balanced. |
| **3.1** Control accounts locked from manual journals in the database | ✅ | Brief §3.1 asks for control accounts to be locked from manual journals "in code AND with a DB trigger"; only the code check existed. Journals now record `IsManual` (`gl.journal.is_manual`, set by the engine from `EnforceManualJournalRules`), and migration `GuardControlAccountsFromManualJournals` adds `trg_journal_line_control_guard`: a line inserted or re-pointed onto an account with a control type is refused when its journal is manual. It also closes a gap in the posted-journal guard: lines could still be *inserted* into a posted journal (unbalancing it). The line guard now covers INSERT, allowing it only for journals inserted by the current transaction - tracked in a transaction-local list by a new `trg_journal_mark_new` (an `xmin` check would not work: EF saves inside savepoints, so the row carries a sub-transaction id). `is_manual` is frozen once posted. Test: `ControlAccountGuardTests` - the engine refuses a manual journal to AR control; a manual (deferred) journal is flagged manual and re-pointing its line to AR control in SQL is refused by the trigger; a sales invoice still posts to AR control; copying a line into the posted invoice journal in SQL is refused. |
| **3.6** Stock provisions (IAS 2: NRV, slow-moving, obsolete) | ✅ | Brief §3.6 asks for slow-moving / obsolete / NRV write-downs with a journal and report; there were none. Pure `StockProvision.Assess` (Application/Inventory; `StockProvisionPolicy` - cost to sell %, slow-moving days / %, obsolete days / %; default 10% / 180 d 25% / 365 d 100%) takes the larger of the NRV write-down (cost − qty × price × (1 − cost to sell)) and the ageing provision, capped at cost. `IStockProvisionService.AssessAsync` builds every holding as at the date from the stock movements (never current balances), with days since last issue (or first receipt) and the default price list's base-currency price, and reports the target against the provision already booked on the contra-stock account; `PostAsync` books only the difference (Dr write-down expense / Cr provision, or the reverse to release), as one unit of work. Endpoints `GET` / `POST /api/stock/provision` (policy overridable by query). Tests: unit `StockProvisionTests` (no provision, NRV write-down, slow-moving / obsolete bands, larger of the two, empty holdings); accounting `StockProvisionAccountingTests` - at 31 Aug widget 19 (NRV 81 vs cost 100), gadget 25 (241 days unsold), gizmo 0: 44 booked, a repeat posts nothing; after the gadgets sell in September the run releases 25; TB 1490 Cr 19 / 5190 Dr 19. |
| **3.7** Deferred income earned over time (school fees in advance) | ✅ | Brief §3.7 (IFRS 15) asks for deferred income schedules for fees billed in advance and recognised over the term; school fees went straight to revenue on billing. New `DeferredIncomeSchedule` (Core/Accounting, `gl.deferred_income_schedule`, migration `AddDeferredIncomeSchedules`) earns its amount evenly by day (`EarnedBy`: elapsed ÷ total days, all of it from the last day). A fee structure can `DeferOverTerm(start, end, deferredIncomeAccount)`: billing then credits deferred income and records a schedule per student (each student one unit of work). `IDeferredIncomeService.RecogniseAsync(companyId, asAtDate)` moves what each schedule has earned but not yet recognised from deferred income to revenue in one journal (per account pair), so a repeat posts nothing; endpoint `POST /api/accounting/deferred-income/recognise`. Reusable for annual licences and other advance billing. Tests: unit `DeferredIncomeScheduleTests` (before / during / after the term, invalid schedules); accounting `DeferredSchoolFeesTests` - 1,750 of term fees (Jan–Mar) billed to deferred income, January's run earns 31/90 = 602.77, a repeat posts nothing, March's run earns the remaining 1,147.23, leaving revenue 1,750 and nothing deferred. |
| **3.5** Withholding tax on supplier payments | ✅ | Brief §3.5 asks for withholding tax on supplier payments (rate by tax-clearance status) with certificates and a remittance return; suppliers had a clearance-expiry field and tax codes a Withholding type, but nothing used them. `PaymentRequest.WithholdingTaxCode`: when the supplier has no valid clearance on the payment date, the code's dated rate is withheld - the AP payment settles the full amount, the bank is credited the net and the WHT payable account (the code's output account) the tax; a `WithholdingCertificate` (`trd.withholding_certificate`, migration `AddWithholdingCertificates`) records it and `TradingPostingResult.WithheldBase` reports it. A cleared supplier is paid in full. `IWithholdingTaxService`: `ReturnAsync` (the period's certificates, total and unremitted), `RemitAsync` (Dr WHT payable per code / Cr bank, marks the certificates; a repeat finds nothing), `CertificateTextAsync` via pure `WithholdingCertificateFormatter`. Endpoints `GET /api/ap/withholding/return`, `POST /api/ap/withholding/remit`, `GET /api/ap/withholding/{id}/certificate`. Tests: unit `WithholdingCertificateFormatterTests`; accounting `WithholdingTaxTests` - 1,000 paid to an uncleared supplier withholds 300 (bank 700, invoice settled), a cleared supplier's 500 is paid in full, the March return lists one certificate (300 unremitted), the certificate shows the 700 net, remitting pays 300 over and a repeat posts nothing; TB balanced. |
| **3.5** Withholding in the supplier payment run | ✅ | `PaymentRunRequest.WithholdingTaxCode` passes the code to every payment the run makes, so uncleared suppliers have tax withheld exactly as on a single payment. `PaymentProposalLine.Withholding` shows the tax that will be deducted; `PaymentRunLine.Withheld` / `NetPaid` report it, and the EFT file (header total and each line) carries the net the bank actually pays. Test: `PaymentRunWithholdingTests` - the proposal shows 300 withholding on an uncleared supplier's 1,000 and none on a cleared supplier's 400; the run settles both invoices in full, the EFT header totals 1,100.00 with the uncleared supplier's line at 700.00, creditors are nil and 300 sits unremitted on the April return. |
| **3.6** Landed costs | ✅ | Brief §3.6 asks for freight, duty, clearing and insurance to be apportioned onto goods-receipt cost; purchases carried only the supplier price. Pure `LandedCost.Apportion` (Application/Inventory; `LandedCostBasis` Value / Quantity) shares a charge across a GRN's lines to the cent (largest remainder). `ILandedCostService.ApplyAsync(LandedCostRequest)` finds where each line went into stock (the receipt movement carries the GRN number), capitalises the share for stock still on hand - a zero-quantity `LandedCost` stock movement and `StockBalance.AddValue`, raising the moving average - and charges the share for stock already issued to the item's cost of sales; Cr the clearing account the charges' invoice went to; one application per reference (`Landed.Duplicate`); one unit of work. Endpoint `POST /api/stock/landed-cost`. Weight / volume bases need item dimensions and are not yet offered. Tests: unit `LandedCostTests` (by value, by quantity, cent-exact shares, empty basis refused); accounting `LandedCostAccountingTests` - 32 freight on bolts 100 / nuts 60 splits 20 / 12; with half the bolts issued, 10 goes to cost of sales and 10 onto the bolts (average 10 → 12), the nuts take 12 (2 → 2.40); a repeat is refused; TB stock 132, COGS 60, clearing Cr 32; check data clean. |
| **4.5** IFRS 9 expected credit loss allowance | ✅ | Brief §4.5 asks for a bad-debt provision by expected-credit-loss matrix (IFRS 9, provision % by ageing bucket); there was only write-off. Pure `ExpectedCreditLoss.Compute` with `EclMatrix` (`EclBand` from-days / rate / label; default current 1%, 1–30 3%, 31–60 10%, 61–90 25%, 90+ 50%). `IExpectedCreditLossService.AssessAsync` takes receivables as at the date (`OpenItemsAsAt`), sets each customer's unallocated receipts and credit notes against their oldest debts first, bands the remainder by days past due and compares the target with the allowance already on the contra-receivables account; `PostAsync` books only the difference against credit-loss expense (or releases it), as one unit of work. Endpoints `GET` / `POST /api/ar/expected-credit-loss` (default matrix). Tests: unit `ExpectedCreditLossTests` (band boundaries, rates per band); accounting `ExpectedCreditLossAccountingTests` - at 30 April a 119-day 1,000 (50%), a 10-day 400 (3%) and a not-yet-due 200 (1%) give 514, a fully covered debt adds nothing; posted, a repeat posts nothing; after the old debt is collected the May run releases 468, leaving 46; TB balanced. |
| **4.7** Statement import: MT940 and camt.053 | ✅ | Brief §4.7 lists statement import as CSV / OFX / MT940 / CAMT.053; only CSV existed. Pure `Mt940Parser` (`:61:` statement lines - value date, C / D / RC / RD mark, decimal-comma amount, transaction type, customer reference, `NONREF` as none - with the following `:86:` narrative, continuation lines joined) and `Camt053Parser` (each `Ntry`: amount, `CdtDbtInd`, booking else value date, end-to-end id else the bank's reference, unstructured remittance else additional entry info, reversal indicator; any camt.053 version, matched by local name) both return the CSV parser's result shape. `IBankReconciliationService.ImportStatementFileAsync(…, StatementFileFormat Csv / Mt940 / Camt053, csvLayout?)` imports any of them all-or-nothing (the CSV method now delegates to it); endpoint `POST /api/banking/{id}/statement/file?format=`. OFX is not yet supported. Tests: unit `StatementFileParserTests` (MT940 credit / debit / reversed debit with multi-line narrative, unreadable line reported; camt.053 credit with end-to-end id and debit with additional info, invalid XML); accounting `BankStatementFileImportTests` - an MT940 (2 lines) and a camt.053 (1 line) import with signs and references kept; a broken MT940 is refused. |
| **4** Maker-checker approval of manual journals | ✅ | Brief §4.4 asks for maker-checker approval of manual journals above a threshold. `Company.ManualJournalApprovalThreshold` (null off, 0 all): the posting engine refuses a manual journal worth more than it in base currency unless submitted for approval (`Posting.ApprovalRequired`). `JournalRequest.SubmittedByUserId` makes the engine validate, number and hold the journal (deferred, `AwaitingApproval`, `PreparedByUserId`); held journals are skipped by the deferred-release run and refused by single release (`Posting.AwaitingApproval`). `IJournalApprovalService`: `SubmitAsync`, `AwaitingApprovalAsync`, `ApproveAsync` (refuses the preparer, `Gl.SelfApproval`; approval and posting are one unit, so a failed post keeps nothing) and `RejectAsync` (reason required; new `JournalStatus.Cancelled` keeps the number, so no gap). Check constraints `ck_journal_approved_before_posting` and `ck_journal_approver_not_preparer` enforce it in the database. **FIX** `ReverseAsync` now refuses a journal that was never posted (`Gl.NotPosted`); before, reversing a deferred journal posted a one-sided inverse. Migration `AddManualJournalApproval`; endpoints `POST /api/gl/journals/submit`, `GET /api/gl/journals/awaiting-approval`, `POST /api/gl/journals/{id}/approve`, `POST /api/gl/journals/{id}/reject`. Test: `JournalApprovalTests` - with a threshold of 50, a 100 manual journal is refused direct posting while a 50 one posts; a submitted journal is listed, not released by the run, not self-approvable, posted on a second user's approval; a rejected journal is cancelled, not reversible or approvable; a raw SQL post of a held journal hits the constraint; no numbering gap; TB balanced. |
| **4.4** Overhead recharge rules | ✅ | Brief §4.4 asks for overhead recharge rules (fixed %, headcount, floor area, usage driver) run as a period-end routine. New `AllocationRule` (`gl.allocation_rule` with `gl.allocation_rule_target`): source account and cost centre (the pool), optional charge account, `AllocationBasis` and receiving cost centres / departments with drivers. Pure `OverheadAllocation` (Application/Accounting): `Validate` (fixed percentages add up to 100, drivers non-negative and not all nil) and `Share` (cent-exact, largest remainder, credit pools too). `IOverheadAllocationService`: `CreateRuleAsync` (known active accounts and cost centres, unique code, no self-charge), `SetDriversAsync` (say this month's usage), `PreviewAsync` and `RunAsync` for the fiscal period containing a date. The pool is the net base cost booked in the period to the source account in the source cost centre; rules run in sequence order and a later pool includes what earlier rules charged into it (step-down). One journal per rule, Cr pool / Dr receivers, dated the period end, all in one unit. A rerun recharges only what was booked since, so repeating it posts nothing. Migration `AddOverheadAllocationRules`; endpoints `POST /api/gl/allocation-rules`, `PUT /api/gl/allocation-rules/{id}/drivers`, `GET /api/gl/allocations/preview`, `POST /api/gl/allocations/run`. Tests: unit `OverheadAllocationTests` (shares by driver, cent rounding, credit pool, driver validation per basis); accounting `OverheadAllocationAccountingTests` - rent 1,000 in admin by floor area (IT 200, production 600, sales 200), then IT's 300 + 200 by usage 6:3 (333.33 / 166.67); rerun posts nothing; a late 100 of rent steps down with new usage drivers (production 1,003.33, sales 396.67); invalid rules refused; TB balanced. |
| **FIX** Credit limit under concurrent invoicing | ✅ | The credit-limit check read the customer's balance without a lock. Concurrent invoices were only kept apart by contention on the journal number, so heavy contention exhausted the retries and invoices failed with concurrency errors instead of a clear credit decision. `PostInvoiceAsync` now locks the customer row (`SELECT … FOR UPDATE`) before reading the balance, so invoices for one customer queue and each sees the others' committed items. Test: `CreditLimitConcurrencyTests` - eight concurrent 200 invoices against a 1,000 limit: exactly five post and three are refused for the limit. The test fails without the lock. |
| **3.6** Landed costs by weight and volume | ✅ | Freight is usually charged by weight or volume, which landed costs could not use. `Item.UnitWeightKg` / `UnitVolumeM3`; `LandedCostBasis.Weight` / `Volume`; `LandedCostLine` carries each line's total weight and volume. `ILandedCostService.ApplyAsync` refuses a weight or volume basis when any receipt item lacks the measurement (`Landed.NoMeasurement`), since that item would silently take no share. Migration `AddItemWeightAndVolume`. Tests: unit `LandedCostTests` adds by weight (10 / 6) and by volume (4 / 12); accounting `LandedCostAccountingTests` adds duty of 16 by weight (bolts 5 kg take 10, half to cost of sales; nuts 3 kg take 6) and a refused volume charge; TB stock 143, cost of sales 65, clearing 48. |
| **3.6** FIFO costing | ✅ | Items could be marked FIFO, but every movement was costed at the moving average. New `StockCostLayer` (`inv.stock_cost_layer`): each receipt of a FIFO item opens a layer holding its remaining quantity and value, so a whole layer always leaves at exactly its value. Pure `FifoIssuePlan` (Core) costs an issue from the oldest layers, with any quantity beyond them (stock allowed below zero) at the average; `Apply` takes it only after the journal posts. Internal `StockCosting` routes every path through the item's method: receipts, issues, issue reversals (a new layer at the original cost), transfers (layers move with their dates and costs), stocktake surpluses and shortages, supplier returns and landed costs (capitalised onto what remains of the receipt's own layers). A receipt into negative stock settles the deficit first and leaves the difference in the new layer, so layers always add up to the balance. `StockBalance.ReceiveAtValue` / `IssueAtCost`. Check data gains `IntegrityCheck.CostLayers` (a FIFO item's layers against its balance). Migration `AddFifoCostLayers`. Standard costing is still open. Tests: unit `FifoIssuePlanTests` (oldest first, nothing changes until applied, shortfall at the fallback, pro-rata partial takes clearing exactly); accounting `FifoCostingTests` - receipts 10 at 5 and 10 at 8, issue 15 costs 90, its reversal, a transfer of 8 costing 58 with its layers, a shortage of 2 costing 12, an issue of 10 below zero costing 72.50 and a receipt settling it (2 left worth 25.50); check data clean; TB stock 85.50, cost of sales 72.50; `LandedCostAccountingTests` now runs its nuts as FIFO. |
| **3.6** Standard costing | ✅ | Items could be marked standard-costed, but were valued at the moving average. `Item.StandardCost` and `Item.PriceVarianceAccountCode`: a standard-costed item goes into stock at its standard, and the difference from what it cost posts to the price variance account (receipts, GRNs, landed charges). Issues, reversals, stocktakes and supplier returns then run at the standard as the average. `IInventoryService.RevalueStandardCostAsync` changes the standard and revalues stock on hand in every warehouse against a revaluation account, in one unit (new `StockMovementType.StandardRevaluation` value movements). A receipt of a standard item without a standard or variance account is refused (`Inv.NoStandardCost`). Migration `AddStandardCosting`; endpoint `POST /api/stock/items/{id}/standard-cost`. Test: `StandardCostingTests` - standard 10: 10 received at 12 (variance Dr 20) and 5 at 9 (variance Cr 5) go in at 150; an issue of 6 costs 60; the standard moves to 11, revaluing the 9 on hand by 9; the next issue costs 11; check data clean; TB stock 88, variance 15, cost of sales 71. |
| **3** IAS 29 hyperinflation restatement | ✅ | The brief's optional hyperinflation module: a CPI index table, restatement factors, the monetary gain or loss and a restated trial balance alongside historical cost. New `PriceIndex` (`gl.price_index`, one value per index and month) and `LedgerAccount.Ias29` (`Ias29Classification` Default / Monetary / NonMonetary; by default assets and liabilities are monetary except stock, and equity, income and expenses are not). Pure `Hyperinflation` (Application/Accounting): `IsMonetary`, `Factor` and `Restate` (each month's net movement times closing index over that month's index, to the cent). `IHyperinflationService.SetIndexAsync` (upsert, positive values) and `RestatedTrialBalanceAsync(companyId, asAtDate, indexCode)`: non-monetary balances restated from the month each amount was recorded, income and expenses from within the current financial year, monetary balances unchanged; `RestatedTrialBalance.MonetaryGainLoss` is what balances it. Missing index months are listed (`Ias29.MissingIndex`). Report only: nothing is posted. Migration `AddHyperinflationRestatement`; endpoints `PUT /api/accounting/ias29/index`, `GET /api/accounting/ias29/trial-balance`. Tests: unit `HyperinflationTests` (classification by type and override, factor, restatement, non-positive index refused); accounting `HyperinflationAccountingTests` - CPI 100 / 110 / 121: capital 5,000 to 6,050, equipment 1,000 to 1,210, February sales 1,100 to 1,210, March expenses unchanged, bank unchanged at 4,858, monetary loss 950; a March index correction; April refused for its missing index. |
| **4.7** Statement import: OFX | ✅ | Completes the brief's statement formats (CSV / OFX / MT940 / CAMT.053). Pure `OfxParser` reads both the SGML of OFX 1.x (leaf elements without closing tags) and the XML of OFX 2.x: each `STMTTRN` with its posting date, signed amount, reference (cheque number, else reference number, else the bank's transaction id) and name / memo, entities decoded. `StatementFileFormat.Ofx` through `ImportStatementFileAsync` and the existing endpoint. Tests: unit `StatementFileParserTests` (OFX SGML credit and debit, OFX XML cheque, unreadable transaction and non-OFX file reported); accounting `BankStatementFileImportTests` adds a one-line OFX import. |
| **4.4** Journal approval screen (desktop) | ✅ | The maker-checker queue made usable. New `JournalApprovalsWindow` + `JournalApprovalsViewModel` (**Transactions → Journals Awaiting Approval…**) list the held manual journals (number, date, narrative, value, preparer, marking the user's own). Approve & Post is enabled only for journals someone else prepared; Reject needs a reason. Both go through `IJournalApprovalService`, and the status line reports the outcome. Thin glue over the tested service; build-verified Debug and Release. |
| **4.6** Supplier bank details: dual authorisation | ✅ | The brief calls bank-detail changes "a classic fraud vector" to be made a dual-authorisation event; the supplier's details could be edited freely. `Supplier` bank fields are now set at creation and change only through `ChangeBankDetails` with an approved `SupplierBankChange` (`ap.supplier_bank_change`: requested details, requester, Pending / Approved / Rejected, reviewer, reason). `ISupplierBankDetailsService`: `RequestChangeAsync` (one pending change per supplier, `Ap.BankChangePending`; an account number is required), `PendingAsync` (current against requested), `ApproveAsync` (never the requester, `Ap.SelfApproval`; applies the details) and `RejectAsync` (reason required). The database enforces one pending change per supplier and an approver other than the requester. Payment runs hold a supplier with a pending change: the proposal shows it held (`PaymentProposalLine.BankChangePending`) and the run pays nothing to it. Migration `AddSupplierBankChanges`; endpoints `POST /api/ap/suppliers/{id}/bank-change`, `GET /api/ap/bank-changes`, `POST /api/ap/bank-changes/{id}/approve`, `POST /api/ap/bank-changes/{id}/reject`. Test: `SupplierBankChangeTests` - a requested change leaves the details unchanged and holds the supplier out of the run; a second request and self-approval are refused; a manager's approval changes the details and the next run's EFT line pays the new account; a rejected change leaves them alone. |
| **4.4** Journal import from CSV | ✅ | Brief §4.4 lists importing manual journals from CSV / Excel. Pure `JournalCsvParser` (Application/Accounting): a header naming the columns in any order (Reference, Date, Account, Debit, Credit; optional Cost Centre, Department, Narrative), one row per line, rows sharing a reference forming one journal; dates yyyy-MM-dd or dd/MM/yyyy, thousands separators and quoted fields accepted; every bad row, mixed-date journal and unbalanced journal reported by line. `IJournalImportService.ImportAsync(companyId, csv, preparedByUserId)` is all or nothing: a file with any problem imports nothing (`Gl.ImportParse`), and if the engine refuses any journal (a control account, a closed period) none is kept. Journals over the company's approval threshold are submitted for approval instead of posting. `BankStatementCsvParser.SplitFields` is now shared. Endpoint `POST /api/gl/journals/import` (raw CSV body). Tests: unit `JournalCsvParserTests` (grouping, reordered and optional columns, quoted narrative with a comma, both date forms, every refusal by line); accounting `JournalImportTests` - with a threshold of 500, a 100 journal posts and an 800 journal is held for approval; a file whose second journal hits the AR control account imports nothing; an unbalanced file is refused. |
| **4.4 / 4.18.5** Fund control | ✅ | The brief's hard rule that expenditure cannot be posted against a vote with no appropriation when fund control is on; `LedgerAccount.BudgetEnabled` existed but nothing enforced it. `Company.FundControlEnabled`: the posting engine refuses any debit to a budget-enabled expense account (a vote) that has no positive appropriation in the year's active approved or supplementary budget, company-wide or for the line's department, and for the line's cost centre where the budget names one (`Posting.NoAppropriation`). Because it sits in the engine, every route is covered: journals, supplier invoices, expense claims and the rest. Draft and superseded budgets appropriate nothing. Migration `AddFundControl`. Test: `FundControlTests` - a journal and a supplier invoice to an unbudgeted vote are refused while a non-vote account posts; a draft budget changes nothing; once active both post; a sales department budget appropriates for sales but not for admin. |
| **4.18.4–5** Virements and the vote book | ✅ | Councils and grant-funded bodies need virements between budget lines and the classic vote book; neither existed. `BudgetVirement` (`bud.budget_virement`: budget, from and to lines, period, amount, justification, requester, Pending / Approved / Rejected, reviewer). An approved virement adds a pair of budget lines carrying its id (`BudgetLine.VirementId`), so the approved budget is never edited, fund control and availability see the moved budget at once, and the register can always reconcile. `IBudgetControlService`: `RequestVirementAsync` (active budget, positive amount, a justification, two different lines, and never more than the source line has left after expenditure and commitments), `ApproveVirementAsync` (never the requester; availability re-checked), `RejectVirementAsync` (reason required), `VirementRegisterAsync` (original, in, out, current per line) and `VoteBookAsync` (per vote: original and supplementary appropriation, net virements, expenditure and open commitments to the as-at period, unexpended balance). The database refuses an approver who requested the virement. Migration `AddBudgetVirements`; endpoints under `/api/budgets/…/virements`, `/api/budgets/{id}/virement-register`, `/api/budgets/vote-book`. Test: `BudgetControlTests` - with 1,000 budgeted, 300 spent and 100 committed on office supplies, a 700 virement is refused and a 400 one accepted; self-approval refused; the register shows 600 and 900; the vote book with a 200 supplementary leaves 400 and 900 unexpended; a rejected virement changes nothing. |
| **4.5** Promise-to-pay diary | ✅ | The credit control workbench in the brief includes a promise-to-pay diary; none existed. `PaymentPromise` (`ar.payment_promise`: customer, made on, pay by, amount, note, recorder, Open / Kept / Broken, resolved on; one open promise per customer, enforced in the database). `IPromiseToPayService`: `RecordAsync` (positive amount, pay-by not before made-on, `Ar.PromiseOpen` for a second), `DiaryAsync` (optionally by state, by date promised) and `ReviewAsync(asAtDate)` - kept once receipts dated from the promise up to the promised date cover it, broken once the date passes without them. Dunning leaves alone a customer whose promise is open and not yet due. Migration `AddPaymentPromises`; endpoints `POST /api/ar/customers/{id}/promises`, `GET /api/ar/promises`, `POST /api/ar/promises/review`. Test: `PromiseToPayTests` - two overdue customers promise 500 by 15 May and neither is dunned meanwhile; one pays on 10 May and is found kept, the other is found broken on 16 May and is dunned again; a second open promise and a backwards date are refused. |
| **4.18.3** Budget approval seal | ✅ | The brief requires an approved budget's line set to be hashed and sealed so it can be proved unaltered years later, and editing an approved budget to be impossible. `Budget.SealHash`: a SHA-256 of the lines as approved (order-independent; virement lines left out as separate documents), set by `Approve` or `Activate`; `VerifySeal`; `AddLine` refuses ordinary lines on a sealed budget (changes go in a new version or a virement). `IBudgetControlService.VerifySealAsync` (`Budget.NotSealed` for a draft); endpoint `GET /api/budgets/{id}/verify-seal`. Migration `AddBudgetSeal`. Tests: unit `BudgetSealTests` (sealed on approval, edits refused, virements leave it intact, same lines in any order give the same seal); accounting `BudgetSealAccountingTests` - a draft has no seal; activation seals it and it verifies after reloading and after a virement; an amount changed directly in the database breaks it. |
| **4.18.5** Year-end commitments: lapse or carry forward | ✅ | The brief says commitments outstanding at year end either lapse or carry forward per an approved policy, the carry-forward being an auditable document. `CommitmentStatus.Lapsed` / `CarriedForward`; `BudgetCommitment.Lapse` / `CarryForward` (closes it and opens a commitment against the next year's budget in period 1, linked by `CarriedFromId`) record who approved the decision. `Release` now only frees an open commitment. `IBudgetControlService.CloseYearCommitmentsAsync(companyId, year, nextYear, carryForwardIds, approvedBy)` decides every open commitment of the year in one unit: the chosen ones carry forward, the rest lapse; a commitment not open in the year, or a next year that is not later, is refused. Migration `AddCommitmentYearEnd`; endpoint `POST /api/budgets/commitments/year-end`. Test: `CommitmentYearEndTests` - of 2026's commitments the 100 order carries into 2027 as an open period-1 commitment linked to it, the 50 order lapses with the approver recorded, a released one is untouched; a released id and a backwards year pair are refused; a second run finds nothing to decide. |
| **4.18.6** Variance commentary | ✅ | The brief asks budget holders to explain material variances (threshold by value and %), with the commentary printing alongside the management accounts. Pure `MaterialVariance.IsMaterial` with `VarianceThreshold(Value, Percent)` (default 1,000 or 10%; either way; spending with no budget always material). `VarianceComment` (`bud.variance_comment`, one per account, year and as-at period; a later comment replaces the earlier, the audit trail keeps both). `IVarianceCommentaryService.MaterialVariancesAsync` lists the budgeted accounts whose variance against the active budget is material, each with its explanation or flagged as still owed; `CommentAsync` records one. Migration `AddVarianceComments`; endpoints `GET /api/budgets/material-variances`, `PUT /api/budgets/variance-comments`. Tests: unit `MaterialVarianceTests` (value, percentage, both directions, nil and unbudgeted cases); accounting `VarianceCommentaryTests` - with 1,000 budgeted for stationery and 500 for travel, spending 1,300 and 520 makes only stationery material at 10%; it is owed a comment until one is given, and a revised comment replaces it. |
| **4.18.5** Budget utilisation alerts | ✅ | The brief asks for budget-holder notifications at configurable utilisation thresholds (say 75%, 90%, 100%). Pure `BudgetUtilisation` (`PercentUsed` of the appropriation, spending without one counting as 100%; `HighestCrossed`; `DefaultThresholds`). `IBudgetControlService.UtilisationAlertsAsync` reads the vote book and returns each vote whose expenditure plus commitments has reached a threshold, with the highest reached, most used first. Endpoint `GET /api/budgets/utilisation-alerts` (default thresholds when none given). Sending the alerts by email is left to the notification channel. Tests: unit `BudgetUtilisationTests` (percentages, nil appropriation, highest threshold crossed); accounting `BudgetUtilisationAlertTests` - votes at 125%, 92% (by commitment) and 80% raise 100, 90 and 75 alerts, most used first, while a vote at 10% raises none. |
| **4.5** Bad-debt write-off with VAT relief | ✅ | The brief's credit control includes write-off with VAT relief; a write-off charged the whole gross, VAT included, to bad debt. `ISalesLedgerService.WriteOffWithVatReliefAsync(companyId, openItemId, date, badDebtAccount, reason, minimumDaysOverdue)`: the VAT share of what is still owed follows the invoice's own tax lines (tax-coded, or to the output VAT control account); that share is reclaimed against the output VAT account and only the net goes to bad debt, both allocated to the invoice in one unit. The VAT return, which reads tax account movements, then pays back only the tax actually collected. Relief is refused until the debt has been overdue for the qualifying days (`Ar.VatReliefTooEarly`). Endpoint `POST /api/ar/items/{id}/write-off-with-vat-relief`. Test: `VatReliefWriteOffTests` - 1,000 + 150 VAT part-paid 460 leaves 690; refused at 58 days against a 180-day rule; at 185 days 90 VAT is reclaimed and 600 written off; the invoice is settled and output VAT stands at the 60 collected. |
| **4.5** Lay-bye plans | ✅ | The brief lists lay-bye (layaway) plans with schedules and forfeiture rules for retail. `LaybyPlan` (`ar.layby_plan` with `ar.layby_line`: customer, number, warehouse, opened, final date, forfeiture %, deposit / revenue / forfeiture accounts, paid, Open / Completed / Cancelled). `ILaybyService`: `OpenAsync` (minimum deposit percentage; Dr bank / Cr lay-bye deposits, a liability, not revenue), `PayAsync` (no more than the balance; the payment that clears it completes the plan in the same unit: deposits to revenue and the goods issued from stock at cost), `CancelAsync` (forfeiture to income, the rest refunded through the account given) and `DefaultOverdueAsync` (cancels every open plan past its final date). Goods on open lay-byes count against free stock in `IStockAvailabilityService`. Tax on lay-bye sales is not yet split out. Migration `AddLaybyPlans`; endpoints under `/api/ar/laybys`. Test: `LaybyTests` - a 50 deposit is refused against a 20% minimum and 100 accepted; free stock drops by the item; 300 and 100 complete the sale (revenue 500, cost 200) and an overpayment is refused; a 10%-forfeit plan defaults after its date keeping 20 and owing 180; a 0% plan is cancelled refunding 150; deposits net to nil; check data clean. |
| **4.3** Supplier performance scorecard | ✅ | The brief's supplier master includes a performance scorecard (on-time %, quality %, price variance). Purchase orders gain an optional `RequiredBy` date (`CreatePurchaseOrderRequest.RequiredBy`). Pure `SupplierScoring` (`Rate` to one place, null when nothing was measured; `OnTime`, null when no date was set). `ISupplierScorecardService.ScorecardAsync(companyId, from, to)` scores every supplier with deliveries, returns or invoices in the period: deliveries, how many could be judged and were on time, units received and returned (a return's stock movement and AP credit note share a journal, which names the supplier), invoiced spend, on-time % and quality %. Price variance is not yet scored. Migration `AddPurchaseOrderRequiredBy`; endpoint `GET /api/pop/supplier-scorecard`. Tests: unit `SupplierScoringTests`; accounting `SupplierScorecardTests` - one supplier delivers one order early and one late (50%), an undated order is not judged, 2 of 20 units come back (90%) and 300 is invoiced; another is 100% on both. |
| **4.3** Requests for quotation and tenders | ✅ | The brief's RFQ / tender process: send requirements to several suppliers, capture quotes, compare them in a matrix (price, lead time, terms, score), award to a purchase order, with an adjudication record. `Rfq` (`pop.rfq` with `rfq_line`, `rfq_quote`, `rfq_quote_line`; invited suppliers as an array; Open / Awarded / Cancelled; awarded supplier, order, awarder and reason). Pure `TenderEvaluation.Score` (price and lead time earn points relative to the best quote, weighted 70 / 30 by default; ties rank the cheaper first). `IRfqService`: `IssueAsync` (at least two suppliers, none on hold), `RecordQuoteAsync` (invited suppliers only, by the closing date, pricing every line; a re-quote replaces the earlier one), `CompareAsync` (the matrix with unit prices, totals, points and rank, plus who has not quoted) and `AwardAsync` (a quoting supplier; passing over the top-ranked quote needs a reason; raises the purchase order at the quoted prices with a required-by date from the lead time, in one unit). Migration `AddRequestsForQuotation`; endpoints under `/api/pop/rfqs`. Tests: unit `TenderEvaluationTests`; accounting `RfqTests` - three suppliers invited; a cheap slow quote and a dear quick one; an outsider, a late quote and an incomplete one are refused; the quick quote ranks first (86 against 80); awarding the cheaper one needs a reason and raises an 800 order due 30 days on; a second award is refused. |
| **4.3** Quality inspection holds | ✅ | The brief's goods received note includes a quality inspection hold and rejection back to the supplier. `Warehouse.IsQuarantine` marks an inspection-hold location: stock there is left out of free stock in `IStockAvailabilityService` (unless that warehouse is asked about directly). `GrnInspection` (`pop.grn_inspection` with lines: passed, rejected, reason, inspector, release warehouse). `IQualityInspectionService.InspectAsync(companyId, grnId, request)` works only on goods the receipt put into quarantine and never more than is still uninspected; passed goods transfer to the release warehouse and rejected goods go back to the supplier for credit at the order price (a reason is required), all in one unit with the result recorded. Migration `AddQualityInspection`; endpoint `POST /api/pop/grns/{id}/inspection`. Test: `QualityInspectionTests` - 10 widgets received into quarantine leave nothing free to sell; inspection without a reason for the rejects is refused; 8 pass into the main store and 2 return for a 10 credit; more inspection than remains, and inspecting goods never held, are refused; the scorecard counts the 2 returned; check data clean. |
| **4.3** Invoice dispute queue | ✅ | The brief routes three-way-match mismatches to a query / dispute queue; a mismatch was only an error, and the invoice was lost. `InvoiceDispute` (`pop.invoice_dispute` with the invoice lines as presented: order, supplier, number, date, failure code and reason, Open / Accepted / Rejected, decider, note, posting). `IInvoiceDisputeService`: `SubmitAsync` matches, and queues a price or quantity mismatch instead of failing; `OpenAsync` lists the queue with each invoice's value; `AcceptAsync` posts a price dispute as presented with the decision recorded (a quantity dispute must wait for the goods); `RejectAsync` sends it back. Both decisions need a note. **FIX** a matched invoice used to debit GRNI with the invoice value, stranding any price difference on GRNI for ever. GRNI now clears at the order price, AP takes the invoice price, and the difference goes to the purchase price variance account on the request (`MatchInvoiceRequest.PriceVarianceAccountCode`; `Pop.NoPriceVarianceAccount` when one is needed and missing). Migration `AddInvoiceDisputes`; endpoints `POST /api/pop/invoices/submit` and under `/api/pop/invoice-disputes`. Test: `InvoiceDisputeTests` - invoices at 5.50 against 5 and for 12 against 10 received are queued with their reasons; the quantity one cannot be accepted and is rejected; the price one is accepted (AP 55, GRNI cleared at 50, variance 5); a 2% difference within tolerance needs a variance account; GRNI ends at nil and variance at 7. |
| **4.3** Purchase order amendments and re-approval | ✅ | The brief asks for PO amendments with version history and re-approval rules. `PurchaseOrder.Version`, `AwaitingReapproval`, `LastAmendedByUserId`; `AmendLine` (never below what was received; no price change once invoiced), `MarkAmended`, `Reapprove` (never the amender). `PurchaseOrderRevision` (`pop.purchase_order_revision`) keeps each replaced version - its lines as JSON, total, reason, amender and date. `IPurchaseOrderAmendmentService`: `AmendAsync` (reason required; an increase in value beyond the threshold, 10% by default, holds the order), `ReapproveAsync` and `HistoryAsync`. Receiving goods against an order awaiting re-approval is refused (`Pop.AwaitingReapproval`); cancelled and closed orders cannot be amended. Migration `AddPurchaseOrderAmendments` (existing orders start at version 1); endpoints `POST /api/pop/{id}/amend`, `/reapprove`, `GET /api/pop/{id}/history`. Test: `PurchaseOrderAmendmentTests` - an order for 10 with 4 received cannot drop to 3; raising it to 12 (+20%) makes version 2 and holds it; receiving is refused until a second user re-approves; the history shows version 1 at 50; a fully invoiced order cannot be amended; +5% on another order needs no re-approval. |
| **4.5** Credit limit approval workflow | ✅ | The brief's credit control includes a credit-limit approval workflow; limits were plain editable fields. `Customer.CreditLimit` is now set at creation, lowered directly with `LowerCreditLimit` (less risk, no approval), and raised only by `RaiseCreditLimit` with an approved `CreditLimitRequest` (`ar.credit_limit_request`: current and requested limit, reason, requester, Pending / Approved / Rejected, reviewer). A limit of 0 means none, as the invoice check always treated it; removing a limit counts as raising it. `ICreditLimitService`: `LowerAsync`, `RequestIncreaseAsync` (must raise the limit; one request at a time), `PendingAsync`, `ApproveAsync` (never the requester) and `RejectAsync` (reason required). The database enforces one pending request per customer and an approver other than the requester. Migration `AddCreditLimitRequests`; endpoints under `/api/ar/customers/{id}/credit-limit` and `/api/ar/credit-limit-requests`. Two existing tests now lower limits through `LowerCreditLimit`. Test: `CreditLimitApprovalTests` - a 1,200 invoice is refused at a 1,000 limit; lowering to 800 is immediate and a "lowering" to 1,500 refused; a request for 2,000 cannot be duplicated or self-approved, and once a manager approves the invoice posts; a rejected request to remove the limit leaves it at 2,000. |
| **4.5** Sales rep commission | ✅ | The brief's sales agents / reps with commission rules, statements and accruals; only cash-collection agents existed. `SalesRep` (`ar.sales_rep`: code, name, `CommissionBasis` Invoiced or Collected, rate) and `Customer.SalesRepId`; `CommissionAccrual` (`ar.commission_accrual`, per rep and month). `ICommissionService.StatementAsync` (per customer: on invoiced revenue it reads the income lines of the customers' invoice and credit-note journals, so tax is left out; on collections it counts receipts but not write-offs) and `AccrueAsync` (one journal for every active rep, Dr commission expense / Cr commission payable, booking only the difference from what the month already has, so reruns post nothing and late invoices are picked up). Margin-based commission is not yet offered. Migration `AddSalesCommission`; endpoints `GET /api/ar/sales-reps/{id}/commission-statement`, `POST /api/ar/commission/accrue`. Test: `SalesCommissionTests` - 5% on a 1,000 + 150 VAT invoice is 50; 2% on a 300 receipt is 6, with nothing on a write-off or for an unassigned customer; the March accrual books 56, a rerun nothing, a late 200 invoice 10 more; the statement shows 1,200 earning 60; expense and payable 66. |
| **4.3** Supplier volume rebates | ✅ | The brief lists rebates among supplier transactions. Pure `SupplierRebate` (retrospective tiers: the highest tier reached sets the rate on all spend; `RateFor`, `Earned`). `RebateAgreement` (`pop.rebate_agreement` with tiers: supplier, code, period, accrued so far). `IRebateService.CreateAsync` and `AccrueAsync(companyId, asAt, receivable, income)`: for every agreement in force, spend is the supplier's invoices less credit notes in the period to date; the rebate earned is accrued (Dr rebate receivable, Cr purchase rebates) booking only the change, so crossing a tier lifts earlier spend, a credit note that drops below a tier reverses the excess, and reruns post nothing. Migration `AddSupplierRebates`; endpoints `POST /api/pop/rebate-agreements`, `POST /api/pop/rebates/accrue`. Tests: unit `SupplierRebateTests`; accounting `SupplierRebateAccountingTests` - 600 at 1% accrues 6; 500 more crosses the 1,000 tier so 1,100 at 2% is 22 and 16 is booked; a rerun books nothing; a 200 credit note drops spend to 900 and reverses 13; another supplier's purchases are ignored; receivable and rebates stand at 9. |
| **4.3** Supplier blacklist | ✅ | The brief's supplier master includes a blacklist. `Supplier.IsBlacklisted` / `BlacklistReason` with `Blacklist(reason)` and `Reinstate()` (changes land in the audit trail). A blacklisted supplier cannot be given a new purchase order (`Pop.SupplierBlacklisted`), named on a requisition, or invited to quote; what is already owed can still be paid. **FIX** purchase order creation now checks the supplier exists in the company (`Pop.SupplierNotFound`); it used to accept any id. Migration `AddSupplierBlacklist`. Test: `SupplierBlacklistTests` - once blacklisted, an order, a requisition and an RFQ invitation are refused while an existing invoice is still paid; an order for an unknown supplier is refused; after reinstatement an order goes through. |
| **4.7** Locking reconciled bank periods | ✅ | The brief's bank reconciliation includes locking of reconciled periods. `BankAccount.ReconciledTo` / `ReconciledByUserId` and `SignOff`. `IBankReconciliationService.SignOffAsync(companyId, bankAccountId, asAt, userId)` signs off only a reconciliation that agrees to the cent with every statement line to the date in the ledger (`Cb.NotReconciled`), and only past any earlier sign-off. Once signed off: the posting engine refuses any entry to the bank's ledger account dated on or before it (`Posting.BankReconciled`, whatever module posts it); statement lines on or before it cannot be imported, and matches on or before it cannot be undone (`Cb.ReconciledLocked`). Migration `AddBankReconciliationSignOff`; endpoint `POST /api/banking/{id}/reconciliation/sign-off`. Test: `BankReconciliationLockTests` - a matched March is signed off; a March deposit, a March statement line and undoing the March match are refused while April posts; a second sign-off to March is refused; April cannot be signed off with a bank charge missing from the ledger. |
| **4.7** Transfers between bank accounts | ✅ | The brief's treasury needs transfers between accounts, including cross-currency with a realised exchange difference; the receipting `IBankingService.TransferAsync` only moved one currency between ledger codes. `ITreasuryService.TransferAsync(BankTransferRequest)` works between bank accounts: a same-currency transfer is one journal (Dr receiving bank, Cr paying bank). Across currencies the engine's rule that each currency balances within a journal means each leg posts in its own currency at the day's rate through a clearing account, and a third, base-currency journal moves what is left on the clearing account to exchange gain or loss, so the clearing account always ends at nil. All legs post in one unit. Refused: the same account twice, non-positive amounts, a same-currency transfer that arrives as a different amount, a cross-currency transfer without the amount received or the clearing and exchange accounts, or a missing rate. Endpoint `POST /api/banking/transfers`. No migration. Test: `TreasuryTransferTests` - 500 USD between USD accounts is one journal; 1,100 USD sent arriving as 980 EUR at 1.10 is worth 1,078, so 22 is an exchange loss; the clearing account is nil; invalid transfers are refused and post nothing. |
| **4.8** Discount and promotion vouchers | ✅ | The brief's second voucher kind: promo vouchers and coupons with campaigns, validity windows, eligibility, redemption caps and analysis; only a permission existed. `PromoCampaign` (`vou.promo_campaign` with `promo_code` and `promo_redemption`: percentage or amount off, valid from / to, minimum spend, uses per code, campaign-wide cap). Pure `PromoPricing.Discount` (to the cent, never more than the spend). `IPromoService`: `CreateCampaignAsync` (codes unique across campaigns), `QuoteAsync` (checks without using the code), `RedeemAsync` (locks the campaign so concurrent sales cannot overrun the caps; records the sale reference, spend and discount) and `AnalysisAsync` (codes issued and used, redemptions, spend, discount). Codes are case-insensitive. Eligibility by customer group or item category is not yet applied, and the discount itself is applied by the sale. Migration `AddPromoCampaigns`; endpoints under `/api/vouchers/promo-campaigns` and `/api/vouchers/promo-codes`. Tests: unit `PromoPricingTests`; accounting `PromoCampaignTests` - 10% off 85.55 is 8.56 on a quote that leaves the code unused; below minimum spend, outside the window, unknown and duplicate codes are refused; a single-use code cannot be used twice; the third redemption hits the cap of two; the analysis shows 3 codes, 2 used, spend 285.55, discount 28.56. |
| **4.8** Fuel, meal and allowance vouchers | ✅ | The brief's fifth voucher kind: serial-numbered vouchers issued to staff or contractors and reconciled against supplier invoices. `AllowanceVoucher` (`vou.allowance_voucher`: serial, kind Fuel / Meal / Other, face value, holder, issued, expires, Issued / Redeemed / Cancelled, supplier, use date, invoice). `IAllowanceVoucherService`: `IssueAsync` (consecutive serials under a prefix, never reissuing one), `ReconcileInvoiceAsync` (all or nothing: every voucher billed must have been issued, not cancelled, used within its validity and not billed before, appear once, and the invoice must equal their value; then each is marked redeemed against the invoice) and `OutstandingAsync` (by holder and kind, with how many have expired). Endpoints under `/api/vouchers/allowances`. Migration `AddAllowanceVouchers`. Test: `AllowanceVoucherTests` - five 50 fuel vouchers issued to a driver; an invoice billing three for 150 reconciles; billing one again, an unknown serial, one used after expiry, a repeated serial or a wrong total is refused and changes nothing; two remain outstanding, worth 100, both expired by April. |
| **4.8** Gift card servicing and history | ✅ | The brief's gift cards need top-up, lost / stolen block, reissue / replace and a full transaction history; the entity could top up and block but nothing exposed it. `GiftCardTransaction` (`vou.gift_card_transaction`: issue, redemption, top-up, block, replacement out and in, breakage, each with the balance after); issuing, redeeming and breakage now write to it. `GiftCard.ReplacedByCardId` and `TransferToReplacement`. `IGiftCardServicing`: `TopUpAsync` (Dr cash, Cr gift card liability; live cards only; one unit), `BlockAsync` (reason required), `ReplaceAsync` (a blocked card's balance and expiry move to a new number; the liability is unchanged so nothing posts; a card is replaced once) and `HistoryAsync`. Migration `AddGiftCardHistory`; endpoints under `/api/vouchers/gift-cards/{number}`. Test: `GiftCardServicingTests` - a 100 card is part-spent (30) and topped up 50 (balance 120); it is blocked when lost, can no longer be spent, and is replaced by a new card holding 120 that can be spent; replacing it again or topping up the blocked card is refused; both cards' histories read in order. |
| **4.8** Payment, receipt, journal and petty cash vouchers | ✅ | The brief's accounting vouchers: PV / RV / JV / petty cash documents with their own numbering, approval and a printed layout with signature blocks; only a permission existed. `AccountingVoucher` (`vou.accounting_voucher` with lines: type, number, date, party, cash account, narrative, preparer, approver, Draft / Posted / Cancelled, journal). `IAccountingVoucherService`: `PrepareAsync` (numbered from the type's own sequence, PV, RV, JV or PCV; payments and receipts need a cash account and positive lines; a journal voucher must balance), `ApproveAsync` (never the preparer, also enforced by a check constraint; posts as a manual journal, so control accounts are refused; one unit), `CancelAsync` (drafts only; the number stays used) and `RenderAsync`. Pure `AccountingVoucherFormatter` prints the title for the type, number, date, party, lines with account names, totals, the posting or "awaiting approval", and Prepared by / Approved by / Received by signature lines. Migration `AddAccountingVouchers`; endpoints under `/api/vouchers/accounting` (print as text). Tests: unit `AccountingVoucherFormatterTests`; accounting `AccountingVoucherTests` - a PV for 150 is numbered PV-, refused to its preparer and posted by a second user; an RV posts 200 in; an unbalanced JV is refused and a balanced one posts; a cancelled petty cash voucher cannot be approved; a PV to the AR control account is refused; the print shows the posting. |
| **4.8** Store requisition and issue vouchers | ✅ | The brief's internal stock requisition vouchers with approval and issue notes. `StoreRequisition` (`inv.store_requisition` with lines: number from the SR sequence, store, the department's expense account and cost centre, requester, approver, Requested / Approved / PartIssued / Issued / Rejected; requested and issued per item). `IStoreRequisitionService`: `RaiseAsync`, `ApproveAsync` (never the requester, also a check constraint) and `IssueAsync` (approved requisitions only; never more than outstanding; each issue gets an SIN issue note number and runs in one unit). `IssueStockRequest` gains `ChargeAccountCode` and `CostCentre`, so internal issues charge the department at stock cost instead of cost of sales; an issue reversal now credits whatever account and cost centre the issue charged. Migration `AddStoreRequisitions`; endpoints under `/api/stock/requisitions`. Test: `StoreRequisitionTests` - the canteen asks for 10 cups at 2 each; its requester cannot approve; before approval nothing issues; 6 issue (cost 12 to the canteen's supplies in its cost centre), then over-issuing is refused and the last 4 complete it; reversing an issue credits the canteen, not cost of sales. |
| **4.9** Indigent / social-tariff register | ✅ | The brief's municipal debt management includes an indigent register with means-test review dates and subsidy postings. `IndigentRegistration` (`mun.indigent_registration`: property, household income, subsidy share, approved from, review due, approver, Active / Revoked; one active per property, enforced in the database). `IIndigentRegisterService`: `RegisterAsync` (income at or under the council's threshold), `ReviewAsync` (a fresh means test extends the review date, or revokes a household now over the threshold), `ApplySubsidiesAsync` (for each household covered that month, its subsidy share of the month's bills is credited to the ratepayer through the sales ledger, Dr indigent subsidy; numbered IND-{stand}-{yyyyMM} so a rerun credits nothing twice; one unit) and `ReviewsDueAsync` (due within a window, flagging overdue ones, whose subsidy has stopped). Migration `AddIndigentRegister`; endpoints under `/api/municipal/indigent`. Test: `IndigentRegisterTests` - a 300-income household is registered at 50% and a 900-income one refused; March bills of 300 earn a 150 credit once; the June review shows as due; July's bill after the review date earns nothing; a review finding 600 revokes it; subsidy expense 150. |
| **4.9** Disconnection and reconnection orders | ✅ | The brief's municipal debt management includes a disconnection / reconnection workflow with orders and fees. `ServiceOrder` (`mun.service_order`: number from the DO / RO sequence, property, service, kind, raised, arrears when raised, Open / Executed / Cancelled, fee invoice; one open order per property service, enforced in the database). `IServiceOrderService`: `CandidatesAsync` (properties with the service connected whose overdue balance, less unallocated credits, reaches the threshold), `RaiseAsync` (disconnection only when connected and over the threshold; reconnection only when cut off and back under it), `ExecuteAsync` (disconnection stops the service; reconnection restores it and invoices the reconnection fee, in one unit) and `CancelAsync`. Migration `AddServiceOrders`; endpoints under `/api/municipal/disconnections` and `/api/municipal/service-orders`. Test: `ServiceOrderTests` - at a 200 threshold a stand 300 in arrears is the only candidate; the 100 debtor cannot be ordered; a second open order is refused; executing cuts the water; reconnection waits for payment, then restores the water and invoices a 50 fee; an executed order cannot run again. |
| **4.9** Legal handover, summons register and write-off approval | ✅ | The brief's debt management lists legal handover, a summons register and a write-off approval workflow. `DebtCase` (`ar.debt_case`: number from the DC sequence, debtor, collector or attorney, date, overdue amount handed over, HandedOver / Summonsed / Settled / Withdrawn / WrittenOff, summons number, court, issue and hearing dates, write-off requester, reason and approver; one open case per debtor and a write-off approver other than the requester, both enforced in the database). `IDebtCollectionService`: `HandOverAsync` (only an overdue balance, less unallocated credits), `RecordSummonsAsync`, `SummonsRegisterAsync`, `RequestWriteOffAsync` (reason required), `ApproveWriteOffAsync` (never the requester; writes off every outstanding invoice of the debtor and closes the case, in one unit) and `CloseAsync` (settled or withdrawn). Migration `AddDebtCases`; endpoints under `/api/ar/debt-cases` and `/api/ar/summons-register`. Test: `DebtCollectionTests` - a debtor with nothing overdue cannot be handed over; one with 500 overdue is, and cannot be handed over twice; a summons is recorded and appears on the register; a write-off cannot be approved before it is asked for or by its requester; once approved both invoices are written off and the case closes; bad debt 500. |
| **4.9** Rates clearance certificates | ✅ | A property transfer needs a rates clearance certificate: the ratepayer's arrears plus a number of months of charges in advance must be paid first. Pure `RatesClearance.Quote` (unit-tested) estimates a month's charges as the average billed over the three whole months before the application and adds the advance to the balance, net of any credit. `ClearanceCertificate` (`mun.clearance_certificate`: number from the CC sequence, property, issue date, valid-until date, advance held, issuer). `IClearanceService`: `QuoteAsync` and `IssueAsync` (refused with the amount due until nothing is; valid for a set number of days). Migration `RatesClearanceCertificates`; endpoints `/api/municipal/clearance/quote` and `/api/municipal/clearance`. Test: `ClearanceCertificateTests` - a stand billed 90 a month owes 270 plus 180 in advance; issue is refused while 450 and then 50 is due; once paid the certificate is issued, valid 60 days; an unknown property has no quote. |
| **4.9** Licensing and permits | ✅ | The brief's licensing sub-module: business, liquor, shop, building plan and hawker licences with renewal cycles, inspection records, fees and penalties. `CouncilLicence` (`mun.council_licence`: number from the LIC sequence, kind, holder customer, optional property, what it covers, issue and expiry dates, cycle months, fee per cycle, revenue account, Active / Suspended / Revoked, renewal count, revocation reason) and `CouncilInspection` (`mun.council_inspection`). Pure `LicenceRenewal` (unit-tested): a renewal always runs on from the old expiry (month-end expiries stay on the month end), so a late renewal pays every cycle it missed plus a penalty on the fee. `ICouncilLicensingService`: `IssueAsync` (invoices the first cycle to the holder through AR), `RecordInspectionAsync` (a failure, which needs findings, suspends; a pass reinstates), `RenewAsync` (refused while suspended or once revoked; one invoice splitting fee and penalty across their accounts), `RenewalsDueAsync` and `RevokeAsync`. Migration `AddCouncilLicensing`; endpoints under `/api/municipal/licences`. Test: `CouncilLicensingTests` - a liquor licence (200 a year) and a hawker permit (30 a quarter) are issued and invoiced; the permit is due first; a failed inspection suspends the liquor licence and blocks renewal until one passes; the permit renewed in August pays two quarters plus 10% (66); the liquor licence renewed in December pays 200; a revoked permit cannot be renewed or inspected; fees 490, penalties 6, debtors 496. |
| **4.9** Valuation roll, supplementary valuations and objections | ✅ | The brief's property register keeps a valuation roll with history and effective dates, supplementary valuations and an objections register; until now a property held one value. `PropertyValuation` (`mun.property_valuation`: property, effective date, value, General / Supplementary / Objection, roll name or document number, reason; never changed) and `ValuationObjection` (`mun.valuation_objection`: number from the OBJ sequence, the value objected to, claimed value, grounds, Lodged / Upheld / Dismissed, decision date, revised value and reasons; one open objection per property, enforced in the database). Pure `ValuationRoll` (unit-tested): the value in force on a date (latest effective date; a later record on the same date replaces the earlier) and the back-billing difference over bills already raised. **Rates bills now use the value in force on the bill date.** The first record for a property keeps its register value as an opening entry, so earlier bills keep theirs. `IValuationRollService`: `ImportRollAsync` (a general roll, all or nothing; unknown or repeated stands refused), `RecordSupplementaryAsync` (SV number; invoices or credits the rates difference on bills raised since it took effect), `LodgeObjectionAsync`, `DecideObjectionAsync` (upheld: the revised value replaces the one objected to from its date and the bills since are adjusted; dismissed: nothing changes), `HistoryAsync` and `ObjectionsAsync`. Migration `AddValuationRoll`; endpoints under `/api/municipal/valuation-rolls`, `/api/municipal/properties/{id}/…` and `/api/municipal/objections`. Test: `ValuationRollTests` - 0.1 cents in the dollar bills a 100,000 stand 100 a month; a supplementary 160,000 from February back-bills February and March (120) and April bills 160; an objection upheld at 130,000 credits three months (90) and May bills 130; a second open objection and a second decision are refused; a 2027 roll with an unknown stand is refused, then imported; rates revenue 870. |
| **4.9** Meter reading validation, estimates and replacement | ✅ | The brief's meters section asks for reading validation (high / low consumption, rollover, negative, tamper flags), estimated readings, meter replacement with reading carry-over and consumption history; readings were only checked for going backwards. `Meter` gains dials, install / removal dates, last actual reading and date, consumption estimated since then and a units credit; `MeterReading` gains actual / estimated kind, validation flags, accepted and billed consumption. Pure `MeterReadingRules` (unit-tested): a wrap past the last dial is a rollover, any other lower reading is rejected as negative, suspected tampering is recorded but rejected, consumption outside half to double the meter's own historical daily rate is flagged, an actual reading bills only what was not already estimated, and an over-estimate becomes a units credit netted off later consumption. `IMeterReadingService`: `InstallAsync`, `CaptureAsync`, `EstimateAsync`, `ReplaceAsync` (final reading, new meter at its start reading with the credit carried over, property services moved, old meter retired, in one unit), `ExceptionsAsync` and `HistoryAsync`. Migration `AddMeterReadingValidation` (existing readings backfilled as accepted actuals). Endpoints under `/api/municipal/meters` and `/api/municipal/reading-exceptions`. Test: `MeterReadingTests` - on a four-dial meter a 70 reading against an expected 30 is flagged high; a missed month is estimated at 50; the next actual 30 bills nothing and credits 20, which the next reading nets off; a tampered and a negative reading are rejected; 9180 to 40 is a rollover of 860; replacement takes a final 10, moves the water service and retires the old meter; the exceptions report lists six flagged readings. |
| **4.9** Prepaid vending hooks | ✅ | The brief's prepaid integration hooks: vending token purchase and the credit-off-arrears percentage rule. New `ServiceType.Electricity`. Pure `PrepaidVending.Split` (unit-tested): a percentage of each purchase, never more than is owed, goes to arrears; the rest buys units at the tariff's unit price, rounded down so the council never over-issues. `IPrepaidTokenGenerator` is the hook to a token vendor (an STS key management system); the built-in `PlaceholderPrepaidTokenGenerator` issues a deterministic 20-digit number for testing only and is registered only when the installation has not registered its own. `PrepaidSale` (`mun.prepaid_sale`: number from the PP sequence, property, service, meter, date, amount, arrears part, units, token, channel). `IPrepaidVendingService`: `VendAsync` (the arrears part is receipted to the ratepayer's account, the units part posts Dr bank / Cr the tariff's revenue account in the cash book module, in one unit) and `SalesAsync`. Migration `AddPrepaidVending`; endpoints `/api/municipal/prepaid/vend` and `/api/municipal/prepaid/sales`. Test: `PrepaidVendingTests` - at 0.25 a unit with 100 in arrears, 50 at 20% takes 10 and buys 160 units; 500 at 25% clears the last 90 and buys 1,640; 20 with no arrears buys 80; a stand without electricity and a zero amount are refused; tokens are unique; bank 570, electricity sales 470, account clear. |
| **4.9** Owner register and property transfers | ✅ | The brief's owner register: ownership history, joint ownership shares and transfers with clearance certificate issuance. `PropertyOwner` (`mun.property_owner`: property, owner customer, share, ratepayer flag, owned from / to, clearance certificate or register reference, deeds reference; one current share per owner, enforced in the database; never deleted, so it keeps the history). Pure `OwnershipShares.Problem` (unit-tested): at least one owner, each listed once with a positive share, shares totalling 100%, exactly one ratepayer. `IOwnershipService`: `RegisterOwnersAsync` (first registration only), `TransferAsync` (needs a clearance certificate for the stand, valid on the transfer date and not used before; ends the current owners the day before, records the buyers and makes their ratepayer the property's, in one unit), `OwnersAsync` (as at a date) and `HistoryAsync`. Migration `AddPropertyOwners`; endpoints under `/api/municipal/properties/{id}/owners` and `/transfer`. Test: `PropertyTransferTests` - a sole owner is registered once; a transfer without a certificate, after it expires or with shares of 90% is refused; joint buyers at 60 / 40 take over on 1 May with the seller ending on 30 April and the 60% buyer as ratepayer; the certificate cannot be used again. |
| **4.9** Pro-rata billing for part months | ✅ | The brief's billing run must pro-rate mid-period connections and disconnections; the fixed-charge run billed every active service a full month and skipped ended ones. Pure `ProRata` (unit-tested): the share of the month a service was in place, from its start date to its end date, and the charge to the cent. `RunFixedChargeBillingAsync` now bills every service in place for part of the month - connected by the month end and either running or ended within the month - at that share; a service connected after the month or ended before it is not billed, and one cut off for arrears (inactive without an end date) is still skipped. Single bills through `BillServiceAsync` are unchanged. No migration.
