Test: `ProRataBillingTests` - a 30 refuse charge bills 30 for a stand served all April, 20 for one connected on 11 April and 15 for one ended on 15 April (65), none for stands ended in March or connected in May; May bills three stands in full (90). Known limit: valuation back-billing recomputes a part-month rates bill at the full month's charge. |
| **4.9** Interest exemptions and compounding | ✅ | The brief's interest on arrears needs a configurable rate, compounding rule, grace period and exemption flags; the finance-charge run had the rate and grace period only. `Customer.InterestExempt` marks accounts never charged interest (government, charities, by resolution). `FinanceChargeRunRequest.ExemptIndigent` also exempts ratepayers of properties on the active indigent register, and `Compound` lets unpaid finance charges earn interest themselves (the default stays no interest on interest). `FinanceChargeRunResult.CustomersExempted` counts the overdue accounts left uncharged. Migration `AddCustomerInterestExempt`. Test: `FinanceChargeExemptionTests` - three accounts owe 1,000 from 1 January at 36.5%; the exempt and indigent accounts are skipped and the other is charged 30 for January; February under the compounding rule charges 30 plus 0.90 on the January charge. |
| **4.9** Payment arrangement schedules and default tracking | ✅ | The brief's debt management asks for payment plans with instalment schedules and default tracking; arrangements could be captured and reported but never moved off Active. Pure `InstalmentSchedule` (unit-tested): monthly instalments from the start date with a smaller last one, and an arrangement's standing on a date - receipts applied to instalments in order, the shortfall on those past their grace days, missed instalments, the next one due, honoured once the total is paid and defaulted once a set number are missed. `IArrangementMonitor`: `ScheduleAsync` and `ReviewAsync` (measures each active arrangement against receipts on the debtor's account from the month it started and marks it honoured or defaulted, saved together). No migration. Endpoints `/api/receipting/arrangements/{id}/schedule` and `/api/receipting/arrangements/review`. Test: `ArrangementReviewTests` - 600 at 200 a month paid in full is honoured; 900 at 300 with only the first paid has missed two by 31 March and defaults; a new 400 arrangement with its first instalment unpaid stays active; a second review sees only that one. |
| **4.9** Effective-dated tariff versions | ✅ | The brief wants effective-dated tariff versions so historical bills can be recomputed and explained; a tariff code could only exist once, so a rate change meant a new code and relinking every property. A code may now have one version per effective date (unique index on company, code and effective date). Billing resolves the active version of the linked tariff's code in force on the bill date, falling back to the linked version before any is in force, so a new version takes effect without relinking and a switched-off version is passed over. Migration `VersionTariffsByEffectiveDate`. Test: `TariffVersionTests` - a stand linked to the January version (10) bills 10 in June and 12 in July and August, skipping an inactive August version. |
| **4.9** Tariff versions in back-billing and vending | ✅ | Valuation back-billing and prepaid vending still used the linked tariff version. A shared internal `TariffVersions` resolver now serves billing, back-billing and vending: back-billing recomputes each bill at the version in force on its date (new date-aware `ValuationRoll.BackBilling` overload, unit-tested), finds bills under any version's name and credits the revenue account of the version in force on the adjustment date; vending sells at the unit price of the version in force on the sale date. No migration. Test: `TariffVersionTests` extended - 50 buys 200 units at 0.25 in June and 100 at 0.50 in July. |
| **4.9** Reviewed billing run | ✅ | The brief's billing run: select ward and service, generate bills in a reviewable proposed state with an exception report, approve, then post to AR; re-runnable and reversible before posting. Charge calculation is now shared (internal `MunicipalCharges`: properties in place for the month, optionally by ward; the charge at the tariff version and valuation in force, pro rata; the AR posting), used by single bills, the fixed-charge run and the new run. `BillingRun` (`mun.billing_run` with `billing_run_line`: number from the BR sequence, service, bill date and month, ward, prefix, Proposed / Posted / Cancelled, proposer and approver; one proposed run per service and month and an approver other than the proposer, both enforced in the database). Each line keeps the stand, ratepayer, bill number, charge, the last bill for the service, revenue account, narrative, exceptions (already billed, nothing to bill and failed are left out for good; a change beyond the run's tolerance against the last bill is flagged for review) and whether it is included. `IBillingRunService`: `ProposeAsync` (nothing posted), `LinesAsync` (all lines or the exception report), `SetIncludedAsync`, `ApproveAsync` (all included bills in one unit, refused if any was raised since) and `CancelAsync`. Migration `AddBillingRuns`; endpoints under `/api/municipal/billing-runs`. Test: `ReviewedBillingRunTests` - five Ward 1 stands proposed (90 to post, three exceptions) with nothing posted; a second proposal for April is refused; blocked lines cannot be put back; the large-change line is left out; the proposer cannot approve; approval posts two bills of 30; a posted run cannot be cancelled; a cancelled May run can be proposed again. |
| **4.9** Consumer enquiry desk | ✅ | The brief's enquiry desk: one screen showing a property's services, meters, readings, bills, payments, arrears and arrangements. `IPropertyEnquiryService.EnquireAsync` builds a read-only view as at a date: stand, ward, the value in force, ratepayer, current owners, each service with its tariff code, whether it is running, its dates and its meter's last reading; the ratepayer's balance and what is overdue less unallocated credits; the latest transactions, newest first; and the indigent subsidy, active payment arrangement, open disconnection and reconnection orders, licences held by the ratepayer or for the property, and the expiry of a clearance certificate still valid. No migration. Endpoint `GET /api/municipal/properties/{id}/enquiry` (the statement endpoint already prints the account). Test: `PropertyEnquiryTests` - metered water read at 120 and refuse ended in March; 100 and 50 billed with 30 received leave 120, of which 70 is overdue; the receipt and late-March bill are the latest two; subsidy, arrangement, disconnection order and hawker permit show; no valid clearance. |
| **4.9** Reading routes and reading import | ✅ | The brief's meters section asks for reading routes and meter readers, and readings imported from handhelds or a mobile app (CSV / API). `ReadingRoute` (`mun.reading_route` with `reading_route_stop`: code, name, reader, meters in walking order; a meter on one route at most, enforced in the database). Pure `MeterReadingCsvParser` (unit-tested): SERIAL, DATE and READING columns in any order with an optional TAMPER flag, dates as yyyy-MM-dd or dd/MM/yyyy, bad lines reported and the rest kept. `IReadingRouteService`: `CreateRouteAsync` (active meters, none routed twice), `RouteSheetAsync` (each meter in order with its stand and last reading) and `ImportReadingsAsync` (each line captured on its own through the validated reading capture, in date order; counts captured and rejected readings and reports unusable lines). Migration `AddReadingRoutes`; endpoints `/api/municipal/reading-routes`, `/api/municipal/reading-routes/{code}/sheet` and `/api/municipal/readings/import` (CSV body). Test: `ReadingRouteTests` - a two-meter route for a reader (duplicate code, routed meter and unknown meter refused); the sheet lists both in order with the stand and last reading; an import captures one reading, rejects a negative and a tampered one, and reports an unknown meter and a bad date by line. |
| **4.9** Service deposits | ✅ | The brief's service links hold a deposit, a liability not income. `ServiceDeposit` (`mun.service_deposit`: reference from the DEP sequence, property, ratepayer, service, amount, liability account, Held / Refunded / Forfeited, taken and released dates; one held deposit per property service, enforced in the database). `IServiceDepositService`: `TakeAsync` (Dr bank / Cr the liability account, in one unit), `RefundAsync` (Dr the liability account / Cr bank), `ApplyToArrearsAsync` (a receipt on the ratepayer's account funded from the deposit liability - Dr liability / Cr the AR control - leaving a credit the final bill settles against) and `HeldAsync`. Deposits post in the cash book module. Migration `AddServiceDeposits`; endpoints under `/api/municipal/deposits` and `/api/municipal/properties/{id}/deposits`. Test: `ServiceDepositTests` - a 150 water deposit is taken and a second for the service refused; one stand's is refunded; another's is applied to clear a 150 final bill; the consumer-deposits liability and the bank both net to zero and the account clears. |
| **4.9** Area-based tariffs | ✅ | The brief's tariff engine lists area-based charges; the engine had flat, per-unit, stepped and rate-per-valuation only. New `TariffType.AreaBased` with `Tariff.RatePerSquareMetre`: the charge is the availability charge plus the stand's area times the rate, with the minimum-charge floor. `CalculateCharge` gains a three-argument overload taking the area (the two-argument one delegates with no area), and billing passes the property's size. Migration `AddAreaBasedTariff` (one column). Tests: unit `TariffTests` - 5 + 800 × 0.02 = 21, nothing without an area; accounting `AreaBasedTariffTests` - a roads levy bills an 800 m² stand 21 and a 1,500 m² stand 35. |
| **4.9** Tariffs by consumer category and ward | ✅ | The brief's tariff engine varies tariffs by consumer category (domestic, commercial, industrial, institutional, agricultural, indigent) and ward. New `ConsumerCategory` enum; `Tariff.Category` (default domestic) and `Tariff.Ward` (null for the whole council). Pure `TariffCatalogue.Best` (unit-tested): the category must match exactly, a ward-specific tariff beats a council-wide one, and the latest version in force on the date wins. `ITariffCatalogueService`: `ListAsync` (the tariffs offered for a service) and `ResolveAsync` (the one that applies to a consumer). Billing still bills the tariff a service is linked to; the catalogue chooses it when linking. Migration `AddTariffCategoryAndWard` (existing tariffs default to domestic, council-wide). Endpoints `/api/municipal/tariffs` and `/api/municipal/tariffs/resolve`. Tests: unit `TariffCatalogueTests` - ward beats council-wide, category matches exactly, latest in force wins; accounting `TariffCatalogueAccountingTests` - a Ward 3 domestic consumer gets the Ward 3 tariff, Ward 9 the council-wide one, commercial the commercial tariff, industrial none. |
| **4.9** Seasonal tariffs | ✅ | The brief's tariff engine lists seasonal rates. `TariffSeason` (`mun.tariff_season`: a per-unit rate for a range of months, wrapping the year end when the first month is after the last) with `Tariff.AddSeason` and `PerUnitRateFor(month)`. `CalculateCharge` gains a four-argument overload taking the bill month (the three-argument one delegates with none); a per-unit tariff bills consumption at the covering season's rate, or the standard rate when no season covers the month. Billing passes the bill date's month. Migration `AddTariffSeasons` (one table). Tests: unit `TariffSeasonTests` - a wrapped October–March dry season bills at 1.5 and other months at 1, a non-wrapping winter season covers only its months, a month outside 1–12 is refused; accounting `SeasonalTariffTests` - 50 m³ bills 75 in January and 50 in June. |
| **4.9** Facility hire (halls, stalls, grounds) | ✅ | The brief's other council revenue streams - hall hire, market stalls and the like. `Facility` (`mun.facility`: code, name, daily rate, revenue account, active) and `FacilityBooking` (`mun.facility_booking`: number from the FB sequence, facility, hirer, first and last day, fee, purpose, Booked / Cancelled, refund). Pure `BookingCalendar` (unit-tested): inclusive ranges overlap when they share a day, days count both ends, the fee is days times the daily rate and the refund a clamped share of it. `IFacilityBookingService`: `CreateFacilityAsync`, `BookAsync` (locks the facility row, refuses any day already booked, invoices the hire through AR, in one unit), `CancelAsync` (credits the refund percentage and frees the days) and `CalendarAsync`. Migration `AddFacilityBookings`; endpoints under `/api/municipal/facilities` and `/api/municipal/facility-bookings`. Test: `FacilityBookingTests` - the town hall at 200 a day is booked 10–12 May (600); a booking sharing the 12th is refused while 13–14 May is taken (400); cancelling that with a 50% refund credits 200 and frees the 13th for another booking; hire income nets to 1,000. |
| **4.9** Cemetery register | ✅ | The brief's cemetery revenue stream. `CemeteryPlot` (`mun.cemetery_plot`: reference cemetery-section-number, price, capacity, Available / Sold / Full, holder, sale invoice and date) with `CemeteryInterment` (`mun.cemetery_interment`, the burial register: number from the BUR sequence, deceased, date of death, burial date). `ICemeteryService`: `AddPlotsAsync` (no duplicates), `SellAsync` (an available plot only; invoices its price from the CP sequence, in one unit), `RecordIntermentAsync` (a sold plot with room only; invoices any burial fee to the holder; the plot is full at capacity), `PlotsAsync` (by state) and `BurialRegisterAsync`. Migration `AddCemeteryRegister`; endpoints under `/api/municipal/cemeteries`, `/api/municipal/cemetery-plots`, `/api/municipal/interments` and `/api/municipal/burial-register`. Test: `CemeteryTests` - three single plots and a family plot for two are added (a repeat refused); an unsold plot cannot take a burial; a single plot is sold once, takes one burial with a 50 fee and is then full; the family plot takes two burials and refuses a third; the available list and burial register match; cemetery income 650. |
| **4.10.3** Housing waiting list | ✅ | The brief's waiting list: application intake, configurable qualification rules, registration with a protected registration date, priority scoring with a printable explanation, and applicant enquiry. `HousingApplicant` gains a waiting-list number (WL sequence), category (high / medium / low density, flats, rental), date of birth, residence date, dependants, disability, income, current tenure and status (Active / Suspended / Removed / Allocated); one record per national ID and unique waiting-list numbers, enforced in the database. `WaitingListPolicy` (`hse.waiting_list_policy`, one per council): minimum age, minimum residency years, income ceiling and the points per factor. `Customer.NationalIdNumber` lets the list check ownership. Pure `WaitingListRules` (unit-tested): full years, every failed qualification rule (age, residency, income, already owns property, already allocated) and the score - years on the list, years resident, dependants, disability and housing conditions - line by line. `IWaitingListService`: `SetPolicyAsync`, `RegisterAsync` (refuses a duplicate ID, an owner of a council stand or a current municipal owner matched on national ID, and anyone not qualifying), `RankingAsync` (by seniority, or by score with seniority breaking ties) and `EnquireAsync` (by waiting-list number or ID: position, status, score explanation). Migration `AddHousingWaitingList`; endpoints under `/api/housing/waiting-list`. Tests: unit `WaitingListRulesTests`; accounting `WaitingListTests` - two registrants; a duplicate ID, a two-year resident and a current property owner are refused; by seniority the older leads, by score (122 against 112) the order is explained line by line. Still to come: dual-authorised registration-date changes, renewal cycles, duplicate-applicant queue. |
| **4.10.4** Stand allocation runs | ✅ | The brief's allocation - the highest-fraud-risk process - with its hard controls in the database. `AllocationRun` (`hse.allocation_run`: number from the ALR sequence, scheme, category, Seniority / Score / Ballot, ballot seed, the exact candidate and stand inputs and their SHA-256 hash, Proposed / Approved / Cancelled, proposer and approver - never the same, by check constraint) with `StandAllocation` (`hse.stand_allocation`: stand, applicant, Proposed / Offered / Accepted / Declined / Lapsed / Cancelled, certificate number from the AC sequence, offer deadline, decision and reason). Unique partial indexes allow **one live allocation per stand and one per beneficiary**, and certificate numbers are never reused. Pure `AllocationDraw` (unit-tested, with draws checked against an independent computation): the input hash, a seeded ballot using its own SplitMix64 generator and Fisher–Yates shuffle so it never changes with the runtime, and stand-to-candidate pairing. `HousingStand.MarkAllocated` / `ReleaseAllocation`. `IAllocationService`: `ProposeAsync` (available stands without a live allocation; eligible applicants ranked by seniority or score, or drawn by ballot with a recorded seed), `ApproveAsync` (not by the proposer; certificates and offers, stands allocated, refused if a stand has gone), `CancelAsync`, `AcceptAsync` (by the deadline; the applicant leaves the list), `DeclineAsync` (reason required; stand back to the pool), `LapseExpiredAsync` and `VerifyDrawAsync` (re-hashes the stored inputs and re-runs the draw from the stored seed). Migration `AddStandAllocations`; endpoints under `/api/housing/allocation-runs` and `/api/housing/allocations`. Test: `StandAllocationTests` - a seniority run pairs the two longest waiting; nothing more can be proposed while it is live; the proposer cannot approve; approval offers both with certificates; one acceptance (a late one refused), one decline returning its stand; a seeded ballot verifies and reproduces exactly after cancellation; a lapsed offer returns its stand; the database refuses a second live allocation of a stand. Still to come: committee workflow, substitutions, appeals register, offer letters and deposit receipting. |
| **4.10.5** Instalment sales of stands | ✅ | The brief's sale on instalments - deposit, term, flat / reducing-balance / zero interest, early settlement with rebate - accounted per the §4.10.9 ruling. `InstalmentAgreement` (`hse.instalment_agreement`: number from the IA sequence, stand, buyer, cash price, deposit, rate, method, term, signature date, interest account, Active / SettledEarly / Completed; one active agreement per stand) with `InstalmentScheduleLine` (due date, instalment, interest, principal, balance after, raised, rebated). Pure `InstalmentPlan` (unit-tested against independently computed figures): zero and flat plans spread evenly and a reducing-balance plan as a level annuity, every figure to the cent with the last instalment clearing the principal; and the settlement quote. `IInstalmentSaleService`: `SignAsync` (control passes on signature: the stand is sold at the cash price through the housing service - full receivable and cost of sales - and the deposit receipted, in one unit), `RaiseDueAsync` (each instalment falling due raises its interest as income; the principal is already receivable; never twice), `QuoteSettlementAsync` and `SettleEarlyAsync` (instalments due stand, later interest is rebated and never charged). Migration `AddInstalmentSales`; endpoints under `/api/housing/instalment-sales`. Test: `InstalmentSaleTests` - a 13,000 sale with 1,000 down and 12,000 at 12% over a year: sales 13,000 and cost of sales 25,000 on signature; two instalments to March raise 230.54 interest (a repeat raises nothing); settling in mid-March rebates 563.69 and leaves 12,230.54 receivable. Still to come: penalty interest on instalment arrears, escalation, discounting of long-term receivables, dedicated housing AR controls. |
| **4.10.7** Council rental housing | ✅ | The brief's council rental stock: unit register, tenancies, deposit held as a liability, the monthly rent debit run, receipting and arrears through AR, and voids. `RentalUnit` (`hse.rental_unit`: code, description, house / flat / room / hostel bed / cottage, rooms, monthly rent, rental income account, vacant-since date; kept out of the stands inventory) and `Tenancy` (`hse.tenancy`: number from the TN sequence, unit, tenant, start and end, rent at letting, deposit and its liability account, Active / Ended; one active tenancy per unit). Pure `RentalArithmetic` (unit-tested): pro-rata rent for a part month, the deposit split between arrears and refund, void days and rent lost at the annual rent over 365 days. `IRentalHousingService`: `AddUnitAsync`, `LetAsync` (vacant units only; Dr bank / Cr deposits held, in one unit), `RunRentAsync` (each tenancy covering the month, pro rata; a month is never billed twice), `EndTenancyAsync` (bills any rent not yet raised to the last day, applies the deposit to what the tenant owes and refunds the rest, puts the unit back as void, in one unit) and `VoidsAsync`. Migration `AddCouncilRentals`; endpoints `/api/housing/rental-units`, `/api/housing/tenancies`, `/api/housing/rent-runs` and `/api/housing/voids`. Test: `RentalHousingTests` - a flat let from 16 March with a 400 deposit bills 154.84 for March (once) and 300 for April; ending on 15 May bills 145.16, applies 300 of the deposit and refunds 100; voids show the flat at 16 days and a house at 151; rental income 600, deposits and the tenant's account clear. Still to come: the eviction workflow, rent reviews and escalation, maintenance and inspections. |
| **4.10.7** Eviction workflow | ✅ | The brief's eviction workflow: reminder → demand → notice to vacate → legal handover → court order → eviction → unit returned. `EvictionCase` (`hse.eviction_case`: number from the EV sequence, tenancy, arrears at opening, notice period, Open / Evicted / Withdrawn, withdrawal reason; one open case per tenancy) with `EvictionStep` (`hse.eviction_step`: stage, date, reference, user; each stage once). Pure `EvictionWorkflow.Problem` (unit-tested): stages strictly in order and never back-dated, legal handover only after the notice period, a court order only with its reference, nothing after eviction. `IEvictionService`: `OpenAsync` (an active tenancy that owes money; starts with the reminder), `AdvanceAsync` (eviction ends the tenancy through the rental service - rent to the day, deposit applied and refunded, unit returned as void - in one unit), `WithdrawAsync` (reason required) and `RegisterAsync`. Migration `AddEvictionWorkflow`; endpoints under `/api/housing/evictions`. Test: `EvictionTests` - a paid-up tenancy cannot be pursued; a tenant owing March and April rent goes through every stage (a skipped stage, an early legal handover and a court order without reference are refused) and is evicted on 1 August, ending the tenancy and voiding the unit; another case is withdrawn once the arrears are paid. |
| **4.10.9** Repossession of instalment-sale stands | ✅ | The brief's accounting ruling for repossessions: derecognise the receivable, recognise the stand back into inventory at the lower of its previous carrying amount and net realisable value, take the difference to profit or loss and keep it separately reportable. `Repossession` (`hse.repossession`: reference from the RP sequence, agreement, stand, date, receivable derecognised, previous carrying amount, NRV, inventory recognised, reason, requester and approver - never the same, by check constraint; one per agreement). `HousingStand.Repossess` returns the stand to the available pool at its new carrying amount and records the repossession on the tamper-evident ownership chain (`OwnershipChangeType.Repossession`); `InstalmentAgreement.Repossess` closes the agreement so no further interest is raised. `IRepossessionService`: `RepossessAsync` (the buyer's receipts are auto-allocated, every open debt written off to the repossession gain/loss account, the stand restocked against it, in one unit) and `RegisterAsync`. Migration `AddRepossessions`; endpoints `/api/housing/repossessions`. Test: `RepossessionTests` - a stand costing 25,000 sold on instalments for 13,000 defaults with 12,230.54 owed; self-approval is refused; repossession at NRV 20,000 returns it to inventory at 20,000 and the available pool, leaves the ownership chain intact, closes the agreement and books a 7,769.46 gain; a second repossession is refused; receivables clear and stands available stand at 95,000. |
| **4.10.7** Rent reviews and escalation | ✅ | The brief's rent tariffs with effective-dated escalation and a rent review run. `TenancyRentChange` (`hse.tenancy_rent_change`: tenancy, effective from the first of a month, new rent; one per tenancy and date) with `Tenancy.RentOn(date)`; the rent run and final billing now bill each month at the rent in force on its first day, so a month billed late keeps its own rent. `RentalArithmetic.Escalate` (unit-tested). `IRentalHousingService.ReviewRentsAsync` raises the rent of every unit, or every unit of one type, by a percentage from the first of a month and gives each running tenancy on them a rent change from that date, in one unit; a date that is not the first of a month and a repeat review for the same date are refused. Migration `AddRentReviews`; endpoint `POST /api/housing/rent-reviews`. Test: `RentReviewTests` - a 10% flats review from 1 June raises the let flat to 330 while the house stays at 500; May billed afterwards is still 300; June bills 330; a flat let later starts at the reviewed rent. |
| **4.10.7** Rental maintenance and inspections | ✅ | The brief's maintenance (repairs recharged where tenant-caused, cost per unit history) and inspections (findings and follow-up actions). `UnitRepair` (`hse.unit_repair`: number from the RPR sequence, unit, the tenancy at the time, fault, Reported / Completed / Cancelled, cost, tenant-caused, recharge invoice) and `UnitInspection` (`hse.unit_inspection`: unit, date, inspector, condition grade 1–5 by check constraint, findings, follow-up and its closure). `IRentalMaintenanceService`: `ReportRepairAsync` (linked to the running tenancy), `CompleteRepairAsync` (Dr repairs / Cr the account paid from; a tenant-caused repair is recharged to the tenant through AR, refused when the unit was empty; in one unit), `RecordInspectionAsync`, `CloseFollowUpAsync` and `HistoryAsync` (open repairs, repair cost, recharges, latest grade, open follow-ups). Migration `AddRentalMaintenance`; endpoints under `/api/housing/rental-units/{code}/…`, `/api/housing/repairs` and `/api/housing/inspections`. Test: `RentalMaintenanceTests` - a 450 geyser repair and a 120 tenant-caused window repair (recharged); a repair on an empty flat cannot be recharged; a repair cannot be completed twice; an inspection graded 2 with a follow-up, then closed; repairs 570, recharges 120. |
| **4.10.9** Housing debtors expected credit loss | ✅ | The brief's ruling: housing debtors behave nothing like trade debtors and must be provided for separately, by arrears bucket and by scheme. `IHousingEclService` assigns each housing debtor to a portfolio - instalment-sale buyers by the scheme of the stand they bought (or no scheme), council tenants to rentals - and bands their receivables at the date by days past due on the instalment or rental matrix (credits set against the oldest debts), using the existing pure `ExpectedCreditLoss`. `PostAsync` books only the movement on a dedicated housing allowance (Dr impairment / Cr allowance, or the release), so a repeat posts nothing; ordinary trade debtors stay with the general provision. No migration. Endpoints `/api/housing/ecl/assess` and `/post`. Test: `HousingEclTests` - a scheme buyer owing 12,000 fifty days past due needs 2,400 on a 2% / 20% matrix, a tenant owing 300 thirty days past due needs 150 on a 5% / 50% matrix, a trade debtor is excluded; posting raises 2,550 and a repeat posts nothing. |
| **4.10.3** Waiting-list integrity controls | ✅ | The brief protects the registration date fiercely - any change is a dual-authorisation, fully audited event - and requires periodic re-registration with non-responders suspended then removed, with a notification history. `RegistrationDateChange` (`hse.registration_date_change`: old and new date, reason, Pending / Approved / Rejected, requester and decider - never the same, by check constraint; one pending per applicant) and `HousingApplicant.ChangeRegistrationDate`, which only accepts an approved change. `HousingApplicant.LastConfirmedOn` and `Confirm`. `ApplicantNotice` (`hse.applicant_notice`) records each suspension and removal. Pure `WaitingListRenewal.Assess` (unit-tested): overdue applicants are suspended, and removed once the grace period has passed. `IWaitingListControlService`: `RequestDateChangeAsync`, `DecideDateChangeAsync` (approval moves the date), `ConfirmAsync` (reinstates a suspended applicant), `RunRenewalsAsync` and `NoticesAsync`. Migration `AddWaitingListControls`; endpoints under `/api/housing/waiting-list/…` and `/api/housing/date-changes`. Test: `WaitingListControlTests` - a date change waits for a second officer and then lifts seniority from one year to two; a rejected change moves nothing; an annual renewal run removes two long-overdue applicants and spares one who confirmed; a newly overdue applicant is suspended, confirms and is reinstated; the notice history records each step. |
| **4.10.4** Allocation appeals register | ✅ | The brief's appeals register: unsuccessful applicants' appeals, grounds, hearing, outcome and any resulting re-allocation. `AllocationAppeal` (`hse.allocation_appeal`: number from the APL sequence, appellant, run, grounds, Lodged / HearingSet / Upheld / Dismissed, hearing and decision dates, reasons, re-allocation certificate; one appeal per applicant per run). `IAllocationAppealService`: `LodgeAsync` (an approved run only, by an applicant it did not allocate), `SetHearingAsync`, `DecideAsync` (only after the hearing, with reasons), `LinkReallocationAsync` (an upheld appeal, to a certificate actually issued to the appellant) and `RegisterAsync`. Migration `AddAllocationAppeals`; endpoints under `/api/housing/appeals`. Test: `AllocationAppealTests` - the allocated applicant cannot appeal; the other appeals once; no decision before the hearing; upheld with reasons; another applicant's certificate cannot be linked, the appellant's from a later run can; the register shows the appeal through to re-allocation. |
| **FIX** Allocation certificates unique per council | ✅ | The appeals test exposed a real defect: allocation certificate numbers come from each council's own AC sequence, but their unique index covered all councils, so a second council's first approval would fail on the first council's AC-…-000001. `StandAllocation` now records its council (set from the run it is added to), the unique index is on council and certificate, and the migration fills the council on existing allocations from their runs. Verified by `AllocationAppealTests` and `StandAllocationTests` running side by side. |
| **4.10.5** Development covenants | ✅ | The brief's covenant - build within N years or the stand reverts - with plan submission and approval, construction commencement and completion, extensions with a fee, breaches, penalty levies and the notice of intention to repossess. `DevelopmentCovenant` (`hse.development_covenant`: number from the COV sequence, stand, holder, start, deadline, Open / Complied / Breached / NoticeIssued, extensions, breach and notice dates; one live covenant per stand) with `CovenantStep` (`hse.covenant_step`: plan submitted → plan approved → construction started → completed, each once with its reference). `IDevelopmentCovenantService`: `CreateAsync`, `RecordMilestoneAsync` (strictly in order, never back-dated; completion discharges the covenant), `GrantExtensionAsync` (fee invoiced to the holder; a breached covenant reopens; in one unit), `RunBreachesAsync` (open covenants past their deadline are breached and levied once per deadline), `IssueNoticeAsync` (breached only) and `RegisterAsync`. Migration `AddDevelopmentCovenants`; endpoints under `/api/housing/covenants`. Test: `DevelopmentCovenantTests` - one holder completes every milestone (a skipped and a back-dated one refused) and complies; the other is breached and levied 500 once, extended six months for 200, breached and levied again, then served notice and refused a further extension; covenant income 1,200. |
| **4.10.5** Stand leases straight-lined (IFRS 16 lessor) | ✅ | The brief's leases of stands - commercial, industrial, agricultural, up to 99 years with rent reviews - and the §4.10.9 ruling that rental income is straight-lined over the term where escalations are fixed. `StandLease` (`hse.stand_lease`: number from the LS sequence, stand, lessee, use, start on the first of a month, term 1–99 years, first-year rent, review interval and fixed escalation, income and accrued lease income accounts; no overlapping leases of a stand). Pure `LeaseSchedule` (unit-tested): the rent each year, the monthly contractual rent and the straight-line monthly income, last months taking the rounding so both total the rent exactly. `IStandLeaseService`: `CreateAsync`, `ScheduleAsync` and `RunMonthAsync` - each lease covering the month invoices the contractual rent through AR and posts the difference to the straight-line income against accrued lease income; a month is never billed twice. Migration `AddStandLeases`; endpoints under `/api/housing/stand-leases`. Test: `StandLeaseTests` - a three-year lease at 12,000 escalating 10% a year (39,720): January bills 1,000 and recognises 1,103.33; after a year accrued income stands at 1,239.96; billed to the end, income and rent both total 39,720 and accrued income returns to nothing; overlapping and mid-month leases are refused. |
| **4.10.9** Housing grants and subsidies (IAS 20) | ✅ | The brief's ruling: recognise grants in line with the expenditure they compensate, defer them while conditions are outstanding, and disclose unfulfilled conditions. `HousingGrant` (`hse.housing_grant`: number from the GR sequence, grantor, optional scheme, amount, budgeted expenditure, conditions, received date, deferred and income accounts, conditions-assured date, expenditure and income recognised to date; recognised never above the grant, by check constraint). Pure `GrantRecognition.TargetToDate` (unit-tested): the grant's share of the budget spent, capped at the grant, nothing until the conditions are assured. `IHousingGrantService`: `ReceiveAsync` (Dr bank / Cr deferred, in one unit), `AssureConditionsAsync` (recognises any income already earned), `RecordExpenditureAsync` (cumulative; posts only the movement Dr deferred / Cr income) and `DisclosureAsync`. Migration `AddHousingGrants`; endpoints under `/api/housing/grants`. Test: `HousingGrantTests` - a 250,000 grant towards a 1,000,000 budget recognises nothing at 400,000 spent until assured, then 100,000, then 175,000 at 700,000; expenditure cannot go down; the disclosure shows 75,000 deferred and a second grant's unfulfilled conditions with 50,000 deferred. |
| **4.10.9** Compensation on compulsory acquisition (IAS 37) | ✅ | The brief's ruling: compensation payable on compulsory acquisition is a provision once the obligation is probable and measurable, not on payment. `CompensationClaim` (`hse.compensation_claim`: number from the CMP sequence, land parcel, claimant, lodged date, land cost and provision accounts, Lodged / Provided / Settled / Withdrawn, provision held, amount settled). `ICompensationService`: `LodgeAsync` (nothing provided yet), `ProvideAsync` (the best estimate, capitalised into land under development; remeasurement posts only the movement either way), `SettleAsync` (the provision used, the payment made, any difference adjusting the land cost, in one unit), `WithdrawAsync` (the provision released against the land cost) and `RegisterAsync`. Migration `AddCompensationClaims`; endpoints under `/api/housing/compensation-claims`. Test: `CompensationTests` - on a parcel bought for 100,000, one claim provided at 30,000, remeasured to 36,000 and settled for 35,000 (the 1,000 excess comes out of land cost), another provided at 8,000 and withdrawn; land WIP 135,000, provisions nil, bank down 135,000. |
| **4.10.5** Cooperative allocations | ✅ | The brief's cooperative allocations: allocation to a cooperative, which sub-allocates to members - both levels tracked. `Cooperative` (`hse.cooperative`: code, name, registration number) with `CooperativeMember` (`hse.cooperative_member`: national ID, name, joined, active; an ID once per cooperative) and `CooperativeStand` (`hse.cooperative_stand`: cooperative, stand, allocated date, member and sub-allocation date). The database holds both levels: a stand belongs to one cooperative and a member holds one stand. `ICooperativeService`: `RegisterAsync`, `AddMemberAsync`, `RemoveMemberAsync`, `AllocateStandsAsync` (available stands with no individual or cooperative allocation, marked allocated, in one unit), `SubAllocateAsync` (the cooperative's own stand, to an active member holding no other) and `StandsAsync` (the two-level register). Migration `AddCooperatives`; endpoints under `/api/housing/cooperatives`. Test: `CooperativeTests` - a cooperative of three takes two stands (a second cooperative cannot take one); a member gets one stand and is refused a second; a member who left and a stand outside the cooperative are refused; the register shows each stand with its member. |
| **4.10.5** Agreement register with versions | ✅ | The brief's agreement register: number, type, parties, stand, price, terms, signature dates, witnesses, special conditions and the generated agreement document stored against the record; amendments create a new version and the superseded one is retained. `HousingAgreement` (`hse.housing_agreement`: number from the AGR sequence, cash sale / instalment sale / lease / rental / cooperative, party, stand) with `AgreementVersion` (`hse.agreement_version`: version, price, terms, special conditions, amendment reason, the generated document and its SHA-256 hash, signature date and witnesses). Pure `AgreementDocument` (unit-tested): renders the document from its content - the same content always the same text - and hashes it. `IAgreementRegisterService`: `DraftAsync` (version 1 with its document), `SignAsync` (the newest version, two different witnesses; it then becomes the version in force), `AmendAsync` (a signed agreement only; a new unsigned version with its own document while the old one stays in force), `HistoryAsync` and `VerifyDocumentsAsync` (re-hashes every stored document and names any altered). Migration `AddAgreementRegister`; endpoints under `/api/housing/agreements`. Test: `AgreementRegisterTests` - drawn up, refused amendment before signature and signature with one witness; signed; amended to version 2 (version 1 in force until version 2 is signed); both kept; a document altered directly in the database is caught. |
| **4.10.8** Informal settlements & regularisation | ✅ | The brief's settlement register, enumeration exercises (household survey, structures and occupants), eligibility for regularisation, upgrading plan, and conversion of an enumerated household into a waiting-list registration or a direct allocation - with the same anti-double-allocation checks. `InformalSettlement` (`hse.informal_settlement`: code, ward, parcel, cut-off date, approved upgrading plan), `EnumerationExercise` (`hse.enumeration_exercise`, one open per settlement by partial unique index) and `EnumeratedHousehold` (`hse.enumerated_household`: ENU number, head of household, household size, income, occupied-since, structure type and reference, eligibility with reasons, outcome; a person is enumerated once per council and a stand regularised to one household, both by unique index). Pure `RegularisationRules` (unit-tested): occupied on or before the cut-off, adult head (the waiting-list policy's minimum age, on the enumeration date), not already holding council land or property. New `CurrentTenure.InformalSettlement`, scored as homeless. `IInformalSettlementService`: `RegisterAsync`, `ApprovePlanAsync`, `StartEnumerationAsync`/`CloseEnumerationAsync`, `EnumerateAsync`, `ConvertToWaitingListAsync` (through the waiting list's own registration checks), `RegulariseAsync` (approved plan; stand available and not in a live allocation, a cooperative or another regularisation; the person not allocated through the waiting list or a cooperative and holding no council stand or property; takes them off the waiting list) and `SummaryAsync`. Migration `AddInformalSettlements`; endpoints under `/api/housing/settlements`. Test: `InformalSettlementTests` - enumeration needs an open exercise; duplicate ID refused; late occupier and minor ineligible; no regularisation before the plan; stand regularised once; a cooperative stand and a cooperative member refused; a household waiting-listed with informal-settlement tenure; summary counts. |
| **4.10.8** Developer / partner agreements | ✅ | The brief's developer agreements: the developer services and builds, the council contributes land; the stands are split between a council and a developer quota; delivery obligations and penalties; a reconciliation of stands handed over vs contracted. `DeveloperAgreement` (`hse.developer_agreement`: DEV number, scheme, developer account, quotas, deadline, penalty per stand per month, penalties charged) with `DeveloperAgreementStand` (`hse.developer_agreement_stand`, a stand on one agreement by unique index) and `DeliveryMilestone`. Pure `DeveloperDelivery` (unit-tested): months or part months late, and the penalty on council stands - delivered ones to their hand-over, outstanding ones to the assessment date. `IDeveloperAgreementService`: `CreateAsync`, `AddMilestoneAsync`/`CompleteMilestoneAsync`, `AssignStandsAsync` (scheme stands only, within the quota, available and unclaimed; blocked while being developed), `HandOverAsync` (a council stand returns to the council's pool), `ReconcileAsync` (contracted / assigned / handed over / outstanding by quota, overdue milestones, penalty accrued vs charged) and `ChargePenaltiesAsync` (invoices the developer only the penalty accrued since the last charge; DVP sequence). New internal `StandClaims` - the one anti-double-allocation check (live allocation, cooperative, regularisation, developer agreement until a council stand is handed over) now shared by cooperatives, regularisation and developer agreements. Migration `AddDeveloperAgreements`; endpoints under `/api/housing/developer-agreements`. Test: `DeveloperAgreementTests` - quota, scheme and claimed-stand refusals; milestones; hand-overs; reconciliation and a 200 penalty at 15 June; no double charge; the extra month invoiced after a late hand-over; the handed-over council stand then goes to a cooperative while a developer stand is refused. |
| **4.10.6** Caveats, disputes & blocks on stands | ✅ | The brief's blocks: a stand flagged blocked with a reason (court interdict, family dispute, suspected fraud, double allocation under investigation, boundary dispute) cannot be allocated, transferred or have title processed; only a senior role places or lifts a block, both audited with a mandatory reason and supporting document. `StandBlock` (`hse.stand_block`: reason, particulars, document, placed by/on, lifted by/on with reason and document; one block in force per stand by partial unique index; kept after lifting). `IStandBlockService`: `PlaceAsync` (needs `Housing.Caveat` Create), `LiftAsync` (needs `Housing.Caveat` Delete, and a different officer from the one who placed it - SOD-HSE-04 enforced at run time; first service-level permission check, via `IPermissionService`), `HistoryAsync`, `InForceAsync`. Enforcement: `StandClaims.AnyBlockedAsync` (and `AnyClaimedAsync` now includes blocks, so cooperatives, regularisation and developer agreements refuse blocked stands); allocation runs leave blocked stands out and refuse approval if a stand has since been blocked; stand sale, ownership transfer and stand leases refuse a blocked stand. Migration `AddStandBlocks`; endpoints under `/api/housing/stands/{id}/block`. Test: `StandBlockTests` - a clerk without the permission refused; document required; one block at a time; the run skips the blocked stand; cooperative, sale, transfer and lease refused; approval refused after a later block; self-lift and clerk lift refused; lifted by a second officer, the history kept and the stand allocatable again. |
| **4.10.6** Title deed pipeline | ✅ | The brief's pipeline: application to the deeds registry, conveyancer assigned, documents lodged, queries raised and resolved, deed number and registration date captured, deed received into council custody, hand-over to the owner with signed receipt, uncollected deeds tracked, and the pipeline's age at every stage. `TitleDeed` (`hse.title_deed`: TD number, stand, holder, stage and since when, conveyancer, deed number, registration date, received by; title applied for once per stand by unique index) with append-only `TitleDeedStep` (`hse.title_deed_step`). Pure `TitlePipeline` (unit-tested): the allowed transitions (no skipping, no going back; any number of query rounds) and ageing by stage (count, average and oldest days). `ITitleDeedService`: `ApplyAsync` (a sold/transferred stand's holder, fully paid - no running instalment sale and nothing owing on the holder's AR account net of credits; refused on a blocked stand), `AdvanceAsync` (validated transition; conveyancer, query note, deed number and signed receipt required at their stages; refused while the stand is blocked) and `PipelineAsync` (as at any date, rebuilt from the steps taken by then - never the current stage). Migration `AddTitleDeedPipeline`; endpoints under `/api/housing/titles`. Test: `TitleDeedTests` - unsold and unpaid refused; once only; skipped stage and missing particulars refused; a query round; a caveat stops the second deed; ageing in June and August (a deed 30 days uncollected in custody); handed over, it leaves the pipeline while the August view is unchanged. |
| **4.10.6** Change of ownership & cession applications | ✅ | The brief's transfer process: transfer types each with a required-document checklist; mandatory pre-conditions with an attachment and verifying officer; municipal clearance pulled live from §4.9 ("do not let an officer type cleared"); housing account clear; fees receipted; dual approval; registers updated atomically. `TransferApplication` (`hse.transfer_application`: TRF number, stand, type - sale, cession, inheritance, donation, court order, matrimonial, company/trust, change of name, correction, deceased joint holder - from/to holder, consideration, status lodged → submitted → approved → completed / rejected, clearance certificate relied on; one open application per stand by partial unique index) with `TransferCheck` (item, document, verifying officer) and `TransferApproval`. Pure `TransferChecklist` (unit-tested): the default checklist per type and consideration, and which items are live. `ITransferApplicationService`: `LodgeAsync` (current holder; only a change of name / correction keeps the holder; refused on a blocked stand), `VerifyAsync` (needs `Housing.OwnershipChange` Edit; live items cannot be ticked; fees must be a receipt from either party on the sales ledger), `ChecklistAsync` (live checks evaluated: a rated stand needs a current, unused clearance certificate on the municipal register; the transferor owes nothing net of credits and no instalment sale is running), `SubmitAsync` (lists what is outstanding), `ApproveAsync` (needs `Housing.OwnershipApprove` Approve; two different approvers, neither of whom verified or submitted it - SOD-HSE-05), `CompleteAsync` (re-runs the live checks, then cedes the stand on its hash-chained ownership chain and transfers the municipal owner register on the certificate, in one unit of work) and `RejectAsync`. Migration `AddTransferApplications`; endpoints under `/api/housing/transfers`. Test: `TransferApplicationTests` - the cession checklist; permission, live-item, not-required, duplicate and unreceipted-fee refusals; submission blocked by arrears and no certificate; approver who verified refused, same approver twice refused; new arrears stop completion; completed - new holder on the chain (still verifying) and the municipal owner on the certificate; a change of name's shorter checklist, rejected. |
| **4.10.1** Land acquisition: due diligence & cost build-up | ✅ | The brief's acquisition record (method, seller/grantor, council resolution, valuation obtained) with a due-diligence checklist and mandatory sign-off before an acquisition can be marked complete (title search, encumbrances and bonds, caveats, servitudes and way-leaves, boundary disputes, outstanding rates, EIA, heritage, flood-plain/geotechnical, wetland, occupants/compensation liability), and the acquisition cost build-up (price + survey + legal + valuation + occupant compensation + duty/fees + capitalisable interest = total parcel cost). `LandAcquisition` (`hse.land_acquisition`: ACQ number, parcel reference, method - purchase, donation, state grant, compulsory, swap, joint venture, escheat, regularisation - counterparty, resolution, valuer/date/value, status open/completed/abandoned, the parcel created) with `DueDiligenceCheck` (one per item, revisable, finding, acceptable, officer) and `AcquisitionCost` (element, amount, reference, journal). Pure `AcquisitionReadiness` (unit-tested): what stops completion - items not signed off, findings not acceptable, no valuation where value changes hands (purchase, compulsory, swap), no purchase price on a purchase - and the build-up by element. `ILandAcquisitionService`: `OpenAsync` (one per parcel reference, not already in the land bank), `RecordValuationAsync`, `SignOffAsync` (needs `Housing.Acquisition` Edit), `AddCostAsync` (Dr land WIP / Cr funding as incurred), `PositionAsync`, `CompleteAsync` (the parcel enters the land bank at the total built-up cost - already in WIP, so no further journal) and `AbandonAsync` (Dr expense / Cr WIP for everything capitalised). Migration `AddLandAcquisitions`; endpoints under `/api/housing/acquisitions`. Test: `LandAcquisitionTests` - duplicate parcel (any case) refused; build-up totals; completion blocked until signed off, valued and priced; clerk refused; an unacceptable occupants finding revised after compensation; parcel at 117,000; closed to further costs; a donation abandoned and its 1,500 written off. |
| **4.10.2** Layout plan register with version history | ✅ | The brief's layout plan register: layout number, planner, date submitted, approval authority, approval date, approved density, number of stands by use (residential / commercial / industrial / institutional / open space / roads / schools / clinics), amendments and re-approvals with version history. `LayoutPlan` (`hse.layout_plan`: layout number, parcel; one layout per parcel by unique index) with `LayoutPlanVersion` (planner, density, submitted, amendment reason, authority, approved) and `LayoutUseCount` (stands by use). Pure `LayoutChanges` (unit-tested): what an amendment changed, use by use, and the stands a layout creates (every use but roads). `ILayoutPlanService`: `SubmitAsync`, `ApproveAsync` (the version awaiting approval), `AmendAsync` (only once the latest is approved; the approved version stays in force until the amendment is approved), `HistoryAsync` (each version with its changes) and `ReconcileAsync` (plan in force vs stands on the register for the parcel). Migration `AddLayoutPlans`; endpoints under `/api/housing/layouts`. Test: `LayoutPlanTests` - a roads-only layout and a second layout for the parcel refused; amendment refused while pending; approved; 10 planned vs 11 on the register; amended (+1 residential, +clinic, −open space) with version 1 in force until approval; reconciled at 11; history shows each change. |
| **4.10.2** Stand selling price build-up, versioned & frozen at offer | ✅ | The brief's selling price build-up: land cost + servicing cost + overhead/administration recovery + development levy + margin/cost-recovery factor = price, set by scheme, by stand size band or per stand, with effective dating and an approval workflow; price changes are versioned and a beneficiary's price is frozen at offer date. `StandPricingRule` (`hse.stand_pricing_rule`: scope stand / size band / scheme, effective from, overhead %, levy per stand and per m², margin %, rounding, proposed by, approved by/on; never edited - a change is a later rule). Pure `StandPricing` (unit-tested): the build-up from the stand's capitalised cost (rounding up goes to margin) and the rule that applies - a stand's own before its size band's before its scheme's, the latest effective in that scope, approved only. `IStandPricingService`: `ProposeAsync` (needs `Housing.Pricing` Edit), `ApproveAsync` (needs `Housing.Pricing` Approve; not the proposer), `QuoteAsync`. Allocation offers now freeze the price: `StandAllocation.OfferedPrice` set from the price in force on the offer date (internal `StandPrices` resolver), shown on `AllocationLine`. Migration `AddStandPricing`; endpoints under `/api/housing/pricing`. Test: `StandPricingTests` - clerk and unapproved refused; self-approval refused; size band beats scheme; 13,700 and 26,740 built up; frozen on the March offers; an April price rise changes new quotes (17,000) but not the offered prices. |
| **4.10.2** Development / infrastructure levies | ✅ | The brief's levies: endowment fee, intrinsic land value charge, infrastructure development levy, connection fees - configurable charge types applied at allocation or at transfer. `DevelopmentLevy` (`hse.development_levy`: code, name, trigger allocation / transfer, basis flat / per m² / % of price, rate, income account, active) and `LevyCharge` (`hse.levy_charge`: levy, stand, customer, event - certificate or transfer number - amount, invoice; one per levy per event by unique index). Pure `LevyCalculator` (unit-tested). `IDevelopmentLevyService`: `DefineAsync`, `DeactivateAsync`, `ChargeAllocationAsync` (an accepted allocation; the beneficiary's customer account found by national ID; percentages on the price frozen at offer) and `ChargeTransferAsync` (the incoming holder; percentages on the consideration; not on a rejected transfer) - each raises every active levy not yet raised on the event as an AR invoice (LEV sequence), so re-running never double-charges. Migration `AddDevelopmentLevies`; endpoints under `/api/housing/levies`. Test: `DevelopmentLevyTests` - duplicate and >100% refused; not before acceptance; 250 + 600 + 500 raised once; deactivated levy skipped; beneficiary without an account refused; transfer levies 100 + 400 once; a rejected transfer refused. |
| **4.10.6** Joint ownership & survivorship | ✅ | The brief's joint ownership: multiple holders with shares (%), each with their own ID; transfer of one share only; survivorship rules. `StandCoOwnership` (`hse.stand_co_ownership`: the regime - joint tenancy with survivorship, or tenancy in common; one per stand) and append-only `StandHolding` (`hse.stand_holding`: holder, share, held from/to, reason, deceased marker; one current share per holder by partial unique index). Pure `JointHolding` (unit-tested): shares valid (positive, unique, 100%); part or all of one share transferred (merging with an existing holder, dropping a holder left with nothing); on a death, joint tenancy passes the share to the survivors in proportion (rounding to the largest) while tenancy in common leaves it with the estate. `IJointOwnershipService`: `RegisterAsync` (the current holder among them), `TransferShareAsync` (severs a joint tenancy; refused on a blocked stand), `RecordDeathAsync` and `HoldersAsync` (as at any date). Migration `AddJointOwnership`; endpoints under `/api/housing/stands/{id}/holders`. Test: `JointOwnershipTests` - holder not listed, shares not totalling 100 and second registration refused; survivorship to 100% with the earlier view kept; over-transfer and same-day change refused; a 20% share sold; the deceased's share held for the estate then inherited; severance stops survivorship; a caveat freezes the shares. |
| **4.10.6** Sectional title / flats | ✅ | The brief's sectional title: building → units → common property, unit participation quotas, body corporate levies, and transfer of a unit rather than a stand. `SectionalScheme` (`hse.sectional_scheme`: code, building, the stand - one scheme per stand) with `SectionalUnit` (number, floor area, participation quota), append-only `UnitOwnership` (owner, from/to, consideration; one current owner per unit by partial unique index) and `SectionalLevyRun` (once per scheme per period by unique index). Pure `SectionalQuotas` (unit-tested): quotas from floor areas to four decimals and a levy split to the cent, rounding on the largest unit so both total exactly. `ISectionalTitleService`: `RegisterAsync`, `TransferUnitAsync` (refused while the stand is blocked), `UnitsAsync` and `RaiseLevyAsync` (invoices each owned unit its quota share; the unowned units' share reported unbilled). Migration `AddSectionalTitle`; endpoints under `/api/housing/sectional`. Test: `SectionalTitleTests` - duplicate unit numbers and a second scheme on the stand refused; quotas 25 / 37.5 / 37.5; levy 250 + 375 billed with 375 unbilled, once only; a unit resold on its own (old owner closed the day before); a caveat stops unit transfers. |
| **4.10.10** Housing → municipality link | ✅ | The brief's integration point: on title registration or on allocation (configurable trigger), create the billable property, link the owner as the ratepayer, start rates and service charges pro-rata from the effective date (live clearance for transfers was done with the transfer applications). `MunicipalLinkSettings` (`hse.municipal_link_settings`, one per council: trigger - allocation accepted, sale or title registered - ward, and the tariffs to start, one per service, as a `bigint[]`). `IHousingMunicipalLinkService`: `ConfigureAsync` and `OnEventAsync` (acts only for the configured trigger: creates the `Property` with the stand's size and price as valuation, the holder as ratepayer, each tariff's service starting on the date - the fixed-charge run pro-rates the first month - and registers the holder as 100% owner via `IOwnershipService`; a stand already on the roll is left to the transfer process). Hooked into `AllocationService.AcceptAsync` (now one unit of work; the beneficiary's account by national ID), `HousingService.SellStandAsync` and `TitleDeedService.AdvanceAsync` on registration (now one unit of work, passing the deed number). Migration `AddHousingMunicipalLink`; endpoint `/api/housing/municipal-link`. Test: `HousingMunicipalLinkTests` - two tariffs for one service and an unknown tariff refused; a sale on 16 April creates the property (Ward 9, buyer ratepayer and 100% owner, valuation 8,000, refuse from 16 April); April's refuse run charges 50 (15/30 of 100); a stand already rated keeps its ratepayer; a title event does nothing under the sale trigger. |
| **4.10.10** GIS hook | ✅ | The brief's GIS hook: store coordinates/polygons (PostGIS-ready columns) and expose an `IMapProvider` so stand maps, scheme layouts and ward overlays can be plotted later without a schema change. Owned `MappedBoundary` on `LandParcel` and `HousingStand` (`boundary_wkt` - WKT that loads straight into a PostGIS geometry - `centroid_longitude`, `centroid_latitude`, `mapped_area_m2`; all optional). Pure `GeoPolygon` (unit-tested): parses and validates a WKT polygon (closed ring, three distinct corners, world coordinates, non-zero area, clear reasons), measures centroid and area (shoelace on an equirectangular projection - ample for stands), the mapped-vs-surveyed discrepancy, and GeoJSON output with escaping. `IMapProvider` with the register implementation `RegisterMapProvider` (parcels and stands layers with status and size, stands filterable by scheme; a PostGIS provider can replace it). `ISpatialRegisterService`: `MapStandAsync`, `MapParcelAsync`, `DiscrepanciesAsync` (beyond a tolerance, largest first) and `GeoJsonAsync`. Migration `AddMappedBoundaries`; endpoints `/api/housing/stands/{id}/boundary`, `/api/housing/parcels/{id}/boundary`, `/api/housing/map/discrepancies`, `/api/housing/map/{layer}` (application/geo+json). Test: `SpatialRegisterTests` - an open ring refused; a 20 × 30 m stand near Harare measures ~600 m² with its centroid; the stand surveyed at 500 m² flagged ~20% out at 2% tolerance; the scheme layer holds its two stands only; the parcel layer exports. |
| **4.10.2** Servicing cost per stand (job costing feed) | ✅ | The brief's servicing projects: link a servicing contract (§4.12 Job Costing) to a scheme so actual servicing cost flows in, then apportion servicing cost to stands by area, by frontage, by equal share or by zoning weighting, producing an auditable cost per stand (and §4.10.10's job-costing feed per scheme and stand). `ServicingLink` (`hse.servicing_link`: scheme, job contract - one scheme per contract - basis, apportioned to date) and `ServicingCharge` (`hse.servicing_charge`: each stand's share per run with its journal); `HousingStand.FrontageMetres` / `ZoningWeight` and `AddServicingCost`. Pure `ServicingApportionment` (unit-tested): the four bases to the cent, rounding on the largest stand, a missing measure named. `IServicingCostService`: `LinkAsync`, `SetMeasuresAsync`, `ApportionAsync` (per contract, the actual cost to the date less what was apportioned before; only unsold stands - a sold stand's cost has gone to cost of sales; Dr stands available / Cr servicing WIP; one unit of work) and `CostPerStandAsync`. Migration `AddServicingApportionment`; endpoints under `/api/housing/schemes/{id}/servicing`. Test: `ServicingCostTests` - no contract refused; one link per contract; 9,000 of roads by area (3,000 / 6,000), a rerun moves nothing; frontage missing refused (the whole run rolled back), then 1,200 more roads by area and 3,500 water by frontage; cost per stand 5,900 / 10,800 with the sold stand untouched. |
| **8** Notifications & communications | ✅ | The brief's §8: in-app notification centre, email and SMS gateway abstraction (WhatsApp/Telegram can plug in the same way), templates with merge fields per document type and language, previewable, and bulk despatch jobs with delivery status tracking and retry (statements, bills, dunning letters, fee reminders). New `msg` schema: `MessageTemplate` (code, document type, language, channel, subject, body; unique per code/language/channel), `DespatchBatch`, `OutboundMessage` (recipient, merged subject/body, status queued/sent/failed, attempts, last error, provider reference) and `UserNotification` (the notification centre, read/unread). Pure `TemplateRenderer` (unit-tested): `{{Field}}` merge ignoring case, `{{{{` for a literal, every unmerged field reported so a half-merged letter never goes out, and the fields a template uses. `IMessageGateway` per channel (`InAppGateway` built in; email/SMS gateways register the same way - a channel without one keeps its messages queued with the reason). `INotificationService`: `SaveTemplateAsync`, `PreviewAsync` (language with fallback to English), `QueueAsync` (unmergeable or address-less recipients failed at once), `DespatchAsync` (retry up to the attempts allowed; each outcome saved as it happens), `BatchStatusAsync`, `NotifyUserAsync`, `InboxAsync`, `MarkReadAsync`. `AccountingFixture.BuildServiceProvider(configure)` lets tests add fake gateways. Migration `AddNotifications`; endpoints under `/api/messages` and `/api/notifications`. Test: `NotificationTests` - email template without subject refused; Shona template and English fallback previewed; a statement batch where one recipient lacks a field (failed at once), one address bounces (retried, then failed) and one is sent; SMS without a gateway waits; in-app delivered to the clerk's centre; inbox unread/read. |
| **4.16** Municipal fault ticketing with SLA timers | ✅ | The brief's service desk: ticketing for municipalities (burst pipe, no water, refuse not collected) linked to property and service, with SLA timers, assignment, resolution and a public reference number. `TicketCategory` (`mun.ticket_category`: service, response and resolution targets in hours) and `ServiceTicket` (`mun.service_ticket`: FLT reference, category, property, priority, reporter and contact, description, response/resolution due, assignee, responded/resolved/closed, resolution) with append-only `TicketEvent` history. Pure `SlaClock` (unit-tested): round-the-clock targets shortened by priority (critical ×0.25, high ×0.5, low ×2) and each target's state - met, on track, at risk from 80% of the time, breached. `IServiceDeskService`: `DefineCategoryAsync`, `LogAsync` (property by stand number), `AssignAsync` (first assignment is the response; the officer is notified in the §8 notification centre), `ResolveAsync`, `CloseAsync`, `ReopenAsync` (the fix no longer counts), `PublicStatusAsync` (only what the public may see; endpoint open without login) and `SlaBoardAsync` (as at any moment, rebuilt from the history: each ticket's response and resolution state, breaches, average hours to resolve). Migration `AddServiceDeskTickets`; endpoints under `/api/municipal/faults`. Test: `ServiceDeskTests` - bad targets, unknown property and category refused; a critical burst due in 3 h, the foreman notified, at risk at 2 h 25, resolved, public status, reopened, fixed again, closed; a refuse complaint answered late and left open; the board shows the refuse ticket breached on both targets and the burst met, average 2.8 h. |
| **4.17** Data Health dashboard | ✅ | The brief's System Manager Data Health dashboard: orphan records, unbalanced batches, control account variances, negative stock, unposted batches. Pure `DataHealthGrading` (unit-tested): unbalanced batches, control variances and negative stock are errors; unposted batches, orphans and integrity warnings are warnings; the dashboard is as bad as its worst indicator. `IDataHealthService.SnapshotAsync` gathers six indicators, each with count, money involved and up to 20 findings for drill-down: unbalanced batches (from the ledger integrity check), control variances (the control reconciliation as at the date), negative stock (by item and warehouse, valued at average cost), unposted batches (drafts, journals awaiting approval and deferred journals), orphans (open AR/AP balances on deleted customers or suppliers, stock on deleted items) and the rest of the integrity check (cached balances, stock history, open items, cost layers, numbering gaps). No new tables. Endpoint `GET /api/accounting/data-health`. Test: `DataHealthTests` - a clean company all OK; then a deferred journal (unposted 75, warning), a system journal into AR control (variance, error), a deleted customer owing 250 (orphan, warning) and stock forced to −2 at 10 (negative stock 20, error, also caught by the integrity check); overall error. |
| **4.17** Scheduled jobs | ✅ | The brief's System Manager scheduled jobs. `ScheduledJob` (`sys.scheduled_job`: code, registered task key, hourly / daily / weekly / monthly with time, weekday or month day, enabled, next run, last run and outcome) and `JobRun` (`sys.job_run`: each run with its outcome message). Pure `JobSchedule` (unit-tested): the next run strictly after a moment, in UTC - a monthly day beyond the month's end runs on its last day. `IScheduledTask` (registered by key; built in: `messages.despatch` - the §8 despatch with three attempts - and `housing.lapse-offers`) and `IJobScheduler`: `DefineAsync` (registered tasks only), `SetEnabledAsync`, `RunDueAsync` (every company's due jobs, each rescheduled; a task that throws is recorded as failed and its half-done work discarded, the rest still run), `RunNowAsync` (schedule unchanged) and `HistoryAsync`. The server runs due jobs once a minute through `SchedulerHost` (a `BackgroundService`, one scope per pass; `Scheduler:Enabled=false` keeps a node out). Migration `AddScheduledJobs`; endpoints under `/api/system/jobs`. Test: `SchedulerTests` - unknown task and weekly job without a day refused; an overdue monthly despatch and an hourly job that throws both run (the throw recorded, its customer never saved); the daily job's first run; disabled jobs skipped; run on demand. |
| **8** Bulk customer statements by email | ✅ | The brief's bulk despatch of statements, wired end to end. `Customer.Email` and `Customer.MobileNumber` (migration `AddCustomerContactDetails`). `IStatementDespatchService.QueueAsync`: every customer owing at least the minimum as at the date (balances from the as-at open items, net of credits) with an email address gets the printed statement merged into the template - fields Name, Account, Balance, Currency, AsAt and Statement - queued as one §8 despatch batch; customers without email and balances below the minimum are counted. Scheduled task `statements.email` queues last month's statements (template STATEMENT). `IJobScheduler.RunDueAsync` takes an optional company so one company's jobs can be run alone. Endpoint `POST /api/messages/statements`. Test: `StatementDespatchTests` - missing template refused; of four customers only the one with email owing 500 is queued (one without email, one owing 0.50, one paid up); subject and body merged with the statement text; the monthly job queues April's statements on 1 May. |
| **13.2** Report definition version history, diff & rollback | ✅ | The brief's version history for report definitions with diff and rollback; system reports read-only but copyable to a user version - never edited in place. Saving a design used to delete the company's previous version. `ReportDefinitionVersion` (`rpt.report_definition_version`: every version's JSON as jsonb, who, when, why; unique per report and version; existing company reports back-filled at their current version). Pure `JsonDiff` (unit-tested): structural comparison ignoring property order and formatting, every differing leaf reported with its JSON path as added / removed / changed. `IReportDefinitionService`: `CopySystemReportAsync` (once per company), `SaveAsync` (validates the `.mrpt` JSON; creates or adds a version keeping the replaced one; refuses a save that changes nothing), `HistoryAsync`, `DiffAsync` and `RollbackAsync` (restores an old version as the newest - history is never rewritten). The desktop designer now saves through it. Migration `AddReportDefinitionVersions`; endpoints under `/api/reports/{code}`. Test: `ReportVersioningTests` - unknown system report and a second copy refused; the title changed as v2; an unchanged and an invalid save refused; the diff names `$.name`; rollback to v1 saved as v3 identical to v1; history with notes; the system report untouched. |
| **13.3** Scheduled report delivery with distribution list & delivery log | ✅ | The brief's report scheduling: run reports on a schedule, deliver by email with a distribution list and a delivery log. `ReportSubscription` (`rpt.report_subscription`: report, format, stored parameters as jsonb, distribution list, active) and `ReportDelivery` (`rpt.report_delivery`: each recipient per run with the document and outcome). Pure `ReportParameterTokens` (unit-tested): relative dates resolved per run - `@Today`, `@Yesterday`, `@MonthStart`, `@MonthEnd`, `@PrevMonthStart`, `@PrevMonthEnd`, `@YearStart`, `@PrevYearEnd` - ISO dates and whole numbers typed, unknown tokens refused. §8 messages can now carry an attachment (`OutboundMessage.Attach`, `MessageAttachment`, `INotificationService.QueueDirectAsync`; `IMessageGateway` gained an attachment overload with a default that sends the text only). `IReportSubscriptionService`: `SubscribeAsync` (known report, valid tokens, real addresses, duplicates removed), `SetActiveAsync`, `RunAsync` (renders through `IReportRunner`, one email per recipient with the document attached, every delivery - or the render failure - logged), `RunAllAsync` and `DeliveryLogAsync`; scheduled task `reports.subscriptions`. Migration `AddReportSubscriptions`; endpoints under `/api/reports/subscriptions`. Test: `ReportSubscriptionTests` - bad token, unknown report and bad address refused; on 3 June the trial balance as at 31 May goes to two distinct recipients with the HTML attached; the despatch hands each attachment to the gateway; the monthly task skips the subscription while inactive and runs it once reactivated. |
| **13.3** Audited report runs: permissions, run log & numbered reprints | ✅ | The brief's report security: report-level permissions and every print/export written to the audit log, plus the viewer's reprint audit record. `ReportRun` (`rpt.report_run`, append-only: report, user, purpose view / print / export / reprint, format, canonical parameters, size, SHA-256 of the document, copy number). Pure `ReportRunAudit` (unit-tested): canonical parameter text (sorted, ISO dates, invariant numbers, empties dropped), the `Reporting.Report` flag each purpose needs, a print of something printed before reclassified as a numbered reprint, and the fingerprint. `IAuditedReportRunner`: `RunAsync` (permission-checked when the user is known - a would-be reprint needs the Reprint flag; refused runs are not logged) and `RunLogAsync`. The desktop report viewer's previews and exports now go through it (unattributed until desktop sign-in carries the user). Endpoints `POST /api/reports/{code}/run` (user from the token; returns the file) and `GET /api/reports/{code}/runs`. Migration `AddReportRunLog`. Test: `AuditedReportRunTests` - a viewer can view but not export; a printer prints copy 1 but cannot reprint; a clerk's print becomes reprint copy 2; other parameters start again at copy 1; an unattributed export is logged; five runs with one fingerprint for the identical prints. |
| **5.2** Desktop session carries the signed-in user | ✅ | The desktop knew who signed in only inside the shell's view model; other windows could not act as that user. New `DesktopSession` (a desktop singleton set at login - and by the dev auto-login) holds the signed-in user; the report viewer now runs previews and exports through the audited runner as that user, so they are permission-checked against `Reporting.Report` and attributed in the report run log. Release build verified; no schema change. |
| **4.17** Data Health dashboard window (desktop) | ✅ | **Utilities → Data Health…** - `DataHealthWindow` + `DataHealthViewModel` over `IDataHealthService`: the six indicators with status (coloured OK / warning / error), count and money, a verdict banner coloured by the worst indicator, and the selected indicator's findings for drill-down (the worst one selected on load). Formatting only; the rules stay in the tested service. Release build verified. |
| **8** Notification Centre window (desktop) | ✅ | **Transactions → My Notifications…** - `NotificationCentreWindow` + `NotificationCentreViewModel` over `INotificationService` for the signed-in user (`DesktopSession`): notifications newest first with unread ones in bold, an unread-only filter, the unread count, and mark-read (e.g. fault assignments from the service desk land here). Release build verified. |
| **4.16** Fault Desk window (desktop) | ✅ | **Transactions → Fault Desk…** - `FaultDeskWindow` + `FaultDeskViewModel` over `IServiceDeskService`: log a fault (category, priority, stand, reporter, contact, description - the public reference is shown to give the caller), the SLA board of open tickets with the resolution state coloured (at risk amber, breached red) and the board summary (open, breached, average hours to resolve), and on the selected ticket Take (assign to the signed-in officer, who is notified), Resolve, Close and Reopen with a note. | **4.10.6** Title Deed Pipeline window (desktop) | ✅ | **Enquiries → Title Deed Pipeline…** - `TitlePipelineWindow` + `TitlePipelineViewModel` over `ITitleDeedService`: the pipeline as at any date (re-aged when the date changes) - deeds, average and oldest days per stage, and every open application with its stand, stage, days at the stage, days since applied and conveyancer - and the next step for the selected application with its particulars (conveyancer, the registry's query, deed number or who signed), validated by the tested service.
