The audited catalogue list is now complete. Test: `FinalCatalogueReportsTests`. |
| **13** Sub-reports and rich text | ✅ | `ElementKind.RichText` - safe markup (`**bold**`, `*italic*`, `__underline__`, line breaks, `{Field}` placeholders filled from the row or parameters, formatted like a field and never re-read as markup) parsed by pure `RichText.Parse` into `RichRun`s on `RenderCell.Runs` (plain text kept in `Text` for exports and search); HTML draws strong / em / u, PDF one span per run. `ElementKind.SubReport` - `SubReportCode` + `SubReportLinks` (child parameter ← parent field, else the parent parameter of that name; the parent's parameters pass through): the engine stays data-free, asking `ReportInput.SubReports` for the child input, building it, laying its bands into the element's box and growing the band; no resolver or no definition shows a note; nesting capped at `SubReports.MaxDepth` (3). `ReportRunner` fetches every distinct child parameter set once beforehand (recursively, 500 runs at most, hidden sensitive fields stripped). Designer: Rich text and Sub-report buttons, SubReportCode and SubReportLinks (`Param=Field; ...`) as editable properties. Tests: `SubReportAndRichTextTests` (unit), `SubReportRunnerTests` (a stored company report running AR46 per rep). |
| **4.1** POS voucher tender and split bill | ✅ | Voucher tender at the till: `PosTenderRequest.VoucherNumber`; `CompleteSaleAsync` checks each card before posting (found, active, unexpired at the sale date, same currency, balance covers everything taken from it in the sale, no change from a voucher - `Pos.VoucherTender` / `Pos.VoucherNotUsable` / `Pos.VoucherCurrency` / `Voucher.InsufficientBalance` / `Pos.VoucherOverpays`) and charges it only after the sale posts (Dr the gift-card liability, `GiftCardTransaction` Redeemed); `SaleTender.VoucherNumber` (migration `AddSaleTenderVoucherNumber`); `VoidSaleAsync` gives the amount back (`GiftCard.Reinstate`, event `Reinstated`). Split bill: pure `SplitBill.Plan` (parts take whole lines or part quantities; net, tax and discount follow the quantity, the last share takes the rounding; every line fully taken) and `SplitBill.Evenly`; `IPosService.SplitBillAsync` checks the basket's item limits once, then completes each part as its own sale `{number}-1..n` with its own tenders in one unit of work - any part refused undoes them all. Till: VOUCHER button with gift-card number box (amount = entry or balance due, capped at the card's balance less what the ticket already takes), gift-card account prefilled; `PosTicket.AddTender(..., voucherNumber)`. Tests: `SplitBillTests` (unit), `PosVoucherAndSplitBillTests`. |
| **3.5** Supplier-side certificate enforcement | ✅ | Certificates gain a purpose (`CertificatePurpose.TaxExemption` / `TaxClearance`, migration `AddCertificatePurposeAndDefaultWithholding`). Purchases: an exempt tax code needs the supplier's valid exemption certificate (`Tax.CertificateRequired`) and a supply under one carries no tax (`Tax.SupplierExempt`) - the mirror of the customer rules. Withholding: shared `TraderCertificates` decides clearance (the supplier's clearance date or an unrevoked clearance certificate covering the date) for single payments, payment runs (proposal and EFT net) and subcontract certificates alike; `Company.WithholdingWithoutClearanceTaxCode` is deducted from payments to uncleared suppliers that name no code. Clearance certificates are for suppliers only (`Tax.ClearanceForSuppliers`); the expiry list shows each certificate's purpose and a later certificate of the same purpose silences the warning. Desktop: the certificates operation records exemption or clearance and sets the default withholding code. Test: `SupplierCertificateEnforcementTests`. |
| **4.1** Till split-bill screen | ✅ | Touch till "Split Bill" button opens `SplitBillWindow`: 2–8 payers, a grid giving each payer a quantity of every line (or Split evenly), each payer's tender (cash, card, mobile money or voucher with its gift card) and the amount each pays worked out live by `SplitBill.Plan` with any problem shown. `PosTerminalViewModel.CompleteSplitAsync` posts it through `IPosService.SplitBillAsync` (each payer pays their part exactly), then shows and prints every part's receipt. `PosTicket.BuildBasket` (the lines without tenders; `BuildSale` now builds on it). Fix: a voucher sale is no longer queued offline - a gift card cannot be checked or charged without the connection (the offline envelope also had no card number). Split bills need the connection. Test: `SplitBillTests` (basket). |
| **3.4** Fiscal calendars, adjustment period and reversible year-end (fourth audit #1) | ✅ | **FIX**: the year-end opened the next year as January–December whatever the year (a July–June council lost July–December); it now starts the day after the year ends and follows the same calendar, with the code moved on (`FiscalCalendars.NextCode`: 2026 → 2027, 2026/27 → 2027/28, FY26 → FY27). Calendars: monthly from any month or 4-4-5 weeks (`FiscalCalendar`, `FiscalCalendars.Periods/Detect`, `IMasterDataService.CreateFiscalYearAsync(..., calendar, months, adjustmentPeriod)`). Adjustment ("13th") period on the year's last day (`FiscalPeriod.IsAdjustment`, `FiscalYear.AddAdjustmentPeriod`): reached only with `JournalRequest.ToAdjustmentPeriod` (`Posting.NoAdjustmentPeriod` otherwise), reversals of its journals stay in it, the closing journal goes there, the budget gate ignores it. Year-end now closes (not permanently): `IYearEndService.ReverseAsync` (reason required; reverses the closing journal in its own period, reopens the year) and re-run; `HardLockAsync` makes it final (`FiscalYear.IsHardLocked`, every period permanently closed). Migration `AddAdjustmentPeriodAndYearHardLock`. Desktop: General Ledger → Year-end (run / reverse / hard lock); Create a fiscal year takes the calendar and adjustment period. Tests: `FiscalCalendarTests` (unit), `YearEndCalendarTests`. |
| **4.1** Till tiles from the item master and live search (fourth audit #2) | ✅ | **FIX**: the till's product tiles were restaurant menu items added with item id 0, so a tapped sale posted revenue but never issued stock or posted cost of sales. Tiles now come from `IPosCatalogueService.TilesAsync`: active items with a revenue account and a price for one from the default price list (the explicit line with the largest break not above one, else the list's cost-plus rule; unpriced items are left off and can still be scanned once priced), grouped by category; each tile carries its item, so a tap sells it like a scan. Live search: typing in the scan box narrows the tiles as you type (`PosTile.Matches`: every word in code, name, barcode or PLU); Enter still adds the exact match. Also cleared two nullable warnings in the report runner and budgeting provider. Tests: `PosTileTests` (unit), `PosCatalogueTests`. |
| **2.4** Idempotency keys on posting (fourth audit #3) | ✅ | **FIX**: `JournalRequest.IdempotencyKey` was accepted but never checked, so a retried call could post twice. Engine: the key is stored on the journal (`JournalEntry.IdempotencyKey`, unique per company where set) and a request with a used key returns the first journal's receipt instead of posting (`Posting.KeyUsed` if that journal was rejected); a simultaneous duplicate loses the race on the unique index and its retry finds the first. Endpoints: any POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE under /api with an `Idempotency-Key` header runs once (`IIdempotencyStore` over `sys.idempotency_record`, unique per user and key, request hashed over method, path, query and body): a retry gets the first response back (`Idempotent-Replayed: true`, still passing the field-stripping), the key reused for a different request is refused (422), one still running is reported (409), and a server failure (5xx) lets the key go so the retry runs. Keys are kept 24 hours; the `system.idempotency-purge` scheduled task removes older ones. Migration `AddIdempotencyKeys`. Test: `IdempotencyTests` (engine and store; the server middleware is compile-checked). |
| **3.2 / 3.3** Reporting currency kept on every posting (fourth audit #4) | ✅ | **FIX**: `Company.ReportingCurrency`, the journal lines' and period balances' reporting columns existed but nothing filled them. The posting engine now sets every line's reporting amount when the company has a reporting currency: the transaction amount for a line already in it, the base amount when it is the base currency, otherwise the base amount at the base→reporting rate of the posting date (rounding evened out on the largest line so each journal balances in it); a posting with no rate is refused (`Posting.NoReportingRate`) rather than kept at nil. Reversals repeat the original reporting amounts (`JournalLineRequest.ReportingAmount`), period balances accumulate them, and the integrity rebuild already restates them. Report GL2R Trial Balance - Reporting Currency (base and reporting balances with a nil-difference check). Desktop: Set the reporting currency (applies from then on - posted journals cannot change, so set it before go-live). Per-trader rate types are left for later. Test: `ReportingCurrencyTests`. |
| **3.5** Fiscalisation wired into the till (fourth audit #5) | ✅ | **FIX**: `IFiscalDeviceProvider` existed but nothing called it (and TX223 claimed receipts were fiscalised). Now: opening a till opens the fiscal day; every completed sale (split-bill parts too) is sent to the device and its fiscal code, verification (QR payload) and day are kept on the sale (`Sale.FiscalStatus/FiscalCode/FiscalVerification/FiscalDayNumber/FiscalAttempts/FiscalError`, migration `AddSaleFiscalisation`); an unreachable or refusing device does not stop the sale - it waits as Queued and `IPosService.RetryFiscalAsync` / scheduled task `pos.fiscal-retry` sends the queue again; closing the last open till closes the day. Every attempt and day event is written to the till event log (`PosEventKind.FiscalSubmission`). Receipts print the fiscal code and verification text, or FISCAL SIGNATURE PENDING; TX223 shows the sale's real fiscal status with a PASS/FAIL check. `SimulatedFiscalDeviceProvider` (ONE4ALL_FISCAL_SIMULATE=1, with an Offline switch) stands in for a revenue-authority device until a real one (e.g. FDMS) is plugged in through the same interface. Test: `FiscalisationTests`. |
| **4.5 / 4.6** Customer and supplier addresses and contacts (fourth audit #6) | ✅ | Customers and suppliers had no addresses or contacts (and suppliers no email), so documents carried no address and remittances / orders could not be emailed. New `TraderAddress` (postal / physical / delivery, one default per kind) and `TraderContact` (role, email, phone, primary, receives statements / remittances / orders) for both ledgers (migration `AddTraderAddressesAndContacts`), kept through `ITraderDirectoryService`. The correspondence address (default postal, else physical) prints under "To:" on the invoice / credit note, statement, reminder letter, purchase order and remittance advice. `EmailStatementAsync` / `EmailRemittanceAsync` / `EmailPurchaseOrderAsync` queue the document as an attachment to the contacts who receive it, else the primary contact, else the customer's own email (`Trader.NoRecipient` when nobody). Desktop: Customers / Suppliers → Addresses and contacts, and Email a statement (remittance, order). Test: `TraderDirectoryTests`. |
| **7** Workflow engine drives documents, with approver notifications (fourth audit #7) | ✅ | The generic `IWorkflowService` was complete but no document used it and nobody was told anything. Now: `IWorkflowDocumentHandler` per document type applies a workflow's end in the same unit of work as the last decision (if the document cannot follow - say the preparer approving their own journal - the decision is not kept); `ActAsync` is atomic. Purchase requisitions (`SubmitAsync(..., submittedByUserId)`) and manual journals held for approval start the company's `PurchaseRequisition` / `Journal` workflow when one is defined, and direct approval or rejection is then refused (`Workflow.Governs`); approved → the requisition is approved with its budget gate and commitment / the journal is approved by the last approver and posts; rejected or returned → rejected with the comment. A workflow started on its own (reference not one of the module's documents) still runs as before. Notifications: the named approvers of each stage (users, added approvers, role holders, requester's manager) get an in-app notification when a document reaches them, and the requester is told the outcome. Desktop requisition submit passes the signed-in user. Test: `WorkflowDocumentTests`. |
| **4.15.1–4.15.2** Payroll master data maintenance (fourth audit #8, part 1) | ✅ | Employees, pay elements and tax tables could only be created in code. `IPayrollSetupService`: engage (unique number; a contract needs its end date) and update employees (bank details excluded); create / update pay elements (formula needed for a formula element, contribution expense account for an employer contribution, account must exist); assign and end elements per employee; create tax tables with the bands checked (start at nil, follow on, last open-ended, 0–100%); seed the standard set BASIC / OVERTIME / HOUSING / TRANSPORT / PAYE (tax-table basis) without duplicating; bank and mobile-money changes are requested and decided by a different person (`DetailChangeRequest.RequestedByUserId`, migration `AddDetailChangeRequester`, `Payroll.SelfApproval`). Desktop (HR & Attendance): Employee record, Pay elements and tax tables (bands as "0-100:0; 100-300:20; 300-:30"), Bank detail change. Termination and final pay follow. Test: `PayrollSetupTests` (including a run taxed by the new table). |
| **4.15.8** Termination and final pay (fourth audit #8, part 2) | ✅ | There was no termination process (only a bare status change used by tests). `ITerminationService`: typed terminations (`TerminationType`: resignation, dismissal, retrenchment, retirement, death, end of contract, absconding) with entitlements by type (pure `FinalPay.Entitlements`: retrenchment notice + gratuity + severance; retirement / death / end of contract gratuity; leave always); the final pay worked out with its working (`FinalPayStatement`: last month pro rata unless already paid in a run, notice, leave days and pay, gratuity, severance, PAYE on ordinary pay from the tax table in force, the terminal rate on benefits, loans recovered as far as the pay allows and the rest left owed); `PreviewAsync` changes nothing, `TerminateAsync` posts one journal (expense, terminal benefits, PAYE, each loan's receivable, net pay) and settles the loans, terminates the employee (later runs leave them out) and records `Termination` with a clearance checklist (final pay, loans, company property, system access, staff housing) cleared item by item; `CertificateOfServiceAsync` (dates, years, grade, department, reason - no pay). Migration `AddTerminations`. **FIX**: employee department / cost centre must be the ledger's codes (up to 3 characters) or every payroll posting failed (`Payroll.DimensionCode`). Desktop: HR & Attendance → Termination and final pay. Tests: `FinalPayTests` (unit), `TerminationTests`. |
| **4.1** Till: on-account sales and foreign-currency tenders (fourth audit #9) | ✅ | On account: `CompleteSaleRequest.CustomerId` / `Sale.CustomerId` (migration `AddSaleCustomer`); a credit tender (not a staff purchase) needs the customer, refuses one on hold (`Pos.CustomerOnHold`), an account not in the base currency (`Pos.AccountCurrency`) or a charge past the credit limit given what they owe (`Pos.CreditLimit`), posts to the receivables control and raises an invoice open item on the sale's journal (due by the customer's terms); voiding raises `VOID-{sale}` credit and allocates it to the invoice. Foreign currency: `IPosService.TenderCurrenciesAsync` (base plus every currency with a daily rate to it, direct, inverse or through another); `PosTicket.AddForeignTender` (cash / card / mobile at the rate, base worked to four places as the server posts), `BalanceDueIn(rate)` (rounded up), change exact in base; `BuildSale` sends the currency and rate. Till: "Pay in" currency picker with the balance in that currency, customer account box and ACCOUNT button. Tests: `SplitBillTests` (ticket), `PosAccountAndCurrencyTests`. |
| **13.1** Images, logos, barcodes and QR codes in reports (fourth audit #10, part 1) | ✅ | **FIX**: image elements and barcodes rendered as nothing (the engine returned empty text and the PDF renderer drew only text). `Barcodes` (pure): Code 128 set B with its checksum, drawn as a greyscale PNG by a small built-in PNG writer, and QR codes through QRCoder (MIT); `RenderCell.ImageData` (data URI) for images - the element's own picture, a field holding one, or the company logo for expression `CompanyLogo` (`Company.Logo`, migration `AddCompanyLogo`, passed to sub-reports) - and barcodes of a field / expression / text (the cell's text keeps the value for exports). HTML draws `<img>`, PDF draws the picture fitted to the box, the WPF viewer shows it. Designer: Image (from a file, 512 KB at most), Logo and Barcode buttons, BarcodeSymbology editable (Code128 / QR). Desktop: System Manager → Set the company logo. Test: `BarcodeAndImageTests`. Report permissions / row scopes and burst delivery follow. |
| **13.3** Report permissions and row-level scopes (fourth audit #10, part 2) | ✅ | Report security was one switch (`Reporting.Report`); `Payroll.Reports` and `Budget.Reports` were defined but never checked and no report filtered rows by the user's scope. Now the audited runner builds a `ReportAccess` (hidden columns, the user's `DataScope`, and a module check): a report whose module has a "{Module}.Reports" permission needs it (payroll, budget - and any module that adds one), a report can name its own `ReportDefinition.RequiredPermission`, refused as `Rpt.ModuleNotAuthorised`; rows naming a branch, department, cost centre, warehouse, ward or job outside the user's scope - or with it blank - are left out (`ReportRowScope`, pure), in sub-reports too (which also need their own rights). Super administrators and system runs are unrestricted. Tests: `ReportAccessTests` (unit), `ReportSecurityTests` (a department-scoped HR officer sees only their department on PR4; a clerk without the payroll right is refused). |
| **13.3** Burst delivery (fourth audit #10, part 3) | ✅ | `ReportBurster` existed but nothing used it. `IReportRunner.BurstAsync` runs a report once (with the user's access) and renders each key's rows as its own document (`BurstSlice`); `IReportBurstService` emails each part as an attachment to the address in a field of the rows, or to the contacts of the customer / supplier the key names (code, or a unique name as most reports show it - statement / remittance contacts, else the primary contact or the customer's own email), listing keys nobody could be found for. Report subscriptions can burst (`BurstKeyField`, `BurstRecipientField`, `BurstLedger`, migration `AddSubscriptionBursting`), logging each part's delivery and each unaddressed key. Desktop (System Manager): Burst a report by email; Report subscription (list or burst). Test: `ReportBurstTests` (AR29 aged debtors burst per customer, a part holding only its customer's rows, and a bursting subscription). Fourth audit top ten complete. |
| **4.9 / 15** Chunked, resumable municipal billing runs | ✅ | Approval posted every bill in one transaction and one change tracker: a single failure stopped the whole run, nothing could resume and there was no progress. Now approval is one unit (checks, then the run marked `Posting`) and the bills post in chunks (`BillingPostOptions`: chunk size, an optional cap for a batch window, `IProgress<BillingRunProgress>` after each chunk), each bill on its own: a failed bill is set aside with its reason (`BillingRunLine.MarkFailed`) while the rest carry on; the change tracker is cleared after each chunk so memory stays flat; `ResumeAsync` carries on a run left Posting and a bill already on the account (posted before an interruption) is marked, not posted twice; the run finishes `Posted` with posted / failed counts in `BillingRunSummary`. `BillingRunLine.IsPosted / PostedOpenItemId` (migration `AddBillingRunChunkedPosting`, `BackfillBillingRunPosted` marks lines of earlier posted runs). Desktop: billing run Resume posting step and counts. Test: `ChunkedBillingRunTests` (30 stands, capped and resumed, one ratepayer on hold, an interruption). |
| **4.6** Supplier payment run approval | ✅ | A payment run paid everything due the moment it was run: nobody could review it, leave a supplier out or part-pay. Now a run can be saved (`PaymentRunBatch` / `PaymentRunBatchLine`, numbered `PRUN-yyyyMMdd-nnn`) with every supplier due, held suppliers and those with bank details changing left out with the reason; lines are left out or part-paid (up to what is due, the oldest invoices settled first); approval must come from someone other than the proposer (also a database check) and pays exactly the approved amounts in one unit, recording the payment numbers; a saved run can be cancelled. `Company.PaymentRunApprovalRequired` refuses direct runs; payment runs can also be limited to one currency. Migration `AddPaymentRunApproval`. Desktop: Suppliers › Payment run approval (save, set line, approve, cancel, show, require approval). Test: `PaymentRunApprovalTests`. |
| **4.1** Per-currency and blind till cash-up | ✅ | Cash-up counted one base-currency figure: foreign notes taken at the till were lost in the base total and the cashier could see what was expected before counting. Now the drawer is expected per currency (the float, pickups and top-ups in base; cash taken less change in the currency it changed hands in) and counted per currency (`CashUpRequest.Counts`, `CashCount`; typed at the till as "120; ZAR 500; EUR 20", pure `CashCounts.TryParse`); each currency's variance is valued at the session's average rate for it (`CurrencyCashUp`) and posted to the cash account its tenders used, the net to over/short; `CashUpResult.ByCurrency`. Blind cash-up per till (`PosTerminal.BlindCashUp`): `DeclareCountAsync` records the count first (`TillCashCount`, final once declared), `ExpectedCashAsync` and closing are refused until it is; every count is kept. Migration `AddBlindMultiCurrencyCashUp`. Desktop: till close declares the count then cashes up and shows each currency; Retail › Till cash-up position (position, blind on / off). Tests: `CashCountsTests`, `BlindCashUpTests`. |
| **4.10.2 / 4.10.3** Stand state machine, consolidation, re-subdivision, spouse | ✅ | Stands had four states set freely, no audit of moves, no consolidation or re-subdivision, and an applicant's spouse was never checked. Now `StandStatus` carries the brief's lifecycle (raw land → planned → surveyed → under servicing → serviced → available → reserved / offered → allocated → sold / transferred → fully paid → title applied → registered → handed over; side branches withheld, surrendered, offer cancelled, consolidated away, re-subdivided), enforced by pure `StandLifecycle.CanMove` (no jumps); every move is audited (`StandStatusChange`: from, to, date, user, authority, reason), including those made by allocation, sale, cession and repossession. `IStandLifecycleService`: manual moves need an authority and cannot reach document-driven states; a hold (`Withheld`, `StatusBeforeHold`) blocks sale and transfer and is released back to where it was; `ConsolidateAsync` merges free stands of one scheme and stage (sizes and costs added, the old ones retired at nil cost, `ConsolidatedIntoStandId`); `ResubdivideAsync` splits by area (`ParentStandId`); `HistoryAsync` gives the moves, the next allowed states and lineage back to the parcel. A sale is refused from a reserved / pre-release state. Spouse / co-applicant on the waiting list (`MaritalStatus`, `MaritalRegime`, `SpouseIdNumber`): refused when the spouse is already listed (either way round) or owns a stand / property; an allocation run leaves out an applicant whose spouse holds a stand or a live allocation (`ExclusionReason.SpouseHolds`). Migration `AddStandLifecycleAndSpouse`. Desktop: Housing › Stand lifecycle; spouse fields on registration. Tests: `StandLifecycleRulesTests`, `StandLifecycleTests`. |
| **4.12** Job cost from purchasing, commitments and overheads | ✅ | Job costs came only from stock issues, timesheets, plant and subcontracts typed against the job; purchases never reached a job and nothing recovered overheads. Now a purchase order line can be bought for a job (`PurchaseOrderLine.JobContractId / JobCostHead`, the job's WIP account as its expense account, an open job): it counts as committed cost until received, is received straight to the job (into stock and out to WIP in the same unit, captured as `JobCostSource.GoodsReceived`), and an invoice priced differently charges the difference to the job's WIP account and cost rather than price variance. A direct supplier invoice can be charged to a job (`PurchaseInvoiceRequest.JobContractId / JobCostHead`). Overhead recovery rates per kind of direct cost (`JobOverheadRate`, `SetOverheadRateAsync`) are added as each cost is captured, by every capture route (internal `JobCosts.CaptureAsync`). `JobProfitability.CommittedCost / Overheads / CostToDateAndCommitted`. Migration `AddJobCostFromPurchasing`. Desktop: purchase order job fields; Contracts › Job cost position and overhead rates. Test: `JobCostFromPurchasingTests`. |
| **4.11** Optional school fees and pro-rata billing | ✅ | Term billing charged every student the compulsory total for the whole term: optional items were never billed and joiners and leavers paid the full term. Now a student takes optional items (`StudentFeeOption`; `FeeItem.RevenueAccountCode`), billed on the term invoice to their own revenue accounts (`RevenueLines`) without bursary or sibling discount (`StudentBillResult.OptionalFees`). Term dates and pro rata per fee structure (`SetTermAsync`, `FeeStructure.ShareOfTerm` by days enrolled): a joiner (`Student.AdmittedOn`) pays from the admission date (`StudentBillResult.ShareOfTerm`); `WithdrawAsync` withdraws a student (`Student.LeftOn`) and credits the unused part of the term invoice, set against it; with deferred fees the credit comes out of deferred income and the recognition schedule ends on the leaving date (`DeferredIncomeSchedule.Curtail`, same daily rate). Migration `AddSchoolOptionsAndProRata`. Desktop: Sales & Schools › Term dates, optional fees and leavers. Tests: `TermShareTests`, `SchoolOptionsProRataTests`. |
| **4.9** Municipal billing cycles and instalments | ✅ | Every tariff was billed monthly: an annual or quarterly charge (rates, refuse) would have been raised every month. Now a tariff has a cycle (`BillingCycle` monthly / quarterly / half-yearly / annual, `CycleStartMonth`, `Instalments`; `SetBillingCycleAsync` on every version of the code), its amounts per period. Pure `BillingCycles` (period of a date, instalment slot, share of the period a service is in place, instalment amounts with the last taking the rounding): a period's charge is raised once by the first run in the period (the bill number carries the period start, so later runs skip it silently), or over monthly instalments from the period start (numbered per month; nothing due after the last); a stand connected part-way pays its share of the period. Both the reviewed billing run and the fixed-charge run are cycle-aware (`ComputedCharge.BillMonth / CycleNote`); bill narratives are unchanged so previous-charge and back-billing matching still work. Migration `AddTariffBillingCycles`. Desktop: Municipal › Tariff billing cycle. Tests: `BillingCyclesTests`, `TariffBillingCycleTests`. |
| **10** Opening-balance importers for the module registers | ✅ | Migration covered customers, suppliers, items, stock, open AR/AP items and the trial balance, but not the module registers a council or school brings with it. Now `IVerticalImportService` imports, validate-first (no line written while any is wrong, each error with its line) and all-or-nothing, re-runnable (what exists is skipped): fixed assets with cost and depreciation to date (recorded up to the as-at month so the next run charges the month after; one journal Dr cost / Cr accumulated depreciation against migration suspense), employees (department / cost centre held to the 3-character ledger codes), staff loan balances (posted to the loan accounts against suspense), municipal properties with valuation, size and `service:tariff` links, and students under their guardian accounts. `ReconcileRegistersAsync` checks the fixed-asset register (net book value) and staff loans against the accounts they are held in; the desktop opening reconciliation lists them and go-live readiness needs them to agree. Desktop: Master data › Import master data or opening balances (five new kinds). Test: `VerticalImportTests`. |
| **4.13** Asset class master and IAS 36 impairment | ✅ | An asset's class was free text and every asset carried its own method, life and accounts; a write-down went through revaluation by cutting cost, with no impairment record and no reversal rules. Now `FixedAssetClass` (`SaveClassAsync`: method, life, residual % of cost, rate, cost / depreciation / expense / impairment accounts) fills in whatever a registration leaves blank (`Fa.ClassDefaults` when neither gives the method and accounts). `ImpairAsync` tests an asset against its recoverable amount: a loss is booked within accumulated depreciation and impairment (Dr impairment, Cr accumulated depreciation; `FixedAsset.AccumulatedImpairment` held separately), after which straight-line depreciation spreads the new carrying amount over the months left; a recovery reverses losses held, never above the carrying amount with no impairment (`CarryingAmountWithoutImpairment`); each test is kept (`AssetImpairment`: carrying amount before, recoverable amount, loss or reversal, indicator, journal). Migration `AddAssetClassesAndImpairment`. Desktop: Fixed Assets › Asset classes and impairment. Test: `AssetClassImpairmentTests`. |
| **4.15** Leave policy engine | ✅ | Leave was accrued by hand, days at a time, with no entitlement rules, service bands, pro rata or carry-over limits. Now a `LeavePolicy` per leave type (`SavePolicyAsync`: yearly days, `LeaveServiceBand`s raising them after completed years, earned monthly or granted at the leave-year start, pro-rata joiners, a carry-over cap) and pure `LeavePolicyRules` (years of service, entitlement, the days a month earns: a twelfth monthly with joiners and leavers pro rata by days employed; a yearly grant at the start month, or the months left for a joiner). `RunMonthAsync` applies each policy to every employee for a month, idempotently (`EmployeeLeaveBalance.LastAccruedPeriod`), and as the leave year turns applies the carry-over cap first (`ApplyCarryOverCap`, `ForfeitedDays`, once per year); the `payroll.leave-accrual` scheduled task runs the month just ended. Migration `AddLeavePolicies`. Desktop: HR & Attendance › Leave policies (save, run month). Tests: `LeavePolicyRulesTests`, `LeavePolicyTests`. |
| **4** Document attachments | ✅ | Records could not carry their supporting files (scans, contracts, delivery notes). Now `IAttachmentService` attaches a file to a journal, customer, supplier, ledger document, purchase order, fixed asset, employee, property, student or contract (`AttachmentTarget`; the record must exist): pure `AttachmentRules` allows PDF, images, Word / Excel, CSV, text and e-mail up to 10 MB, checks the content signature against the extension and refuses executables; each file is stored with its SHA-256 (`Attachment`, bytes apart in `AttachmentContent` so lists stay light), the same file twice on a record is refused, and opening verifies the checksum (`Att.Tampered` when altered outside One4All). Nothing is deleted: removal keeps the file with who, when and why, and the support behind posted journals and ledger documents cannot be removed. Migration `AddAttachments`. Desktop: Master data › Attachments (list, attach, save a copy, remove). Tests: `AttachmentRulesTests`, `AttachmentTests`. |
| **4.1** Till exchanges and drawer refunds | ✅ | Line refunds against a receipt, refunds without one and whole-sale voids existed, but a cash refund paid out of the drawer never reached the cash the drawer should hold at cash-up, and there was no exchange. Now a refund (receipted or not) given a till session records the cash paid out (`TillCashKind.Refund`, counted at cash-up; not keyable by hand, `Pos.RefundThroughService`). `IPosEventService.ExchangeAsync` takes goods back and sells new ones in one unit: the return is refunded through the till's cash account (checked like any refund: supervisor, never beyond what was sold) and the same value tenders the new sale, so the drawer moves only by the difference - paid in (in cash, or the given tenders) or given back as change; a failed part refuses the whole exchange (`PosExchangeResult`). Desktop: Retail › Exchange goods; till session on both refund screens. Test: `TillExchangeTests`. |
| **4.4** Full cash-flow forecast | ✅ | The only forecast bucketed open receivables and payables into 30-day bands with no opening cash. Now `ICashForecastService` forecasts week by week (1–52 weeks) from the bank accounts' ledger balances: receivables and payables by due date (overdue in the first week), saved payment runs on their payment date (their suppliers' invoices not counted twice), open sales and purchase orders not yet invoiced on delivery / required date plus the trader's terms, standing orders by their schedule, and payroll repeating the last regular run (net pay on the pay day, deductions ten days later). Pure `CashForecastBuilder` gives each week's opening, inflows, outflows, closing and net by source, flags weeks below a minimum (`FirstShortfall`, `LowestClosing`) and keeps every flow for drill-down. Desktop: Banking › Cash-flow forecast. Tests: `CashForecastBuilderTests`, `CashForecastTests`. |
| **4.13** Date-effective prices | ✅ | A price list held one price per item and quantity break, changed in place: no future-dated price, no history, and an order took today's price whatever its date. Now a price line can carry `EffectiveFrom` (unique per list, item, break and date): `PriceList.Resolve(item, quantity, asAt)` / `InForce` pick, per break, the latest price effective on the day (a standing undated price until a dated one takes over). `SchedulePriceAsync` sets a price from a future date; `ScheduleAdjustmentAsync` schedules a list-wide percentage change on the prices in force the day before (e.g. an annual increase); `PriceHistoryAsync` lists every price kept. `PriceAsync(…, asAt)` resolves as at a date and sales orders price at their order date; an immediate change once a dated price is in force becomes a new price from today, keeping the history. Migration `AddEffectiveDatedPrices`. Desktop: Retail › Future prices. Tests: `EffectivePriceTests`, `EffectivePricingTests`. |
| **7** Workflow for payment runs, billing runs, credit notes and write-offs | ✅ | Only requisitions and manual journals went through the approval workflow. Now, when the company defines a workflow for the type (`WorkflowDocumentTypes.PaymentRun / BillingRun / CreditNote / WriteOff`), the document starts it as it is raised (internal `WorkflowGate`) and cannot then be approved, cancelled or written off directly (`Wf.Governed`); handlers apply the outcome: a payment run is paid as approved or withdrawn; a billing run is authorised (its bills then post through the resumable chunked posting) or cancelled; a customer credit note is held (`PendingCreditNote`, `ICreditNoteApprovalService`; posted at once when no workflow is defined) and posts only when approved, a rejection leaving the ledger untouched; a write-off request carries its bad-debt account (`DebtCase.WriteOffAccountCode`) and is written off on approval or withdrawn on rejection. Approvers are notified through the workflow's inbox as for the other documents. Migration `AddWorkflowForRunsCreditsWriteOffs`. Desktop: credit notes submitted through approval; API write-off request takes the account. Test: `WorkflowRoutingTests`. |
| **3** Per-trader exchange-rate types | ✅ | Every customer and supplier document converted at the daily rate. Now `Customer.RateType` / `Supplier.RateType` name the rate type a trader converts at (e.g. a contract `Fixed` rate or a bank rate in the rate table): invoices, credit notes, receipts and payments use a rate keyed on the document, else the trader's own type found on or before the date (direct, inverse or triangulated through `ExchangeRateResolver`), else the daily rate. Migration `AddTraderRateTypes`. Desktop: Customers / Suppliers › Exchange-rate type. Test: `TraderRateTypeTests`. |
| **4.3** Goods receipt over-receipt tolerance (fifth audit #1) | ✅ | A goods received note added whatever was keyed to a purchase order line: unlimited over-receipt. Now the company sets an over-receipt tolerance (`Company.OverReceiptTolerancePercent`, a supplier may have its own, `Supplier.OverReceiptTolerancePercent`); a receipt taking a line beyond ordered × (1 + tolerance) is refused (`Pop.OverReceipt`, every offending line listed) unless a supervisor holding Procurement › GRN authorise (`AuthoriseLevel1`, now in the permission catalogue) accepts it - never the receiver (`Pop.OverReceiptSelf`) nor a user without it (`Pop.OverReceiptNotAuthorised`); the authoriser is kept on the GRN (`GoodsReceivedNote.OverReceiptAuthorisedByUserId`). Migration `AddOverReceiptTolerance`. Desktop: Receive goods takes the supervisor. Test: `OverReceiptTests`. |
| **11.5** Licence clock-rollback detection switched on (fifth audit #2) | ✅ | The validator could detect a clock set back to stretch a licence, but no high-water-mark store existed, so the check never ran. Now `ClockHighWaterMarkStore` keeps the latest time seen sealed with AES-GCM under a key bound to the machine identity, in both the database (`lic.clock_mark`) and a file (`ClockMarkPath`, default the common application-data folder); it reads the later of the two (losing either copy does not wind it back), ignores a tampered or foreign copy, only moves forward and writes at most once a minute. `LicenceService` passes it to the validator wherever the licence is enforced (`LicensingOptions.DetectClockRollback`, default = `Enforce`), so a clock set back beyond a day is `TamperDetected`. Migration `AddLicenceClockMark`. Test: `ClockMarkStoreTests`. |
| **5.1** Back-date and credit-limit override permissions enforced (fifth audit #3) | ✅ | The operation grid carried BackDate and OverrideCreditLimit but nothing checked them: anyone could post into the past, and an invoice over a credit limit had no override path at all. Now the company sets a back-date allowance (`Company.BackDateAllowanceDays`; none = no control) and the posting engine refuses a posting made by a named person (`JournalRequest.PostedByUserId`, or the submitter of a manual journal) dated further back unless they hold General Ledger › Manual Journals back-date (`Posting.BackDateNotAllowed`); system postings are unaffected. An invoice over a customer's credit limit goes through only with a supervisor holding Sales › Customers credit-limit override (`SalesInvoiceRequest.CreditLimitOverrideByUserId`; never the person raising it, `Ar.CreditOverrideSelf`), and each override is kept (`CreditLimitOverride`: invoice, limit, balance after, who allowed and who raised it). Migration `AddBackDateAndCreditOverride`. Desktop: customer invoice takes the overriding supervisor. Test: `BackDateAndCreditOverrideTests`. |
| **11.5** Licence count limits at the point of creation (fifth audit #4) | ✅ | Only companies, POS terminals and named users were checked against the licence. Now `LicenceLimitInterceptor` (an EF save interceptor, so every service, screen and import is covered) refuses a save that would take warehouses, municipal properties, housing stands, waiting-list applicants, students or active employees past the licence (the module's limit first - MUNI properties, HSE stands / waiting_list, SCH students, PAY employees - else the global one); nothing in that save is written (`LicenceLimitException`). Sign-in refuses a user when the licence's `concurrent_users` are all signed in (distinct users with live sessions). The licence screen's usage shows the new counts. Inactive when the licence is not enforced. Transactions-per-year is not limited yet. Test: `LicenceLimitTests`. |
| **3.3** Exchange-rate override control (fifth audit #5) | ✅ | Any rate keyed on a customer or supplier document was accepted silently. Now, when the company controls overrides (`Company.RateOverrideControl`), a keyed rate on an invoice, credit note, receipt or payment needs System › Exchange Rates override (now in the permission catalogue) held by the user who keyed it (`RateOverrideByUserId` on every trading request; `Trading.RateOverrideNotAllowed` otherwise), and each override is kept against the rate the table would have given (`ExchangeRateOverride`, `sys.rate_override`). Documents at the table's rate need nothing; receipt reversals keep the original rate. The API now answers a licence-limit refusal with 403 `Licence.LimitReached` instead of a server error. Migration `AddRateOverrideControl`. Test: `RateOverrideTests`. |
| **3.6** Negative stock by exception, corrected on receipt (fifth audit #6) | ✅ | Any caller could set `AllowNegative` and the till always did, with no reason, authority or record, and a later receipt silently spread the mis-costed shortfall over the stock left. Now, under `Company.NegativeStockControl`, an issue taking stock below zero needs a reason and a supervisor with Inventory › Stock Issue authorise (`Inv.NegativeNeedsAuthority` / `Inv.NegativeNotAllowed`); a till sale (`IssueStockRequest.FromTill`) is let through; each is kept (`NegativeStockEvent`: short quantity, cost used, reason, who allowed it). When a receipt lands on a negative average-cost balance, the shortfall is re-costed at the receipt cost (`NegativeStockCorrection` journal, cost of sales against stock) so the stock left carries the receipt cost, and the events are marked corrected. Off by default (existing behaviour unchanged). Migration `AddNegativeStockControl`. Test: `NegativeStockControlTests`. |
| **4.1** Promotions, coupons and customer prices at the till (fifth audit #7) | ✅ | The promotions engine and customer price lists existed but the till priced everything from the default list and only ever applied manual discounts. Now the ticket keeps a promotion discount per line apart from manual ones (`PosTicketLine.PromotionDiscount / PromotionCode / PromotionAccountCode`); `PromotionBasket()` hands the engine the lines promotions may price (no manual discount or override - they never stack) and `ApplyPromotions` lands its discounts on them, which then post as the promotion's discount with its account and code (`SaleLines`). The till re-prices promotions whenever the basket changes until payment starts, takes coupon codes (Coupon button), and prices an item from the account customer's price list as at today when one is set. Desktop: POS terminal. Test: `TicketPromotionTests` (the till window itself is compile-checked only). |
| **4.2** Batch / serial capture, enforcement and recall trace (fifth audit #8) | ✅ | Lots could only be recorded by hand after a movement; receipts, GRNs, issues and till sales never touched them, and nothing traced a batch for a recall. Now receipts and issues carry their batches / serials (`ReceiveStockRequest.Lots`, `IssueStockRequest.Lots`, `ReceiveLine.Lots` on a GRN) and record them in the same unit. Under `Company.EnforceLotTracking` a tracked receipt without them is refused (`Inv.LotsRequired`); an issue without them takes the earliest-expiry batches on hand (`SuggestPicksAsync`), refused when the known batches fall short (`Inv.LotsShort`), while a till sale takes what there is (`RecordPartialAsync`). `ILotTrackingService.TraceAsync` traces a batch back to its receipts with the supplier (GRN) and forward to the customers it reached (till sale or invoice), with what is left on hand (`LotTrace`, `Customers`). Migration `AddLotTrackingEnforcement`. Test: `LotEnforcementTests`. |
| **4.15.10** Payroll confidentiality: View Salary and the payroll audit garden (fifth audit #9) | ✅ | `Payroll.ViewSalary` was defined but unused: any payroll right (leave approval included) revealed pay in reports and API responses, and every auditor saw payroll changes. Now pay fields are hidden unless the user holds Payroll › View Salary (`FieldSensitivity.HiddenFor`; pay field recognition now also covers gross earnings, total deductions and hourly rates) - so a line manager approves leave without seeing pay, in reports (row stripping) and API responses alike. A new Payroll › Payroll Audit Trail permission walls payroll changes off: `AuditQuery.RequestingUserId` leaves every payroll entity's changes out for anyone without it (super administrators and system queries see all); the desktop audit trail and `/api/audit` pass the signed-in user. Tests: `FieldSensitivityTests` (leave approver), `PayrollWalledGardenTests`. |
| **7** Purchase orders under the workflow; scheduled escalation (fifth audit #10) | ✅ | Purchase orders raised directly had only an amendment re-approval, and overdue approvals escalated only when someone ran it by hand. Now, when the company defines a purchase-order workflow (`WorkflowDocumentTypes.PurchaseOrder`), a new order starts it and waits (`PurchaseOrder.AwaitingApproval`; receiving refused, `Pop.AwaitingApproval`); approval releases it, rejection cancels it (`PurchaseOrderWorkflowHandler`); the raiser is the initiator (`CreatePurchaseOrderRequest.RaisedByUserId`). Escalation of approvals past their stage's time limit runs as the `workflow.escalate` scheduled task. (Stages already take a role as their approver group via `RequiredRoleName`.) Migration `AddPurchaseOrderApproval`. Desktop: purchase order says when it awaits approval. Test: `PurchaseOrderWorkflowTests`. |
| **5.2 / 6** Caller identity and source address in the audit and login trails (fifth audit #11) | ✅ | Sign-ins recorded no source address, and - found on the way - the scoped identity the audit interceptor reads was never signed in by the API or the desktop, so every change they made was audited as "System". Now the API puts each request's caller (from the token) and client IP on the scoped identity (`CurrentUserContext.SetClientAddress`, terminal "api"), the desktop's scopes use the signed-in session (`DesktopCurrentUser`, machine name as terminal and address), `ICurrentUser.ClientAddress` carries the address, sign-in attempts keep it (`LoginEvent.SourceAddress`) and audit entries are written under the caller's name with it (`ChangeLogEntry.IpAddress`). Migration `AddLoginSourceAddress`. Test: `CallerIdentityTests`. |
| **9** Ledger guards in the database (fifth audit #12) | ✅ | The database guarded posted journals against edits and control accounts against manual journals, but nothing in it stopped a negative amount, a line that was both a debit and a credit, or a posted journal that did not balance - only the posting engine did. Now `gl.journal_line` carries CHECK constraints (`ck_journal_line_amounts_not_negative`, `ck_journal_line_one_side`), and deferred constraint triggers (`trg_journal_line_balanced`, `trg_journal_balanced` → `gl.assert_journal_balanced`) refuse, when the transaction commits, any posted or reversed journal whose base debits and credits differ - however it was written. Existing data passed both on migration. Migration `AddLedgerDatabaseGuards`. Tests: `LedgerDatabaseGuardTests` (`LedgerIntegrityTests` now also lifts the new trigger to plant its fault). |
| **4.13** Sales order amendments with history (fifth audit #13) | ✅ | Sales orders could be created, despatched and cancelled but never amended. Now `ISalesOrderAmendmentService.AmendAsync` changes line quantities and prices as a new version (`SalesOrder.Version`, `LastAmendedByUserId`), keeping the version it replaced with its lines, value, reason, who and when (`sop.sales_order_revision`); every line change is checked before any line moves (`CheckAmendment`), a quantity cannot go below what has been despatched or invoiced, an invoiced line's price cannot change, and cancelled or invoiced orders are refused. `HistoryAsync` lists every version. Desktop: Sales & Schools → "Amend a sales order". Migration `AddSalesOrderAmendments`. Test: `SalesOrderAmendmentTests`. |
| **4.3** Min/max replenishment and moving-average forecast (fifth audit #14) | ✅ | Replenishment suggested a fixed reorder quantity once stock fell to a reorder level, with no maximum and no view of demand. Now items carry `MaximumStock`, `LeadTimeDays` and `ForecastWindowDays` (set through `IReplenishmentService.SetPolicyAsync`, which refuses a maximum at or below the minimum and a forecast with no lead time). At or below the minimum, a suggestion orders up to the maximum; with a forecast window, the reorder point rises to the moving-average daily issues over the window (reversed issues left out) times the lead time. Suggestions show the maximum and the average daily usage. Desktop: "Set an item's replenishment policy". Migration `AddReplenishmentPolicy`. Test: `ReplenishmentForecastTests`. |
| **4.15** Leave rules: ceiling, lapse, advance, sick pay tiers (fifth audit #15) | ✅ | Leave policies had entitlement bands, monthly or yearly accrual and a carry-over cap, but no ceiling on the balance, carried days never lapsed, leave could never be taken in advance, and sick days had no effect on pay. Now a policy can carry `MaxAccumulationDays` (accrual stops there, the rest forfeited), `CarryOverExpiryMonths` (what the year turns with is carried, taken first, and any not taken lapses that many months in - `EmployeeLeaveBalance.CarriedOverDays`/`CarryExpiresOn`), `NegativeBalanceDays` (leave taken in advance down to that; an advance cannot be encashed, `Hr.EncashAdvance`) and sick pay tiers (`pay.leave_sick_tier`: from the nth sick day of the leave year, so much of pay). `ILeavePolicyService.ApplySickPayAsync` places a period's sick days in the leave year, prices the unpaid share at basic ÷ working days and sets it as a deduction variable for the pay run. Desktop: "Leave policies" gains the fields and an "Apply sick pay" step. Migration `AddLeaveRules`. Test: `LeaveRulesTests`. |
| **4.11** School fee controls: bursary approval, staff-child discount, do-not-disclose (fifth audit #16) | ✅ | A bursary could be set on a student with no request, approval or screen; staff children paid full fees; and nothing withheld a protected student's identity. Now `ISchoolBursaryService` takes bursary requests (`sch.bursary_award`, one pending per student) that reduce fees only once approved - never by the requester, only with `School.Bursary` Approve, or through the approval workflow when one is defined for `Bursary` (`BursaryWorkflowHandler`). `Company.StaffChildDiscountPercent` and `Student.StaffEmployeeId` give a staff member's child the staff discount while the parent is not terminated - the better of it and the sibling discount (`StudentBillResult.StaffDiscount`). `Student.DoNotDisclose` (with a required note) withholds the name as "Restricted record" in the school reports and student register for readers without the new `School.Restricted` permission. Desktop: "Bursaries", "Staff children and restricted students". Migration `AddSchoolFeeControls`. Test: `SchoolFeeControlTests`. |
| **4.18** Multi-year budget framework, scenario types, Archived/Locked (fifth audit #17) | ✅ | Budgets covered one year, six types, and ended at Superseded. Now `BudgetType` adds Base/Best/Worst case, Zero-based, Rolling forecast, Project and Establishment; `IBudgetFrameworkService` gives a budget up to four indicative outer years (`bud.budget_outer_year`, set before approval, fixed after), shows the framework by account, and rolls an approved framework forward into next year's draft (year 2 spread evenly over the periods, later years moved up, the last carried on; `Budget.RolledFromBudgetId`, once per year). `BudgetStatus` adds Archived (only from Superseded, seal verified) and Locked (only from Archived); a put-away budget takes no lines and cannot be activated. Desktop: Budgeting → "Multi-year framework, archive and lock". Migration `AddBudgetFramework`. Test: `BudgetFrameworkTests`. |
| **4.10.1** Compulsory acquisition sub-process (fifth audit #18) | ✅ | Compensation claims could be provided for, settled or withdrawn, but none of the compulsory acquisition steps existed. Now `ICompensationService` serves the notice (opening an objection window), keeps an objections register (lodged only inside the window, upheld or dismissed), records the compensation assessment, offers (one open at a time, accepted or rejected) and disputes referred to arbitration or court with their award - the assessment, an accepted offer and an award each move the IAS 37 provision to what is still owed - takes part payments out of the provision, and takes possession only after the window closes with no objection open or upheld (`hse.compensation_step`, `CompensationClaim.PaidToDate`, settlement counts part payments). H5 now shows notice, objections, assessed, offered, paid, outstanding and possession. API: `/api/housing/compensation-claims/{claim}/notice|objections|assess|offers|disputes|part-payments|possession`. Migration `AddCompulsoryAcquisition`. Test: `CompulsoryAcquisitionTests`. |
| **4.10.7** Tenancy occupants and lodger permits (fifth audit #19) | ✅ | A tenancy recorded only the tenant: no household, no lodger rules. Now `IRentalOccupancyService` keeps the occupants list (`hse.tenancy_occupant`, moved in / out), a unit occupancy limit (`RentalUnit.MaxOccupants`: tenant, occupants and lodgers together, `Hse.Overcrowded`), and lodger permits (`hse.lodger_permit`: lodger, rooms - the tenant always keeps one, `Hse.LodgerRooms` - period, revocation). A permit's monthly fee is billed on the rent invoice to its own income account for every month it is in force; ending the tenancy moves the household out and ends its permits. API: `/api/housing/tenancies/{t}/occupants|occupancy`, `/api/housing/lodger-permits`, `/api/housing/rental-units/{u}/max-occupants`. Migration `AddTenancyOccupancy`. Test: `TenancyOccupancyTests`. |
| **4.17** BOQ import and BOQ budget by cost head (fifth audit #20) | ✅ | BOQ lines could only be keyed one at a time. Now `IContractControlService.PreviewBoqImportAsync` checks a CSV (item, description, unit, quantity, rate, optional cost head; header row and quoted fields allowed) and lists every line with its error - missing fields, bad numbers, an item repeated in the file or already on the contract - without saving; `ImportBoqAsync` imports all lines or none. `BoqItem.CostHead` budgets the work by cost head, and `BoqByCostHeadAsync` sets each head's BOQ value beside the value measured and the cost captured under it. Desktop: Job costing → "Import a BOQ" (file path or inline lines, preview / import / by cost head). Migration `AddBoqCostHead`. Test: `BoqImportTests`. |
| **4.15** Shift rosters (fifth audit #21) | ✅ | Attendance recorded days with lateness keyed by hand; there were no shifts or rosters. Now `IRosterService` defines shifts (`hr.shift`: times - a night shift ends the next day - grace minutes, an allowance per shift) and rotating patterns (`hr.shift_pattern`, e.g. "D,D,N,N,OFF,OFF"), puts employees on a pattern from an anchor date (`hr.roster_assignment`), generates the roster for a period (`hr.roster_entry`; days changed by hand - swaps, cover - are kept), and shows it beside attendance. Recording a present day with a clock-in and no lateness measures it against the rostered start (across midnight, beyond the grace); `ApplyShiftAllowancesAsync` sets each employee's allowance variable for a pay period from the rostered shifts they were present for. Desktop: People → "Shift rosters". Migration `AddShiftRosters`. Test: `ShiftRosterTests`. |
| **4.1** POS change currency and denomination rounding (fifth audit #22) | ✅ | The till took tenders in any currency but always gave change in base, rounded only to the base cash increment. Now `Company.PosChangeCurrency` (set with `IPosService.ConfigureChangeAsync`, with the base rounding increment and rounding account) makes the till give change in that currency: `ChangeQuoteAsync` / `PosChangeQuote.For` converts the change owed at the day's rate and rounds it to the currency's smallest note or coin (`Currency.SmallestCashUnit`: its smallest denomination, else its rounding increment), the ticket tenders it, and the sale posts with the difference to the rounding account (the tolerance widens to half the change currency's smallest unit); change in any other currency is refused (`Pos.ChangeCurrency`). The till shows the change in the change currency. Desktop: Point of Sale → "Change currency and cash rounding". Migration `AddPosChangeCurrency`. Tests: `ChangeCurrencyTests` (unit), `PosChangeCurrencyTests`. |
| **3.6** Specific-identification costing and usage variance (fifth audit #23) | ✅ | `CostingMethod.SpecificIdentification` existed but costed like an average, and a standard-costed assembly put everything off-standard into one price variance. Now each batch or serial received keeps its unit cost (`StockLotMovement.UnitCost`), and an issue of a specifically identified, tracked item is costed by the units that actually leave - the ones named, or the earliest-expiry batches (`StockCosting.PlanSpecificIssueAsync`, taken out of the balance at that cost). An assembly of a standard-costed product with `Item.UsageVarianceAccountCode` posts components used beyond (or short of) standard, at their issue cost, to the usage variance account (`AssemblyRun.UsageVariance`); the product comes in at the standard quantities' cost, so its receipt's price variance is the rest. Migration `AddSpecificCostAndUsageVariance`. Test: `SpecificCostAndUsageVarianceTests`. |
| **3.6** Catch weight (fifth audit #24) | ✅ | `ItemType.Weighed` existed but nothing weighed. Now `IInventoryService.SetCatchWeightAsync` makes an item catch-weight (`Item.CatchWeightUnit`, `NominalWeight`, `WeightTolerancePercent`): receipts need their weight (`Inv.WeightRequired`) and may be bought by weight (`ReceiveStockRequest.CostPerWeightUnit`), the average per piece must sit within the tolerance of the nominal weight (`Inv.WeightOutOfTolerance`), the balance carries pieces and weight (`StockBalance.WeightOnHand`, `CostPerWeight`), and an issue is costed by the weight that leaves at the stock's cost per weight unit - the last pieces take whatever value and weight remain. Movements record their weight. Desktop: Inventory → "Set an item as catch-weight". Migration `AddCatchWeight`. Test: `CatchWeightTests`. |
| **13.4** Report watermark by expression and folder delivery (fifth audit #25) | ✅ | A report's watermark was fixed text drawn only on screen, and scheduled reports could only be emailed. Now `PageSetup.WatermarkExpression` works out each run's watermark from the parameters, the first row, the user and the date (e.g. DRAFT or CONFIDENTIAL), falling back to the fixed watermark, and the PDF renderer draws it on every page. A subscription can name a `DeliveryFolder`: each run is saved under the configured delivery root (`ReportDeliveryOptions.FolderRoot`; folders outside it are refused, `Rpt.DeliveryFolder`), stamped with the run time and logged, with or instead of the email. Migration `AddReportFolderDelivery`. Tests: `WatermarkExpressionTests` (unit), `ReportFolderDeliveryTests`. |
| **5.1** API access check covers JSON bodies | ✅ | Found while scoping data: the API's access check read the company, branch, warehouse and acting user only from the query string and route, so a POST whose JSON body named another company (or acted as another user) passed it. Now the server reads a JSON body's top-level values (without consuming it) into `ApiAccessRequest.Body`: its scoped fields are checked against the user's scopes like the query's, a body contradicting the query is refused (400), and every field recording who acted or authorised (`ApiAccessRules.BodyActorFields`: entered/submitted/approved/…-by, and the supervisor overrides - over the API a supervisor authorises by making the request) must be the signed-in user (403). Test: `ApiBodyAccessTests`. |
| **11.5** Licence volume limits: transactions a year and budget departments | ✅ | The licence carried `transactions_per_year` and `budget_departments` limits that nothing enforced. Now `LicenceLimitInterceptor` refuses a save that would take the journals dated in a calendar year past `transactions_per_year`, or the distinct departments budgeted for past `budget_departments` (and the budgeting module's `departments` limit) - a new version for a department already budgeted is fine. The licence status endpoint reports both usages. Test: `LicenceVolumeLimitTests`. |
| **10** Payroll year-to-date openings import | ✅ | Cumulative tax read the year to date only from this payroll's own payslips, so the first year after going live mid-year under-taxed everyone. Now `IVerticalImportService.ImportPayrollYtdAsync` takes `employee_number, tax_year, code, amount` (TAXABLE for taxable pay so far, or a pay element such as PAYE for the tax already deducted), checked line by line before anything is written and replacing an earlier figure for the same employee, year and code (`pay.ytd_opening`); the payroll run adds them to its year to date. Desktop: "Import master data or opening balances" → PayrollYtd. Migration `AddPayrollYtdOpenings`. Test: `PayrollYtdOpeningTests`. |
| **10** Meter register import | ✅ | Meters could only be installed one at a time. Now `IVerticalImportService.ImportMetersAsync` loads the register from `serial, stand, service, reading, date[, digits, multiplier]`: each meter is registered with its opening reading as an accepted actual reading and linked to the property's active service of that type; a stand without the service, a service already metered (or twice in the file) and a serial already on the register fail the file before anything is written. Desktop: "Import master data or opening balances" → Meters. Test: `MeterRegisterImportTests`. |
| **4.15.3** Split-currency tax in the pay run | ✅ | Elements could be paid in another currency and PR35 worked out each currency's tax afterwards, but the run taxed everything on the base table. Now a tax-table element marked `SplitCurrencyTax` taxes each currency apart in the run: earnings paid in a currency with its own table in force are converted at the pay date's rate, taxed on that table and converted back; the rest bear their share of the base table's tax on the whole (PR35's rule). The run builds each employee's taxable earnings by currency and the pay date's rates; a currency with a table but no rate is refused (`Payroll.NoRate`). Period tax only. Migration `AddSplitCurrencyTax`. Test: `SplitCurrencyTaxTests`. |

Built on the services already tested. |

Every application service apart from internal plumbing (API access, JWT, permissions, number sequences) now has a desktop screen. |
| **2 / 5.1** Company query filter in the DbContext | ✅ | Every company-scoped entity (208 types) carries a named `Company` query filter (`CompanyScope == null || CompanyId == CompanyScope`), read from the context at query time; soft delete becomes the named `SoftDelete` filter, and the 92 reads that include deleted rows now lift only that filter, so they stay inside the company. An authorised API request naming a company scopes the request's context to it, so another company's rows are invisible even by id. `WithoutCompanyScope()` lifts it for the services that work across companies by design (intercompany posting, consolidation); installation-wide licence counts and the intercompany mirror report still read every company. Unscoped contexts (background jobs, the desktop, tests) behave as before. No schema change. |
| **5.1** Scope-rule row filters | ✅ | Role scope rules now filter rows in the DbContext, not only request parameters: every entity with a `BranchId`, `WarehouseId`, `TerminalId` (till), `JobContractId`, `CostCentre`, `Department`, `Ward` or `Zone` column carries a named `Scope.*` query filter reading the context's allowed list (codes upper-case, ids resolved from codes by `IDataScopeService.RowScopeAsync`); untagged rows stay visible. The API applies the user's row scope to every GET request, so lists, enquiries and reports show only the branches, warehouses, tills, jobs, cost centres, departments and wards the user may see; writes stay held to the request checks (they may need rows across scopes, such as a transfer between warehouses). No schema change. |
| **6B** Offline encrypted terminal store | ✅ | `ILocalDataProtector` (`LocalDataProtector`: AES-GCM under a random 256-bit key kept in `%LOCALAPPDATA%/One4All/local.key`, the key itself DPAPI-protected for the signed-in Windows user) seals everything the terminal keeps: queued offline sales are now written sealed (`.o4a`; plain `.json` entries from before still flush), and a new `ILocalCatalogueStore` keeps a sealed snapshot of the company, store, terminal, priced catalogue, tax rates, posting accounts, tender currencies, customers and the open till session, refreshed on every online load and when a till opens or closes. When the database is unreachable the touch terminal loads from the snapshot (resuming the open till session so selling continues), scans by barcode, PLU or code against it at the saved price, and queues sales; the first online sale afterwards syncs the queue. A tampered or foreign snapshot is ignored. Deviation: sealed files rather than SQLite (no new database engine on the terminal); promotions are not yet in the snapshot, and a till cannot be opened while offline. |
| **13.1** Report engine: band flow, typed grouping, page numbers | ✅ | Band options the model declared but the engine ignored now work: `SuppressWhen` hides a band per row, and the new `KeepTogether`, `PageBreakBefore` and `PageBreakAfter` join `RepeatOnNewPage` in pagination (a group header repeats on each page its group continues on; a kept-together header never sits alone at a page foot; PDF honours the page breaks). Groups sort by value (numbers and dates as such, text ignoring case) and honour `Descending`; a group header's aggregates now cover its own group (they covered the whole report). Aggregates gain First and Last and a `GrandTotal` scope. `PageNumber` / `TotalPages` in expressions now print the real page (they were always 1; PDF draws them per page). Functions: `Case` / `Case2` / `Case3`, `FiscalPeriod`, `FiscalYear`, `DateDiffIn(unit, …)`. Legal paper was laid out as A4 in preview, HTML and PDF - fixed. The designer session sets band flow options with undo (`SetBandOptions`). |
| **13.1** Report parameters: lists, cascading, multi-select | ✅ | A parameter's `LookupQuery` now offers choices: `IReportParameterLookupService` runs it through the same guarded read-only path as report queries (one SELECT filtered by `@CompanyId`, no system schemas), first column the value and second the label (up to 1,000). New `DependsOn` makes a list cascade: it waits until its parents have values, then binds them (`@Prefix`, `@Branch`…). `MultiSelect` parameters (comma/semicolon separated) reach the report's query as typed arrays (`= ANY(@Codes)`), parsed by the shared `ReportParameterValues`. API `GET /api/reports/{code}/parameters/{name}/options` (other query values are the entries so far); the desktop report viewer shows an editable drop-down for listed parameters and reloads dependent lists when a parent changes. |
| **13.1** Report barcodes and chart types | ✅ | Barcodes gain Code 39 (start/stop asterisks, wide 3:1) and EAN-13 (twelve digits get their check digit, thirteen must carry the right one; first-digit parity) beside QR and Code 128. Chart elements gain `ChartType` (Bar, Column, StackedColumn, Line, Area, Pie, Donut) and `ChartSeriesField`: anything but Bar is drawn as an RGB PNG (`ChartImages`, summed by category and series, up to 30 categories and 8 series, negatives below the axis) that every renderer already places, with category labels and a legend (pie slices with their totals) as text cells. PDF417 is not yet drawn. |
| **13.1** Report element text options | ✅ | Elements gain `NullText` (shown when a field or expression has no value), per-side borders (`BorderLeft/Top/Right/Bottom`, falling back to the thickness), `Padding`, `WordWrap` (off: one line cut short), `AutoShrink` (the font steps down to 5pt until the text fits its box) and `Rotation` (0/90/180/270), and the long-declared `VAlign` now reaches the output. `RenderCell` is now a record, so layout copies keep every property. HTML, PDF and the desktop preview / print draw them all; the designer's property grid lists them. Note: the desktop preview now honours VAlign (default Top) instead of always centring, matching PDF and HTML. |
| **13.1** Report page setup: columns, background, tray, duplex | ✅ | `PageSetup` gains `Columns` / `ColumnGap`: detail rows flow across the columns then down (`Columns.Arrange`, in the engine so every renderer gets it; group and other bands still span the page and restart the columns) - label sheets and cards. `BackgroundImage` prints a picture behind every page (letterhead, pre-printed form, certificate border) in PDF, HTML and the desktop preview / print. `Tray` (Auto, Manual, Cassette, Tractor, Sheet feeder) and `Duplex` (long / short edge) are preselected in the desktop print dialog (`PrintPreferences`), which the user can still change. |
| **13.3** Report exports laid out like the report, and Word | ✅ | `LayoutGrid` turns a rendered report into real rows and columns: the page header once (column titles), group headers, detail lines and totals in order, columns taken from where text sits across the page (within 3pt), each line of a band a row, figures as numbers (thousands separators, bracketed negatives) and codes with leading zeros as text. New formats `XlsxLayout` (headings and totals bold), `CsvLayout` and `Docx` (a hand-written WordprocessingML table) beside the raw-data `Xlsx` / `Csv`, offered first in the desktop viewer. Fix: the financial statements' Excel option fell through to HTML; it now gives a workbook. |
| **13.2** Designer: band flow and page setup | ✅ | The banded designer gains a "Flow…" button on every band strip (hide-when expression, repeat on new page and keep-with-next for group headers, page break before / after; the strip shows what is set) and a "Page setup…" toolbar dialog (paper incl. receipt rolls and custom size, orientation, margins, label columns and gap, watermark text or expression, background picture from a file, printer tray, two-sided). Both apply as one undoable step (`ReportDesignSession.SetBandOptions` / new `SetPage`, which keeps elements inside a narrower page). Chart type and series and the new element text options are in the property grid. |
| **2 / 5.1** Audit log partitioned by year; archival with the licence holder's key | ✅ | Migration `PartitionAuditLogAndArchive` turns `audit.change_log` into a table partitioned by year of `occurred_on_utc` (2020–2040 plus a default partition; `audit.ensure_change_log_year(y)` adds more), copying every row with its id and keeping the identity running; the primary key becomes (id, occurred_on_utc); UPDATE / DELETE stay refused by the row trigger and TRUNCATE on the parent and on every partition. `IAuditArchiveService.ArchiveYearAsync` archives a closed year only with a signed `MA1.` authorisation from the licence holder's trusted key for `audit.archive` of that year (`MaintenanceAuthorisationCodec`, Ed25519, with expiry), oldest year first: it records each company's chain head and a SHA-256 of the year's row hashes in `audit.archive` (itself append-only), then detaches the partition into `audit_archive` (still readable, kept append-only). Chain verification and new entries carry on from the archived heads. Desktop: System Manager → "Archive an audit year" (lists years, live or archived). The audit log no longer maps `xmin` (PostgreSQL cannot return it from an insert into a partitioned table; the log is never updated). The other tables the brief lists for partitioning remain unpartitioned: their primary keys are referenced by foreign keys, which a partition key would break. Migration applied. |
| **12** Industry Setup Wizard: item categories, POS terminal profile, report set | ✅ | Each vertical now also seeds item categories (new `ItemCategory`, unique per company), its POS terminal at head office (Retail "TILL01", School "TUCK01") and its report set: the vertical's documents (ratepayer, tenant, fee and customer statements, official receipt, receipt/refund slip layouts) and key reports, recorded as `FeaturedReport` and listed first (★) in the desktop report viewer. Featured rather than copied, so system improvements to those reports still reach the company. Adding a vertical again adds nothing; the wizard reports the new counts. Every featured code is checked against the catalogue. Migration applied. |
| **13.1** PDF417 barcodes | ✅ | Barcode elements take `PDF417` (error correction level 2, UTF-8, quiet zone), encoded by ZXing.Net (the library the brief names; Apache 2.0) and drawn as a greyscale PNG two pixels a module across and three down, so every renderer shows it. Tests read the drawn symbol back with ZXing's PDF417 reader and get the exact value. All five symbologies the brief lists are now drawn (QR, Code 128, Code 39, EAN-13, PDF417). |
| **5.2 / 6** API sessions revocable; audit chain safe under concurrency (sixth audit #1) | ✅ | API tokens now carry their sign-in session (`sid`): every request re-checks it (`OnTokenValidated` → `TouchSessionAsync`), so a session ended by logout, the session limit, an administrator or idle timeout stops its token at once. New `POST /api/auth/logout` and `POST /api/auth/password` (ends the session; sign in again); a token issued with a pending password change is refused everywhere else in the API until it is changed. The built-in JWT signing key (public in the source) is now allowed only in Development - the server refuses to start elsewhere without `Jwt:SigningKey`. The audit hash chain is extended under a per-company PostgreSQL advisory lock (`AuditChainLock`, in a transaction of its own when the save had none), so concurrent writers no longer link to the same previous hash and break verification (tested with 24 concurrent writers); explicit audit events carry on from archived chain heads too. |
| **3.2** Posting engine checks cost centres and departments (sixth audit #2) | ✅ | A journal line may only name a cost centre or department the company has set up and still uses (any case; another company's, an unknown or a deactivated one is refused with `Posting.CostCentreUnknown` / `Posting.DepartmentUnknown`); a line naming none is still fine. Before, any string went into the ledger. Flows that post an employee's or a requisition's department now need that department set up in the GL dimensions. |
| **6** Second net widened (sixth audit #3) | ✅ | Migration `WidenDatabaseChangeSecondNet` adds the database change-capture trigger to the journal headers, chart of accounts, cost centres, departments, and every table that decides who may do what (groups, group roles, group membership, user permission overrides, scope rules). Journal lines are captured on any update or delete, and on inserts that do not come from the application (a `WHEN` on `application_name`), so the application's own postings - already in the hash-chained audit log - are not doubled. Migration applied. |
| **3.4** Period soft close (sixth audit #4) | ✅ | A Closed period is now a soft close, as the period status always described: ordinary postings are refused, but a user granted the new `GeneralLedger.PeriodAdjustment` (Post) permission can still post adjustments into it (checked against the journal's poster / submitter). Permanently Closed is the hard close and takes nothing. A year the year-end routine has closed takes no adjustments at all (they would miss the closing entries - reverse the year-end instead). Refusals keep the `Posting.PeriodNotOpen` code. |
| **3.3 / 4.1** Multi-currency journal lines; POS foreign tenders post in their currency (sixth audit #5) | ✅ | `JournalLineRequest.Currency` lets a line carry its own currency (amount, rate and base) inside a journal in another; the engine holds a multi-currency journal to balance in base, and a single-currency journal still to balance in its currency too. Point-of-sale tenders in a foreign currency (and their voids) now reach the drawer's GL account as that currency at the till's rate, so FX revaluation can retranslate the cash held, instead of base-currency amounts only. |
| **4.15.4** Part-period pay and pay frequencies (sixth audit #6a) | ✅ | Employees gain a `PayFrequency` (Monthly, Fortnightly, Weekly; default Monthly, migration `AddEmployeePayFrequency`). `PayPeriods` gives each frequency's window (the calendar month, or the week / fortnight ending on the pay date), periods a year and tax-year period number. Someone engaged (or leaving) within the window is paid basic for the calendar days employed (formulas also see DAYSWORKED, DAYSINPERIOD, PRORATA, PERIODSPERYEAR); a given basic (back pay, settlements) is used as it is. Tax on the (monthly) tables scales the bands to the frequency (12/52 weekly, 12/26 fortnightly) for period, cumulative and annual-payment tax. Weekly staff are paid in their own pay group run. Migration applied. |
| **4.15.1** Employee placement and personal details (sixth audit #6b) | ✅ | Employees gain the branch they work at (`BranchId`, so branch-scoped users see only their branches' staff), the GL project and fund their pay is charged to, whom they report to, next of kin (name, phone, relationship), marital status, nationality, social security number and trade union (migration `AddEmployeePlacementAndPersonalDetails`). Create and update check the branch, project (open) and fund (active) exist and that nobody reports to themselves. The payroll journal now charges pay to the employee's project, fund and branch as well as department and cost centre. The desktop employee record takes every new field, and the pay frequency. Migration applied. |
| **4.15.4** Pay element rules (sixth audit #6c) | ✅ | Pay elements gain the brief's per-element flags: pensionable, subject to social security, overtime base and leave-pay base (earnings build the formula variables PENSIONABLE, SOCIALSECURITYBASE, OVERTIMEBASE, LEAVEBASE); element groups (GROUP_name) and a new `PercentOfGroup` basis; a floor and a ceiling (e.g. a social security earnings ceiling); rounding to a unit (nearest, up, down); `Memo` elements (on the payslip, neither paid nor posted) and `Provision` elements (employer accruals: Dr expense, Cr provision); and expense accounts per department ("DEPT=ACCOUNT;…"). Migration `AddPayElementRules`; the desktop pay element screen takes every option. Migration applied. |
| **4.15.4** Taxable benefits in kind (sixth audit #6d) | ✅ | New `EmployeeBenefit` (motor vehicle, housing, school fees, medical, other; from / to dates) and the effective-dated `VehicleBenefitScale` (bands by engine capacity or cost).
