Each pay period a benefit applies it is valued (`BenefitValuation`: its own monthly value, else the vehicle scale in force), scaled to the pay frequency, added to taxable pay and shown on the payslip as a memo line (not paid, not posted; counted in year-to-date taxable pay for cumulative tax). A cheap or interest-free staff loan's benefit is taxed too: the outstanding balance at the company's deemed rate (`Company.DeemedLoanInterestRatePercent`) less the loan's own rate (`EmployeeLoan.InterestRatePercent`). Desktop: HR & Attendance → "Benefits in kind" (benefits, scale bands, deemed rate, loan rate). Migration `AddBenefitsInKind` applied. |
| **4.15.5** Staff loans repaid from pay (sixth audit #6e) | ✅ | Each pay run now charges a staff loan its period's interest at the loan's rate (a memo line; Dr the loan, Cr `Company.StaffLoanInterestAccountCode`) and deducts the instalment (Cr the loan), never more than the balance with its interest and never taking net pay below zero; the loan's balance moves when the run is committed and settles at zero. Loans can be suspended (unpaid leave; interest still accrues) or kept off pay (`DeductFromPay`: new loans on; loans that existed before stay off, as they were repaid by hand). Reversing a run puts the repayments back (reopening a settled loan) and takes the interest off. Desktop: "Benefits in kind" also suspends / resumes loans and sets the interest account. Migration `AddLoanRepaymentFromPay` applied. |
| **4.15.7** Payroll calculation trace stored (sixth audit #6f) | ✅ | Every payslip line now keeps its own workings from the run (`PayslipLine.Workings`, migration `AddPayslipLineWorkings`): the basis and the actual values used (e.g. "5% of basic 2,000.00", a formula with each variable it named and its value, the tax table on the period's taxable pay and the band scale), a part-period basic ("basic 3,100.00 × 21/31 days"), and any floor / ceiling / rounding, arrears or net-pay cap applied; benefits and loan lines carry theirs. Report PR6 shows the stored workings instead of rebuilding them from today's element settings. Migration applied. |
| **4.15.1** Salary scales, increments, acting and the post register (sixth audit #6g) | ✅ | Grades now carry effective-dated salary scales by notch (`SalaryScale`, `hr.salary_scale`); the annual increment run moves each active employee on a scaled grade one notch up to that notch's salary, skipping anyone at the top or incremented within the year (`Employee.LastIncrementOn`). Acting appointments (`hr.acting_appointment`, from–to) feed `ACTINGALLOWANCE` = the acted grade's scale less the employee's basic, per period. Once a company keeps a post register, engaging or moving staff into a department/grade without an approved post, or into a filled one, is refused (`Payroll.NoSuchPost` / `Payroll.PostFilled`); the establishment screen lists approved, filled and vacant posts. Desktop: Set a salary scale notch, Run the annual increment, Appoint to act. Migration `AddSalaryScalesAndActing` applied. |
| **4.2** Kit explosion on sale (sixth audit #7) | ✅ | Selling a kit (`ItemType.Kit`) now moves the components of its active bill of materials, scaled from the batch to the quantity sold, with kits inside kits exploded in turn (internal `KitExplosion.StockLinesAsync`, depth-guarded): POS sales issue them, POS refunds with and without a receipt restock them (at the issued cost / average cost), sales-order despatch issues them into cost of sales and despatch returns put them back, and a completed lay-bye issues them. POS void already reverses every issue under the sale number. No migration. |
| **4.14** Assets from supplier invoices + depreciation conventions (sixth audit #8) | ✅ | `IFixedAssetService.CapitaliseFromInvoiceAsync` capitalises an asset from a posted AP invoice: the invoiced amount on the account it was coded to becomes the cost (no reposting when coded to the class cost account; a reclassification journal Dr cost / Cr the coded account otherwise), split across several assets if needed but never beyond what is left uncapitalised (`FixedAsset.SourceOpenItemId` / `SourceAccountCode`; `Fa.InvoiceCapitalised`, `Fa.InvoiceAmbiguous`). Depreciation conventions on the asset and class (`DepreciationConvention` FullMonth / MidMonth (after the 15th: next month) / NextMonth / DailyProRata first month by days held), applied by the run, `MonthsDepreciated` and the forecast. Desktop: Capitalise from a supplier invoice; convention on asset classes. Migration `AddAssetConventionsAndInvoiceSource` applied. |
| **7** Master-data approvals, attachments and email approve links (sixth audit #9) | ✅ | Changes to tariff rates and to customer, supplier and item fields go through `IMasterDataChangeService` (`sys.master_data_change`, whitelisted fields, typed parsing, one pending change per field): held for the company's `MasterDataChange` workflow when defined and applied by its handler on approval, applied at once otherwise, with the value before and after. Approvers see the files attached to a document (`AttachmentTarget.WorkflowDocument` keyed `{DocumentType}/{Reference}`, plus a PO's or journal's own) as `PendingApproval.Attachments` and in the notice. With `Company.ApprovalLinkBaseUrl` set, each approver with an email is also queued a single-use link (`wf.action_token`, SHA-256 only, 7 days, valid only while the document is at that stage): `GET /api/workflow/email/{token}` shows the document and asks, `POST` approves or rejects (`IWorkflowService.ActByTokenAsync` / `DescribeTokenAsync`). Desktop: Request a master-data change, Email approval links. Migration `AddMasterDataChangesAndApprovalLinks` applied. |
| **10** API keys, rate limiting, OpenAPI and list paging (sixth audit #10) | ✅ | Integrations sign in with `X-Api-Key: {clientId}.{secret}` (`ApiClient`, `sec.api_client`, SHA-256 of the secret only, constant-time compare; `IApiClientService` create / rotate / revoke / list / authenticate; optional expiry and own rate limit) and act as a named service user, so the same permission, company, actor and scope checks apply - a policy scheme picks the API-key handler when the header is present, JWT otherwise. Rate limiting (`AddRateLimiter`, fixed one-minute windows): per API client, else per user, else per address at `RateLimiting:PermitPerMinute` (600) or the client's own; sign-in attempts at `RateLimiting:SignInPerMinute` (20) per address; 429 with Retry-After. The OpenAPI document is served outside development when `OpenApi:Enabled` is set, and then only to signed-in callers. Every endpoint maps through one route group whose filter gives a GET's list result an `X-Total-Count` header and pages it with `?skip=` / `?take=` (≤ 1,000). Smoke-tested live: bad key and no auth 401, the fourth sign-in in a minute 429, a key signs in as its user, the inbox carries X-Total-Count. Desktop: API keys for integrations. Migration `AddApiClients` applied. |
| **4.6** Till search, favourites, category drill-down, refund window, rate policy, approval machine and hash (sixth audit #11) | ✅ | `IPosCatalogueService` now searches the whole item master on the server (code, PLU, item / pack / alias barcodes, synonyms, brand, description - via `IItemCatalogueService.SearchAsync`) and prices the hits; keeps favourites per till or for every till (`PosFavourite`, `pos.favourite`, the till's own first); and drills categories (`ItemCategory.ParentCode`, `CategoriesAsync`, `CategoryTilesAsync` including every sub-category). The till screen gains a ★ Favourites tab, shows a category's sub-categories with it, and asks the server when typed text matches no local tile. Refund window: `Company.RefundWindowDays` - past it the authoriser needs `Pos.Refund` AuthoriseLevel2 (`Pos.RefundWindow`). Exchange-rate policy by document type or ledger (`ExchangeRatePolicy`, `sys.exchange_rate_policy`): a trader's own rate type first, then the document type's, then AR/AP's, then daily. Each workflow decision keeps `Machine` (terminal or client address) and `SnapshotHash` (SHA-256 of the snapshot). Desktop: Till favourites, Refund window, Category under a category, Exchange-rate policy. Migration `AddTillFavouritesRatePolicyAndApprovalHash` applied. |
| **4.9** Council service types, connection quantity, deposit and status (sixth audit #12a) | ✅ | A council defines its own services (`MunicipalServiceType`, `mun.service_type`: code, name, the built-in category it bills as, metered, default deposit and deposit account; `IMunicipalServiceTypeService`). Connecting one to a stand (`PropertyServiceLink.ServiceCode`, `Quantity` default 1, `DepositAmount`) checks the tariff is of that category and invoices the deposit to the ratepayer into the deposit liability account (`DEP-{stand}-{code}-{date}`). Connections carry a status (`ServiceLinkStatus` Active / Suspended / Disconnected / Closed; only active is billed, closed ends it). Charges are the tariff times the quantity; a billing run may be limited to one council service (`BillingRunProposal.ServiceCode`, `BillingRun.ServiceCode`) and the built-in run keeps billing only connections without a code. Desktop: Council service types, Service connections; billing run takes a service code. Migration `AddMunicipalServiceTypesAndConnections` applied. |
| **4.11** School academic structure (sixth audit #12b) | ✅ | Academic years (`AcademicYear`, `sch.academic_year`, no overlaps) and their terms (`SchoolTerm`, `sch.term`, within the year, no overlaps): saving a term gives its dates to every fee structure naming it as their term (for pro-rata billing and deferred income); `TermOnAsync` says which term a date falls in (a holiday has none). Classes by grade and stream (`SchoolClass`, `sch.class`) with capacity, class teacher and the grade they move up to; placing a student respects capacity (`Sch.ClassFull`); the year-end promotion (`IAcademicStructureService.PromoteAsync`, once per year) moves every enrolled student up to their class's next grade, keeping their stream - final-year classes stay, students with no class set up are listed. Desktop: Academic years and terms, Classes and streams. Migration `AddAcademicStructure` applied. |
| **4.12 / 4.10** Contract advances, variations and defects liability; housing financing component (sixth audit #12c, #12d) | ✅ | Client certificates now value approved variation work apart (`ProgressCertificate.VariationsToDate`, never beyond the approved VOs - `Job.VariationsExceed`) and recover a client advance (`IJobCostingService.ReceiveAdvanceAsync`: Dr bank / Cr advance liability; each certificate recovers its percentage of the gross, never past what is outstanding, Dr advance / Cr revenue so revenue stays the gross). Practical completion with a defects-liability period (`SetPracticalCompletionAsync`): no retention before completion, half of all retention until the period ends, the rest after (`Job.RetentionNotDue`; contracts with no completion recorded release as before). Housing: with `Company.HousingMarketRatePercent` set, an instalment sale over more than a year below that rate is a significant financing component (IFRS 15) - pure `FinancingComponent.Measure` gives the instalments' present value and the discount's effective-interest unwind; the sale is discounted (Dr sales / Cr deferred financing, refused before anything is sold without the account), each instalment raised earns its share (Dr deferred / Cr interest income) and settling early earns the rest. Desktop: Client certificates, advance and retention; instalment sales take the deferred account and set the market rate. Migration `AddContractAdvanceDefectsAndFinancingComponent` applied. |
| **4.18** Budget deadlines, reminders and emailed alerts (sixth audit #12e) | ✅ | Each preparation unit carries a submission deadline (`DepartmentUnit.SubmissionDeadline`); `IBudgetNotificationService.BoardAsync` lists every department's deadline, latest budget status for the year and days left; `RemindAsync` emails the budget holders whose department is due within the window (or overdue) and has not started or is still in draft. Utilisation alerts (`AlertAsync`) email `Company.BudgetAlertEmail` the votes crossing `Company.BudgetAlertThresholds` (default 75 / 90 / 100) in the period of the date, each vote and threshold once a year (`BudgetAlertSent`, `bgt.alert_sent`; a higher threshold alerts again). Scheduled task `budget.notices` runs both daily (reminders 7 days ahead for the year being prepared). Desktop: Preparation deadlines and budget alerts (board as its list). Migration `AddBudgetDeadlinesAndAlerts` applied. |
| **6** Business events in the audit trail and accent-insensitive search (sixth audit #12f, #12g) | ✅ | The audit trail now names the business events, not only the row changes (internal `SemanticAudit` via `IAuditService.RecordAsync`, chained under the audit lock, with the caller's user and terminal / address): every posting and every released deferred journal writes **Post**, a journal reversal **Void** (with its reason) and a POS void **Void**, and each workflow decision **Approve** or **Reject** (the approver, the stage, the comment). Journals keep the terminal or address they were entered from (`JournalEntry.Terminal`). Quick search: names are compared without accents on either side (`public.f_unaccent`, an immutable wrapper over the `unaccent` extension, with trigram expression indexes replacing the plain ones on item, customer and supplier names), and it now covers properties (stand number, address) and students (admission number, name) with their own trigram indexes. Scheduled task `budget.notices` registered under the Budget module. Migration `AddJournalTerminalAndAccentSearch` applied. |
| **11** Migration from Excel, stands and budgets; licence trials, usage, passphrase prompt and certificate QR (sixth audit #12h, #12i) | ✅ | Migration: every import also reads an Excel workbook (pure `XlsxReader` - first or named sheet to CSV; shared / inline strings, numbers, booleans, date-styled cells as yyyy-MM-dd, gaps kept, quoting; no new package); new imports for the housing stand register (`ImportStandsAsync`: stand, parcel, size[, cost, scheme, status from raw land to available]; a parcel not yet registered is created from its stands' totals, nothing posted) and a budget (`ImportBudgetAsync`: account, period, amount[, cost centre, quantity] for the fiscal year of the date, through `IBudgetingService`). Licensing: a Trial-type licence with a `trial_days` limit runs that many days from its first installation on this database and is read-only after (`TrialPeriod`, applied on load and install - reinstalling does not restart it); `ILicenceUsageService` counts each limit across all companies and the licence status shows it (flagging 90% and over); the key generator asks for the passphrase (hidden) instead of needing it on the command line and refuses to write a plaintext key unless `--plaintext`; licence certificates carry a QR code of `O4A-LIC|licence|customer|valid to|token fingerprint` (QRCoder, as the reporting engine). No migration. |
| **4.1 / 3.1** Tab line transfer and merge, serial / USB devices, seed charts of accounts (sixth audit #12j, #12k, #12l) | ✅ | Restaurant: a quantity of a line moves to another open tab (`IRestaurantService.TransferLineAsync`; `TableOrder.TakeFrom` / `TableOrderLine.MoveOut` - its share of the tax and its modifiers go with it, an item already fired is not fired again) and tabs merge (`MergeOrdersAsync`: everything unbilled moves; a tab left with nothing billed is cancelled and its table freed); desktop order steps Move line to tab / Merge into tab. Devices: `DeviceConnection.Serial` (COM port, or a USB device appearing as one - port, baud, parity, data / stop bits, handshake, scale request) for receipt and label printers (the drawer opens through the printer), scales and pole displays (`SerialRawPrinter`, `SerialScale`, `SerialCustomerDisplay`, System.IO.Ports); a missing port gives no weight / a printer message / a dark display, never a failed sale. Seed charts of accounts per industry in `docs/coa/{core,retail,municipal,housing,school,construction}.csv` (about 240 accounts: code, name, type, control type, report category, budget vote), embedded and merged by `ChartOfAccounts` into the Industry Setup Wizard's templates - the templates' own accounts keep their names and control types and take the chart's report category and budget flag; accounts are created with them. No migration. |
| **15 / 11** Operations console localised; meter readings partitioned by year (sixth audit #12m, #12n - list complete) | ✅ | The Operations console shows its titles, descriptions, field labels, list titles, buttons and list column headers through the company's text catalogue (`TextConverter` binding converter, `{loc:T}` for fixed text, column headers translated as they are generated while the tables keep their English column names; the window follows right-to-left cultures); the Portuguese catalogue gains the module names, the common field labels and column headers (186 strings) - operation titles not yet translated show in English. `mun.meter_reading` is partitioned by year of reading date like the audit log (primary key (id, reading_date), partitions 2020–2040 plus default, `mun.ensure_meter_reading_year`; xmin no longer mapped for it). ADR-0004 records why the line tables (journal, sale, payslip, budget, ownership lines) stay unpartitioned for now and when to revisit. Migration `PartitionMeterReadings` applied. |
| **12** Menus regrouped Sage style: Maintenance / Transactions / Enquiries / Utilities | ✅ | Every console action now sits under the menu for what the user is doing: set-up in **Maintenance**, documents and postings in **Transactions**, look-ups in **Enquiries**, period-end runs and housekeeping in **Utilities** (pure `MenuAreas.Classify` reads the action's title - set / add / create / save and named set-up nouns, show / find / statements / positions, year-end / revalue / import / archive / recurring runs - with explicit overrides; `Operation.Area` can pin one; today 182 transactions, 99 maintenance, 27 enquiries, 38 utilities). Each of the four menus keeps its own windows and then lists the modules with actions of that kind; choosing one opens the module's console showing just those actions, headed "Module - Area" (`OperationsWindow(…, area)`, `OperationsViewModel.Area` / `Heading`). The former Operations menu (each module's full console) moves under View → All Operations by Module. Portuguese labels for the areas added. No migration. |
| **4.15.1** Net pay split by percentage (seventh audit #16) | ✅ | A pay split is a fixed amount or a percentage of net pay (`EmployeePaymentSplit.Percent`, `AmountFrom(net)`; "60% to bank A, 40% to mobile money"); the bank-file writer and the split-pay report use it, and `EmployeePaymentSplit.Problem` refuses neither-or-both and an employee's percentages above 100. Desktop: HR & Attendance → Split net pay (set, change or remove; list of splits). Migration `AddPaymentSplitPercent` applied. |
| **11.5 / 4.3 / 4.20** Desktop licence enforcement; lay-bye output VAT; council licence fee deferral (seventh audit #1, #4) | ✅ | The desktop no longer trusts the development key or installs the development licence in a shipped install: it reads the vendor keys and `Licensing:Enforce` from appsettings and only a development install (appsettings.Development.json beside the app, or `DOTNET_ENVIRONMENT`/`ONE4ALL_ENVIRONMENT=Development`) falls back to the dev licence. The shell re-reads the licence every hour, refreshes the status bar, and from seven days before expiry (or while in grace/read-only/unusable) shows a once-a-day reminder (`LicenceReminder`). The area menus hide modules the licence or company does not enable. A lay-bye can carry a tax code; on completion the tax-inclusive total is split into revenue and output tax (`LaybyPlan.TaxIn`). A council licence can name a deferred-income account: the fee (on issue and each renewal) is held there and a `DeferredIncomeSchedule` earns it day-weighted over the licence cycle. Migration `AddLaybyTaxAndLicenceDeferral`. Tests: `LicenceReminderTests`, `LaybyVatAndLicenceDeferralTests`. |
| **5.2** Full permission grid with user overrides (seventh audit #2) | ✅ | The Roles & Permissions grid now has a column for every one of the 21 operation flags (authorise levels 1–5, price/discount/credit-limit overrides, view cost/margin, reprint, back-date as well as the common ones), each enabled only where the feature offers it, and groups features by module under collapsible headers. The picker lists users as well as roles: for a user each cell is three-state (tick grants, clear denies, indeterminate inherits from the roles), shaded green where the user ends up with the operation, with a tooltip saying why. `PermissionCells` (pure) maps cells to grant/deny overrides; `IPermissionService.RoleGrantsAsync`/`SetOverridesAsync` read the inherited grants and save overrides (an empty override is removed so the feature inherits again). Tests: `PermissionCellsTests`, `UserPermissionOverrideTests`. |
| **4.14** Weighbridge loads invoiced (seventh audit #3) | ✅ | `IWeighbridgeService.InvoiceTicketsAsync` bills a customer for every completed outbound load up to the invoice date not yet invoiced: net tonnes at a price per tonne to the sales account, each load's haulage to a haulage account (default sales), tax on top from a tax code, posted through the sales ledger in one unit of work with each ticket stamped with the invoice number (`WeighbridgeTicket.InvoiceNumber`) so no load is billed twice; loads still on the bridge are left out. Stock already leaves the stockpile at the second weigh. Desktop: Weighbridge › Invoice weighbridge loads lists completed un-invoiced loads. Migration `AddWeighbridgeInvoiceNumber`. Test: `WeighbridgeInvoiceTests`. |
| **4.9** Pro-rata settlement on property transfer (seventh audit #5a) | ✅ | `IOwnershipService.SettleTransferAsync` settles the period's fixed charges between seller and buyer after a transfer: for each unmetered service, `TransferApportionment` (pure) compares what the seller was billed for the period so far (the whole period, or the instalments to date for a cycle tariff) with the seller's share for the days they owned the stand; the difference is credited to the seller and invoiced to the buyer per revenue account (a shortfall the other way round), numbered from the clearance certificate so it is settled once. Metered services settle on the final reading. Desktop: Owners & transfers › Settle transfer. Tests: `TransferApportionmentTests`, `TransferSettlementTests`. |
| **4.9** Billing runs by route or cycle; bill delivery (seventh audit #5b) | ✅ | A billing run proposal can be limited to a meter-reading route (stands with a meter on the route) and/or a billing cycle (stands whose tariff for the service is on that cycle), recorded on the run (`BillingRun.RouteCode`/`Cycle`). `IBillingRunService.DespatchBillsAsync` emails a posted run's bills: each ratepayer billed in it with an email address is queued their statement through the despatch service (now able to target given customers under a batch title); ratepayers without email are counted for printing. Desktop: Billing run gains Route, Cycle and an Email bills step. Migration `AddBillingRunRouteAndCycle`. Test: `RouteBillingRunTests`. |
| **7** Workflow routing for leave, stocktakes, budgets and allocation runs (seventh audit #6) | ✅ | Four more document types run through the approval engine when a company defines a workflow for them (`WorkflowDocumentTypes.LeaveApplication`, `Stocktake`, `Budget`, `AllocationRun`). A leave application starts its workflow on applying (value: days) and HR cannot decide it directly; approval takes the leave. Posting a stocktake holds the variances (`StocktakeSession.PendingPostingDate`/`PendingAdjustmentAccountCode`, value: absolute variance at cost) until approved, then posts them; a rejection releases them for recounting. A recommended budget goes to the workflow (value: budget total); its approval approves the budget with the approver's comment as the resolution, a rejection returns it to the preparers. A proposed allocation run (value: stands) is approved by its workflow, sending offers open for the proposal's `OfferDays`; a rejection cancels it. Handlers: `LeaveWorkflowHandler`, `StocktakeWorkflowHandler`, `BudgetWorkflowHandler`, `AllocationRunWorkflowHandler`. Migrations `AddStocktakeApprovalHold`, `AddAllocationRunOfferDays`. Tests: `LeaveAndStocktakeWorkflowTests`, `BudgetAndAllocationWorkflowTests`. |
| **4.4** Hard stock reservation for sales orders (seventh audit #7) | ✅ | `ISalesOrderService.ReserveAsync` holds free stock in a warehouse for an order's lines still to despatch (`SalesOrderLine.ReservedQuantity`/`ReservedWarehouseId`), first come first served after other orders' reservations, reporting what was short; `ReleaseReservationsAsync` gives it back and cancelling an order releases it. `InventoryService.IssueAsync` now refuses (`Inv.Reserved`) any issue that would dip into stock reserved for other orders - a till sale, a direct issue, another order - while the reserving order's despatch (`IssueStockRequest.SalesOrderLineId`) takes its own reservation, which despatching reduces. Desktop: Sales › Reserve stock for a sales order (reserve/release, lists reservations). Migration `AddSalesOrderReservations`. Test: `StockReservationTests`. |
| **4.6** Virement bands and dimensions (seventh audit #8) | ✅ | A virement can move budget between cost centres (`FromCostCentre`/`ToCostCentre`) and to another department's active budget of the same year and type (`ToBudgetId`; the lines land in each budget with their cost centres). Delegated limits: `VirementBand` rows (`SetVirementBandsAsync`, validated by the pure `VirementBanding.Problem`) set the authorisation level an approver needs on Virement Approval for the band the amount falls in, one level more between budgets (`VirementBanding.RequiredLevel`, capped at 5); without bands any other user may approve as before. Desktop: Virements gains cost centres, a receiving budget and a Bands step. Migration `AddVirementBandsAndDimensions`. Tests: `VirementBandingTests`, `VirementBandTests`. |
| **4.6** Incremental budgets, reforecast and the cash budget (seventh audit #9) | ✅ | `IBudgetMethodService`: `BuildIncrementalAsync` fills a draft budget from a prior year's actual monthly movements or its active budget (same type and department), with an uplift %, for income and expense accounts (optionally by prefix), leaving accounts the draft already has alone; `ReforecastAsync` creates a new Reforecast version whose months up to the as-at period are the actuals and the rest the budget (the outturn); `CashBudgetAsync` turns a budget into a twelve-month cash budget from an opening balance, each line collected or paid by its timing (`CashTiming` by account prefix, e.g. sales 50/30/20 over three months; pure `CashBudget.Project`) with the cash falling after the year end reported. Desktop: Budget methods and forecasts. Tests: `CashBudgetTests`, `BudgetMethodTests`. |
| **4.15** Leave calendar and recall; bonus and gratuity provisions (seventh audit #10a) | ✅ | `IAttendanceService.LeaveCalendarAsync` lists who is on leave in a window (optionally one department); `CancelLeaveAsync` cancels leave not yet begun (all days back; `LeaveRecord.IsCancelled`, the dates freed) or curtails it on recall - it ends the day before the return and the days not taken (pro rata to calendar days unless given) go back to the balance (`EmployeeLeaveBalance.Restore`). `ILeaveService.RaisePayrollProvisionAsync` trues up the 13th-cheque bonus provision (earned through the bonus year, pro rata for joiners) or the gratuity provision (days' pay per year of service) to what active employees have earned (pure `PayrollProvisions`), posting the movement. Desktop: Leave calendar and recall; Bonus and gratuity provisions. Migration `AddLeaveCancellation`. Tests: `PayrollProvisionsTests`, `LeaveRecallAndProvisionTests`. |
| **4.15.3** Payroll tax credits (seventh audit #10b) | ✅ | `TaxCredit` (fixed a month, or a percentage of a pay element such as half the medical-aid deduction; optionally from an age; for everyone or only employees given it via `EmployeeTaxCredit`) comes off the tax a tax-table element works out - cumulative or not, before a split-currency split - scaled to weekly/fortnightly periods and never below nil (pure `TaxCredits`: `Eligible`, `Total`, `Apply`). `IPayrollSetupService.SaveTaxCreditAsync` / `SetEmployeeTaxCreditAsync`. Desktop: Tax credits. Migration `AddTaxCredits`. Tests: `TaxCreditsTests`, `TaxCreditPayrollTests`. |
| **7** Workflow routing for housing prices and ownership transfers (seventh audit #6, rest) | ✅ | Two more housing document types run through the approval engine when defined: a proposed stand pricing rule (`StandPrice`, reference `PR-<id>`, value the margin %) waits for its workflow - direct approval is refused while it runs and the workflow's approval puts the price into force - and a submitted ownership transfer (`OwnershipTransfer`, the application number, value the consideration) is approved for completion by its workflow in place of the two approving officers (`TransferApplication.ApproveThroughWorkflow`), a rejection rejecting it. Handlers: `StandPriceWorkflowHandler`, `OwnershipTransferWorkflowHandler`. Test: `HousingApprovalWorkflowTests`. |
| **4.4** Customer debit notes; hand-keyed cash-forecast flows; journal import from Excel (seventh audit #14, part) | ✅ | `ISalesLedgerService.PostDebitNoteAsync` posts a further charge against a customer's existing invoice like an invoice, kept as a `DebitNote` open item (document type `SalesDebitNote`, narrative naming the original); an invoice not on the customer's account is refused. `ICashForecastService.AddEntryAsync`/`RemoveEntryAsync` key flows into the forecast by hand (`CashForecastEntry`, once or monthly until a date; source `Manual`). Journal import (desktop and `/api/gl/journals/import`) also takes an Excel workbook, reading its first sheet. Desktop: Cash-flow forecast gains Add/Remove a flow. Migration `AddCashForecastEntries`. Test: `DebitNoteAndForecastEntryTests`. |
| **4.4** Payment runs by cheque or cash; cheque printing (seventh audit #14, rest) | ✅ | A payment run (direct or saved and approved) pays by EFT file (as before), by cheque or in cash (`PaymentRunRequest.Method`, kept on `PaymentRunBatch`). A cheque run writes a cheque to each supplier in the cheque register of the bank account kept on the run's ledger account, numbered on from the first cheque number keeping its width (000100, 000101…), for the net paid with the payment number as its document; cheque and cash runs make no bank file. `IDocumentLayoutService.ChequeAsync` lays a cheque out for its leaf - date, payee, the amount in figures and in words (`NumberToWords`) and the payment it settles; voided or cancelled cheques are not printed. Desktop: Payment run approval gains Pay by and first cheque; Banking › Print a cheque. Migration `AddPaymentRunMethods`. Test: `ChequeRunTests`. |
| **4.4** Dunning groups, customer types and emailed reminders (seventh audit #15, part) | ✅ | `DunningGroup` gives customers their own reminder timetable (a government group chased from 60 days, say) or exempts them (`IsExempt`); `Customer.DunningGroupCode` and `Customer.CustomerType` are set through `IDunningService.SetCustomerAsync`. Assessment uses each customer's group stages (exempt groups never appear). `SendAsync` emails every letter due through the notification service (template fields Name, Account, Level, Overdue, Letter), counts customers without an email address for posting, logs each reminder sent (`DunningLog`) and does not send the same level again within the resend interval. Desktop: Dunning reminders gains Send, Save group and Set customer. Migration `AddDunningGroups`. Test: `DunningGroupTests`. |
| **4.5 / 4.11** Promotion eligibility by customer type; bulk fee reminders (seventh audit #15 rest, #16) | ✅ | A promotion can be limited to customer types (`Promotion.CustomerTypes`, `IsFor`); `BasketRequest.CustomerType` carries the buyer's type, and the till now prices with the account customer's type and as a member when a loyalty member is on the sale (it always priced as a non-member before). `ISchoolBillingService.SendFeeRemindersAsync` emails every guardian or sponsor paying for an enrolled student who owes at least a minimum their statement through the statement despatch (those without email counted for printing). With municipal bills (billing run Email bills) and dunning (Send), bulk sending covers bills, reminders and fees. Desktop: promotions gain customer types; Sales & Schools › Send fee reminders. Migration `AddPromotionCustomerTypes`. Tests: `PromotionEligibilityTests`, `FeeReminderTests`. |
| **4.10.2 / 4.1** Allocation committee, stand swaps and substitution runs; split bill by % or amount (seventh audit #12, #13 part) | ✅ | `IAllocationService.ApproveByCommitteeAsync` approves a run on the allocation committee's resolution, kept on the run (`AllocationRun.CommitteeResolution`; a workflow approval keeps the approver's comment). `SwapAsync` exchanges the stands and frozen prices of two live offers in the same scheme whose stands are allocated and unsold, recorded as a `StandSwap` document (reference and reason); the move goes in three steps so the one-live-offer-per-stand index holds throughout. A proposal can name only some stands (`StandNumbers`) - a substitution run offering stands declined or lapsed to the next on the list, leaving out whoever turned that stand down (`ExclusionReason.DeclinedStand`). POS: `SplitBill.ByPercent` and `ByAmount` split every line by payers' percentages or amounts (the last absorbing rounding), with By % / By amount in the split-bill window. Migration `AddAllocationCommitteeAndSwaps`. Tests: `AllocationCommitteeTests`, `SplitBillByShareTests`. |
| **4.2** Preparation-station routing (seventh audit #13, part) | ✅ | A menu item names the preparation station it is made at (`MenuItem.PrepStation`: grill, bar, pastry…). Firing an order or a course gives each station its own kitchen ticket for its lines (`KitchenTicket.Station`), numbered by station when there is more than one (KT-9-Bar, KT-9-Grill, KT-9-KITCHEN); `IRestaurantService.StationQueueAsync` is a station's display - its tickets not yet served, oldest first, with their lines. Desktop: Menu items gains Prep station and Station queue steps. Migration `AddPrepStations`. Test: `PrepStationTests`. |
| **4.1** Cashier switch: hand the till over mid-shift (seventh audit #13, part) | ✅ | `IPosService.HandOverAsync` hands a till from one cashier to the next in one unit: the outgoing session is counted (per currency when given), cashed up - its over/short posted - and closed, and a session opens on the same terminal in the incoming cashier's name with the counted cash as its float, so each cashier answers for their own drawer (sales are accounted per session). The till window gains Hand over: the drawer count and accounts as for closing, the incoming cashier signs in with name and PIN in the supervisor boxes, and the till carries on in their name. Test: `CashierHandoverTests`. |
| **9** Common report filters by column (seventh audit #11, part) | ✅ | Any report can be narrowed by its columns - branch, cost centre, currency, department or any other - without each report having to support them: filters travel as reserved `Filter.<Column>` parameters (a report never reads them, so they cannot clash with its own parameters) and `ReportRunner` keeps the rows every filter on a column the report has allows (case-insensitive; a filter on a missing column is ignored), before sensitive fields are stripped - for viewing, printing, exporting and bursting, and through the API. The report viewer gains a "Filter rows by column" box (Branch=HQ; Currency=USD). Pure `ReportRowFilters` (`Parse`, `Apply`). Test: `ReportRowFiltersTests`. |
| **13.1** Amounts in words in Portuguese (seventh audit #19, part) | ✅ | `NumberToWordsPortuguese` (pure) writes numbers and amounts in European Portuguese as a cheque reads - "cento e quinze meticais e cinco centavos", "mil e duzentos", "dois milhões de meticais" - with singular/plural units and the "de" after a round million. Report expressions gain `NumberToWordsPt` and `AmountToWordsPt(value, majorOne, majorMany, minorOne, minorMany)` beside the English functions, and a company whose locale is Portuguese prints its cheques in Portuguese (meticais, kwanzas, dólares). Test: `NumberToWordsPortugueseTests`. |
| **4.3** Disassembly costed by relative sales value (seventh audit #20) | ✅ | `DisassemblyRequest.BySalesValue` splits the finished goods' cost over the components recovered by their selling prices on the active default price list times the quantity recovered (the joint-product method - a carcass into cuts), instead of by their cost; refused with no default list or when no component recovered has a price. Desktop: Disassemble finished goods gains "Split by sales value". Test: `DisassemblyBySalesValueTests`. |
| **4.9** Service-desk SLA escalation job (seventh audit #20) | ✅ | Scheduled task `servicedesk.escalate` (Municipality module) runs `IServiceDeskService.EscalateBreachesAsync`: every open fault ticket past its resolution deadline (the category's hours scaled by priority) is escalated once - one priority step up (Critical stays), a note in its history, `ServiceTicket.EscalatedAt` stamped - and its officer is notified. Migration `AddTicketEscalation`. Test: `SlaEscalationTests`. |
| **5.4** Segregation-of-duties rules table (seventh audit #20) | ✅ | The SoD rules are now maintained, not hard-coded: `SodRuleSetting` rows (`sec.sod_rule`) add new conflicts or, by a built-in rule's code, re-tune it or switch it off (inactive); `SegregationOfDutiesCatalogue.Effective` (pure) merges them with the built-ins. `IPermissionService.SodRulesAsync`/`SaveSodRuleAsync` (permissions checked against the catalogue, operations each side offers, the two sides different) and every violation check - control reports, role assignment warnings, housing reports - use the rules in force. Desktop: Approvals › Segregation-of-duties rules. Migration `AddSodRulesTable`. Tests: `SodRulesTableTests`, `SodRulesTableDbTests`. |
| **4.5** Commission on gross margin; sales reps maintained on the desktop (seventh audit #20) | ✅ | A sales rep can earn on `CommissionBasis.Margin`: revenue invoiced to their customers (net of tax, less credit notes) less the cost of the goods despatched to them in the period (their sales orders' deliveries, reversed issues left out). The Sales commissions operation gains Save rep (code, name, basis, rate) and Assign customer - reps could only be seeded before. Test: `MarginCommissionTests`. |
| **4.3** Purchase price variance on the supplier scorecard (seventh audit #20) | ✅ | Each order line matched to a supplier invoice is recorded (`InvoicedOrderLine`: quantity, order price, invoice price, invoice date); the scorecard reports each supplier's price variance for invoices matched in the period - the amount invoiced above (or below) the order prices and the percentage of the matched value - on the desktop, the PAYBSCORECARD report and the API. Migration `AddInvoicedOrderLines`. Test: `ScorecardPriceVarianceTests`. |
| **4.1 / RCP-6** One-click currency switching for cashiers and tellers | ✅ | The till's small "Pay in" dropdown is replaced by a row of currency buttons - one per currency the till takes today, the selected one highlighted, each one's tooltip giving its rate and the balance due in it - with "Paying in USD" shown above and F9 cycling through the currencies; the next cash, card or mobile tender is taken in the chosen currency. The teller's Take a payment gains a "Currency paid in" dropdown listing the company's currencies (base first, then those with exchange rates), and a foreign-currency payment converts at the day's rate. Operation fields can now load their choices from the database (`OperationField.ChoicesFrom`, `CurrencyPick`). |
| **RCP-3** Teller window with live payer search | ✅ | Transactions › Teller - Take a Payment opens a teller screen: type an account code or name, a stand number or a student's name or admission number and the matching payers appear as you type (`IPayerSearchService`: the account, the ratepayer of the stand, or whoever pays the student's fees, each with what it owes); pick one to see the balance in base and in the chosen currency; the currency handed over is one click on a row of currency buttons (rate and balance in the tooltip; F9 cycles); Full balance fills the amount; CASH, CARD, MOBILE or CHEQUE posts the receipt, allocated oldest first, against the teller's open session. Test: `PayerSearchTests`. |
| **HR-CLK** Time-clock punch import | ✅ | People › Import time-clock punches reads the clock's export (CSV or Excel; badge,date time per line, or date and time in separate columns): each employee's first punch is the clock-in and the last the clock-out (`ClockPunches`), recorded as present, late against the rostered shift past its grace, with the hours beyond the shift as overtime at the chosen rate awaiting approval (`IAttendanceService.ImportClockAsync`, all or nothing). Days already recorded are left alone; unknown badges and unreadable lines are reported. Tests: `ClockPunchesTests`, `ClockImportTests`. |
| **HSE-RT** Rent tariffs | ✅ | Housing › Rent tariffs and review: a tariff sets the monthly rent for a kind of unit from the first of a month, optionally only in one suburb (`RentalUnit.Suburb`) and for one income category (`Tenancy.IncomeCategory`); the most specific tariff in force wins (`RentTariffs`, pure). Apply tariffs moves every unit's asking rent and every running tenancy whose tariff differs to it from that month (`IRentalHousingService.ApplyRentTariffsAsync`, rerun-safe); the percentage review is on the same screen. `hse.rent_tariff`, migration `AddRentTariffs`. Tests: `RentTariffsTests`, `RentTariffTests`. |
| **HSE-TEN** Tenure types | ✅ | Instalment agreements now record the tenure they buy (`InstalmentTenure`: instalment sale, rent-to-buy, lease with option to purchase), chosen when signing on the desktop. The agreement register gains rent-to-buy, lease with option, site-and-service, lodger permit and long lease types. H45 debtors age analysis groups by tenure type and scheme; H96 delivery return names the tenure. Migration `AddInstalmentTenure`. Test: `HousingFinanceReportsTests`. |
| **HSE-QR** Qualification rules | ✅ | Housing › Waiting-list policy and qualification rules sets the policy (minimum age, years resident, overall income ceiling, score weights) and the further configurable rules: first-time buyers only (never held a stand or owned property on the municipal register), refusing those whose earlier instalment agreement was repossessed or cancelled, a rates clearance (no overdue charges on any of the household's accounts), and an income ceiling per category (`WaitingListIncomeCeiling`). Registration checks every rule for the applicant and the spouse, reporting all failures (`WaitingListRules.Problems(QualificationRules, QualificationFacts)`, pure). Migration `AddQualificationRules`. Tests: `QualificationRulesTests`, `QualificationRulesDbTests`. |
| **BUD-CCY** Budget header currency and rate | ✅ | Budgeting › Set a budget's currency and rate: an unsealed budget is prepared in a currency at a budget rate held on its header (`Budget.Currency`, `Budget.BudgetRate`, `IBudgetingService.SetBudgetCurrencyAsync`). Its lines are planned in that currency; a new rate re-states their base equivalents, lines in another currency and driver-based lines keep theirs, and the FX variance measures against the budget rate. The base currency clears it; a sealed budget is refused. Migration `AddBudgetHeaderCurrency`. Tests: `BudgetCurrencyTests`, `BudgetCurrencyDbTests`. |
| **HSE-COOP** Cooperative accounts | ✅ | Housing › Housing cooperatives gains Account (links the cooperative's own receivables account), Charge (invoices it), Contribution (an active member's payment receipted on the cooperative's account and kept against the member, `CooperativeContribution`, all or nothing) and Statement (opening balance, charges and receipts with the running balance, closing balance and each member's contributions; `ICooperativeService.StatementAsync`). H86 shows contributions and the balance owed. Migration `AddCooperativeAccounts`. Test: `CooperativeAccountTests`. |
| **SYS-PRN** Printers per workstation | ✅ | System Manager › Printers per workstation sets the printer, tray and copies each computer uses for reports, documents, receipts, cheques, labels and payslips (`WorkstationPrinter`, `IWorkstationPrinterService`); workstation * is the default for every computer without its own, and the printer is chosen from those this computer can reach. The report viewer's print dialog opens on the workstation's report printer, tray and copies (a printer it cannot reach is ignored); the report's own tray and duplex preferences still apply on top. Migration `AddWorkstationPrinters`. Test: `WorkstationPrinterTests`. |
| **LIC-CERT** Licence certificate PDF | ✅ | The licence generator's `certificate` command now writes the certificate as an A4 PDF by default (`LicenceDesk.CertificatePdf`, `CertificateLayout`): customer, licence, edition and term, each module with its end date, the activation string, the verification code, any revocation, and the verification QR code drawn as vector squares. `--out cert.html` still writes the HTML version. Re-binding a licence to a new machine stays the `reissue` command. Test: `LicenceDeskTests`. |
| **IMP-COV** Import coverage: accounts, warehouses, rental units | ✅ | The master data import gains the chart of accounts (code, name, type, optional category and manual / budget flags), warehouses (code, name) and council rental units (code, description, type, rooms, rent, rent account, optional vacant date, suburb and occupancy limit; no vacant date means let) - each with the same preview, all-or-nothing commit, duplicate and existing-code handling (`IVerticalImportService.ImportAccountsAsync` / `ImportWarehousesAsync` / `ImportRentalUnitsAsync`). Test: `MasterDataImportCoverageTests`. |
| **IND-HOSP** Hospitality industry setup | ✅ | The Industry Setup Wizard gains Hospitality (bars, restaurants, lodges): a seed chart (`docs/coa/hospitality.csv`: waiter floats, tips clearing, food / beverage / liquor / linen stock, tips and service charge payable, function deposits, open tabs clearing, food / beverage / liquor sales, accommodation, functions, service and cover charges, cost of food / beverages / liquor, spillage, staff meals, comps, laundry, kitchen fuel and liquor licences), Kitchen / Bar / Cellar stores, Waiter and Restaurant Manager roles, bar and restaurant tills, item categories, featured POS and stock reports, and demo suppliers and items. Tests: `ChartOfAccountsTests`, `IndustryTemplatesTests`, `IndustryReportSetTests` (every vertical). |
| **IND-SEED** Demonstration data for every industry | ✅ | Every industry template now brings demonstration data when the wizard's demo option is ticked: municipal ratepayers, a chemicals supplier and a water meter item; a housing beneficiary and surveyors; a school guardian, uniform supplier and uniform item; a contract client, subcontractor, materials supplier and cement item (alongside the existing retail and hospitality data). Codes never clash across industries. Tests: `IndustryTemplatesTests`, `IndustrySetupTests`. |
| **IND-LAYOUT** Industry document layouts in the setup wizard | ✅ | The Industry Setup Wizard now seeds document layouts: each industry names the receipts it prints and on what paper (`VerticalExtras.DocumentLayouts`), and the company gets its own copy of the shipped layout on that paper - the official receipt on an 80 mm roll for retail and hospitality (narrow margins), on A5 for councils and schools, the banking slip on A5, and kitchen order tickets on an 80 mm roll for hospitality. A company copy already there is kept; the setup result counts the layouts. Tests: `IndustrySetupTests`, `IndustryReportSetTests`. |
| **BUD-HOLDER** Monthly budget-holder statements | ✅ | Budgeting › Budget-holder statements: each department's active budget against actuals to the period of the date - budget and actual to date, the variance and the share of the year's budget used per account, with votes past the company's utilisation thresholds called out (`IBudgetNotificationService.HolderStatementAsync`). Send emails every department's budget holder with an address (`HolderStatementsAsync`); the scheduled budget notices send them on the first of each month for the month just ended. Test: `BudgetHolderStatementTests`. |
| **POS-FIND** Till product finder filters | ✅ | The till's Filters… bar finds items by brand, supplier, price band, in stock only, on a promotion in force and sold this week, within the chosen category and its sub-categories (`IPosCatalogueService.FindAsync` with `PosFinderFilter`; brands and suppliers offered from `FinderChoicesAsync`). Offline, the category and price band still filter the tiles on hand. Test: `PosFinderTests`. |
| **POS-OFFPROMO** Promotions at the till while offline | ✅ | The till's sealed offline snapshot now carries the promotions running then or later and the terminal's branch, and an offline sale is priced by the same rules as online: dates, branch, happy-hour windows (across midnight too), members, coupons and category promotions (`PromotionPricing` / `PromotionDefinition`, pure, now also used by `IPromotionService.PriceBasketAsync`; `DefinitionsAsync` fills the snapshot). Tests: `PromotionPricingTests` (including the snapshot round trip), the existing promotion tests. |
| **RPT-COMMON** Common report options: unposted journals and reporting currency | ✅ | The trial balance, income statement and balance sheet take the brief's common parameters: Include unposted (draft and deferred journals counted with the posted ones, a preview of what posting them would do) and Reporting currency (base amounts translated at the spot rate on the report date; the base currency when no rate is held) - `LedgerReportOptions` on `IGeneralLedgerReports`, read by the ledger report provider. Test: `LedgerReportOptionsTests`. |
| **RPT-USERVER** Per-user report versions | ✅ | Reports now resolve user version → company version → system version (brief §13.2): the report runner and the financial renderer pick the signed-in user's own version first. The designer's "Save as my own version" keeps a user's layout for that user alone (`IReportDefinitionService.SaveUserVersionAsync`, with version history); `RemoveUserVersionAsync` returns them to the company's. Test: `UserReportVersionTests`. |
| **RPT-DATA** Designer data side: field discovery and query builder | ✅ | The banded designer's Data source… dialog sets a report's source - a SQL SELECT filtered by @CompanyId, a reporting view or function - and Discover fields reads its columns and types without fetching a row (`SELECT … LIMIT 0` under the same safety checks, read-only, `IReportFieldDiscovery` on the SQL provider). Build query from view writes the SELECT from a rpt view, chosen columns and an order (`ReportQueryBuilder`, pure, identifiers checked). Tests: `ReportQueryBuilderTests`, `SqlReportSourceTests`. |
| **POS-OFFTAB** Tabs while the till is offline | ✅ | Parking a sale (a tab under a name, table or customer) works while the till is offline: the ticket is sealed on this computer (`ILocalParkedSaleStore` / `FileLocalParkedSaleStore`, the same protection as the offline catalogue), listed beside the server's parked sales marked "(this till)", and resumed here once; the server's list still loads whenever the connection is there. Test: `LocalEncryptedStoreTests`. |
| **REL-UPDATE** Installer and auto-update channel | ✅ | `installer/build.ps1` publishes the desktop app (self-contained win-x64) and the server and compiles `installer/One4All.iss` (Inno Setup 6: installs over the previous version, keeps appsettings.json) when Inno Setup is installed. Releases go on an update channel as a manifest signed with the vendor key (`UpdateManifest` / `UpdateManifestCodec`, UP1 tokens, Ed25519 like licences; the generator's `update` command hashes the installer and signs it). Help › Check for Updates reads the channel (`Updates:Channel`, a URL or share path; `IUpdateService`), offers a newer or required release, downloads the installer, refuses it unless its SHA-256 matches the signed one, then runs it and closes. Tests: `UpdateManifestTests`, `UpdateServiceTests`. The .iss itself is not compiled in this environment (no Inno Setup). |
| **PERF-PACK** Performance test pack and word-by-word item search | ✅ | `PerformanceTests` (run with ONE4ALL_PERF=1; skipped otherwise) checks the brief's targets on real volumes: item lookup on 100,000 SKUs (median 59 ms against the 100 ms target) and a 100,000-row report (rows fetched and laid out in about 1 s against the 5 s first-page target; the full PDF of some 2,000 pages takes 10–20 s, held under 30 s - QuestPDF page writing is the remaining cost). Item search now needs every typed word to match the code, description or brand ("sugar pack 9"), each word served by the trigram indexes. |
| **DB-PAGE** Audit trail paged in the database | ✅ | The audit query takes a keyset cursor (`AuditQuery.BeforeId`: entries older than the last one seen, newest first), so a page deep in a long log is a primary-key seek in SQL rather than an offset; `GET /api/audit` accepts `beforeId` and returns the next cursor in `X-Next-Before-Id`. Other API lists keep the generic skip/take paging. Test: `AuditPagingTests`. |
| **RPT-STREAM** Report viewer: progressive preview and cancel | ✅ | A long report shows its first pages straight away - the headers and the first 300 rows (`RenderModel.Head`, without the report-footer totals that belong to the whole report) - and the rest replaces it once laid out in the background, so the viewer answers at once on a 100,000-row report. A Cancel button appears while a report runs and stops it (the cancellation reaches the audited runner and the data fetch). SQL sources stay capped at 50,000 rows in memory. Test: `ReportHeadTests`. |
| **PAY-EXC** Payroll pre-commit exception report, complete | ✅ | The payroll draft's exception report now carries every check the brief lists, beside negative / zero net pay, large movements and suspended deductions: no bank account or wallet, bank accounts / wallets / national IDs shared between employees, no tax number, terminated employees still in the run, contracts expired, basic salary below or above the grade's scale in force, negative leave balances, bank details changed since the previous run (from the audit trail) and employees paid in the previous run missing from this one (`PayrollRunChecks`, pure). The approver sees them with the totals. Tests: `PayrollRunChecksTests`, `PayrollApprovalTests`. |
| **WF-ATTR** Approval rules by document attributes | ✅ | Workflow stage conditions now see each document's attributes (brief §4.3 "rules by value band, currency, department, cost centre, item category, project, requester"): every document carries its Requester; requisitions add Currency, Supplier and the main item Category; purchase orders Currency, Supplier and Project (the job contract); journals Currency, Department, CostCentre and Project; payment runs Currency; credit notes Currency, Customer and Reason; budgets Department; leave Department, CostCentre and LeaveType; stocktakes Warehouse (`WorkflowGate.StartIfDefinedAsync` / `AttributesAsync`). They are kept on the instance and in each decision's snapshot, so a stage such as `Category = TOOLS` or `Currency != USD` applies only where it should. Test: `WorkflowAttributeRoutingTests`. |
| **PAY-SETTLE** Payroll Pay and Close stages | ✅ | A committed run now goes on to Pay and Close (brief §4.15.6): People › Pay and close a payroll run posts the run's net pay in one journal, debiting the net pay control the run credited and crediting the account for each pay method (bank transfers, cash, mobile money) with what its employees are paid (`IPayrollSettlementService.PayAsync`); Close closes it only once the run's own journals leave nothing on the net pay control (`CloseAsync`), and Stage shows paid / closed / outstanding. The run register keeps the net pay account, payment journal and dates (`PayrollRunRecord.NetPayAccountCode/PaidOn/PaymentJournalNumber/ClosedOn`, migration `AddPayrollRunSettlement`). Reversed runs cannot be paid; nothing is paid or closed twice. Multi-currency pay settlement is not covered. Test: `PayrollSettlementTests`. |
| **HSE-DEFAULT** Instalment default notices and penalty interest | ✅ | Housing › Instalment arrears and default notices lists the active agreements in arrears (the buyer's balance less principal not yet billed) with how long the oldest unpaid instalment has been due and the next notice due: a reminder from the first unpaid instalment, a letter of demand after 30 days, a notice of breach after 60 and a notice of cancellation after 90 once the breach notice has stood 30 days - never skipping a stage, each issued only after the last one is served (`InstalmentDefault`, pure). Each notice is a dated, numbered document kept with its proof of service (when, how, by whom; `InstalmentNotice`). An agreement's annual penalty rate (`InstalmentAgreement.PenaltyRatePercent`) charges a month's penalty interest on its arrears once a month (`IInstalmentDefaultService.ChargePenaltyInterestAsync`). Migration `AddInstalmentDefault`. Escalation of instalments is still open. Tests: `InstalmentDefaultTests` (unit and database). |
| **BUD-ISO** Department isolation in budget preparation | ✅ | A user tied to departments - by their employee record or a department data scope - now prepares only those departments' budgets (brief §4.18 "Departments cannot see or edit each other's numbers unless granted"): revising figures and submitting another department's budget, or the company-wide one, is refused (`Budget.OtherDepartment`), and the desktop's budgets-in-preparation list shows only their departments. `Budget.ViewAllDepartments` (Finance) or super administration reaches every department; a user tied to no department is not confined (`BudgetDepartmentAccess`, pure; `IBudgetPreparationService.DepartmentsForAsync`). Review steps stay Finance's. Tests: `BudgetDepartmentAccessTests`, `BudgetDepartmentIsolationTests`. |
| **PAY-TAXYEAR** Configurable tax year | ✅ | People › Set the tax year sets the month the tax year starts in (`Company.TaxYearStartMonth`, default January; e.g. 3 for a March–February year - brief §4.15.3 "tax-year straddling a fiscal year"). Year-to-date taxable pay and tax deducted are gathered from that start, cumulative tax numbers its periods from it, and YTD openings are matched to the tax year by its starting calendar year (`PayPeriods.TaxYearStart` / `TaxYear` / `Number(…, startMonth)`). A January year behaves exactly as before. Migration `AddTaxYearStartMonth`. Test: `TaxYearTests`. |
| **AR-CREDITPOL** Credit-limit check policy per customer | ✅ | Each customer has a credit-limit policy (brief §4.5 "credit limit & credit-limit check policy"; `Customer.CreditLimitPolicy`): Block (the default - refused unless a supervisor who may override credit limits lets it through), Warn (the invoice posts, the result is flagged `OverCreditLimit` and the breach is logged in the override register as a warning) or Ignore (not checked). Set from Customers › Credit limits › Policy. Migration `AddCreditLimitPolicy`. Test: `CreditLimitPolicyTests`. |
| **PAY-SLIP** Fuller payslip content | ✅ | The payslip (printed, emailed as a PDF, and in self-service) now shows, beneath earnings, deductions and net pay: how the net pay was paid (bank and the last four digits of the account, the wallet, or cash), the employer's contributions, taxable benefits as memo items, the period's taxable pay and tax, the tax year to date (gross, taxable, tax, net - from the company's tax-year start), leave balances and loan balances (brief §4.15.7). Each section prints only when it has something to show (`PayslipDocument`, `PayslipFormatter`; built by `StoredPayslips`). Laying the payslip out in the report designer is still open. Test: `PayslipSectionsTests`. |
| **HSE-REGISTER** Stand, parcel and scheme register details | ✅ | Housing › Stand, parcel and scheme details completes the registers the brief lists (§4.10.1–4.10.2): a stand's street address, ward / suburb / zone, intended use, zoning, servicing phase and service availability (water, sewer, road, electricity, storm water), with a dated remarks history added to, never edited (`StandRemark`); a parcel's locality, ward, land use, tenure (freehold / state land / leasehold / communal / customary / trust), improvements and occupation (vacant / occupied / informally settled / under dispute); a scheme's funding source and start / completion dates (`IHousingRegisterDetailsService`). Survey steps and valuations were already kept. Migration `AddHousingRegisterDetails`. Test: `HousingRegisterDetailsTests`. |
| **HSE-INTAKE** Applicant intake, document verification and the duplicate queue | ✅ | Housing › Applicant intake and duplicate queue (brief §4.10.3): an applicant's gender, physical and postal addresses, phone and employer; supporting documents (ID copy, proof of residence, payslip, affidavit) lodged and verified one by one with the officer's name, the applicant verified once all are (`ApplicantDocument`); a deceased register (`DeceasedPerson`). Scan queues suspected duplicates across the waiting list and stand holders - same ID, similar name and birth date, ID one character apart, and now the same phone under different IDs - and applicants on the deceased register, each once (`DuplicateCase`); an officer merges (the later registration removed, the earlier keeps its seniority), rejects or clears each with a reason (`IApplicantIntakeService`). Migration `AddApplicantIntake`. Tests: `ApplicantIntakeTests`, `DuplicatePhoneTests`. |
| **POS-PROFILE** Terminal profiles and favourites per cashier | ✅ | A terminal has a profile (general, supermarket, bar, butchery, weighbridge kiosk, restaurant; `PosTerminal.Profile`) shown on the till; bar and restaurant tills hide the Weigh button. Favourites can be pinned for one cashier as well as a till or every till, on pages (★ Favourites, ★ Favourites 2…) and in a colour: a cashier sees their own first, then the till's, then every till's, each item once in its nearest pin's colour (`IPosCatalogueService.SetFavouriteAsync(…, userId, …, page, colour, …)` / `FavouriteTilesAsync`). Point of Sale › Till favourites sets both. Drag-to-arrange on the till is still open. Migration `AddTerminalProfilesAndCashierFavourites`. Test: `PosFavouritesByCashierTests`. |
| **PR-STDELEM** The standard pay elements, seeded | ✅ | "Add standard elements" now seeds the brief's 31 standard elements (§4.15.2): basic (pensionable, social-security, overtime- and leave-pay base), overtime at 1.5×, 2× and public holiday, housing, transport, acting (from the acted grade), responsibility, shift and night, standby and call-out, commission, bonus, 13th cheque, backpay, leave pay and encashment, gratuity, severance and notice pay; PAYE on the tax table, pension, social security, medical aid, union dues, salary advances, garnishee orders, funeral and savings schemes, staff purchases and housing rent (from their balances) and overpayment recovery. Amounts and rates are set per employee or entered as the period's variables. | PayrollSetupTests |
| **HR-MASTER** The rest of the employee master | ✅ | An employee now has a physical and a postal address, a phone, a job title, a notice period in days, a workers' compensation class and a photograph (JPEG, PNG or BMP up to 2 MB); beneficiaries (with shares that may not exceed 100%) and medical-aid dependants (with member numbers) are kept per employee (`hr.employee_dependant`). Employment types add fixed-term, seasonal, intern and consultant, all of which need a contract end date. Service `IEmployeeProfileService`; People op "Employee contacts, photo and dependants". | EmployeeProfileTests |
| **MUN-PARTY** Tenants, occupiers and managing agents | ✅ | A property keeps its tenants, occupiers and managing agents over time (`mun.property_party`: role, name, own account, phone, e-mail, address, from–to, copied on bills) and a correspondence address separate from the property's own. "Bill goes to" lists the ratepayer at the correspondence address and every current party copied on bills; the enquiry desk shows the parties and the correspondence address. `IOwnershipService.AddPartyAsync/EndPartyAsync/PartiesAsync/SetCorrespondenceAddressAsync/BillRecipientsAsync`; Municipal op "Property ownership" gains the steps. | PropertyPartyTests |
| **VOU-CHECK** Vouchers checked, supported and routed | ✅ | An accounting voucher can be checked by a second user (not the preparer, and the checker cannot also approve) and prints prepared / checked / approved signature blocks with the count of supporting documents. Supporting documents attach to a voucher by number (attachment target AccountingVoucher) and stay with it once posted. With an AccountingVoucher workflow defined, a prepared voucher waits in the approvals inbox (attributes Type, Party, CashAccount, Requester): direct approval is refused, inbox approval posts it, rejection cancels it. | VoucherCheckAndWorkflowTests |
| **INV-HELD** Supplier- and customer-owned stock | ✅ | Goods held for others are kept per owner, item and location outside our stock value (`inv.held_stock` with its movements): a supplier's consignment stock with the agreed price, or a customer's goods in our keeping. Return hands them back; Use (supplier-owned only) takes them into our stock at the agreed price crediting the consignment clearing account and raises the supplier's invoice through it, in one unit. `IHeldStockService`; Stock op "Supplier- and customer-owned stock". | HeldStockTests |
| **RST-KOTPRINT** Kitchen tickets printed per station | ✅ | Each preparation station (KITCHEN for dishes with none) maps to a Windows printer by name or a network printer by host and port, with copies (`rst.station_printer`). Firing an order or a course prints each station's ticket at its station - station and ticket in double size, where it goes, order, time and course, then each dish with its quantity - and reports per station whether it printed, failed or is display-only; reprints are marked REPRINT and counted on the ticket. Pure `KitchenTicketFormatter` (text and ESC/POS); `IKitchenPrintService`; Restaurant op "Kitchen station printers". | KitchenTicketFormatterTests, KitchenStationPrintTests |
| **MUN-ENQ2** Enquiry desk: tickets, readings and correspondence | ✅ | The consumer enquiry desk now also shows the complaints and service tickets logged against the property (category, status, what was reported), each meter's last twelve readings (actual or estimated, accepted or not), and the correspondence - messages sent to, or received from, the ratepayer's and the property parties' e-mail and phone - newest first. | PropertyEnquiryTests |
| **PR-COMMCHQ** Commission to payroll; cheque pay | ✅ | A sales rep can be linked to an employee; "To payroll" sends every linked rep's commission for a period to the pay period's COMMISSION element as a payroll variable (replacing an earlier send, so reruns are safe; reps who are not employees are left out). Cheque is a pay method: payslips say "Paid by cheque" and Pay and close takes the bank account cheques are drawn on. `ICommissionService.ToPayrollAsync`, `SalesRep.EmployeeId`, `PayMethod.Cheque`. | CommissionToPayrollTests |
| **REG-DETAIL** Item pictures and dimensions; stand GPS; meter details | ✅ | Items carry a long description, length / width / height in cm (the unit volume follows, for landed costs by volume), weight and a picture (JPEG, PNG, GIF or WebP up to 1 MB, kept apart in `inv.item_image`). Stands carry a suburb and a GPS position (both coordinates, range-checked); meters carry their type, size in mm and where they are on the stand. `IItemDetailsService`, `IPropertyRegisterService`; Stock op "Item picture, description and dimensions", Municipal op "Stand location and meter details". | RegisterDetailsTests |
| **AR-REMIT** Statement remittance slip; bursary expiry | ✅ | Customer statements end with a tear-off remittance slip (account, statement date, balance due, overdue, amount and date paid, the reference to quote); supplier statements do not. A bursary can be given its last day: fees billed after it get no bursary (`Student.BursaryValidUntil`, `BursaryInForce`), and "Expiring" lists bursaries ending within so many days for renewal (`ISchoolBursaryService.ExpiringAsync`). | StatementFormatterTests, SiblingDiscountRuleTests |
| **AP-PRIORITY** Payment runs by priority and to mobile money | ✅ | Suppliers carry a payment priority (1 paid first … 9 last; existing suppliers start at 5) and a mobile-money wallet, which changes only through the approved bank-detail change (a wallet-only change keeps the bank account). A proposal given the funds available (in the run's currency) pays by priority then supplier code until the funds run out, showing what is deferred; saved runs keep those amounts. A Mobile money run refuses a supplier without a wallet and writes a bulk payout file (header with count and total, a line per wallet). | PaymentRunPriorityTests |
| **HR-CASES** Grievances, property issued and medical records | ✅ | Grievances are lodged and followed (investigating, then resolved or withdrawn with the outcome; a closed one cannot reopen). Company property issued to staff is recorded with serial and value and its return logged; anything still out goes onto the leaver's termination clearance list. Medical records (fitness, illness, injury, disability) are seen and kept only by users with the Hr.Medical permission, their notes encrypted at rest. `IHrCasesService`; People ops "Grievances and property issued" and "Medical records (restricted)". | HrCasesTests |
| **SEC-BULK** Compare roles and change many at once | ✅ | The permission grid gains Compare - where two roles differ, permission by permission (only the first, only the second, both) - and Bulk change, which adds, removes or sets operations on a permission code or a pattern such as Payroll.* across several roles at once, audited per role; system roles are refused. Pure `RoleComparison` (Compare, Matching, Apply); `IUserAdminService.CompareRolesAsync/BulkGrantAsync`; System op "Compare roles or change many at once". | RoleComparisonTests, RoleBulkChangeTests |
| **INV-SEASON** Seasonal replenishment | ✅ | An item's replenishment policy can carry twelve monthly seasonal factors (January first); the moving-average usage forecast is multiplied by the factor for the month the lead time ends in, so the reorder point rises in season and falls out of it. Pure `SeasonalFactors` (TryParse, For, Format); Stock op "Set an item's replenishment policy" takes the factors. | SeasonalFactorsTests, ReplenishmentForecastTests |
| **PR-LEAVEADV** Leave pay in advance | ✅ | For leave of a type starting in a coming window, the leave days' pay (basic ÷ working days a month, per day) goes into this pay period as LEAVEPAY and comes back in the recovery period as the LEAVEADV deduction, when those days are paid in the ordinary way; reruns replace the figures. LEAVEADV ("Leave pay advance recovery") joins the seeded standard elements (now 32). `ILeavePolicyService.AdvanceLeavePayAsync`; People op "Leave policies" step "Advance leave pay". | LeaveRulesTests, PayrollSetupTests |
| **WB-CONCRETE** Concrete slump and strength tests | ✅ | Slump tests (mm, passing within the tolerance of the target) and 7- or 28-day cube strengths (MPa, passing at or above the specified strength) are recorded against a concrete batch run (not before it was made) and judged at once; a run's tests can be listed and the failures in a period followed up (`inv.concrete_test`). `IConcreteTestService`; Weighbridge op "Concrete slump and strength tests". | ConcreteTestRulesTests, AssemblyTests |
| **HSE-VOIDS** Void reasons, refurbishment jobs and planned maintenance | ✅ | A void rental unit records why it is empty and its refurbishment work order (a job contract number, checked); the voids list shows both, and letting the unit records the re-let date and clears them. A planned maintenance programme puts recurring tasks on units (every so many months, or once); "Planned due" lists what is due and marking one done raises the repair that carries its cost and moves it to its next date (`hse.planned_maintenance`). `IRentalMaintenanceService.SetVoidDetailsAsync/PlanAsync/DueAsync/MarkDoneAsync`; Housing op "Rental maintenance" gains the steps. | RentalVoidsAndPlannedMaintenanceTests |
| **BUD-CAPCF** Capital carry-forward; rental units under IAS 40 | ✅ | Unspent approved capital is proposed per capital vote (budget less spent less already carried) and carried into a later year's capital budget only by an approval act with its reference (`bud.capital_carry_forward`); carried lines sit outside the budget seal like virements. Rental units are carried as investment property (cost or fair value model) or PPE: designation posts them into their asset account, and fair-value units are remeasured with the change to profit or loss. `ICapitalCarryForwardService`, `IRentalPropertyAccountingService`; Housing op "Rental property accounting (IAS 40)". | CapitalCarryForwardTests, InvestmentPropertyTests |
| **RST-FLOOR** Floor plan and tab warnings | ✅ | Tables are placed on a floor-plan grid (across, down; one table per spot) with a shape (square, round, booth, bar seat). The plan shows every table with its open tab, the tab's total and minutes open; tab warnings (restaurant policy) flag tabs open longer than so many minutes or over a total. `IFloorPlanService`; Restaurant op "Floor plan and tab warnings". | FloorPlanTests |
| **POS-OFFTILL** Open a till offline; sync state | ✅ | A till can be opened while the terminal cannot reach the database (once it has a saved catalogue): it is queued under an offline key with its float and opening time, the sales rung up in it carry the key, and on reconnect the till opens centrally first (idempotently, at its offline time) and its sales post into it - a sale whose till has not synced waits and retries. The till header shows the sync state: offline, how many are queued, when it last synced, and a till opened offline. `IPosService.OpenTillOfflineAsync`, `PosOpenTillEnvelope`, `PosOpenTillOfflineHandler`, `TillSession.OfflineKey`. | PosOfflineSyncTests |
| **UI-SCALE** Scalable text | ✅ | Every desktop window scales its content to the user's text size - 90% to 200% in steps, by Ctrl + / Ctrl − / Ctrl 0 or View › Larger Text / Smaller Text / Normal Text Size - remembered on the computer for the Windows user. Pure `UiScale` (Larger, Smaller, Parse, Label); `UiScaling` hooks every window as it loads. | UiScaleTests, LocalizationTests |
| **LIC-ONLINE** Online activation interfaces | ✅ | The online activation contract is in place for the vendor's service to implement later: paths (api/v1/activate, api/v1/renew), request (activation request code, customer key, licence in use) and response (signed licence, optional revocation list, message), `IOnlineActivationClient` for sites and `IActivationAuthority` for the service. Sites get an HTTPS JSON client when Licensing:ActivationUrl is set (service errors and outages come back as refusals), otherwise an offline-only client explaining the file route; the Licence op gains Activate online / Renew online, which install what the service returns. | OnlineActivationClientTests |
| **POS-CARDLOGIN** Card swipe and fingerprint sign-in | ✅ | Cashiers and supervisors can identify at the till by swiping or scanning a cashier card into the name box (track 1 or 2 stripes and plain numbers are read; only the card's hash is kept, one card per user), or - with nothing typed - through a fingerprint reader (the `IFingerprintReader` device hook returns the matched enrolment, linked to one user). Every attempt is logged with its method. `IAuthenticationService.AuthenticateByCardAsync/AuthenticateByBiometricAsync`, `IUserAdminService.SetCashierCardAsync/SetBiometricIdAsync`, pure `CashierCards`; System op "Cashier card and fingerprint". | CashierCardsTests, CashierCardLoginTests |
| **AR-TENURE** Receivables control per tenure type | ✅ | A customer can post to a dedicated receivables control account instead of the company's main one (the lowest-numbered receivables control); each instalment tenure type (instalment sale, rent-to-buy, lease with option) can map to one, and a buyer goes onto it when their agreement is signed. A customer's control is set only before they have items and only for base-currency debtors, so revaluation and realised FX are unaffected. Invoices, receipts, credit notes, reversals and till account sales post to the customer's control, and the control reconciliation matches each control to its own customers. `IReceivableControlService`; Housing op "Receivables control per tenure type". | TenureControlAccountTests |
| **SEC-ROWSCOPE** Row scopes in the desktop | ✅ | The signed-in user's scope rules (branches, departments, warehouses, tills, jobs, cost centres, wards) now apply to every database context the desktop opens after sign-in, as the server already did per request - so payroll and HR show and process only the employees in the user's branches and departments. `RowScopeHolder` (host-wide scope applied by the context's constructor), `DesktopRowScope.LoadAsync` at sign-in. | DesktopRowScopeTests |
| **PR-MCPAY** Net pay settled in several currencies | ✅ | Paying a run, earnings paid in another currency (an element's paid-in currency) go out of that currency's account in the currency at the pay date's daily rate - capped at the employee's net, deductions coming off the base part first - and the rest by pay method in the base currency, in one journal that clears the net pay control. `IPayrollSettlementService.PayAsync(…, currencyAccounts)`, `PayrollPayment.ByCurrency`; the Pay step takes "EUR:1120; ZAR:1130". | PayrollSettlementTests |
| **POS-FAVDRAG** Drag to arrange favourites | ✅ | On a favourites tab of the till, dragging a tile onto another moves it there; the page is saved as the cashier's own arrangement at that till (their own pin for each item, in the new order, keeping the colour the tile showed in). Pure `FavouriteOrder.Move`; `IPosCatalogueService.ArrangeFavouritesAsync`. | FavouriteOrderTests, PosFavouritesByCashierTests |
| **HSE-ESCAL** Escalating instalments | ✅ | An instalment agreement can step its instalment up by a yearly percentage: zero and flat plans spread the same total over the rising instalments (principal and interest in the plan's ratio); a reducing-balance plan sizes the first instalment so the escalating payments' present value equals the amount financed, charging interest on the balance. Every plan still clears to the cent; no escalation gives the level plan. `InstalmentPlan.Build(…, escalationPercent)`, `InstalmentAgreement.EscalationPercent`; the Sign step takes the escalation. | InstalmentPlanTests |
