| **PR-PAYSLIPDOC** Designable payslip | ✅ | Report PR74 "Payslip (designable)" gives every payslip line in a period - employee, number, department, section, element, description, amount, with the slip's gross, deductions and net - for one employee or all, as the data source a company copies and lays out as its own payslip in the report designer. | PayrollSettlementTests, ReportCatalogueDocumentTests |
| **JOB-OPEN** Open a job contract; capture costs | ✅ | Contracts can now be opened from the desktop (number, name, customer, value, budgeted cost, retention %, revenue account) and manual costs captured to a contract's heads by source (purchases, stock, labour, plant, subcontract, overhead) - the existing WBS, BOQ, variation, certificate and subcontract operations hang off them. Contracts op "Open a job contract". | (UI over tested services) |
| **BUD-CAPCF-UI** Capital carry-forward in the desktop | ✅ | Budgeting op "Carry unspent capital forward": Propose lists each capital vote's approved budget, spend, amount already carried and what may still be carried; Approve carries VOTE:amount pairs into a later year's capital budget under the approval reference. | CapitalCarryForwardTests |
| **CRM-PIPE** Opportunity pipeline | ✅ | Opportunities move through the pipeline (lead, qualified, proposal, negotiation) with their probability and expected close date, and close as won or lost (lost needs the reason; a closed opportunity stays closed, with its close date). The CRM op "Opportunity pipeline" also shows the weighted forecast by stage, an opportunity's timeline, and converts it to a sales order (ITEM:qty[:price] lines). `IOpportunityService.MoveAsync`, `Opportunity.Close/ExpectedCloseOn/LostReason`. | OpportunityPipelineTests |
| **HR-ALERTS** HR expiry alerts | ✅ | What lapses for active employees within so many days - work permits, contract ends (fixed-term and the like), probation ends, filed documents and training certificates - listed soonest first, and sent as one in-app notification to every HR user (roles covering employee records, and administrators). A daily scheduled task (hr.expiry-alerts, payroll module) does it for 30 days ahead. `IHrAlertService`; People op "HR expiry alerts". | HrExpiryAlertTests |
| **RCP-3B** Cash office actions in the desktop | ✅ | The cash office's remaining actions are now in the desktop: pickups to the safe and petty cash payouts from the drawer, a supervisor verifying a closed session, reprinting a receipt (counted copies), confirming a cheque or transfer awaiting clearance; and on debtors - capturing a payment arrangement, recording a dishonour with an optional penalty, allocating money held in suspense, and refunding a credit balance. Receipting ops "Cash office drawer" and "Arrangements, dishonours and refunds". | (UI over tested services) |
| **SCH-SETUP** School setup and term billing | ✅ | A school can now be set up and billed from the desktop: fee structures per grade and term with compulsory and optional items (accounts checked), students admitted under their guardian's account, hostels and transport routes with capacity and fee per term, and students placed in them within capacity (a hostel makes them a boarder); then a term run bills every enrolled student of the grade (reruns skip those billed) or one student. `ISchoolSetupService`; Sales & Schools ops "Fee structures and term billing" and "Students, hostels and routes". | SchoolSetupTests |
| **POS-VOIDREFUND** Void and refund at the till | ✅ | At the till, a completed sale (its number typed or scanned into the scan box) can be voided - the sale and its stock reversed - with a supervisor's Pos.Void authority, or refunded in full in cash with a supervisor's Pos.Refund authority (the late-refund rule still applies). Till buttons "Void Sale" and "Refund Sale". | (UI over tested services) |
| **LIC-FEATURES** Licence feature flags enforced | ✅ | The licence's feature flags now gate what they name when the licence is enforced: MULTI_CURRENCY (posting in a foreign currency), OFFLINE_POS (the till selling offline), API_ACCESS (integration API keys), CONSOLIDATION (consolidated and translated trial balances) and HYPERINFLATION (IAS 29 restatement). A licence listing no features predates the flags and keeps them all. The generator issues each edition's default features (Standard: multi-currency, offline POS; Professional: + API access; Enterprise and the sector editions: all) unless --features is given. `LicenceFeatures`, `LicenceFeatureGate`. | LicenceFeaturesTests, LicenceDeskTests |

Cashier "Open or close my session" accepts the float or the cash counted either way and shows the breakdown; the POS till count (`CashCounts`) accepts note-by-note per currency ("50x10+20x5; ZAR 100x3"). Unit tests |

| **SEC-SCREEN** Fine-grained screen permissions | ✅ | Every desktop screen is gated by a `Module.Feature` of the permission catalogue, not only by its module: `Application.Security.ScreenPermissions` maps a console module + title to a feature (ordered rules, a default per module; `Operation.Permission` overrides) and says which flags running it needs - Print for a document, Approve / Authorise for approving and deciding, VoidReverse / Post for reversing and cancelling, View for an enquiry, otherwise any flag that changes data, always limited to what the feature offers so the grid can grant it. The console refuses runs, lists (View) and deletes (Delete) with a message naming the feature and code; the navigation leaves out screens whose feature the user has nothing on. The catalogue gains the features the screens needed (System.Workflow / Industry / Integration / Import / Printer / Attachment / Encryption, GeneralLedger.Tax / Accrual, Sales.PriceList / Commission / Layby / Contract, CashBook.Cheque / StandingOrder / Forecast / BankFileLayout / PettyCash / Teller / CashOffice, Inventory.Assembly / Price / Replenishment / Consignment, Pos.Loyalty / TillSetting / Restaurant / Kitchen / RestaurantSetup, Municipal.Facility / Prepaid, Payroll.Attendance / SelfService / Expense / HrRecord / Medical, School.AcademicYear / Wallet, and more); the System Administrator role picks up new features at start-up. `ScreenPermissionsTests` checks every screen (a snapshot in `console-screens.tsv`) maps to a catalogue feature and needs only flags it offers |
| **PAY-SLIP-DESIGN** Designable payslip PDF and the employee's own password | ✅ | The payslip PDF can now come from the company's own design in the report designer: `Company.PayslipLayoutCode` names the layout report (start from PR74 "Payslip (designable)"), the report runs for the employee and the period's pay dates (`IReportRunner.RenderPageImagesAsync` → `PdfReportRenderer.RenderImages`, QuestPDF pages as JPEGs) and its pages become the PDF through `PdfTextDocument.BuildFromImages`, protected with the same Standard Security Handler (AES-128) as the text payslip - so a designed payslip is still password-locked. `PayslipPasswordRule.EmployeeSet`: the employee sets their own password in self-service (`IEmployeeSelfService.SetPayslipPasswordAsync`, `POST /api/ess/payslip-password`) or HR sets it for them; `Employee.PayslipPassword` is stored encrypted. Desktop "Payslips: save or email" gains Payslip settings (rule, layout) and Set password. Migration `AddPayslipLayout`. Tests: `PdfTextDocumentTests` (picture pages sized from the JPEG, decrypt back), `PayslipDistributionTests` (employee password, designed layout) |
| **BILLING-BULK** Billing run posts bills through a batch path | ✅ | The 50k target is met: the posting engine gains `PostBatchAsync` (several journals as one unit of work - company, periods and accounts read once, the journal counter locked once and allocating every number, the period balances the batch touches read in one query and updated in memory, one save and one audit entry; refused with `Posting.BatchUnsupported` for what needs the single path: deferred, submitted, manual, adjustment-period, keyed, budget-overridden journals and fund control), the sales ledger gains `PostInvoicesAsync` (customers, exemption certificates, exempt tax codes and the control account read once per batch; refused for a credit-limit policy, a foreign currency or a delivery-date tax point) and the billing run posts each 50-bill transaction batch that way first, falling back to the per-bill savepoint loop when the batch is refused or fails, so a bad bill still fails alone. Single-journal posting is unchanged (its line building and checks moved into a shared `BuildJournalAsync`). Measured 2,000 bills: 13.6 s propose + 10.0 s post (was 43.9 s post); the real 50,000-account run (ONE4ALL_PERF=1): proposed in 303 s, posted in 325 s - 10.5 min in all against the 15 min target. Proposing is now the larger half; the next step there would be a set-based charge computation | BatchPostingTests, ChunkedBillingRunTests, PerformanceTests | ✅ |
| **RPT-THEME** Report house style in one click | ✅ | Brief §13: a customer's house style across all reports. `ReportHouseStyle` (Reporting.Model; JSON on `Company.ReportThemeJson`; distinct from the designer's ReportTheme palette that restyles one layout at design time): font family and size for standard text, title colour (bold ≥ 12 pt in the report header), column-heading strip colours (coloured labels in page and group headers), line colour, a footer line on every page, and the logo placed top-right on reports that do not place it themselves. `ReportHouseStyles.Apply` runs after layout in `ReportEngine.Build` (sub-reports inherit it), so a report's own choices win over the theme; the runner and the burster pass the company's theme, so the viewer, printing, subscriptions and bursting all take it. Desktop Administration ▸ Reports ▸ "Report house style" (Apply / Clear; colours checked as #RRGGBB). Migration `AddReportTheme` | ReportHouseStyleTests | ✅ |

Not done: group / ungroup of elements | ReportDesignSessionTests (copy / paste, guides) | ✅ |
| **RCP-9b** RC10 average handling time | ✅ | The till stamps when a receipt begins - the payer chosen or the first digit keyed (`TellerWindow`, cleared after posting) - and sends it as `ReceiptRequest.StartedAtUtc`; `Receipt.StartedOnUtc` keeps it (migration `AddReceiptStartedOn`; API callers may send it too). RC10 shows, per hour and station, the average seconds from start to capture over the receipts that carry a stamp (`ReceiptingReportDataProvider.AverageHandlingSeconds`); receipts posted without a stamp leave the column blank rather than skewing it | ReceiptingReportRenderTests | ✅ |
| **DB-PARTITION** Journal, sale and payslip lines partitioned by year | ✅ | Brief §2's remaining partition tables: `gl.journal_line`, `pos.sale_line` and `pay.payslip_line` are now partitioned by RANGE on the year of their header's date (`posting_date`, `sale_date`, `pay_date`), each line stamped with its header's company and date when the header adds it (`JournalLine.Stamp` from `JournalEntry.AddLine`, and likewise `SaleLine`, `PayslipLine`), so a year can be detached or dropped and reads by company need no join (index `(company_id, date)`). Migration `PartitionLineTables` rebuilds each table in SQL: backfill with the table's own triggers quiet, a `{schema}.ensure_{table}_year(year)` function making 2015–2040 plus a DEFAULT partition, the primary key becoming `(id, date)` (ids stay unique through the identity), the FK to the header, the indexes and the triggers put back (constraint triggers on each partition, kept in `sys.line_partition_trigger`), and dependent views (the `rpt.sales_by_day` snapshot) captured in `sys.line_partition_dependent`, dropped and recreated with their indexes; Down returns to plain tables. The three entities carry no xmin row version (PostgreSQL cannot return it from a partitioned insert; `One4AllDbContext.PartitionedEntityTypes`). `bud.budget_line` stays unpartitioned: budget lines are few per budget and carry no date; `mun.bill_line` / `hse.ownership_history` do not exist under those names (bills are AR open items; ownership history is `hse.ownership_change`) | LinePartitionTests | ✅ |
| **RCP-FX** Foreign-currency payments allocated at the counter | ✅ | Eleventh audit #1: a payment in another currency used to sit as credit on account until the allocation workbench matched it. The counter now allocates it straight away: `IAllocationService.AllocateAcrossCurrenciesAsync` lets a credit settle a debit in another currency - the credit's base value settles the debit at the day's rate of the debit's currency (rate 1 for base-currency bills, the daily table otherwise; `Alloc.NoRate` when there is none), the two base values' difference is realised exchange gain or loss posted as before, and the allocation keeps the debit's own amount (`Allocation.DebitTransactionAmount`, migration `AddAllocationDebitAmount`) so unallocation restores each item in its own currency. `ReceiptingService` spreads a foreign payment's base value over the account's base-currency charges by the receipt's strategy (directed amounts in base), leaving only what no charge can take as credit on account in the payment's currency; charges in other currencies still wait for the workbench | ReceiptCurrencyTests | ✅ |
| **PAY-BANKFILE** Payroll bank files per currency and per bank; payslip net pay by currency | ✅ | Eleventh audit #2–3 (brief §4.15.3, §4.15.7). `BankFileService` now writes one file per bank **and currency**: earnings paid in another currency (a pay element's `PaidInCurrency`) go out in that currency at the pay date's daily rate (`Bank.NoRate` when there is none), the rest of the net pay and the splits in the base currency; a split can name its own bank and branch (`EmployeePaymentSplit.BankName` / `BankBranch`, migration `AddSplitBank`; the Split net pay screen takes them) so it lands in that bank's file. The payslip adds, under NET PAY, how it goes out when more than one currency is involved - each currency's amount with the base value and rate used (`PayslipDocument.NetPayByCurrency`), on the printed, emailed and self-service payslip | BankFileTests | ✅ |
| **PAY-FLAGS / FA-DISPOSAL** Pay element flags, balance-driven basis; depreciation to the disposal date, half-year convention | ✅ | Eleventh audit #4–5. Pay elements (brief §4.15.2) gain **Pro-rated** (an earning follows the days employed in a part period, like basic), **Arrears-recoverable** (on by default; off means a deduction net-pay protection cannot take is simply not taken, not carried), **Terminal benefit** and **Union dues** (earnings feed the `TERMINALBASE` and `UNIONBASE` formula variables, so a dues or gratuity element takes a percentage of them), and the **Balance-driven** basis: a fixed amount per period until the employee's balance for the element is used up (`EmployeePayElement.Balance`, given when assigning; committed runs draw it down; migration `AddPayElementFlags`). Fixed assets (§4.14): a disposal first charges depreciation for every month not yet run up to the disposal month - that month by the convention (full; after the 15th for mid-month; the days held for daily) - in the disposal journal, so the gain or loss is on the carrying amount at the date; and the **MidYear** (half-year) convention charges six months over the acquisition year's remaining months, then full years | PayElementFlagTests, AssetDisposalDepreciationTests | ✅ |
| **BUD-HOLDER-ALERTS / HSE-BLOCK-LOG** Budget alerts to the holders at their own thresholds; refused second allocations logged | ✅ | Eleventh audit #6–7. Budget utilisation alerts (§4.18.5) now go to each department's budget holder for the votes that department's budget carries, at the department's own thresholds when it has them (`DepartmentUnit.AlertThresholds`; the Preparation deadlines and budget alerts screen's Alert settings with a department named), as well as to the company's alert address; each vote-and-threshold is still alerted once. Housing (§4.10.4): an allocation run's approval refused because a stand is no longer available or has been blocked since is kept as an `AllocationBlockLog` entry (run, stand, reason, who, when) after the approval's unit of work rolls back, and H39 Double Allocation Exception Report lists those attempts beside stands promised through more than one route. Migration `AddBudgetHolderThresholdsAndAllocationBlockLog` | BudgetNoticeTests, AllocationAppealTests | ✅ |

| **PERF-PACK-2** Performance pack covers the rest of §15: POS sale <2 s, list page <1 s on 1M rows, allocation run 100k <60 s, duplicate sweep <10 min, 40 departments at once, driver change <10 s | ✅ | Eleventh audit #10. `BriefPerformanceTests` (runs with ONE4ALL_PERF=1, cleans up its bulk rows): a 12-line sale posted and its receipt rendered six times (all but the warm-up under 2 s); a million customers seeded by SQL and the desktop-style page (company, code order, 500 rows) timed first and 1,000 pages in (each under 1 s); 100,000 applicants (ONE4ALL_PERF_APPLICANTS for a smaller try) with 200 stands proposed by seniority under 60 s and the duplicate sweep over them under 10 minutes (pro rata); 40 departments each creating a 120-line budget, revising it three times and submitting, all in parallel scopes, none failing, under 60 s; 5,040 driver-based lines previewed and re-computed by a FEE change, each under 10 s. |
| **POS-OFFLINE-2** The till voids, refunds and cashes up while offline | ✅ | Eleventh audit #11 (brief §4.1 offline resilience: the till covered only sale and open-till offline). Three more queued operations replayed in order on reconnect by their own handlers: `PosVoidEnvelope` (by sale number; waits for the sale to sync; a redelivery after the void is a no-op), `PosRefundEnvelope` (full cash refund against the sale's lines once synced; the reason's offline reference keeps a redelivery from paying twice; the till's session resolved by offline key), `PosCashUpEnvelope` (the count declared on the terminal, cashed up and closed centrally after the till's queued sales have posted; empty count = plain Z-read; a closed session is left alone). Offline, only a sale rung up on this terminal (still in its queue) can be voided or refunded here; a supervisor authorises with name + PIN verified against the sealed snapshot, which now carries `OfflineSupervisor`s (active users with a PIN holding Pos.Void / Pos.Refund, their PIN hashes). Close Till offline queues the cash-up and closes the till locally. Test: PosOfflineSyncTests (void, refund, cash-up replay after their sales; redelivery idempotent). |

System Manager › **Document layouts** names the company's own copy per kind (`Company.DocumentLayoutsJson`, `DocumentLayoutSettings`); the Documents screens (plus new Tax invoice or credit note and Purchase order document) then print through that layout as a PDF at the path given, or as the standard text when none is set. The allocation run's input lists are no longer capped at 100,000 characters (a 100k-row waiting list is kept in full for re-verification; migration AddDocumentLayoutsAndUnboundedAllocationInputs). Tests: DocumentLayoutTests (DOC01/03/12 rows, settings round trip, PDF through the layout), InvoiceDocumentTests (DOC11). |
| **GL-COMMON-PARAMS** The common parameters on every general ledger report | ✅ | Eleventh audit #13 (brief §13: currency base/reporting, branch, cost centre, department, include unposted on all financial reports). `LedgerReportOptions` gains `Branch` (journal lines posted for a branch), and every ledger report takes the options: trial balance by type, trial balance movement, statement of cash flows, financial snapshot, nominal activity and the journal daybook gain overloads (the daybook and nominal activity count draft and deferred journals when asked, only the lines of the branch, cost centre or department, in the reporting currency; a journal with none of those lines is left out). The definitions GL-TB, GL-IS, GL-IS-FN, GL-BS, cash flow, departmental income statement, trial balance by type, financial snapshot, trial balance movement and journal daybook all prompt for Include unposted, Reporting currency, Branch, Cost centre and Department; the departmental income statement runs through the same path. Test: LedgerReportOptionsTests (branch on each report, unposted on the daybook and nominal activity, reporting currency on the snapshot). |
| **ADR-DESKTOP / LOGGING / ANALYSIS-CODES / CASHIER-SWITCH** Eleventh audit #14–#15: the desktop-to-PostgreSQL decision recorded; Serilog to file and table; analysis codes on traders; quick cashier switch | ✅ | **ADR-0005** records that the desktop connects to PostgreSQL directly with the server as a second host of the same services (API, scheduler, webhooks) and the API client mode as the WAN option; **ADR-0006** records why MediatR, FluentValidation, Mapster, Hangfire, Testcontainers and Bogus are not used and what stands in their place; **ADR-0007** the logging stack. **Logging** (brief §2.2): `One4AllLogging.AddOne4AllLogging` wires Serilog in both hosts - a daily rolling file per host (31 kept) and `sys.app_log` (`AppLogEntry`) through the product's own batched, never-blocking `PostgresLogSink`; a host's `Serilog` configuration section adds Seq or console; System Manager › **Application log** lists the latest lines and removes old ones. **Analysis codes** (brief §4.5): `Customer`/`Supplier.AnalysisCode1–3` on the account screens, in the customer/supplier take-on files (`analysis1..3`) and on the Customer and Supplier Register reports. **Quick cashier switch** (brief §4.1): `IPosService.SwitchCashierAsync` passes the open session to the next cashier with no count (recorded as a `CashierSwitch` till event; the sale on the screen stays), beside the counted hand-over; the till's **Switch** button. Migration AddAnalysisCodesAndAppLog. Tests: ApplicationLogTests, TraderAnalysisCodeTests, CashierSwitchTests. |
| **PAY-OPTIONS** Pay element options settable; seeded recoveries recover; tax table currency; employee's housing tenancy | ✅ | Twelfth audit #1. **FIX**: the standard STAFFPURCH and HOUSERENT elements were seeded with `Recovers = None`, so earlier staff purchases were deducted again every run and rent taken from pay was never receipted against the tenancy - they now recover (`PayRecovery.StaffPurchases` / `HousingRent`), and HOUSING / TRANSPORT report as benefits (PR34 was always empty). `NewPayElement` and the new `PayElementOptions` (`IPayrollSetupService.SetPayElementOptionsAsync`) carry what the run already honoured but nothing could set: what a deduction recovers, the taxable-benefit category, the currency the element is paid in, cumulative tax, tax per currency and annual payment (a recovery only on a deduction; the currency must be a code). The Pay elements screen gains those fields and a "Set element options" step; Create tax table takes the table's currency (one table per currency for split-currency tax); Employee record › Update links the employee's housing tenancy by its number ('-' clears). Tests: PayrollSetupTests. |
| **WF-DEFINE** Workflow definitions can be created | ✅ | Twelfth audit #2 (brief §7 "Definition: document type → stages → rules → approvers → quorum → SLA/escalation"). Until now only tests built a `WorkflowDefinition`, so nothing could ever be routed. `IWorkflowService.DefineAsync(companyId, WorkflowDefinitionInput)` saves a workflow for a document type with its stages (`WorkflowStageInput`: sequence, name, quorum, value band, condition, approving role, named approvers by user name, the requester's manager, escalation after N hours to a user), validating each (role and users must exist, conditions parse); one definition is active per document type (the earlier one is kept, switched off, for documents in flight); `SetDefinitionActiveAsync` switches one off or on. Approvals › **Define a workflow** takes the stages one per line (sequence \| name \| quorum \| from value \| condition \| role \| approvers \| Manager \| escalate hours \| escalate to) and lists definitions with their stage summaries. Test: WorkflowDefinitionTests (validation, replacement, a small and a large requisition routed through one and two stages, disable/enable). |
| **TARIFF-VERSIONS** Tariffs created and versioned from a service and screen; an approved change is a new version | ✅ | Twelfth audit #3 (brief §4.9 "effective-dated tariff versions so historical bills can be recomputed and explained"). **FIX**: an approved master-data change to a tariff overwrote the version in force (so bills already raised could no longer be explained); it now becomes a new version effective from the day it is applied, blocks, seasons and cycle carried over (`MasterDataChangeService`). `IMunicipalTariffService.SaveVersionAsync(companyId, TariffInput)` creates a tariff (name, service, type, revenue account and its charges, blocks and seasons) or, for an existing code from a later date, a version that carries over whatever is not given; `WithdrawVersionAsync` takes a version out of force (the one before it applies again). Municipality › **Tariffs and versions** (blocks as from-to:rate, seasons as fromMonth-toMonth:rate; the list shows every version with its blocks, seasons and whether in force). Tests: TariffServiceTests (essentials, duplicate date, carry-over, charge, catalogue by date, withdraw, approved change → new version). |
| **POS-GIFTCARD-SALE** Gift cards sold and topped up at the till | ✅ | Twelfth audit #4 (brief §4.8.1 "issue (sold at POS → liability, not revenue)"; before, only the back-office Activate screen could load a card, outside the till session). A ticket line on the GIFTCARD item (`PosSaleLineRequest.GiftCardNumber`, `PosTicket.AddGiftCardLine`, the till's **SELL GIFT CARD** key with the card number in the gift card box and the value on the pad) loads the card when the sale posts: printed stock activates, an active or emptied card tops up (`GiftCardTransaction` Activated / ToppedUp), the value credits the gift card liability the line names (never revenue, no tax, no discount), and the tender is whatever the customer pays with, in the session, on the receipt, in the X/Z reads and the cash-up. `SaleLine.GiftCardNumber` keeps the link (migration AddSaleLineGiftCard); voiding the sale takes the load back, refused with `Pos.GiftCardSpent` once the card has been spent below it. Offline envelopes carry the number. Test: GiftCardSaleTests. |
| **FISCAL-2** Fiscalisation beyond sales: refunds, voids, exchanges, e-invoicing, the Z report, an HTTP reference provider | ✅ | Twelfth audit #5 (brief §3.5: "receipt signing… Z-report submission… one reference implementation skeleton"; only till sales were signed). `FiscalReceiptRequest` now says what it signs (`FiscalDocumentKind` Sale / Refund / Invoice / CreditNote), the document it reverses or credits and the buyer's tax number; `IFiscalDeviceProvider.SubmitDayReportAsync(FiscalDayReport)` carries the Z report (sales, refunds and voids counts and totals) submitted before the day closes. Till refunds, refunds without receipt, exchanges (through their refund) and voids are signed as refund receipts referencing the original (`PosEvent.FiscalCode / FiscalVerification / FiscalQueued`); sales-ledger tax invoices and credit notes are e-invoiced when posted (`OpenItem.FiscalCode …`); a device that is down queues them and the fiscal retry task now sends waiting refunds, voids, invoices and credit notes as well as sales. `HttpFiscalDeviceProvider` is the reference skeleton for an authority's fiscal data management system over HTTPS (`Fiscal` options or ONE4ALL_FISCAL_URL / ONE4ALL_FISCAL_KEY: open and close the day with its report, sign each document, queue on failure); the simulator records what it signed. Migration AddFiscalCodesToEventsAndOpenItems. Test: FiscalisationExtrasTests. |
| **ALLOC-COMMITTEE** The allocation committee acts on individual pairings and the run keeps the meeting | ✅ | Twelfth audit #6 (brief §4.10.4 "committee members, meeting date, minute reference, approvals, rejections with reasons, substitutions (a substitution must state why the skipped applicant was skipped)"). `IAllocationService.RejectLineAsync` cancels one proposed pairing with the committee's reason (the applicant is recorded on the run as `ExclusionReason.CommitteeRejected` with the note; the stand stays available and the approval offers only the pairings that stand); `SubstituteLineAsync` puts the next eligible applicant in the run's own order on the pairing and records the skipped one as `CommitteeSkipped` with the stated reason (`Hse.NoSubstitute` when nobody is left); `ApproveByCommitteeAsync(…, CommitteeMeeting)` keeps the minute reference, meeting date and members present on the run (`AllocationRun.MeetingDate / CommitteeMembers`). Housing › Allocation run gains Reject line and Substitute line steps and the meeting fields; the list shows resolution, meeting and members. Migration AddCommitteeDecisions. Test: AllocationCommitteeDecisionTests. |
| **NUMBER-SEQUENCES** Number sequences configurable | ✅ | Twelfth audit #7 (brief §2.4 "per company / branch / document type, with prefix, padding, year reset option, and gapless mode"; sequences could only be created on first use as `<type>-00001` and nothing ever set gapless). `INumberSequenceService.ConfigureAsync(companyId, documentType, branchId, NumberSequenceSetup)` creates a sequence or changes its format (`NumberSequence.Configure`: prefix up to 20, padding 1–12, yearly reset, gapless) without touching the numbers already given out, and moves the next number forward (`AdvanceTo`, never back - `Sequence.StartBehind`); `ListAsync` shows every sequence with its next number and year. System Manager › **Number sequences** screen (type, branch code, prefix, padding, restart each year, gapless, next number). Test: NumberSequenceTests. |
| **SPONSOR / VEHICLES / STANDS-PER-BENEFICIARY / GROUP-APPROVERS** Small gaps from the twelfth audit #8 | ✅ | **Schools**: `ISchoolSetupService.SetSponsorAsync` names (or removes) the customer billed for a student instead of the guardian (Students screen › Sponsor step), so sponsor billing and SC179 can be used. **Weighbridge**: `IWeighbridgeService.SaveVehicleAsync` and the Operations › Vehicle register screen keep the vehicles (haulier, stored tare, permitted gross, barred), so stored-tare weighing and overload flags can trigger. **Housing**: how many stands one beneficiary may hold is configurable - `Company.StandsPerBeneficiary` (default 1) and `HousingScheme.StandsPerBeneficiary` for a scheme's own rule - the allocation run counts a household's stands held and live allocations on applicant and spouse national IDs against the limit (brief §4.10.4 "one stand per scheme/council, configurable to N"). **Workflow**: a stage may name a user group whose members approve (`WorkflowStageInput.GroupName`, `WorkflowStage.RequiredGroupName`; the Define a workflow line's last field), and a role held through a group now counts for a role stage. Migration AddStandsPerBeneficiaryAndGroupApprovers. Tests: SchoolSetupTests, WeighbridgeTests, WorkflowDefinitionTests. |
| **PETTY-CASH-FUNDS** Petty cash imprest funds with counts, reconciliation and input tax | ✅ | Twelfth audit #9 (brief §4.7 "petty cash (imprest system with top-ups and reconciliation)", §S "Petty Cash Book & Imprest Reconciliation"; petty cash had only an account-level disburse/top-up with the float typed each time, no fund record, no count and no input VAT). `PettyCashFund` (`cb.petty_cash_fund`: code, float, petty cash and bank accounts, over/short account, custodian, branch) saved from `IPettyCashService.SaveFundAsync`; `DisburseFromFundAsync` pays an expense from a fund, refusing a payment the fund cannot cover, and when the receipt carries a tax code claims the recoverable share of the input tax to the code's input account (or the VAT input control account) with the rest left in the expense; `ReimburseFundAsync` tops the fund up to its float; `CountAsync` records a physical count (`cb.petty_cash_count`: counted, book balance at the date, denominations, note, who) and `ReconcileAsync` writes the shortage off or takes the overage in through the over/short account and reimburses the float, keeping both journal numbers on the count. General Ledger › **Petty cash funds** screen (List, Save fund, Pay out, Top up, Count, Reconcile); report **CB77 Petty Cash Counts & Imprest Reconciliation**. Migration AddPettyCashFunds. Test: PettyCashTests. |
| **SUPPLIER-ADVANCES** Advances and prepayments to suppliers | ✅ | Twelfth audit #10 (brief §4.4 "prepayments/advances to suppliers", AP64 "with prepayments and debit balances highlighted"; a payment on account was the only way to pay ahead, which put the money into the purchase ledger and let the next payment run net it off). `SupplierAdvance` (`trd.supplier_advance`) is paid from `ISupplierAdvanceService.PayAsync` into an advances (prepayment) asset account - Dr advances / Cr bank - against a purchase order number, refused for a supplier on hold or blacklisted; `ApplyAsync` moves it into the purchase ledger when the invoice arrives (Dr AP control / Cr advances at the rate it was paid at, raising a Payment open item `<advance>/A<n>` allocated to the invoice, same currency only); `RefundAsync` takes the supplier's refund of the balance (Dr bank / Cr advances); `ListAsync` lists them. Suppliers › **Supplier advances** screen (Pay, Apply, Refund, List). Report **AP67 Supplier Advances & Prepayments**; **AP64** gains an Advances held column and flags "Advance held". Migration AddSupplierAdvancesAndRecurringSchedules (shared with #11). Test: SupplierAdvanceTests. |
| **RECURRING-SCHEDULES** Recurring journal schedules, auto-reversal and currency; currency on journal import | ✅ | Twelfth audit #11 (brief §3.1 recurring journals "frequency, start/end, count, auto-reverse"; templates posted monthly only, in base currency, with no end). `RecurringSchedule` on `IRecurringJournalService.CreateAsync`/`SetScheduleAsync`: interval 1, 3, 6 or 12 months (quarter months counted from the start month, else January), start month, end date, at most N times (`RecurringJournal.Occurrences` counts), auto-reverse (the same lines the other way round on the first day of the next month, document `<code>-REV`, type RecurringReversal) and a template currency (lines post in it at the table rate). `PostAsync` refuses a date the template is not due on (`Gl.RecurringNotDue` with the reason); `PostAllActiveAsync` skips those with the reason in `RecurringRunLine.Note` and reports the reversal journal. Journal import: optional `Currency` and `Rate` columns per journal (blank: base at the table rate; mixed currencies in one journal are refused). GL22 shows the schedule and "Not due: …". General Ledger › Recurring journals screen: frequency, start, end, times, auto-reverse, currency; Schedule step. Columns added by migration AddSupplierAdvancesAndRecurringSchedules (shared with #10). Tests: RecurringScheduleTests. |
| **TAX-DEFAULTS / CATEGORY-COSTING / FX-BY-TRADER** Default tax codes on accounts and suppliers, costing method per item category, FX revaluation before/after by trader | ✅ | Twelfth audit #12 (brief §3.5 default tax codes; §3.4 costing per category; §3.1 "revaluation report showing before/after and the difference per account/trader"). `LedgerAccount.DefaultTaxCode` and `Supplier.DefaultTaxCode` (the customer's existed but was never applied): a sales or purchase invoice entered without a tax code or amount takes the trader's default tax code, else the revenue or expense account's, and the tax at that code's rate on the document date to the code's output or input account (else the VAT control account) - `TradingService.WithTaxDefaultsAsync`; the invoice requests are records so defaults are applied with `with`. `ItemCategory.CostingMethod`: an item created in that category without an explicit method takes it (Retail › Category under a category screen sets it; Add a ledger account and Add a supplier screens take the default tax code). Report **GL26 FX Revaluation by Trader (before / after)**: each debtor and creditor with foreign open items at the date, the base value booked, the value at the closing rate and the difference, from the revaluation preview without posting. Migration AddTaxDefaultsCategoryCostingAndPosLineExtras (shared with #13). Test: TaxDefaultsTests. |
| **POS-LINE-EXTRAS** Till line notes, serial prompt, gift wrap, scale tare/hold, manual weight audit, cash-up sign-off | ✅ | Twelfth audit #13 (brief §4.1 line notes, serial prompt, scale tare/hold, manual weight permission and audit, variance supervisor sign-off). `PosTicketLine.Note/SerialNumber/GiftWrap` reach `PosSaleLineRequest`, the offline envelope and `SaleLine` (printed as cart detail); a serial-tracked item (`Item.Tracking == Serial`) is refused without its serial or with more than one per line (`Pos.SerialRequired`, `Pos.SerialOnePerLine`), and the till flags the line "S/N needed" on scanning it. Kitchen notes: `TableOrderLine.Note` via `IRestaurantService.NoteLineAsync`, carried onto `KitchenTicketLine.Note` and printed after the dish. Scale: Tare (typed container weight taken off every reading), Hold (the last stable reading serves the next weighed line), Manual kg (a weight keyed by hand under the new `Pos.ManualWeight` permission with a supervisor, logged as `PosEventKind.ManualWeight` with the weight it replaced). Cash-up: `PosTerminal.CashUpSignOffVariance` - a variance over it needs `CashUpRequest.SupervisorUserId` (not the cashier, holding Pos.TillSession authorise), recorded on `TillSession.SignedOffByUserId` and as a `CashUpSignOff` till event; the offline cash-up envelope carries the supervisor. Columns added by migration AddTaxDefaultsCategoryCostingAndPosLineExtras (shared with #12). Test: PosLineExtrasTests. |
| **REPORT-COMMON-PARAMS** Common parameters on sales, purchasing, cash book and tax listings; "rate basis used" header | ✅ | Twelfth audit #14 (brief §9 "common parameters: currency basis, branch, cost centre, department, unposted" and the rate-basis line; only the ledger listings took them). Every listing in the Sales, Purchasing, CashBook and Tax modules now declares `ReportingCurrency`, `RateBasis` (Closing / Average / Keyed / daily), `Rate`, `Branch`, `CostCentre`, `Department` and `IncludeUnposted` (`SystemReports.CommonParameters`, parsed by `ReportCommonParameters`). The report runner translates the listing's money columns into the reporting currency at the chosen basis (`ReportCurrencyTranslation`: closing rate at the report date, period average, keyed rate, else daily; the inverse of a quote the other way round) and adds a header line naming the basis used - "Amounts in EUR at 0.8333 EUR/USD (closing rate at 2026-08-31); branch EAST" - or that no rate is on file. The cash book and tax providers narrow the journal lines they read to the branch, cost centre and department and take draft and deferred journals in when unposted ones are wanted; debtor and creditor listings (open items carry no dimensions) take the currency basis only. Tests: ReportCommonParametersTests. |
| **MESSAGE-TEMPLATES** Message templates screen, seeded standard templates, message batches | ✅ | Twelfth audit #15 (brief §8; templates could only be saved from code or the API, no company started with any, and a batch's outcome was only visible through the service). `MessageTemplateSeeder` adds the standard English templates a company is missing - STATEMENT (customer statement), FEES (school fee statement), REMINDER (dunning), BILL by email and by SMS - with the merge fields the bulk senders supply, at every start-up (desktop and server, after the administrator seeder) and from the screen; a reworded template is never overwritten. System Manager › **Message templates** screen (List, Save with {{Field}} merge fields and \n line breaks, Preview with sample values listing fields still blank, Seed standard) and **Message batches** screen (every bulk send with queued / sent / failed counts, Status with failures by recipient, Despatch now). Test: MessageTemplateSeederTests. |
| **REST-TRADING-API** REST endpoints for master data CRUD and document posting | ✅ | Twelfth audit #16 (brief §11 "REST API: customers/suppliers/items/prices CRUD; post invoice, credit note, receipt, sale"; the API had only enquiry and workflow endpoints for these). `TradingApiEndpoints` (server): `/api/master/customers`, `/api/master/suppliers`, `/api/master/items` - list with search and paging, get, create (through `IMasterDataService`, so codes, currencies and standard-cost rules hold), change (name, contact and tax details, hold, accounts, category, active) and retire (soft delete); `/api/pricing/lists/{list}/items/{item}` get the price history and put a price (effective now or from a date). Documents post through the same services the desktop uses: `/api/trading/sales-invoices`, `sales-credit-notes`, `receipts`, `purchase-invoices`, `purchase-credit-notes`, `supplier-payments` and `/api/pos/sales` (lines with note, serial and gift wrap; tenders by type and currency). The API's own document records (`TradingApiModels`: currencies and enums as text) map onto the application requests in `TradingApiMapping`; errors come back as 400 / 404 / 409 / 403 with the error code. Tests: TradingApiMappingTests (unit). |
| **TAKE-ON-AGREEMENTS-ARREARS** Opening balances: instalment agreements and municipal arrears by property and service | ✅ | Twelfth audit #17 (brief §4.9 / §4.10 take-on; sale and instalment agreements in force could not be imported, and open-items take-on knew no property or service). `IVerticalImportService.ImportInstalmentAgreementsAsync` (CSV agreement, stand, buyer, cash price, deposit, rate, method, term, signed, first due, interest account [, tenure, escalation, arrears]): records the stand sold to the buyer unless the register shows it, puts the buyer on the tenure's receivables control, rebuilds the schedule, marks every instalment due on or before the opening date raised (history) and raises the arrears as an open item on the buyer against the migration suspense account; an agreement already on file is skipped. `ImportMunicipalArrearsAsync` (stand, service, invoice, amount [, date, narrative]): an unpaid bill per property and service on the ratepayer against suspense, each on an opening billing run for its service, so arrears age, statements and revenue by service carry the take-on balances; an invoice already in the ledger is skipped. Both on the System Manager › Import screen (kinds InstalmentAgreements, MunicipalArrears) with import templates. Test: OpeningRegisterImportTests. |
| **DRILL-THROUGH** Drill-through to invoices, orders, GRNs, receipts, bills, payslips, stands; VAT drill-down | ✅ | Twelfth audit #18 (brief §9 drill-through; the viewer opened only journals, accounts, customers, suppliers and items). `IDrillThroughService.DescribeAsync(companyId, target, key)` renders the record behind a cell as text: **Document** (an invoice, credit note, debit note, receipt, payment or bill by number - the printed invoice where there is one, else the open item with its allocations), **PurchaseOrder** (the printed order), **Grn** (lines received), **Receipt** (the cash-office receipt with its tenders, else the ledger receipt), **Stand** (register entry and chain of title), **Payslip** (key employee:period, the printed payslip) and **TaxCode** (the VAT drill-down: every posted line carrying the code in the last twelve months, with totals). `DrillThrough.Infer` maps the usual column names to those targets (Document / Invoice / Bill, Order / PO, Grn, Receipt, Stand, Payslip, TaxCode), so existing listings drill without edits; the viewer sends any target it does not open itself to the service. Test: DrillThroughTests. |
| **RESTORE-FILE-SWITCH-OVER** Restore from a backup file without the record; switch-over to a restored database | ✅ | Twelfth audit #19a (brief §10 disaster recovery; only a backup with a record in the live database could be restored, and nothing made a restored database live). `IBackupService.RestoreFileToNewDatabaseAsync(file, database, "RESTORE name")` restores an off-site copy or another server's archive into a new database after reading it back with pg_restore --list; `SwitchOverAsync(database, "SWITCH TO name")` ends every session on the live and restored databases, renames the live one `<live>_retired_<yyyyMMddHHmm>` (kept, never dropped) and gives the restored one the live name, so the next start-up runs on it. Both on the System Manager › Back up or restore the database screen. Test: BackupTests (skipped where pg_dump is not installed). |
| **SERVER-PACKAGING** Server as a Windows service, PostgreSQL prerequisite check, first-run database setup, single-file licensing tool | ✅ | Twelfth audit #19b (brief §10 packaging; the server could only be run from a console against a database someone had created, and the licence generator was a framework-dependent build). `builder.Host.UseWindowsService` makes `One4All.Server.exe` run as the service **One4All Server**; `installer/server-install.ps1` (shipped in the published server folder) checks that PostgreSQL answers on the connection string's host and port, writes the connection string and port into `appsettings.json` beside the executable (SYSTEM and administrators only), registers the service with automatic start and restart on failure, and starts it (`-Uninstall` removes it). `DatabaseBootstrap.EnsureDatabaseAsync` creates the database on first start (server and desktop) before migrations and seeding; `ServerVersionAsync` is the reachability probe. `installer/build.ps1` publishes the server self-contained and the licence generator as a single executable. `docs/INSTALL.md` documents it. Test: DatabaseBootstrapTests. |
| **DOCS-DELIVERABLES** User manual per module, demo scripts, acceptance test pack, training data note - generated from the system | ✅ | Twelfth audit #19c (brief §12 deliverables: "documentation, training data set, user manual per vertical, acceptance test pack"; only RECEIPTING.md and the report catalogue existed). `tools/One4All.DocGen` (in the solution) derives the documents from the system so they cannot drift: `docs/manual/<module>.md` for every Explorer module (purpose, each group and screen with what it does, its fields with required flags, choices and defaults, its list, and the module's reports with their descriptions) with an index; `docs/demos/<module>.md`, a numbered walkthrough per module (what to open, what to fill in, what to show, which reports to end on); `docs/acceptance/ACCEPTANCE-PACK.md`, every automated scenario as a checklist by project, area and class with the class's own summary; `docs/TRAINING-DATA.md`, how a training company is built (industry set-up, sample data). Regenerate with `dotnet run --project tools/One4All.DocGen -- .` (noted in HANDOVER.md). |
| **PERF-PACK-REST** Performance pack: till start-to-ready, twenty terminals at once, a thousand payslips e-mailed | ✅ | Twelfth audit #19d (brief §15 targets still untested: POS start-to-ready under 10 s, 20 terminals, payslip e-mailing; HTTP concurrency is the fifty-user scenario already in PerformanceTests). `RemainingPerformanceTests` (ONE4ALL_PERF=1, sizes via ONE4ALL_PERF_ITEMS / ONE4ALL_PERF_EMPLOYEES): a till's start-up work (company and terminal, tax rates, tiles, favourites, categories, tender currencies, a search) on a 20,000-product catalogue within 10 s; twenty terminals each opening a session and completing ten sales in parallel with no failures within two minutes; a thousand employees paid and their payslips rendered as PDFs and queued for e-mail within the hour (every one queued). Found and fixed on the way: twenty tills issuing the same item hit optimistic-concurrency failures - the stock balance row is now taken under lock (`FOR UPDATE`) inside the unit of work and a balance still tracked from the till's previous sale is read again before it changes; a unit of work that loses a race or a deadlock retries up to eight times with a jittered pause. |
| **DESIGNER-EXTRAS** Designer: record sorting apart from grouping, element groups, tooltips, gradients, anchoring, pictures from a path or URL | ✅ | Twelfth audit #19e (brief §13.1 designer gaps). `ReportDefinition.Sorts` (expression and direction; "Sort…" in the designer, text `Field; Other desc`) orders the records before grouping, so within each group rows keep that order; **Group / Ungroup** give elements a shared `GroupName` so they move together (`ReportDesignSession.Group/Ungroup/WithGroups`); `ReportElement.Tooltip` shows as the HTML title, `BackgroundGradient` with `GradientVertical` renders a CSS gradient (print keeps the first colour); `AnchorRight` keeps a cell at the right of its column in multi-column pages and `AnchorBottom` carries it down when a band grows for a sub-report; `ImageSource` takes a picture from a file path or an http(s) URL at render time (`ImageSources`, read once per process, missing sources render nothing). All editable in the property grid. Test: DesignerExtrasTests. |
| **DOC-LAYOUTS-ALL** Every §S document designer-editable: twenty text-backed documents (DOC13–DOC32), one print screen for all, Document layouts for all 32 kinds | ✅ | Twelfth audit #19f (brief §S "all designer-editable"). `DocumentKinds` grows to 32 kinds: the twelve headed business documents plus twenty text-backed ones (till receipt, customer / balance-forward / supplier statements, dunning letter, remittance advice, payslip, payment / receipt / journal / petty cash vouchers, withholding tax certificate, weighbridge ticket, pick list, RFQ, certificate of service, budget-holder statement, chain of title, cheque, shelf labels), each identified by its number or code and, where needed, a date. `ITextDocumentService.RenderAsync(company, kind, number, date)` renders any kind as its standard text through the service that owns it (and the business documents, tax invoices and purchase orders too). Designable reports DOC13–DOC32 give one row per line of that text (Title, Number, Date, LineNo, Line) so a company frames the document with its logo, header, footer and fonts in the designer; Document layouts lists all 32 kinds and the new **Print a document** screen prints any of them as text or through its layout as a PDF. Documents that have no text renderer yet (offer letters, lease agreements, notices, permits, asset tags …) gain a layout only when their renderer is written. Tests: DocumentKindsTests (every kind has its DOC report, command and parameters), DocumentLayoutTests (statement and pick-list rows, errors, business documents through the same entry point). |
| **DESIGNER-SAGE-LOOK** Report designer laid out like the Sage Report Designer: menus, toolbars, toolbox placed by clicking, Dynamic Help, Variables, File Explorer, Report Explorer, categorised properties with descriptions, numbered sections, status bar | ✅ | User request (Sage Report Designer look and working). Menu bar File / Edit / View / Toolbox / Sections / Report / Format / Tools / Help; a standard toolbar (new, save, preview, page setup, cut, copy, paste, delete, undo, redo, section, grid) and a formatting toolbar (font, size, bold, italic, underline, colour, text alignment, element alignment, sizing, order, grouping) acting on all selected elements as one undo step (`SetPropertyOnAll`); a toolbox - Pointer, Add Text, Draw Line, Draw Box, Add Image / Logo, Add Company Logo, Add Data Field, Add Barcode, Add Expression, Add Subreport, Add Tick Box, Add Signature Line, Add Chart, Add Rich Text - whose tool is placed where the user clicks in a section (Esc returns to the pointer). Left panes: Dynamic Help ("What would you like to do?" tasks that start the matching action), Variables (fields, parameters, system values and functions in a tree with Find; double-click adds), File Explorer (reports by module; double-click opens). Right panes: Report Explorer (report › page › numbered sections › elements; click selects), Properties grouped Appearance / Behaviour / Data / Design / Layout or alphabetical, with a description pane for the focused property (`DesignerCatalogue`), and the expression editor. Sections carry numbered captions ("1. Page Header", "3. CustomerCode header"); Sections menu adds sections (group sections create their group, `AddGroupBand`), flow and height; Report menu names the report (`SetReportInfo`), sets the data source, sorting, grouping (`SetGroups`), page setup, theme and explains e-mail set-up; View menu shows or hides each pane, zoom and grid; status bar shows the selection's position and size in mm, the page and the zoom. Tests: SageDesignerTests (toolbox, help tasks, every editable property categorised and described, section numbering, group sections, grouping round trip, report info and toolbar formatting as single undo steps). |
| **METERED-BILLING-RUN** Billing runs bill metered consumption: unbilled accepted readings on the stand's meters, marked billed when the bill posts | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #1 (brief §4.9): the route or cycle billing run passed zero consumption to every charge, so water and electricity readings never reached a bill. Now `BillingRunService` sums the accepted, not-yet-billed readings (`MeterReading.BilledOn` null, up to the bill date) on the meters linked to the stand's service for the run's service and code, bills that consumption through the tariff, keeps it on the run line (`BillingRunLine.Consumption`, the reading ids) and shows it on the exceptions listing; when the bill posts (chunked or single) the readings are marked billed with the run and bill date, so the next run bills only what was read since. Migration `AddMeterReadingBilling`. Test: MeteredBillingRunTests (January's 20 units billed at 5 + 20 × 2, the reading marked billed, February bills the fixed charge only, March bills its 40 units). |
| **MANUAL-JOURNAL-GUARD + MATCH-TOLERANCE-RULES** Accounts that do not allow manual journals are refused by the engine and the database; invoice-match tolerances come from company / supplier rules a clerk may only tighten | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #2 and #3. **#2 (brief §3.1):** `LedgerAccount.AllowManualJournal` was never checked - the posting engine refuses a manual journal line on such an account (`Posting.ManualJournalNotAllowed`) and the `gl.journal_line_control_guard` trigger refuses it in the database too; "Add a ledger account" has a "Manual journals allowed" tick and the chart lists it. **#3 (brief §4.3):** the three-way match took the price tolerance typed by the clerk (100% skipped the check). Now `Company.InvoicePriceTolerancePercent` (default 5) and `InvoiceQuantityTolerancePercent`, with per-supplier overrides, are the rule; a tolerance given with the match may only tighten it (`Pop.ToleranceAboveRule` otherwise), and the invoice quantity may exceed the receipt only within the quantity tolerance. New screen **Purchasing › Matching tolerances** (company rules and supplier overrides, over-receipt tolerance included, which had no screen). Migration `AddThirteenthAuditColumnsAndManualJournalGuard` (shared with the transfer increment). Tests: ControlAccountGuardTests (engine, trigger, system posting still allowed), ProcurementTests (rule wider than the company's refused, supplier override, quantity tolerance). |
| **TILL-VOID-NOSALE-LABELS** Till voids keep their reason and supervisor; a No-Sale action opens the drawer under supervision and logs it; shelf labels go to the label printer | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #4 (brief §4.1). The till asked a supervisor for a void and then dropped the supervisor and sent a canned reason: `IPosService.VoidSaleAsync` now takes `authorisedByUserId`, the SaleVoid till event records it and the reason typed in the note box (offline voids replay with their supervisor), so RETLVOIDS shows who authorised every void. New **No sale** button: the reason comes from the note box, a supervisor with Pos.NoSale authorises, the drawer is kicked and a NoSale till event is logged (RETLNOSALE), where before the drawer only opened with a cash receipt and no-sales could only be keyed after the fact. The configured label printer was never used: **Shelf labels** gains "Send to the label printer". Test: FiscalisationExtrasTests asserts the void's reason and authoriser. |
| **TRANSFER-BALANCE-ASSUMED** Ownership transfer: the transferee may formally assume the transferor's housing balance with approval; on completion the balance and the instalment agreement move to the transferee | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #5 (brief §4.10.6 pre-condition 2: "instalments up to date, or the balance settled, or formally assumed by the transferee with approval"). Completion passed a hard-coded "housing account clear" and never moved the account. New step **Assume balance** on Transfer of ownership: an officer with the ownership-approval permission who neither verified nor submitted the application (SOD-HSE-05) records the assumption with the clearing account it passes through (`ITransferApplicationService.AssumeBalanceAsync`); the live housing-account check then reads "assumed by the transferee". On completion the transferor's outstanding debit items are credited and allocated through the clearing account, the transferee is invoiced the same per currency (the clearing account nets to nil, so the AR balance moves without touching revenue), and a running instalment agreement on the stand gets the transferee as its buyer (`InstalmentAgreement.AssignBuyer`). Unallocated credits stay with the transferor. Migration `AddThirteenthAuditColumnsAndManualJournalGuard` (shared). Test: TransferApplicationTests (refused without the permission or the account, assumed 6,000, checklist clear, completion moves the balance, clearing nets to nil, stand re-held). |
| **BUDGET-ACTIVATION-SOD + SERVICING-SPREAD** Budget versions are put into force by a named officer who is neither submitter nor approver, from a screen; servicing cost spreads over every unsold stand | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #6 and #7. **#6 (brief §4.18.4):** nothing on screen activated a budget version, and `ActivateAsync` took no user, so the submit-then-activate segregation could not be checked. `IBudgetingService.ActivateAsync(company, budget, activatedByUserId)` refuses the version's submitter or approver (`Budget.SelfActivation`), records `Budget.ActivatedByUserId / ActivatedOn`, and the new **Budgeting › Activate a budget version** screen (Budget.Review) passes the signed-in officer. Companies that do not require approval may still activate a draft (unchanged, by design); department confinement on driver lines, spreads, the staff build and workbook import remains a known gap (those calls carry no user). **#7 (brief §4.10.2):** servicing cost was apportioned only to Available / Allocated stands, so stands still planned, surveyed, under servicing, reserved or offered carried none and the cost per stand was overstated; now every stand of the scheme not sold, transferred, handed over, consolidated or re-subdivided takes its share. The WIP → Stands Available transfer still happens at subdivision rather than at servicing completion (no servicing-complete event exists yet). Tests: BudgetPreparationTests (approver and submitter refused, Finance activates, recorded), ServicingCostTests (a stand under servicing shares the cost equally; the sold stand takes none). |
| **FX-VALUATION-LOCK + MONETARY + RATE-POLICY** Period-end revaluation passes a signed-off bank reconciliation; only monetary accounts are revalued; the company's exchange-rate policy drives the posting engine and treasury | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #8 (brief §3.3). (a) The posting engine refused any line on a bank account reconciled past the posting date, so a revaluation run after the bank rec was signed off failed: `JournalRequest.ValuationOnly` marks the revaluation and its reversal as valuation entries (base amounts only), which the lock lets through while ordinary entries stay refused. (b) Every currency-restricted asset or liability was revalued, stock and prepayments included: `LedgerAccount.IsMonetary` (default true; "Monetary item" tick on Add a ledger account) and the run skips non-monetary accounts, as IAS 21 requires. (c) `ExchangeRatePolicy` was read only by Trading: `ExchangeRateResolver.PolicyTypeAsync / PolicyRateAsync` apply the policy for the document type (else the source module) with the daily rate as fallback, used by the posting engine for lines without an explicit rate and by treasury's cross-currency transfers ("Treasury" / "CB"). Currency decimal places and rounding rules are still not applied to journal amounts (open). Migration shared: `AddThirteenthAuditColumnsAndManualJournalGuard`. Test: FxPolicyAndLockTests (revaluation after sign-off, stock skipped, GL policy picks the spot rate). |
| **INTERCO-PARTIAL-ELIMINATION + DEFERRED-POSTING-MODULES** Consolidation eliminates intercompany balances to the extent they match; a company can queue any module's postings as deferred journals for a controlled release | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #9 and #10. **#9 (brief §4.4):** elimination was all-or-nothing - only when the translated intercompany totals netted to exactly nil, which a multi-currency group rarely does - so in practice nothing was eliminated; now the matched portion (the lesser of the intercompany debits and credits) is eliminated pro rata across the intercompany accounts and the residue stays visible on them, reported as unmatched. **#10 (brief §3.2 rule 8, Sage 200 deferred posting):** only manual journals could be deferred; `Company.DeferredPostingModules` names the source modules (AR, AP, POS …) whose postings the engine queues as deferred journals (single and batch paths), the sub-ledgers updating at once; the new **General Ledger › Deferred posting** screen sets the modules and shows how many journals wait; SYS245 lists them and Period close releases them. Tests: IntercompanyTests (1,400 against 1,000 eliminates 1,000 and leaves 400), DeferredPostingModulesTests (AR queued then released; a module not queued posts at once). |
| **TILL-VALUE-DISCOUNTS + AGEING-BASIS** Value (amount) discounts on a line or the whole sale at the till; a trader's ageing basis (invoice or due date) applied in aged reports and statements | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #11 and #12. **#11 (brief §4.1 "Discount amount"):** the till gave percentage discounts only; `PosTicket.DiscountLineAmount / DiscountSaleAmount` take a value off a line, or off the sale spread over its lines in proportion (the last line takes the rounding), with the same supervisor rule expressed as the equivalent percentage; the till gains **Amount off** and **Line amount off** keys (type the amount on the pad). **#12 (brief §4.5/§4.6):** `Customer/Supplier.AgeingBasis` was read by one receivables listing only; the aged debtors and creditors reports now age each trader by its own basis, and statements carry the basis (`TraderStatement.AgeingBasis`) so the statement footer's ageing follows it. Tests: PosTicketTests (value discounts, supervisor rule, spreading and rounding), AgeingAsAtTests (invoice-date basis moves the debt to 61–90; the statement carries the basis). |
| **GIFT-CARD-LIABILITY + ALLOWANCE-VOUCHER-REPORTS** POS124 Gift Card Liability by Age and POS123 Allowance Vouchers Outstanding | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #13 (brief §4.8). Nothing reported the gift-card liability or the allowance vouchers in hand (promotion redemption was already POS114). **POS124** lists every card still carrying value as at the To date, aged from issue in 0–90 / 91–180 / 181–365 / over-365-day bands with expiry and status, so the liability on the books and the breakage candidates are visible; **POS123** groups issued, unredeemed allowance vouchers per holder and kind with their count, serial range, value and how many have expired. CATALOGUE.md regenerated. Test: RetailReportsTests (a card and three fuel vouchers appear with the right age and totals; every retail report renders). |
| **OFFER-EXCEPTIONS-QUEUE + STAND-WARD** Expired stand offers wait in an exceptions queue for an officer to lapse or extend with a reason; the municipal link takes the stand's own ward | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #14 (brief §4.10.4 / §4.10.10). The scheduled job lapsed every expired offer by itself; the brief wants expired offers auto-queued for an officer to lapse or extend with a reason. `IAllocationService.ExpiredOffersAsync` is the queue (certificate, stand, applicant, deadline, days overdue, extensions so far), `LapseOfferAsync` and `ExtendOfferAsync` decide one offer with a mandatory reason (`StandAllocation.ExtendOffer` counts extensions and keeps the reason), the `housing.lapse-offers` job now reports the queue instead of deciding, and the allocation screen gains **Expired offers / Lapse offer / Extend offer** steps (the bulk lapse stays for an officer's explicit use). The housing → municipal link created the property under the link's single default ward: it now uses the stand's own ward when set. Left open: a stand already on the roll is still left to the clearance-based transfer process rather than re-pointed to the new holder (deliberate), and more than one live allocation per beneficiary is still blocked by the unique index and the waiting-list status (StandsPerBeneficiary > 1 remains ineffective). Migration `AddOfferExtensions`. Tests: StandAllocationTests (queue, too-early extension refused, extension re-opens the offer, reason required to lapse), HousingMunicipalLinkTests (stand ward). |
| **LICENCE-MODULE-LIMIT-KEYS + PAYROLLS-LIMIT** A module's own limit key (POS "terminals") is honoured; the PAY "payrolls" limit is enforced and shown in usage | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #15 (brief §11.3/§11.5). The till looked up `pos_terminals` inside the POS module's limits while the brief's payload writes `"terminals": 2` there, so a per-module terminal cap was silently ignored (only the global key counted); `LicenceService.WithinLimit` and the usage screen now also try the key without the module prefix. The PAY module's `payrolls` limit was never checked: it now counts the companies running a payroll (with employees on the books), `LicenceLimitInterceptor` refuses a first hire that would start one more payroll than licensed, and licence usage reports `payrolls`. Tests: LicenceEnforcementTests (module "terminals" limit refuses the second till), LicenceLimitTests (a first hire in another company is refused at the payrolls cap; a hire in a company already running payroll is not). |
| **REPORT-ROW-CAP + PAYSLIP-TARGET** The SQL report row cap no longer cuts a 100,000-row report; the payslip e-mail performance test holds the brief's five-minute target | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #16 (brief §15). `SqlReportDataProvider.MaxRows` was 50,000, so the "100,000-row report" performance test measured half the rows plus a cap notice; the cap is now 250,000 (still a guard against a runaway query, with the notice row beyond it) and the test asserts the full 100,000 rows are fetched before timing the first page. The payslip e-mail test asserted under an hour where the brief says under five minutes for 1,000 employees; it now asserts five minutes. Rows are still fetched and laid out in full before the first page prints ("streams thereafter" is not implemented); the SQL statement timeout stays at 15 s. The performance pack runs with ONE4ALL_PERF=1. |
| **DESKTOP-FIELD-HIDING** Desktop lists drop the cost, margin, salary and credit-limit columns a user may not see | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #17 (brief §5.1 field-level sensitivity). The API and the report runner stripped sensitive fields, but the desktop reads the database directly (ADR-0005) and showed every column: basic salary on the notch list, stand cost on the stand register, and so on. `FieldSensitivity.Strip(DataTable, hidden)` removes the columns whose names classify as hidden for the signed-in user (no ViewCost: cost; no ViewMargin: margin; no Payroll.ViewSalary: salary; no Sales.CreditControl: credit limit), and the Operations console applies it to every list it loads (`OperationsViewModel.VisibleListAsync`), on selection, after a run and on refresh. The notch list's "Basic" column is now "Basic salary" so it classifies. Free-text results of an action are not filtered (their screens already sit behind the matching permissions). Test: FieldSensitivityListTests. |
| **SECTION-14-MISSING-REPORTS** GL3 Nominal Activity, AR43-ECL provision matrix, AP57 Payment Run Proposal, SYS237-U permission matrix by user | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #18 (brief §14 reports 3, 43, 57 and 237), the part that had services but no report. **GL3** gives one account's opening balance, every posted line with journal, date, narrative and running balance, and the closing balance (`IGeneralLedgerReports.NominalActivityAsync`; an unknown account gives no rows). **AR43-ECL** lays out the IFRS 9 simplified matrix at the date: exposure and provision per days-past-due band, the total required, the allowance booked on the account given and the adjustment to post (`IExpectedCreditLossService.AssessAsync` with the standard matrix; AR43 stays the write-off listing). **AP57** shows what a payment run would pay per supplier and currency: invoices due by the cut-off, amount due, credits applied, tax withheld, on hold or bank change pending, deferred for lack of funds, to pay (`IPaymentRunService.ProposeAsync`). **SYS237-U** lists every active user with each permission they hold through roles, groups and overrides and the operations allowed; administrators show as holding everything. Still open from #18: A9 direct-method cash flow, A1 trial balance prior-year comparative and summary option, the rates clearance certificate document, and the thin analyses (GL11, GL20, AR34, AR35, PO134, SYS236). Test: SectionFourteenReportsTests. |
| **COMPARATIVE-TB + DIRECT-CASH-FLOW** GL1 Trial Balance - Comparative and GL-CFD Statement of Cash Flows - Direct Method | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #18 continued (brief §14 reports 1 and 9). **GL1** sets each account's balance at the date beside the same date a year earlier, with the change and balanced totals for both columns (the summary option was already GL-TBTYPE). **GL-CFD** is the direct method: in every posted journal that moves a cash account (accounts tagged cash and equivalents), each other line's opposite amount is the cash it brought in or paid out, classified as receipts from customers, payments to suppliers, payments to employees, VAT paid and refunded, cash sales and income, expenses paid in cash, or other operating; investing and financing flows by account; then net cash per section, the net change and the opening and closing cash. The lines add up exactly to the change in cash and transfers between cash accounts net out. Still open from #18: the rates clearance certificate document and the thin analyses (GL11, GL20, AR34, AR35, PO134, SYS236). Test: DirectCashFlowAndComparativeTbTests (operating 900, investing -500, financing 300, net change 700; comparative row with no prior balance). |
| **RATES-CLEARANCE-CERTIFICATE** The rates clearance certificate prints as a document (DOC33, designable) | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #18 continued (brief §14 report 153 "Rates Clearance Certificate document"; M153 was only the register). `DocumentKinds.ClearanceCertificate` (DOC33, identified by certificate number) renders through `ITextDocumentService`: the council, certificate number and issue date, the stand and ward, the ratepayer, the statement that all rates and charges are paid up to the validity date, any advance held, the validity for transfer, who issued it, and signature and stamp lines; like the other text-backed documents it can be framed with the company's own layout in the designer and printed from Print a document. Still open from #18: the thin analyses (GL11, GL20, AR34, AR35, PO134, SYS236). Tests: DocumentKindsTests (33 kinds, 21 text-backed), TransferApplicationTests (the certificate on a completed transfer prints with its stand, ward and validity). |
| **SALES-AND-SPEND-ANALYSES** AR34 adds agent, branch, item category and share of sales; AR35 adds last year, growth, last invoice and lapsed customers; PO134 analyses spend by month, supplier, category and department | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #18 continued (brief §14 reports 34, 35 and 134 asked for analysis where the reports were thin listings). **AR34** Sales Analysis with Margin now carries each line's sales agent (the customer's rep), branch (the till's branch for POS sales), item category and share of total sales next to quantity, sales, cost and margin. **AR35** becomes Top / Bottom Customers, Growth & Lapsed: ranked by sales with share and cumulative share, the same period a year earlier and the growth, and the last invoice date; customers who bought in the twelve months before the period but not in it follow as lapsed (sorting ascending in the designer gives the bottom N). **PO134** Procurement Spend Analysis is at month, supplier, item category and requesting department grain per currency, with orders, lines, items and share of the currency's spend, so the designer's grouping gives each of the brief's views. Still open from #18: GL11 management pack (budget, variance, forecast by cost centre), GL20 year-end pack (TB before and after closing), SYS236 hours active and screens used. Tests: ReceivablesReportsTests (AR35 new columns), ProcurementReportsTests (PO134 grain). |
| **MANAGEMENT-ACCOUNTS-BY-COST-CENTRE + YEAR-END-PACK** GL11-CC P&L by department and cost centre against budget with variance, YTD and forecast; GL20 shows the trial balance before and after closing, the closing entries and the retained earnings movement | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #18 continued (brief §14 reports 11 and 20). **GL11-CC** gives each income and expense account per department and cost centre: actual for the period against the active budget for the same periods, the variance (favourable positive, in each account's natural sign) and % variance, the year to date against budget, the full-year budget and a full-year forecast (year-to-date actual plus the budget still to come). GL11 stays the one-page pack. **GL20** now separates the trial balance before and after the year's closing journals (`YearEndClose`), states the result for the year without them (so a closed year still shows its profit), lists every closing entry with its account and side, and walks retained earnings from the start of the year through other movements and the amount transferred by the closing journal to the year-end balance. Build note: the first GL11-CC version made the C# compiler spin for over an hour (a long chained collection expression with a block-bodied lambda); written as a plain loop it compiles normally. Still open from #18: SYS236 hours active and screens used. Tests: ManagementByCostCentreTests (800 against 1,000: variance 200, +20.0%, forecast 2,800), YearEndTests (pack after a real close). |
| **SCREENS-USED** The desktop logs each screen opened and run; SYS236 shows screens used and actions run, SYS236-S lists the screens per user | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #18, last part (brief §14 report 236 "hours active, screens used"). Nothing recorded which screens a user worked in. `ScreenUse` (sec.screen_use: company, user, module, screen, Open or Run, when) is written by the Operations console when a screen is selected and when an action runs (best effort: logging never stops the user). **SYS236** User Activity gains Screens used and Actions run, and its hours active now count open sessions up to their last activity as well as closed ones. **SYS236-S** Screen Usage lists each screen per user with how often it was opened and run and when it was last used. Migration `AddScreenUse`. This completes #18; the remaining thirteenth-audit items start at #19 (section-S documents without renderers). Test: SectionFourteenReportsTests (screen usage rows). |
| **DOCUMENTS-TRANSFER-ASSET-LEAVE-LOAN** Stock transfer note, asset tag, leave application, leave balance certificate and loan agreement & statement print as designable documents (DOC34-DOC38) | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #19, first batch (brief §S documents that had data but no renderer). **DOC34 Stock transfer note** (by transfer number): from, via and to warehouses, item, quantity, value at cost, received date and quantity, dispatch and receipt signatures. **DOC35 Asset tag** (by asset number): a boxed tag with the company, asset number, description, class, tag number, acquisition date and a barcode of the tag number for asset counts. **DOC36 Leave application** (by employee number/start date): type, dates, days, the balance available, status and who decided, signature lines. **DOC37 Leave balance certificate** (by employee number, as at a date): accrued, taken and balance per leave type, HR certification. **DOC38 Loan agreement & statement** (by loan reference): principal, instalment, interest rate, repayment method, outstanding balance and the monthly schedule still to come, with the agreement and signatures. Like the other text-backed documents each can be framed with the company's layout and printed from Print a document. Still open from #19: proforma invoice, customer debit note, housing letters, agreements and notices, employment contract, budget forms, permits and licence certificates, EFT advice, gift voucher, student receipt, subcontractor certificate. Tests: DocumentKindsTests (38 kinds, 26 text-backed), DocumentsBatchTests. |
| **DOCUMENTS-VOUCHER-EFT-SUBCONTRACT-LICENCE** Gift voucher, EFT advice, subcontractor payment certificate and licence / permit certificate print as designable documents (DOC39-DOC42) | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #19, second batch. **DOC39 Gift voucher** (by card number): a boxed voucher with value, balance, card number, issue and expiry dates, status and a barcode of the card number. **DOC40 EFT advice** (by payment run number): the run, payment date, bank account paid from and file layout, then each included supplier with its bank, account number, currency and amount, totals per currency and two signatory lines; excluded lines are left off. **DOC41 Subcontractor payment certificate** (by certificate number): contract, subcontract and subcontractor, subcontract value, work done to date, previously certified, gross this certificate, retention, advance recovered, tax withheld, amount payable and retention held to date. **DOC42 Licence / permit certificate** (by licence number): the council licence kind as the title, holder, premises stand, activity, issue and expiry dates, renewals, fee, status and any revocation, with the display notice. Still open from #19: proforma invoice, customer debit note, housing letters, agreements and notices, employment contract, budget forms, student receipt. Tests: DocumentKindsTests (42 kinds, 30 text-backed), DocumentsBatchTests. |
| **DOCUMENTS-PROFORMA-HOUSING-LETTERS-CONTRACT** Proforma invoice, housing offer and allocation letters and employment contract print as designable documents (DOC43-DOC46) | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #19, third batch. **DOC43 Proforma invoice** (by sales order number): the order's customer, lines and amount due, marked as not a tax invoice. **DOC44 Housing offer letter** and **DOC45 Housing allocation letter** (by certificate number, dated): the applicant, waiting-list number, stand, scheme, price, a 10% deposit and the acceptance deadline, or the confirmation once accepted (an allocation letter before acceptance is refused). The letter renderer moved from the desktop into HousingLetters so the Accept or decline an offer action and the designable documents print the same letter. **DOC46 Employment contract** (by employee number): the parties, position, department and manager, employment type and dates, probation, salary, grade and notch, payment method, notice period and standard clauses, with signatures.
