Still open from #19: customer debit note, housing agreements and notices, budget forms, student receipt. Tests: DocumentKindsTests (46 kinds, 34 text-backed), DocumentsBatchTests. |
| **DOCUMENTS-AGREEMENT-NOTICE-BUDGET-STUDENT** Instalment sale agreement, instalment default notice, budget submission form and student fee receipt print as designable documents (DOC47-DOC50) | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #19, fourth batch. **DOC47 Instalment sale agreement** (by agreement number): buyer, stand, tenure, cash price, deposit, amount financed, interest and penalty terms, the full schedule (due date, instalment, interest, principal, balance) with totals, the default terms and signatures for buyer, council and witness. **DOC48 Instalment default notice** (by notice number): the stage (reminder, demand or cancellation) as the title, the agreement, buyer, arrears, days overdue and the notice text. **DOC49 Budget submission form** (by budget label, latest version): type, year, department, status, then each account and cost centre by quarter and in total, with prepared, reviewed and approved sign-offs. **DOC50 Student fee receipt** (by AR receipt number): the guardian, amount, each fee invoice it paid with the term and student from the invoice narrative, what is still due on each, and any amount held on account. Still open from #19: customer debit note (AR has no debit-note document of its own yet). Tests: DocumentKindsTests (50 kinds, 38 text-backed), DocumentsBatchTests. |
| **DOCUMENTS-CUSTOMER-DEBIT-NOTE** A customer debit note prints as a debit note through DOC11 and the designable tax invoice | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #19, last item. AR debit notes (posted on an original invoice) could not be printed: the invoice renderer only took invoices, credit notes and interest. It now takes debit notes too, titled DEBIT NOTE, with the Kind column reading "Debit note" for a company's own invoice layout; DOC11 is "Tax invoice / credit / debit note" and finds a debit note by its number. This closes #19: every §S document with data behind it now has a renderer (DOC01-DOC50). Tests: DocumentsBatchTests (debit note). |
| **DESIGNER-MODEL-EXTRAS** Detail-child band, alternating row shading, RowNumber / GroupRowNumber, top-N groups with Others, ellipses, round-cornered boxes and text at any angle | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #20 (brief §13.1 designer model). **Detail child**: a new band type printed under each detail row for the same row (a second line, a narrative, sub-lines), with its own suppress-when. **Alternating rows**: a detail band's AlternateBackground shades every second row across the printable width, under the row's own cells. **Row numbers**: RowNumber (the row in the report) and GroupRowNumber (restarting in each innermost group) can be used in any expression and are offered in the expression editor; a sub-report numbers its own rows. **Top-N groups**: a group level's TopCount keeps the largest groups within each parent, ranked by the total of TopBy (or by rows); the rest are left out, or gathered into an OthersLabel group that prints after the top groups whichever way the level sorts, its group field reading the label. **Shapes**: an Ellipse element (Draw Ellipse in the toolbox) and CornerRadius on boxes, drawn in HTML, PDF (as SVG) and the desktop viewer. **Free rotation**: Rotation takes any angle (90, 180 and 270 still turn the box), in PDF, HTML and the viewer. Also fixed the misplaced doc comment on ReportSort. Tests: DesignerModelExtrasTests. |
| **SUPPLIER-BANK-CHANGE-WORKFLOW** Supplier bank-detail changes go through the company's approval workflow when one is defined | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #22. A bank-detail change was approved by any second user, outside the workflow engine, so a company could not route it to treasury or set stages and value-free approver rules. A new workflow document type, SupplierBankChange (reference SBC- and the change id), now governs it: when the company has an active definition, the request starts the workflow with the supplier, its category and whether it is a bank account or a wallet as attributes; Approve and Reject on the change itself are refused while the workflow runs; the inbox approval applies the new details to the supplier and a rejection records the approver's reason, leaving the supplier as it was. Without a definition the second-user approval works as before. Tests: SupplierBankChangeWorkflowTests. |
| **FISCAL-WEEK-CALENDARS** 4-5-4, 5-4-4 and 13 x 4-week fiscal calendars, and 53-week years | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #23 (brief §3.4). Besides monthly and 4-4-5, a fiscal year can now be 4-5-4 or 5-4-4 weeks a quarter, or 13 periods of 4 weeks; any week calendar can carry a 53rd week in its last period (Create a fiscal year: "53 weeks"). The year-end rolls a week calendar forward on the same pattern and adds the 53rd week on its own when 52 weeks would end more than three days before the company's anchor year-end (the month and day its first week-based year ended), so the calendar keeps to its year-end instead of drifting a day or two a year; a 29 February anchor falls back to the 28th. Recognising a year's calendar now knows every pattern, with or without the extra week. Tests: FiscalCalendarTests, YearEndCalendarTests (week calendar rolling into a 53-week year). |
| **TAX-CODE-SUPPLY-AND-RETURN-BOX** Tax codes for goods or services enforced on quotes and orders; jurisdiction and return box drive TX226 VAT Return by Box | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #23. A tax code's AppliesTo (goods, services or both), Jurisdiction and ReturnBox can now be set when it is added (and are listed); a quotation or sales order line whose tax code (its own, else the item's) is for goods on a service item, or the other way round, is refused naming the item. New report **TX226 VAT Return by Box**: per jurisdiction and return box, each tax code with what it applies to, the net supplies it was charged on (sales positive, purchases negative; a journal posted under one code counts its ordinary lines) and the tax (output positive, input negative), with a payable total per jurisdiction. Tests: TaxCodeSupplyTests. |
| **ITEM-TRADE-DETAILS** An item's HS code, country of origin, brand and tax code can be set | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #23. These item fields had no way in. Item picture, description and dimensions now also takes the customs (HS) code (4 to 12 digits, dots allowed), the country of origin, the brand and the tax code its sales take, which must be an active code that applies to the item's kind (a services-only code is refused on goods); a blank field leaves a value as it is, and the list shows them. Tests: ItemTradeDetailsTests. |
| **PAY-ELEMENT-EDIT** A pay element can be edited in full from Pay elements and tax tables | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #23. An element could only have its amount, rate, formula and taxability changed (and no screen offered even that). Pay elements and tax tables has a new step, Edit element: the element is keyed in full as for a new one and everything but its code and type changes - name, calculation basis, sequence, account, amounts, formula, tax table, the bases it counts in (pensionable, social security, overtime, leave pay, terminal benefits, union dues), group, percent-of-group, floor and ceiling, rounding, department accounts, lookup table and options - checked exactly as a new element is (formula for a formula element, contribution expense account, readable lookup bands, floor not above ceiling, an account that exists). A different type is refused. Tests: PayElementEditTests. |
| **SCHOOL-LEAVER-EXTRAS-CREDIT** A pupil leaving mid-term is credited the unused transport and boarding too | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #23. A withdrawal credited the unused share of the term's tuition invoice only; transport (-TR) and boarding (-HS), billed for the whole term on their own invoices, stayed in full. On a pro-rata fee structure the withdrawal now credits each of them for the days after leaving (-TR-CR, -HS-CR) to the route's or hostel's own revenue account and sets the credit against its invoice, and the result reports what came back on them (ExtrasCredited). Tests: SchoolOptionsProRataTests (bus and boarding leaver). |
| **PREPAID-TEST-TOKEN-WARNING** A prepaid sale says when its token comes from the built-in test generator | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #23. Without a vendor's STS token generator registered, prepaid tokens come from a placeholder no meter accepts, and nothing told the cashier. The token generator now says whether its tokens are live; a sale's result carries TestToken, and Prepaid vending shows a warning above the sale (do not hand the token to the customer) and explains the test generator in its description. A vendor generator registered in its place is live by default. Tests: PrepaidVendingTests. |
| **DUPLICATE-APPLICANT-FUZZY-MATCH** Duplicate applicants matched more closely: IDs one character apart, names that sound alike or give an initial, swapped birth dates | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #23 (brief §14 H23). Records were compared only when they shared an exact ID, birth date, name key or phone, so "John Moyo" and "Jon Moyo" with IDs one character apart never met. Records now also meet on every ID with one character left out, on the name's sound (a Soundex-style code per word: Tendai / Tendayi, Dube / Dhube) and on the birth date with day and month in either order. Names are close within one edit when the shorter has under eight letters and two otherwise, when they sound the same, or when one gives an initial for a word the other spells out (T Dube / Tendai Dube); a similar name with the birth date's day and month swapped is a new reason. Tests: DuplicateApplicantsFuzzyTests (and the existing duplicate tests unchanged). |
| **CASH-BUDGET-FORECAST** B41 Cash Budget vs Actual Cash Flow runs to the year end on the cash forecast | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #23. B41 stopped at the report date. It now shows every month of the year: up to the date the actual net movement on the bank accounts, after it the cash forecast's flows dated in that month (receivables and payables falling due, saved payment runs, scheduled and manual flows), each against the cash budget with the variance; a new Actual / forecast column says which. Tests: BudgetingReportsTests (a manual flow in June lands in June's forecast). |
| **TERMINAL-TAX-TABLE** Gratuity and severance taxed on a terminal tax table above a tax-free amount | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #23 (brief §4.15.8). Terminal benefits were taxed at one flat rate typed on the form. The terminal policy now has a tax-free amount, and a termination can name a terminal tax table: gratuity plus severance above the tax-free amount is taxed on that table's bands (in force on the pay date), else at the flat rate as before; an unknown table is refused. Termination and final pay takes both; the final settlement report keeps its flat-rate parameter. Tests: TerminalBenefitsTests, TerminationTests (banded preview). |
| **BUDGET-VERSION-COMPARISON** B12 compares any two versions of a budget, per account, period or cost centre | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #23. B12 compared only each budget's latest version with the one before, per account. It now takes a budget label, a from and to version (any two, either way round) and a breakdown: per account as before, per account and period, or per account and cost centre (a new Period / cost centre column); without parameters it reads as before. Tests: BudgetingReportsTests. |
| **PAYROLL-VARIABLE-IMPORT-FORMATS** Payroll variable import reads named columns in any order and a sheet with a column per pay element | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #23 (brief §4.15.6). The import took only the template's four columns in a fixed order and split every comma, so a quoted "1,250.00" broke it. Besides the template (with or without its header), a file may now name its columns in any order (Employee, Element, Units or Hours, Amount; a Name or Department column is ignored), or be a sheet with a row per employee and a column per pay element, each filled cell a variable (units for a per-unit element, otherwise an amount) and an unknown column reported as an error. Cells are separated by commas, semicolons or tabs, quotes may hold the separator, and a comma is read only as a thousands separator (7,5 is refused, not taken as 75). The preview-and-error step and replacing the period's earlier import are unchanged. Tests: PayrollVariableCsvTests (and the existing variable import tests). This closes #23 apart from items kept for later. |
| **WORKFLOW-COMMENT-THREAD** Documents under approval carry a comment thread | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #24 (brief §7). The only words on an approval were the comment with a decision, so an approver could not ask the requester a question without approving or rejecting. A document under approval now has a thread (wf.comment, migration AddWorkflowComments): the requester, anyone who has acted on it, an approver added to it, or anyone who may approve its current stage can add a remark (up to 2,000 characters); anyone else is refused. The thread reads the remarks and every decision with its comment in time order, with user names. New screen Approvals: Discuss a document (My work), which adds a remark when one is given and shows the thread. Tests: WorkflowCommentTests. |
| **ACCOUNT-ENQUIRY-DRILL-ON** The account enquiry drills on from a journal to its lines' accounts and its source document | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #24. The nominal enquiry showed a posting's journal and a text description of its source document, and went no further. Double-clicking a journal line now moves the enquiry to that line's account for the same period, and Open document opens the journal's source document through the report drill-through - an invoice, credit or debit note as it prints, or what the drill-through finds for the number. Desktop only; covered by the build. |
| **MASTER-DATA-EXPORT-MORE** The master-data export writes thirteen registers in their import layouts | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #24. Export covered six registers (accounts, warehouses, customers, suppliers, items, employees) while the import took nearly thirty. It now also writes stock on hand (item, warehouse, quantity, average cost), fixed assets (cost, accumulated depreciation, life, accounts, residual, method, rate), staff loans still owed, cost centres, departments, enrolled students (with the guardian's account) and stands (parcel, size, cost, scheme, status), each in the layout its importer reads, so a register can be corrected in a spreadsheet or loaded into another company; every export is recorded in the audit trail as before. Tests: MasterDataExportTests. |
| **STANDS-PER-BENEFICIARY-N** A household can be allocated more than one stand where the scheme or council allows | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #14 (left over). The stands-per-beneficiary limit could be set above one but never reached: a unique index allowed one live allocation per applicant, and an applicant marked allocated was dropped from later runs. The index is no longer unique (migration AllowStandsPerBeneficiary) and, where the limit is above one, an allocated applicant stays a candidate until the household reaches it. A household's stands are now counted once each: live allocations (an accepted one stops counting once its stand has a holder, who is counted instead), and an applicant marked allocated with no allocation on record counts one. Tests: StandsPerBeneficiaryTests (a second stand under a two-stand scheme, none past the limit), and the housing allocation tests unchanged. |
| **RATEPAYER-MISMATCH** H98 Stand Holder vs Municipal Ratepayer, and the municipal link reports a ratepayer that differs | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #14 (left over). When a sale or title registration fired the municipal link for a stand already on the property register, the link said "already linked" and the bills stayed with the old ratepayer unseen. The ratepayer moves only through a transfer with a rates clearance certificate, so the link now returns RatepayerDiffers in that case instead of changing it, and new report **H98 Stand Holder vs Municipal Ratepayer** lists every stand whose housing holder is not the property's ratepayer, with any transfer application in progress and the action to take. Tests: RatepayerMismatchTests. |
| **COMMON-PARAMETERS-FINANCE** General ledger, receivables, sales order, fixed asset, budget and job costing listings take the common parameters | ✅ | Thirteenth audit #21 (brief §9). Only sales, purchasing, cash book and tax listings took the common parameters. The general ledger, receivables, sales order, fixed asset, budget and job costing listings now declare them too: a reporting currency translates their base-currency amounts at the closing, average, daily or a keyed rate (the header names the rate used), and the ledger listings that read posted lines narrow them by branch, cost centre and department and can count unposted journals. Columns already in a currency of their own (transaction, foreign, reporting) and periods, years, lives, ages, versions, scores, sizes and areas are no longer taken for money. Operational modules (payroll, housing, municipal, stock, till) keep their own parameter lists. Tests: LedgerCommonParametersTests (GL2 by cost centre and in a keyed reporting currency), ReportCommonParametersTests. |
| **REPORT-FILTERS-HONEST** Listings prompt for branch, cost centre, department and unposted only where they apply them, and the header says so | ✅ | Fourteenth audit #1 (brief §9, §14). Every listing of the finance modules asked for a branch, cost centre and department and printed "narrowed to..." in its header, but only the cash book, tax and ledger listings actually narrowed their rows - receivables, payables, sales and the rest showed every posting under a header claiming a filter. Those listings now take the reporting currency only; the dimension filters and unposted journals are prompted only on listings whose provider applies them (cash book, tax, ledger), and if they are passed to any other listing (by the API, say) the header says plainly that they do not apply. Tests: ReportCommonParametersTests (AR29/AP64 parameter lists, AR29 header). |
| **PAY-SPLIT-APPROVAL** Splitting net pay to another account needs a second user's approval | ✅ | Fourteenth audit #2 (brief §4.15.1). Bank details changed only through an approved request, but Split net pay wrote a new split straight to the employee, so one clerk could send someone's pay to any account. A split (add, change or remove) is now a bank-detail change request: Split net pay asks, the request waits under Bank detail change with a readable description ("MobileMoney 0771234567 40%"), the person who asked cannot approve it, only one split change per employee may wait at a time, and the split is written only on approval. An approved split since the last run raises the run's bank-details-changed exception like a new account does. Tests: PaymentSplitApprovalTests. |
| **ALLOCATION-APPROVAL-AND-SWAP** Approving an allocation run needs the allocation-approval right; a stand swap is a document approved by another officer | ✅ | Fourteenth audit #3 (brief §4.10.4, SOD-HSE-01). Every allocation step sat behind the right to run allocations, so the Housing.AllocationApprove permission (and the segregation rule built on it) never stopped anyone approving a run; and a stand swap moved two allottees' stands at once on one officer's say. Approving a run on the Allocation run screen now needs Housing.AllocationApprove (proposer and approver still differ). A swap is asked for as a document (hse.stand_swap gains status, requester and rejection reason; migration StandSwapApproval, with earlier swaps marked approved) and nothing moves until another officer approves it - the offers and stands are checked again at approval - or rejects it with a reason; only one swap may wait per offer. Tests: AllocationCommitteeTests (swap asked, self-approval refused, approved by another). |
| **TARIFF-VERSION-CONTROL** Tariff versions keep to unbilled periods, used versions cannot be withdrawn, and a tariff workflow puts a version in force only on approval | ✅ | Fourteenth audit #4 (brief §4.9, §7). A new tariff version went straight into force, could be dated back into a period already billed, and a version bills had been raised under could be withdrawn - leaving those bills unexplained. A version may now take effect only after the end of the latest period billed for its service (Mun.TariffBackdated), a version that bills were raised under cannot be withdrawn (Mun.TariffVersionUsed), and where the company defines a TariffVersion workflow (reference TV- and the version id) a new version is saved out of force and its approval in the inbox puts it in force. Approved master-data changes to a tariff keep their own approval. Tests: TariffVersionControlTests, TariffServiceTests. |
| **WRITE-OFF-APPROVAL** A customer bad-debt write-off is asked for by one user and written off only when another approves it | ✅ | Fourteenth audit #5 (brief §4.5, §7). The Write off step on Allocate customer items wrote the debt off at once for anyone with the allocation right; only write-offs raised from debt-collection cases went through approval. A write-off is now a request (ar.write_off_request, migration AddWriteOffRequests): it records the invoice, what is open, the bad debt account, the reason and the VAT-relief threshold, one may wait per invoice, and nothing is written off until another user decides it - Approve write-off needs the bad debt write-off approval right, a rejection needs a reason, and the person who asked cannot approve. Where the company has a write-off workflow the request (WO- and its id) goes to the inbox instead and the inbox decision writes it off. Tests: WriteOffRequestTests, the debt-collection write-off tests unchanged. |
| **STANDARD-COST-BY-REVALUATION** An item's standard cost on stock on hand changes only by revaluation | ✅ | Fourteenth audit #6 (brief §3.6). Editing an item on Add an item set its standard cost directly, so a standard-costed item with stock on hand took a new unit cost without its stock being revalued - the stock register and the stock control account then disagreed. Item edits now go through IMasterDataService.UpdateItemAsync: description, accounts, variance accounts and category change as before, and so does the standard cost while nothing is on hand, but a standard-costed item with stock on hand is refused (Master.StandardCostRevalue) and pointed to "Revalue an item's standard cost", which revalues the stock and posts the difference. Tests: ItemTradeDetailsTests (standard cost with and without stock on hand). |
| **WAITING-LIST-RENEWAL-NOTICES** Waiting-list renewal reminds, suspends, then removes - and tells the applicant each time | ✅ | Fourteenth audit #7 (brief §4.10.3). The renewal run removed an overdue applicant in one step and only wrote a notice row; nothing reached the applicant and no renewal reminder ever went out, so people lost their seniority with no proof they were contacted. Now: 30 days before the renewal falls due the applicant is reminded (once per cycle); when it is overdue they are suspended and told; and only when the suspension has stood for the grace period are they removed and told. Each notice goes by SMS through the messaging service when there is a phone number, otherwise it is marked for a letter to the address on file, and the notice records the channel and where it went (hse.applicant_notice gains channel and recipient; migration ApplicantNoticeChannel). The run reports reminders and letters to send. Tests: WaitingListRenewalTests, WaitingListControlTests. |
| **PURCHASE-VAT-TREATMENT** Supplier invoices self-assess reverse-charge VAT and split partly recoverable VAT between input tax and cost | ✅ | Fourteenth audit #8 (brief §3.5). A supplier invoice under a reverse-charge or deemed (import) code credited the supplier with the VAT and never self-assessed the output tax, and a code's recoverable percentage was ignored outside petty cash, so exempt and partly exempt bodies over-claimed input VAT. A supplier invoice now posts its tax by its code: a self-assessed code works the tax out at the code's rate when none is keyed, owes the supplier the net only, and charges the tax to the output account while claiming it as input; a code with a recoverable percentage below 100 claims that share on the input account and puts the rest on the (first) expense account as cost, analysed like it. Tests: PurchaseVatTreatmentTests (reverse charge 1,000 + 150 self-assessed; 40% recoverable 15 split 6 / 9). Per-line tax codes on mixed-rate invoices remain open. |
| **FINAL-PAY-ON-PAYE-RETURNS** A leaver's final pay is on the PAYE return and tax certificate; the certificate and YTD follow the company's tax year; only annual leave is paid out | ✅ | Fourteenth audit #9 part A (brief §4.15.3, §4.15.8). Final pay is taxed outside the payroll run, so its taxable pay, PAYE and terminal tax were missing from the PAYE return (PR27) and the employee tax certificate (PR28); the certificate and the year-to-date report started on 1 January whatever tax year the company keeps; and leave encashment paid out every leave balance, sick leave included. PR27 and PR28 now add each posted termination (pro rata salary, notice, leave, gratuity and severance as taxable; ordinary and terminal tax as deducted) in the month or tax year of its last day, a leaver with no payslip in the period still gets a row, the certificate and YTD start at the company's tax year start month, and termination pays out only the ANNUAL leave balance. Tests: TerminationTests (a 30-day sick balance not paid; PR27 and PR28 carry 1,339.86 tax). Final-pay payslip documents and POS staff-account clearance remain open. |
| **LEAVER-STAFF-ACCOUNT-CLEARANCE** A leaver who still owes on the till staff account has it on the clearance checklist, holding the final pay | ✅ | Fourteenth audit #9 part B (brief §4.15.8). Till purchases charged to an employee's staff account are recovered through payroll, but once the employee is terminated payroll no longer runs for them, so the balance was silently left on the staff purchase account. Termination now works out the staff account still owed (staff tenders to the last day less the staff-purchase deductions already made) and, when anything is owed, adds an uncleared clearance item naming the amount, so the final pay is held until it is collected or recovered. Tests: PayrollRecoveryTests (Rae leaves owing 10.00, Sam owes nothing and gets no item). A payslip document for the final pay remains open. |
| **FUND-VOTE-CONTROL** Budget control and the vote book respect the fund: one fund's appropriation never pays for another's | ✅ | Fourteenth audit #10 (brief §3.11). Budget lines carry a fund, but the posting engine's appropriation check, the budget gate's availability and the commitments ignored it, and the vote book grouped by account only, so a council's water fund vote could be spent from the general fund. Fund control now finds an appropriation only on budget lines for the line's fund (or with no fund); the gate checks a demand against its fund (under fund accounting a demand with none is from the company's default fund) and, where the budget appropriates the account by fund, counts only that fund's budget, spend and open commitments, reporting the fund on the gate line; commitments keep their fund (migration BudgetCommitmentFund) and carry it forward; and the vote book can be kept by fund (VoteBookAsync byFund; the desktop vote book uses it under fund accounting), with spend from posted journal lines and anything in an unbudgeted fund on the account's row with no fund. Tests: FundVoteControlTests (general fund refused on a water-only vote; water 1,000 less 300 spent and 200 committed leaves 500; vote book row WAT 1,000 / 300 / 200). |
| **STATEMENTS-PER-CURRENCY** Customer and supplier statements keep each currency apart: a statement per currency, each with its own balance and ageing | ✅ | Fourteenth audit #11 (brief §4.5). A trader's open items can be in more than one currency (documents raised in another currency, cross-currency allocations), but the statement summed every outstanding amount into one "balance due" in the trading currency, so 500 USD and 8,000 ZWG showed as 8,500 USD. A statement line now carries its document's currency and the statement is split: the trading-currency lines, balance and ageing, then one statement per other currency (TraderStatement.OtherCurrencies, built by TraderStatement.Split). The printed statement renders each currency as a statement of its own (with its own remittance slip for customers), the balance-forward statement is kept in the trading currency, and the statement report rows name each line's currency. Tests: StatementFormatterTests (USD 500 and ZWG 8,000 never added; ageing in USD only). |
| **UI** Analytics dashboard | ✅ | A one-glance desktop dashboard (**Enquiries → Analytics Dashboard…**) over the tested read-side services: KPI tiles for DSO/DPO/DIO and the cash conversion cycle plus the receivables/payables/inventory balances (from `IWorkingCapitalService`, year-to-date), today's cash position per bank account with unreconciled-line counts and total cash (`IBankReconciliationService.CashPositionAsync`), and the open sales pipeline by stage with the weighted forecast and win-rate (`IOpportunityService`). `AnalyticsViewModel` only formats the service results for the screen; a Refresh command reloads everything. Build-verified (WPF, no desktop test project); every figure it shows is produced by services already covered by tests. |
| **4.5** Dunning reminder letters | ✅ | Completes dunning end-to-end: assessment → a printable reminder per customer. `DunningReminder` + the pure `DunningLetterFormatter` render a letter whose wording escalates by level (friendly reminder → second reminder → final notice threatening collection), listing each overdue invoice with due date, days overdue and outstanding, and the total. `IDunningService.RenderReminderTextAsync` picks the level from the customer's oldest overdue item (refusing a customer with nothing overdue, or one still inside the first threshold, or a missing customer) and builds the letter from their AR open items. Tests: `DunningLetterFormatterTests` (3: final-notice content, escalating wording, table width) + `DunningLetterTests` (two overdue invoices at 90/31 days produce a FINAL NOTICE listing both with a 1,500 total; a clean customer and a missing customer are refused). |
| **1++** Analytics / treasury / CRM API endpoints | ✅ | Makes the recent read models consumable by dashboards and mobile clients through the JWT API. New `[Authorize]` endpoints on `One4All.Server`: `GET /api/analytics/working-capital?companyId&from&to` (DSO/DPO/DIO/CCC), `GET /api/treasury/cash-position?companyId&asAt`, `GET /api/crm/pipeline-forecast?companyId` and `GET /api/crm/conversion?companyId`. A shared `ValueResponse<T>` helper maps a value-bearing `Result<T>` to 200 / 404 / 400. The underlying services are already covered by their own tests; the endpoints are build-verified (no API test harness). |
| **4.7** Cash position (treasury view) | ✅ | The daily treasury report. `IBankReconciliationService.CashPositionAsync` lists every active bank account's GL cash-book balance as at a date (movements after the date excluded; inactive accounts excluded) with the number of imported statement lines still unreconciled, and the total cash - reusing the banking service's existing bank-account and cash-book-line helpers. Test: `CashPositionTests` posts a sale into the main bank and a transfer to the safe, plus a post-date movement that must not count, imports one unreconciled statement line, and asserts MAIN 600 (1 unreconciled) / SAFE 200 / total 800 with the closed account excluded. |
| **3** Trial balance by type in the report catalogue (GL-TBTYPE) | ✅ | Exposes the summary trial balance through the generic report engine. New `SystemReports.TrialBalanceByType()` (`GL-TBTYPE`, Type/Debit/Credit columns, AsAtDate param), registered in the runner's built-in definitions and the `ReportCatalogueSeeder`; `GeneralLedgerReportDataProvider` handles the `TrialBalanceByType` command by flattening `TrialBalanceByTypeAsync` into per-type rows. Test: `TrialBalanceByTypeReportTests` renders `GL-TBTYPE` to HTML and asserts the Asset/Income lines and the 900.00 figure appear. |
| **2** Trial balance by account type | ✅ | A summarised trial balance grouped by account type. `IGeneralLedgerReports.TrialBalanceByTypeAsync` nets each account, then nets all accounts of a type together so each type (Asset/Liability/Equity/Income/Expense) shows on a single side with type totals that still balance overall. Test: `TrialBalanceByTypeTests` posts a cash sale and a bank-paid expense and asserts Assets net to 700 Dr, Income 1000 Cr, Expense 300 Dr, and total Dr = total Cr = 1000. |
| **4.6** Supplier remittance advice (print) | ✅ | The printable remittance to complete the document set (POS receipt / payslip / remittance). The remittance *data* already existed (`ITradingReports.RemittanceAdviceAsync` - the invoices a payment settled); this adds the pure `RemittanceFormatter` that renders it to fixed-width text under a company heading, and `IRemittanceService`/`RemittanceService` which fetches the data for a payment open item and formats it. No model duplication - it reuses the existing `RemittanceAdvice`/`RemittanceLine` records. Test: `RemittanceAdviceTests` posts an AP invoice, pays it, allocates the payment (payment = debit, invoice = credit in AP), and asserts the rendered advice shows the supplier, payment number, the settled invoice and 250.00 total; a missing payment is NotFound. |
| **3.5** Document number allocator | ✅ | A cross-cutting auto-numbering service so callers no longer invent document numbers. `INumberSequenceService.AllocateAsync` loads the sequence for a company/document-type/branch (creating a sensible default - `{type}-` prefix, 5-digit padding - on first use), allocates and increments the next formatted number over the `NumberSequence` aggregate's `Allocate`, and persists it under the entity's optimistic-concurrency token. Supports per-type independent counters and yearly reset. (Note: the service namespace is `…Numbering`, not `…System`, to avoid shadowing the BCL `System` namespace inside Infrastructure.) Test: `NumberSequenceTests` allocates AR.Invoice-00001…00004 interleaved with an independent PO-00001, and confirms the counter persists across scopes (…-00005). |
| **4.7** Manual bank-statement matching | ✅ | Operator-driven reconciliation to complement the amount-based auto-match. `IBankReconciliationService.MatchLineAsync` matches an unreconciled statement line to a specific cash-book journal line on the bank's GL account - refusing a line already reconciled, a journal line that is not a posting on that account, or one already matched to another statement line - and `UnmatchLineAsync` returns a reconciled line to the pool. Test: `BankManualMatchTests` manually matches a deposit line to its cash-book posting, confirms it is reconciled, refuses a re-match, un-matches it, and refuses matching to a non-cash-book journal line. |
| **4.9** Municipal fixed-charge billing run | ✅ | The periodic billing cycle for a non-metered service (rates, refuse), on top of the per-property `BillServiceAsync`. `IMunicipalBillingService.RunFixedChargeBillingAsync` bills every property with an active link to the service at its tariff's fixed charge (zero consumption), raising each on the ratepayer's AR account, and returns the property count and total. Test: `MunicipalBillingRunTests` links two properties to a flat 15 refuse tariff (and one property without the service), runs the cycle, and asserts 2 properties billed / 30 total, the unlinked property skipped, and the trial balance balances with 30 refuse revenue. |
| **4.8** Gift-card bulk breakage run | ✅ | The periodic sweep that releases stale gift-card liabilities to income, on top of the per-card breakage. `IVoucherService.RecogniseExpiredBreakageAsync` finds every active card whose expiry has passed and that still holds a residual balance and recognises each as breakage (Dr gift-card liability / Cr breakage income) via the existing single-card path, returning the count and total. Test: `BreakageRunTests` issues two cards that expire before the run (one part-redeemed to a 20 residual) and one still valid, and asserts the run sweeps 2 cards / 70 income, leaves the live card untouched, and the trial balance balances. |
| **4.7** Petty cash (imprest system) | ✅ | Petty cash on the imprest model. `IPettyCashService`/`PettyCashService`: `DisburseAsync` posts an expense out of the float (Dr expense / Cr petty cash), and `ReimburseToFloatAsync` computes the shortfall (set float − current petty-cash balance) and, when positive, posts Dr petty cash / Cr bank to restore the float - a full float is a no-op. All postings go through the central engine. Test: `PettyCashTests` establishes a 100 float from empty, spends 30, reimburses (only the 30 shortfall is drawn), confirms a further reimbursement is a no-op, and checks the float is restored to 100, the expense is 30, and the trial balance balances. |
| **CRM** Opportunity conversion (win-rate) | ✅ | `IOpportunityService.ConversionMetricsAsync` reports how many opportunities were won versus lost, the total value won, and the win rate (won ÷ decided, zero when none are decided) - the headline sales-effectiveness metric alongside the pipeline forecast. Test (in `PipelineForecastTests`): a Won (5000) and a Lost give Won 1 / Lost 1 / WonValue 5000 / win rate 50%. |
| **CRM** Weighted-pipeline forecast | ✅ | A sales-forecast aggregate over the CRM opportunities. `IOpportunityService.PipelineForecastAsync` groups the open opportunities (won and lost excluded) by stage and reports, per stage, the count, total estimated value and total probability-weighted value (using the opportunity's own `WeightedValue = estimate × probability`), plus the overall estimated and weighted totals - the forecast number sales uses. Test: `PipelineForecastTests` seeds a Lead (1000 @ 10%), a Proposal (2000 @ 50%), a Won and a Lost, and asserts only the two open stages appear with weighted 100 and 1000, and totals of 3000 estimated / 1100 weighted. |
| **2** Working-capital metrics (DSO / DPO / DIO / CCC) | ✅ | The full working-capital efficiency set. The pure `WorkingCapital.DaysOutstanding` computes balance ÷ flow × days (zero when there is no flow or period), unit-tested. `IWorkingCapitalService`/`WorkingCapitalService` reads the AR, AP and stock control balances as at the period end and the revenue, purchase and cost-of-sales flows over the period from posted journals, and returns DSO, DPO, DIO and the **cash conversion cycle** (DSO + DIO − DPO). Cost-of-sales accounts are identified cleanly from the items' own `CostOfSalesAccountCode` and excluded from the DPO purchases so COGS is not double-counted. Tests: `WorkingCapitalTests` (the formula) + `WorkingCapitalAccountingTests` (a 1000 AR invoice, a 600 AP invoice and inventory received 100 @ 3 / issued 20 over a 31-day period give DSO 31, DPO 31, DIO 124 and CCC 124). |
| **2** Cost-centre income statement | ✅ | The sibling of the departmental P&L on the other analysis dimension. `IGeneralLedgerReports.IncomeStatementByCostCentreAsync` sums only journal lines tagged with the given cost centre, so each cost centre's contribution is reported separately and they sum to the company-wide statement. Refactored the income/expense classification into a shared `BuildIncomeStatement` helper now used by the company-wide, departmental and cost-centre statements (removing the duplicated loop). Test: `CostCentreIncomeStatementTests` posts CC1 (sales 900, expense 200) and CC2 (sales 300) and asserts CC1 nets 700, CC2 nets 300, and the company-wide statement is their 1000 sum. |
| **3** Departmental P&L in the report catalogue (GL-DEPT-IS) | ✅ | Exposes the departmental income statement as a catalogue report so it renders, prints and exports through the generic engine. New `SystemReports.DepartmentalIncomeStatement()` (`GL-DEPT-IS`, a `Department` string parameter + FromDate/ToDate), registered in the runner's built-in definitions and the `ReportCatalogueSeeder`; `GeneralLedgerReportDataProvider` handles the `DepartmentalIncomeStatement` command by flattening `IncomeStatementByDepartmentAsync` into income/expense/net-profit rows. Test: `DepartmentalIncomeStatementReportTests` renders `GL-DEPT-IS` for the NTH department to HTML through `IReportRunner` and asserts the sales figure and net profit appear. |
| **2** Departmental income statement | ✅ | A profit-and-loss restricted to one department, exploiting the department dimension journal lines already carry. `IGeneralLedgerReports.IncomeStatementByDepartmentAsync` reuses the income-statement build but sums only journal lines tagged with the given department, so each department's contribution can be reported separately and the departments sum to the company-wide statement. Test: `DepartmentalIncomeStatementTests` posts NTH (sales 1000, expense 300) and STH (sales 400), and asserts the NTH statement nets 700, STH nets 400, and the company-wide statement is their 1100 sum. |
| **2** Multi-company consolidation | ✅ | The CONSOLIDATION feature the licence gates. `IConsolidationService`/`ConsolidationService` sums each account code across a set of companies from their posted journals into one consolidated trial balance (net debit/credit per account, account names merged), requiring the companies to share a base currency (intercompany eliminations and FX translation are out of scope). Because each company's own postings balance, the consolidated trial balance balances too. No new schema - the caller supplies the company set. Tests: `ConsolidationTests` (two companies' 500 + 300 sum to an 800/800 balanced consolidated TB, a single-company consolidation shows only its own figure, and companies with different base currencies are refused). |
| **4.5** Customer dunning levels | ✅ | Escalating overdue reminders, complementing the finance charges. The pure `Dunning.LevelFor` maps days-overdue to a reminder level against configurable `DunningStages` (default 7/30/60 → first/second/final), unit-tested. `IDunningService`/`DunningService` scans unsettled AR debit open items past due, groups by customer, and assigns each a level from the age of its oldest overdue item with the overdue total and oldest-days - the basis for reminder letters and collection follow-up (read-only, never posts). Tests: `DunningTests` (the level thresholds and labels) + `DunningAccountingTests` (a 90-day customer gets a final notice, a 17-day customer a first reminder, and a 4-day customer is not dunned; notices ordered by level). |
| **3.6** VAT return settlement (posting) | ✅ | Turns the read-only VAT return into a posted settlement. `IVatReturnService`/`VatReturnService` clears every VAT-output and VAT-input control account for a period (zeroing each by the opposite of its net movement, so multi-rate charts work) and posts the net to a VAT payable/refundable account owed to the authority, through the central engine - so the control accounts start the next period clean. Returns output tax, input tax, net payable and the journal number. Because the settlement posts back into those control accounts within the period, re-running a settled period nets to zero (nothing to post), giving natural idempotency without a new entity. Test: `VatReturnSettlementTests` accrues 150 output and 60 input, settles → 90 net to VAT payable, confirms the control accounts are cleared and the trial balance balances, and that a second run is a no-op. |
| **4.15** Payroll payslip (print) | ✅ | An employee payslip, the payroll parallel to the POS receipt. `PayslipDocument` + the pure `PayslipFormatter` render a payslip to fixed-width plain text - centred company heading and "PAYSLIP", employee name/number, period and pay date, an EARNINGS section and a DEDUCTIONS section (element names resolved from the pay-element catalogue), and Gross/Total-deductions/NET PAY, every row padded to width (default 44). `IPayrollService.RenderPayslipTextAsync` reuses the same `BuildPayslip` computation the run uses, so the printed figures match the posted journal exactly, splitting the typed payslip lines into earnings and deductions. Tests: `PayslipFormatterTests` (3: content/sections/totals, width fit, right-alignment) + `PayslipTests` (a seeded employee's payslip shows basic 2,000, pension 100 and net 1,900; a missing employee is NotFound). |
| **4.18** Budget commitment control (encumbrances) | ✅ | Adds the reservation side of fund control the brief names. `BudgetCommitment` (Core, `bud.commitment`, migration `AddBudgetCommitments`) records an amount reserved against a budget line by a purchase order, with Open/Released status; `IBudgetingService.CommitAsync`/`ReleaseCommitmentAsync` raise and free them. `CheckAvailabilityAsync` now subtracts open commitments - **available = budget − actual − committed** - and `AvailabilityResult` carries the new `Committed` figure, so a second order can't over-commit the same funds. Commitments never touch the GL (control-side only, like budgets). Tests: `BudgetCommitmentTests` (an open 300 commitment drops availability from 500 to 200 and flips the pass/fail, releasing restores it, and a double release is refused); the existing `BudgetingTests` still passes unchanged (backward-compatible with no commitments). |
| **3** Cash Flow in the report catalogue (GL-CF) | ✅ | Exposes the IAS 7 cash flow statement as a catalogue report so it renders, prints and exports through the generic engine like the income statement and balance sheet. New `SystemReports.CashFlow()` definition (`GL-CF`, Activity/Amount columns, FromDate/ToDate params), wired into the report runner's built-in definitions and the `ReportCatalogueSeeder` so it appears in the WPF report viewer and the `/api/reports` listing; `GeneralLedgerReportDataProvider` handles the `CashFlow` command by flattening `CashFlowStatementAsync` into activity rows (operating/investing/financing/unclassified, net change, opening/closing cash). Test: `CashFlowReportTests` renders `GL-CF` to HTML through `IReportRunner` and asserts the operating (800), investing (−300) and net-change (500) figures appear. |
| **2** Statement of Cash Flows (IAS 7) | ✅ | The third headline financial statement, alongside the income statement and balance sheet. A new `CashFlowCategory` tag on each ledger account (None/Operating/Investing/Financing/CashAndEquivalents; migration `AddAccountCashFlowCategory`, `SetCashFlowCategory`) drives `IGeneralLedgerReports.CashFlowStatementAsync`, which derives the statement by the **movement method**: each account's net debit movement over the period is classified by its tag, and because every journal balances, the sign-reversed sum of the non-cash movements equals the change in the cash-and-equivalents accounts. The result gives Operating/Investing/Financing/Unclassified activity, opening/closing cash, and both `NetChange` and `ClassifiedChange` (equal when fully tagged) - no manual judgement, just the tag plus GL algebra. Test: `CashFlowStatementTests` posts a cash sale, expense, asset purchase and loan drawdown, then asserts Operating 900 / Investing −500 / Financing 300 and that the classified change reconciles to the 700 change in cash. |
| **4.2** Stocktake sessions (batch count + variance) | ✅ | A multi-line physical stocktake on top of the per-item `AdjustAsync`. `StocktakeSession`/`StocktakeLine` (Core, `inv.stocktake` + `inv.stocktake_line`, migration `AddStocktake`) freeze each item's on-hand quantity and average cost when the count starts, capture the physical counts, and expose the per-line variance (counted − system, valued at average cost). `IStocktakeService`/`StocktakeService`: `StartAsync` snapshots every stock balance in a warehouse into a draft session; `RecordCountAsync` captures a count; `PostAsync` adjusts each counted line's variance into stock through the inventory service (surplus increases stock, shortage decreases it) at moving-average cost, then closes the session - uncounted lines untouched, re-posting refused. Tests: `StocktakeLineTests` (3: uncounted, shortage, surplus variance) + `StocktakeTests` (a shortage and a surplus post together, stock reconciles to the counts, the books balance, and a second post is refused). |
| **4.5** AR finance charges (overdue interest) | ✅ | Raises interest on overdue customer balances. The pure `FinanceCharge.Interest` computes simple interest (overdue amount × annual rate × days/365, actual/365), unit-tested. `IFinanceChargeService`/`FinanceChargeService` scans unsettled AR debit open items whose grace-adjusted due date has passed (base currency only), accrues interest per item from its due date to the run date, sums per customer, and - when it meets the minimum charge - posts one finance-charge invoice per customer through the sales ledger (Dr AR control / Cr interest income), so the charge ages and collects like any invoice. Returns per-customer overdue amount, max days overdue, interest and document number. Tests: `FinanceChargeTests` (7: the 90-day/12% worked example, a full-year check, and the non-positive-factor cases) + `FinanceChargeAccountingTests` (an overdue customer is charged 29.59 and a finance-charge open item posted, a customer inside grace is not charged, and the trial balance balances). |
| **4.1** POS receipt (thermal print) | ✅ | A completed sale can be printed as a receipt. `PosReceiptModel` + the pure `PosReceiptFormatter` render a sale to fixed-width plain text (thermal-friendly): centred company heading and "SALES RECEIPT", receipt number + timestamp, cashier/terminal, one right-aligned line per item, Subtotal/Tax/TOTAL, the tenders and change (change suppressed when zero), and a footer - every row padded to the requested width (default 40). `IPosReceiptService`/`PosReceiptService` loads a stored sale (lines, tenders, company, cashier, terminal) and formats it. The touch terminal renders the receipt after each sale posts and offers a **Receipt** button to save it to a `.txt`. Tests: `PosReceiptFormatterTests` (4: content, width fit, right-alignment, zero-change suppression) + `PosReceiptTests` (a posted sale renders company/number/terminal/line/total/tender/change; a missing sale is NotFound). |
| **1++** Offline outbox admin + API | ✅ | Operationalises the offline machinery. New `OfflineOperation.Requeue()` (a dead-lettered op back to Pending, attempts/error cleared) and `Discard()` (→ new `Discarded` status, kept for audit); both only valid from Failed. `IOfflineAdminService`/`OfflineAdminService` (Application, unit-tested over a fake outbox) lists failed operations and requeues/discards by id with NotFound/Rule results; `IOfflineOutbox` gained `GetByStatusAsync`/`FindAsync`. The `One4All.Server` API exposes it under `[Authorize]`: `POST /api/offline/sync` (drain → report), `GET /api/offline/failed`, and `POST /api/offline/{id}/requeue` / `…/discard` (mapping NotFound→404, Rule→409). Tests: domain transitions + `OfflineAdminServiceTests` (list/requeue/discard, not-found, non-failed rule) and `OfflineAdminTests` (end-to-end over the real DB: dead-letter → list → requeue → re-fail → discard → never re-drained). API endpoints build-verified. |
| **4.1** POS cash-up / Z reconciliation | ✅ | Closing a till can now reconcile the drawer. `IPosService.CashUpAndCloseAsync(CashUpRequest)` computes the expected cash (opening float + cash received − change given, from the session's cash tenders) via the unit-tested pure `CashUpCalculation`, compares it to the counted cash, and posts the over/short variance to a cash-over/short GL account against the till cash account before taking the Z-read - an overage debits cash / credits over-short, a shortage the reverse, and a zero variance posts no journal. Returns expected/counted/variance, the variance journal number and the Z number. The touch terminal's **Z / Close** uses it when a counted-cash amount is entered (with editable cash and over/short accounts, the latter pre-filled from the chart), else falls back to a plain Z-read. Tests: `CashUpCalculationTests` (3: expected, overage, shortage) + `PosCashUpTests` (over-variance posts a balanced journal and closes Z#1, a re-cash-up is rejected, an exact count posts no journal and closes Z#2, and the trial balance still balances). |
| **6B** Terminal offline capture + reconnect sync | ✅ | Wires the touch terminal to the offline machinery so a dropped connection no longer loses a sale. When `CompleteSaleAsync` throws, `OfflineDetection.IsConnectivityError` (matches `DbException`/`SocketException`/`TimeoutException` through the inner-exception chain - so a connection loss is queued, but a domain failure like a closed period is surfaced) decides: a connectivity failure captures the sale into a terminal-local queue instead. `ILocalOfflineQueue` / `FileLocalOfflineQueue` is a DB-independent, one-file-per-entry store (default `%LOCALAPPDATA%/One4All/offline-queue`, enqueue-once by operation id, survives restart) - needed because when Postgres itself is down the *central* outbox can't be written. On reconnect (opening the till, or the new **Sync Offline** button) the terminal hands each local entry to the durable central `IOfflineOutbox` (idempotent) and drains it via `IOfflineSyncService`, then clears the local copy; an `OFFLINE • N queued` badge and the pending count keep the cashier informed. Tests: `OfflineDetectionTests` (4: DbException/socket/timeout/wrapped are connectivity, domain/unrelated/null are not) + `FileLocalOfflineQueueTests` (5: round-trip, enqueue-once, capture order, durability across instances, corrupt-file tolerance). |
| **6B** Offline POS sync (outbox + replay) | ✅ | The offline half of 6B: operations captured while a terminal is offline are queued and replayed to the central database when it reconnects. `OfflineOperation` (Core, `sync.offline_operation`) is the outbox row - a client-generated `OperationId` idempotency key, `Kind`, JSON payload, status (Pending/Synced/Failed), attempts and last error. `OfflineSyncService` (Application, unit-tested with fakes) drains pending operations in capture order through the handler registered for each `Kind`: success → synced; failure → retried until an attempt ceiling then dead-lettered; a throwing handler is isolated (retryable, never aborts the run); an unknown kind is dead-lettered immediately. `EfOfflineOutbox` (Infrastructure) persists the queue with a unique index on `OperationId` (enqueue-once) and a `(status, created)` index for the drain; `PosSaleOfflineHandler` replays a `PosSaleEnvelope` (a serializable snapshot that rebuilds the `CompleteSaleRequest`) through `IPosService`, **idempotent by sale number** so a redelivery never double-posts. Migration `AddOfflineOutbox`. Tests: `OfflineSyncServiceTests` (6: replay, no-handler dead-letter, retry-then-dead-letter, throwing handler, capture order, enqueue-once) + `PosOfflineSyncTests` (end-to-end: capture → enqueue → drain posts once, duplicate sale-number op is an idempotent no-op, unknown kind dead-lettered, empty second drain). |
| **6B** Touch POS terminal (shell) | ✅ | Full-screen WPF touch terminal (**Transactions → Point of Sale - Touch Terminal…**) over the existing `IPosService`. The cart/tender maths are a dependency-free, unit-tested engine `PosTicket` (Application): tax-exclusive line net/tax/gross, quantity merging, tender tracking with balance/change, and `BuildSale` → a **balanced** `CompleteSaleRequest` (over-tender emitted as an `IsChange` cash tender so net tender == gross, the invariant the service enforces). `PosTerminalViewModel` is a thin shell over it: loads priced menu items as category-filtered product buttons, resolves the tax rate per line from the item's `TaxCode`, self-provisions a default terminal if none exists, opens/closes the till (X/Z reads), takes Cash/Card tenders via a numeric pad, and completes the sale (posting the revenue/tax/tender journal). GL tender accounts (cash/card/tax) are pre-filled from the chart by name and editable. Debug+Release clean; the WPF screen is verified by build + run (no desktop test project), the engine by `PosTicketTests` (9 tests: line/ticket maths, tender/change, balanced request, missing-account and underpaid failures). |
| **1++** WMI machine-fingerprint provider | ✅ | Implemented the Windows machine-id provider the licensing layer was designed for: `WmiMachineIdProvider : IMachineIdProvider` gathers stable identifiers via WMI (system/motherboard UUID, baseboard & BIOS serials, Windows install serial, the deterministically-chosen primary physical NIC MAC, and the system-drive volume serial), discarding OEM placeholder values and degrading to portable environment components off-Windows - every WMI query is guarded so it never throws or blocks startup. `LicenceService` now fingerprints the machine once (lazy, cached) and validates the installed licence against it, so machine-bound licences actually bind; the serialized fingerprint is exposed as `ILicenceService.MachineFingerprint` for System Manager to display. New `System.Management` package (Infrastructure); platform APIs guarded with `[SupportedOSPlatform("windows")]` + `OperatingSystem.IsWindows()` (no CA1416). Tests: `MachineIdBindingTests` (provider → fingerprint → validate: bound machine passes, one component changing still passes, different machine rejected) + `WmiMachineIdProviderTests` (real host: no-throw, stable, parseable fingerprint, Windows enrichment). |
| **3E++** XLSX report export | ✅ | New `ReportFormat.Xlsx` + `XlsxReportExporter` - exports report rows to a valid single-sheet `.xlsx` (OpenXML SpreadsheetML) built with the BCL `ZipArchive` only (no new dependency): a bold header row of field names, one row per record, numeric values written as numbers (so Excel totals/formats them) and everything else as inline strings. Wired into `ReportRunner` (like CSV) and offered as an export format in the WPF report viewer. Tests: exporter unit tests (valid package, typed cells) + an accounting test rendering GL-COA to a readable workbook through the runner. |
| **3E** WPF viewer + designer | ✅ | **Viewer and designer both DONE.** Designer: `ReportDesignerWindow` + `ReportDesignerViewModel` - open a catalogue listing report, **drag its column blocks left/right on a design canvas to reorder** (WPF `DragDrop`), edit each column's field/header/width/money in a property panel, add/remove/move columns, and **Save as a company-scoped report** that overrides or varies the system one (rebuilt through `SystemReports.Listing`, reusing the source's data command + parameters, so it renders identically and the existing provider serves it). New `SystemReports.ExtractListingColumns`/`CommandOf` decompose a listing definition; a unit test round-trips extract → edit/reorder → rebuild. Opened from **Reports → Report Designer…**. Viewer: `ReportViewerWindow` + `ReportViewerViewModel` (Desktop): pick a catalogue report (system `ReportDefinitions`), fill parameters (prompts built from the definition, typed by `ParameterType`), **Run** to preview the HTML in a `WebBrowser`, and **Save** to export HTML/PDF/CSV/text via `SaveFileDialog` - all through the same `IReportRunner` the server uses. Opened from the shell **Reports → Report Viewer…** (`ShellWindow` now receives `IServiceProvider`). Builds Debug+Release; the runner it drives is covered by the accounting suite. |

**Phase 3 status:** the reporting **engine** (definition model, expression engine, banded rendering to HTML/PDF/CSV/ESC-POS, persistence, bursting, and the first bound report) is complete and tested - later modules can ship reports on it now. The visual WPF designer/viewer is the remaining piece.

## Phase 4 - AR + AP + Cash Book
| Increment | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **4A/4B/4C** Trader master + sub-ledgers + allocation | ✅ | `Customer`/`Supplier` master; unified `OpenItem`/`Allocation` sub-ledger (`trd` schema, AR+AP). `ISalesLedgerService` (invoice, receipt) + `IPurchaseLedgerService` (invoice, payment) post through the central engine and create open items. `IAllocationService` matches debit↔credit items, reduces outstanding, and **realises FX** from differing base rates (posts to FX gain/loss + control). `ITradingReports`: aged debtors/creditors + open-item customer statement. Migration `AddTradingLedgers`. 3 trading tests green (settle + no FX, cross-rate realised gain 10, AP ageing) |
| **4E+** Customer/supplier contra | ✅ | `IAllocationService.ContraAsync` offsets a debtor invoice against a creditor invoice for the same trading partner: raises an AR receipt and an AP payment for the amount through a **contra clearing account** (which nets to zero) and allocates each against the named items - settling both sides without cash moving. Refuses an amount over either item's outstanding. Reuses the receipt/payment/allocation machinery; Contra Clearing account (1900) added to the Scenario; no migration. Test: AR invoice 300 + AP invoice 200 for one partner → contra 200 (over-contra refused) → AR outstanding 100, AP cleared, debtor ages 100, creditor drops off, clearing nets to zero; TB balances. |
| **4F++** Remittance advice | ✅ | `ITradingReports.RemittanceAdviceAsync(paymentOpenItemId)` builds a supplier remittance advice from the payment's AP allocations - supplier, payment number/date/currency, and the invoices it settled (number, date, allocated amount) with the total. Read-side over the `Allocation` links; no migration. Test: two AP invoices (100, 150) settled by one 250 payment → advice lists both invoices totalling 250; a non-payment item returns null. |
| **4F+** Supplier statement | ✅ | `ITradingReports.SupplierStatementAsync` mirrors the customer statement on the AP side: outstanding payables for a supplier as open-item lines (date, type, doc, due, amount, outstanding) with the balance - a payable invoice is the positive balance, a payment/credit note reduces it; fully-settled items drop off. No migration. Test: two AP invoices (100, 200) + a 60 payment allocated to the first → statement shows P1 outstanding 40 and P2 200, balance 240; unknown supplier → null. |
| **4A++** Bad-debt write-off | ✅ | `ISalesLedgerService.WriteOffAsync` writes off an uncollectable AR open item: posts the remaining outstanding to a bad-debt-expense account (Dr bad debt / Cr AR control, via the receipt path) and allocates it against the invoice so it settles and stops ageing. Requires a reason; refuses a settled item. Reuses the receipt + allocation machinery (no new posting path). Bad Debts account (6400) added to the Scenario; no migration. Test: 300 invoice → write off → AR item settled, debtor drops off ageing, bad-debt expense Dr 300, revenue stands, AR control cleared; re-write-off and blank reason refused; TB balances. |
| **4A+** Customer credit control | ✅ | `PostInvoiceAsync` (AR) now enforces the existing `Customer.OnHold` and `Customer.CreditLimit`: an on-hold customer is refused any new invoice, and an invoice that would take the customer's net AR outstanding (open-item debits − credits, base currency) over the credit limit is refused (limit 0 = unlimited, so existing flows are unaffected). Also protects restaurant house-account billing, which posts through this path. No migration. Test: on-hold customer refused; a 1000-limit customer takes 600 (ok), 500 more (→1100, refused), 400 more (→1000 exactly, ok); debtors show 1000, the held customer nothing. |
| **4D** Credit notes (AR + AP) | ✅ | `PostCreditNoteAsync` on both ledgers (reverse of an invoice), allocatable like any credit item. Test: sales credit note reduces the debtor to 95 |
| **4E** Payment run + bank reconciliation | ✅ | `IPaymentRunService` (selects due AP invoices, pays per supplier through the engine, allocates, generates a bank EFT file); `IBankReconciliationService` (import statement lines, auto-match to GL cash-book postings by amount, reconciliation statement with GL vs statement balance + unpresented items). `AddCashBookAndCreditNotes` migration (`cb.bank_account`/`cb.bank_statement_line`). 2 tests green (payment run pays 300 + EFT; bank rec auto-matches, difference 0) |
| **4E++** Bank transfer | ✅ | `IBankingService.TransferAsync` moves funds between two of the company's own cash/bank accounts (Dr destination / Cr source, source module CB). Refuses a non-positive amount or the same account both sides. No migration. Test: 500 bank → cash on hand; cash Dr 500, bank Cr 500; non-positive + same-account refused; TB balances. |
| **4F** Statement rendering | ✅ | `SystemReports.CustomerStatement()` + `IFinancialReportRenderer.RenderCustomerStatementAsync` renders a customer's open-item statement to HTML/PDF/CSV/text on the Phase 3 engine (burstable for bulk). Test: statement renders with balance 65. Dunning letters are a thin variant of the statement (follow-up). |

_Later addition (RCP-7 foundation):_ `IAllocationService.UnallocateAsync` - reverses an allocation, restoring both items to their pre-allocation outstanding (`OpenItem.RestoreOutstanding`), reversing any realised FX (netting the original gain/loss to nil in the same FX account), and marking the `Allocation` reversed (retained, never deleted; re-reversal refused). Tested: same-currency restore (invoice 400 / receipt 300 both restored, allocation flagged) and cross-currency FX reversal (FX gain/loss nets to zero, TB balances). `AddAllocationReversal` migration.

**Phase 4 status: COMPLETE.** AR/AP/Cash Book - trader master, invoice/receipt/payment/credit-note posting, open-item allocation with realised FX, ageing, **statements rendered to PDF**, **payment runs with EFT**, and **bank reconciliation**. 23 accounting tests. (Dunning letters + a full remaining-report seed are light follow-ups on the same engine.)

## Phase 5 - Inventory + Procurement + Workflow
| Increment | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **5A** Generic Workflow/approval engine (§7) | ✅ | `WorkflowDefinition`/`WorkflowStage` (ordered stages, quorum, value-band routing, eligible approvers), `WorkflowInstance` with pure advancement logic (approve/quorum/advance, reject, return, resubmit), `IWorkflowService` (start/act/status) with eligibility enforcement. Reusable by requisitions/POs/journals/payment-runs/budgets/master-data. `AddWorkflowEngine` migration. 7 unit + 1 integration test green |
| **5B** Inventory (items, warehouses, costing, movements) | ✅ | `Item`/`Warehouse` master; `StockBalance` with **moving weighted-average** cost; `StockMovement` history; `IInventoryService` (receive → Dr stock/Cr offset + recompute average; issue → Dr COGS/Cr stock at average; stock valuation). `AddInventory` migration. Test: receive 100@2 + 100@3 → avg 2.50, issue 50 → COGS 125, valuation 375 |
| **5B++++++** Negative-stock guard | ✅ | `IssueAsync` refuses an issue that would take the balance below zero (prevents overselling) unless `IssueStockRequest.AllowNegative` is set. **Committed transactions pass it** - a POS sale and a restaurant bill are for goods already served, so they still post and drive stock negative (reconcile by stocktake); **deliberate actions keep the guard** - sales-order despatch, manual issues and restaurant wastage refuse an over-issue. No migration. Tests: 10 on hand → issue 15 refused / issue 10 ok / issue 3 with `AllowNegative` → −3; a 3-burger bill on 1 patty of stock still posts (patty → −2) while wastage of 5 missing pattties is refused. |
| **5B++++++~** Slow-moving stock report | ✅ | New **INV-SLOW** report lists on-hand items (per warehouse) with no stock movement since a given `SinceDate` - the capital tied up in dead stock, ranked by value, with each item's last-movement date. Read-side, no migration. Test: a widget last received in January and a fast mover received in April → with a 1-March cutoff, only the January item is listed (value 200). |
| **5B+++++** Reorder report | ✅ | New **INV-REORDER** report lists items at or below their `ReorderLevel` (aggregating on-hand across warehouses) with the suggested reorder quantity (`ReorderQuantity`, else the shortfall) - a purchasing aid. Items with no reorder policy (level 0) are excluded. Read-side, no migration. Test: low item (15 ≤ 20) listed with suggested 100; a healthy item (50 > 20) and a no-policy item excluded. |
| **5B++++** Stock movements report | ✅ | New **INV-MOVE** report lists every stock movement over a period - receipts, issues, transfers, adjustments and reversals - with item, warehouse, type, signed qty/value and reference (the inventory transaction history behind the valuation). Read-side via `VerticalReportDataProvider`, no migration. Test: receive 100 + transfer 40 + issue 10 + adjust 58 → INV-MOVE shows all four types and their references. |
| **5B+++** Stocktake adjustment | ✅ | `IInventoryService.AdjustAsync` reconciles stock to a physical count: computes the variance vs on-hand and posts it at moving-average cost against a **stock-adjustment account** - a surplus increases stock (Dr stock / Cr adjustment), a shortage decreases it (Dr adjustment / Cr stock); a nil variance is a no-op; a zero cost-basis moves quantity only. Records an Adjustment movement, returns the signed variance value. No migration; Stock Adjustment account (5300) added to the Scenario. Test: 100 bolts @4 → count 95 (shortage −20), nil recount (no-op), count 98 (surplus +12) → on-hand 98 valued 392, adjustment account nets 8 Dr; TB balances. |
| **5B++** Stock transfer between warehouses | ✅ | `IInventoryService.TransferAsync` moves stock from one warehouse to another at the source's moving-average cost. Both warehouses share one stock-control account, so the transfer is **GL-neutral** (no journal) - it moves quantity and value between the balances and records paired Transfer movements. Refuses a same-warehouse move or an over-quantity transfer. No migration. Test: receive 100 flour @3 in the store, transfer 30 to the kitchen → store 70 (value 210) / kitchen 30 (value 90), stock-control account unchanged, TB balances. |
| **5B+** Stock issue reversal | ✅ | `IInventoryService.ReverseIssueAsync(movementId)` returns an issued quantity to the warehouse at the **original** unit cost applied and posts the exact inverse (Dr stock control / Cr cost of sales), recording a `StockMovement` of new type **Reversal** and flagging the original `IsReversed` (a double reversal, or reversing a non-issue, is refused). Foundational primitive for undoing bills/sales/despatches. `AddStockMovementReversal` migration. Test: receive 100@5, issue 30 (COGS 150, on-hand 70) → reverse it → on-hand back to 100, COGS nets to zero, re-reversal and reversing a receipt both refused; TB balances. |
| **5C** Procurement (PO→GRN→3-way match) | ✅ | `PurchaseOrder`/`PurchaseOrderLine` (ordered/received/invoiced qty + status), `GoodsReceivedNote`/`GrnLine`; `IProcurementService`: create PO, receive goods (GRN accrues **GRNI** via the inventory receipt Dr stock/Cr GRNI + moving-average), and **3-way invoice match** (qty + price tolerance) clearing GRNI→AP and raising an AP open item. `AddProcurement` migration. Test: PO 100@2 → GRN accrues 200 → price mismatch rejected → matched invoice clears to AP 200 (creditors 200, stock 100@2=200). Requisition approval routes through the §7 workflow engine (5A). |

**Phase 5 status: COMPLETE.** Generic approval workflow, inventory with moving weighted-average costing, and procurement (PO→GRN→3-way match) - all integrated through the posting engine and tested. (Requisition UI, RFQ/tender, landed costs, batch/serial, budget-check-at-gates are follow-ups on this base.)

## Phase 6 - POS
| Increment | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **6A** POS transaction/accounting core | ✅ | `PosTerminal`, `TillSession` (float, X/Z read), `Sale`/`SaleLine`/`SaleTender`; `IPosService`: open till, **complete sale** (multi-currency tenders + change → Dr tenders/Cr revenue/Cr output VAT; then Dr COGS/Cr stock via the inventory issue), **X-read** (non-resetting) and **Z-read** (sequential, immutable, closes the session). `AddPointOfSale` migration. Test: sale 10@2 net 20+VAT 3, cash 25 with 2 change → gross 23, COGS via stock issue, X/Z reads, stock 100→90. Fixed a posting-engine bug (same account on multiple journal lines duplicated period balances). |
| **6A+** POS sale void / refund | ✅ | `IPosService.VoidSaleAsync` reverses a completed sale rung in error: posts the exact inverse of the sale's tender/revenue/tax journal (refunding the tenders, unwinding revenue and output VAT) - reconstructed from the persisted `Sale` lines/tenders - and reverses each cost-of-sales stock issue via `IInventoryService.ReverseIssueAsync` (found by the sale number), marking the sale `Voided`. Requires a reason; refuses a non-completed sale. No migration (reuses the existing `Voided` status + inventory reversal primitive). Test: sell 10@2 (net 20 + VAT 3, cash 23, COGS 10, stock 100→90) → void → revenue/VAT/COGS net to zero, stock back to 100, re-void refused; TB balances. |
| **6B** Full-screen touch POS UI + offline sync | ⬜ | Large WPF UI follow-up (like the report designer); the transaction engine is complete |

**Phase 6 status:** POS transaction + accounting core complete and tested (till sessions, multi-currency tendering, COGS, X/Z reads). The full-screen touch UI + offline sync are the remaining UI work.

Test: issue 50 → redeem 20 → over-redeem rejected → breakage 30. |

Refuses more than the despatched-uninvoiced balance or a blank reason. No migration. Test: order 20 → despatch 20 (stock 80, COGS 40) → return 8 (over-return + blank reason refused) → stock 88, line despatched 12, COGS nets to 24 Dr; TB balances. |

## Phase 8 - Municipality

Tests: 6 unit (stepped 5→7.5, 25→25, 40→45; per-unit; rate-per-valuation; minimum-charge floor) + 1 accounting (stepped water: read 25 → consumption 25 → bill $25 → debtors $25, trial balance balances). |

Test: parcel 100k → 4 stands @25k → sell 1 for 40k (COGS 25k, debtors 40k) → cession blocked without clearance then allowed; ownership chain verifies; trial balance balances. |

**Phase 8 status: COMPLETE.** Municipal billing (tariff engine + property/meter register + consumption/valuation billing→AR) and Housing land-bank (IAS 2 WIP→stands→COGS + tamper-evident ownership chain + cession controls). 124 tests green (85 unit + 9 integration + 30 accounting). Deferred depth: waiting-list allocation/ballot run, instalment schedules, council rental/voids, servicing-cost apportionment from job costing, housing permissions wiring.

## Phase 11 - Fixed Assets + CRM
| Increment | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **11A** Fixed Assets (§4.14) | ✅ | `fa` schema. `FixedAsset` (cost, residual, accumulated depreciation, NBV; **straight-line** and **reducing-balance** methods, per-asset accounts). `IFixedAssetService`: **register** (capitalise Dr cost/Cr funding), **monthly depreciation run** (one journal across all active assets, Dr expense/Cr accumulated, never below residual), **dispose** (clear cost + accumulated, take proceeds, recognise profit/loss as the balancing figure). `AddFixedAssetsAndCrm` migration. Test: van 24k SL/24m → 3 monthly runs @1k → dispose for 22k (NBV 21k → gain 1k); cost & accum cleared, gain 1k, expense 3k, TB balances. |
| **11A++** Fixed-asset transfer | ✅ | `IFixedAssetService.TransferAsync` moves an asset to a new location and/or custodian (asset-register movement - operational, no GL); a null attribute is left unchanged; a disposed asset is refused. No migration. Test: transfer location + custodian, then a custodian-only transfer leaves the location unchanged; empty transfer refused. |
| **11A+** Fixed-asset revaluation (IAS 16) | ✅ | `IFixedAssetService.RevalueAsync` revalues an active asset to a new carrying amount: an **uplift** raises the asset cost against a revaluation-reserve equity account (Dr cost / Cr reserve), a **write-down** reduces it against an impairment-expense account (Dr expense / Cr cost); accumulated depreciation is unchanged so NBV moves by the delta and future depreciation follows the revalued amount. Nil-delta is a no-op; a disposed asset or a negative carrying amount is refused. Revaluation Reserve (3300) + Impairment Loss (6500) added to the Scenario; no migration. Test: land 100k → uplift to 130k (reserve Cr 30k) → write-down to 120k (impairment Dr 10k) → cost 120k, NBV 120k; negative refused; TB balances. |
| **11B** CRM & Service Desk (§4.16, lightweight) | ✅ | `crm` schema. `Opportunity` pipeline (Lead→Qualified→Proposal→Negotiation→Won/Lost, stage-default probabilities, **probability-weighted value**, open/closed). 4 unit tests. |
| **11B++** CRM pipeline report | ✅ | New **CRM-PIPE** report lists open opportunities (excludes Won/Lost) with stage, win probability, estimated value and probability-weighted value, ranked by estimate. Read-side via `VerticalReportDataProvider`, no migration. Test: a Proposal-stage opp (10,000 @ 50%) shows weighted 5,000; Won and Lost opps excluded. |
| **11B+** Opportunity → sales-order conversion | ✅ | `IOpportunityService.ConvertToSalesOrderAsync` turns a won opportunity into a sales order for its linked customer (creates the order via the SOP service with the quoted lines, marks the opportunity Won) - ties CRM to the order-to-cash flow. Refuses a lost opportunity, one with no linked customer, or empty lines. No migration; DI registered. Test: convert a negotiation-stage opp with a customer → sales order raised + opp Won; an opp with no customer is refused. |

**Phase 11 status:** Fixed-asset register with depreciation runs and disposals through the engine, plus a lightweight CRM opportunity pipeline. Deferred depth: revaluation (IAS 16)/impairment (IAS 36), units-of-production, componentisation, CWIP capitalisation, asset verification/count, part-period conventions; CRM activities/quotation links/service-desk tickets.
